# hopper glossary

The application's words. Code, tests, events, API fields and UI use these and no
synonyms. Rename here first, in the same commit as everything else.

| term | meaning | not |
|------|---------|-----|
| **Job** | One unit of work pushed through the API: an executor name, a payload, a priority. | task, run |
| **Pointed job** | A record that points at work in any GitHub repository, owned or not (issue #425, tracking issue #424): its **repository** (`owner/name`), its **pointer** — an issue or a pull request, by number and URL —, its **directive** (what to do there), the lane that works it, a **pointed job status** and the **source** that made it (`api`, or a job source). A job the hopper already has, whose item names a repository and an issue, reads as one, its status from the job (`pointedJobOf`). A record only: nothing runs one yet. `src/domain/pointed-jobs.ts`. | target job, tracked job |
| **Direct job** | A job a person submits straight to the hopper, with no GitHub issue behind it (issue #901): a **title**, a **brief** (what to do), routing **labels**, the **authorizations** the submitter gives it (`vault` among them is the submitter's **vault grant** for its item), and optionally the one **repository** its work may open a pull request on. Kept as a record (`direct_jobs`); its job comes from the **`direct` job source**, through the same gates as any job. Its brief and results stay in the hopper. A pull request its job opens on that repository is followed, tended and merged in yolo mode as a job of an issue's is, with the user's own GitHub connection (issue #1068). `src/domain/direct-jobs.ts`, `src/direct-jobs/`. | manual job, ad-hoc job, private issue |
| **Direct query** | A question a caller asks the hopper straight, with no GitHub issue behind it (issue #2184): a **direct job** with a `query` — the caller's **request id** (the same id again answers the query kept), the **callback** (the name of a **webhook subscription**), and **hints** (repositories and clusters to look at first, which allow nothing). Its job only looks: routed by the label `direct:query`, no vault grant, no repository, and the GitHub proxy and the git proxy refuse its writes and pushes (`LOOK_ONLY`). Where it stands: `accepted`, `running`, `waiting`, `answered`, `failed`, `cancelled`. Its answer is its job's result, kept in the hopper and sent as `direct_query.answered`; a question its job asks that reaches a person is sent as `direct_query.question_asked`. Its UI home is the Ask a question tab of **Ask hopper** (`#ask/direct-queries`; `#direct-queries` and `#ask/question` still open it, issues #2375, #2374). `src/domain/direct-jobs.ts`, `src/http/ui/direct-queries.ts`, `ui/src/views/direct-queries.tsx`. | ad-hoc question, chat request, lookup job |
| **Ask hopper** (view) | The UI's one home of **direct jobs** and **direct queries** (issue #2375, `#ask`, `ui/src/views/ask.tsx`), a merged page (issue #2374): two tabs, **Run a job** (`#ask/direct-jobs`, submit a direct job and read them back) and **Ask a question** (`#ask/direct-queries`, a direct query; `#ask/question` still opens it). It was the Direct jobs and Direct queries views; `#direct-jobs` and `#direct-queries` still open the matching tab. A UI name only: the API, events and code keep *direct job* and *direct query*. | Direct jobs view, Direct queries view, chat |
| **Callback** (of a direct query) | The **webhook subscription** a **direct query** names: its `direct_query.*` events are delivered to it whatever events it lists, signed with its own signing secret, kept and retried as every delivery is (issue #2184). A person sets its URL and secret through the UI session; a caller only names it. | reply URL, response hook |
| **Promotion** (to GitHub) | A person's act of posting a finished **direct job**'s outputs to GitHub (issue #946): a new issue, or a comment on an issue they pick, on a repository they choose, posted with their own GitHub connection after a **preview** they edit and confirm. It carries the title, the brief and the results cleaned, the routing labels (never the hopper's own), the chosen **artifacts** as links that need a hopper sign-in (or, ticked, as **committed artifacts**), who promoted it, and the requester only when asked. One is live per direct job: made again, it edits what it posted. A **revoke** ends the link in the hopper and unpublishes nothing. Kept as a record (`direct_job_promotions`), never with the text. `src/domain/promotions.ts`, `src/promotions/`. | publish, export, share (an artifact's **share** is inside the hopper) |
| **File an issue** | A person's act of filing an issue that their own signed-in AI connection drafted (issue #471): they describe the problem or request, attach a job's context if they want, see exactly what is sent, and get an **issue draft** back, which they read, edit, draft again or discard; only on the **confirm step**'s Confirm is it filed, with their own GitHub connection, on one of their **job repositories**, with no label and no assignee, so it never queues a job. The **draft constraints** run on the draft and again on the edited text; the suggested labels are dropped. `src/domain/issue-drafts.ts`, `src/issue-drafts/`. | AI issue, auto-file, report (Report a bug is its own act) |
| **Preview** (promotion) | What a **promotion** would post, answered before anything is posted (issue #946): the text with secrets and personal data masked, the labels, the artifacts, the footer, the repository's visibility, what the scan found and what a revoke does. The person edits it, then confirms. | draft (a draft pull request is GitHub's), dry run |
| **Committed artifact** (promotion) | A small text **artifact** (at most 256 KiB) a person ticked in a **promotion**'s **preview** (issue #946, part 2): scanned as the text is, committed with their own GitHub connection to the promotion's branch `hopper-promotion/<direct job>`, never the default branch, and linked there, so it opens with no hopper sign-in. Any other artifact is linked only. | attachment (GitHub's API takes none), upload |
| **Stale** (promotion) | An **artifact** a live **promotion** linked that has a newer **revision** now (issue #946): GitHub shows the older one until the person promotes again. | outdated, out of sync |
| **Promotion offer** | A finished **direct job** with no live **promotion** that is shown first, for a person to promote (issue #946): `asked` when its submitter ticked "promote when done", `suggested` when its job made an **artifact**. Nothing is posted by the offer. | auto-promote, auto-publish |
| **Search** | Finding the user's jobs, artifacts, infra plans, issues and pull requests by their words, with filters and facets (issue #945): `GET /api/search`, its type-ahead `GET /api/search/suggest`. Over the **search index**; each hit decided by Access (`search_hit#can_view`). `src/search/`, `src/domain/search.ts`. | keyword search, lookup, query |
| **Search view** | The UI's view of a search (`#search/<search>`, issue #945): the hits with their matched words marked, the facets that filter, why search is degraded. The **search box** in the header opens it, and suggests titles as a person types. `ui/src/views/search.tsx`, `ui/src/app/search-box.tsx`. | search page, results page, search bar |
| **Search index** | The Solr core `hopper` (compose service `solr`) that search reads: a copy of what Postgres holds, rebuilt from it at any time, so losing it loses nothing. Each **search document** carries its hopper's instance id and its user. | Solr collection, search database |
| **Search document** | One record in the search index, built from the database each time it is written, every text masked. Its type: `job` — its title, brief, status, labels, repository, requester, machine, lane, result, questions and answers, failure and verdict, times —; `artifact` — its title, summary, note, repository, job and its text where that is cheap —; `infra-plan` — its target, tool, summary, risks, blast radius, status (`recorded`, `approved`, `failed`), repository and job, never the plan's text —; `issue` — an issue of the **issue graph** (an intake item among them): its title, state, approved text, links, job, and what intake did with it —; `pull-request` — a pull request of the issue graph: its repository and number, state, links, and the job of an issue it closes. A direct job's is **private**: only its owner sees it; so are its artifacts and plans. | indexed job, entry |
| **Pending index update** | A mark that a search document must be written to the search index again (`search_pending`, `job:<id>`, `artifact:<id>`, `infra-plan:<id>`, `issue:<url>`, `pull-request:<url>`): set from the events that change it, and from each read of the issue graph, cleared once Solr took the write. While Solr is down they wait, and search is **degraded**. Not the **GitHub outbox**. | outbox, queue item |
| **Backfill** (search) | Every job, artifact, infra plan, issue and pull request of a user marked as a pending index update at once: by the hopper at the first start that finds none of the user's documents in the search index, or by a person with **Reindex** (`POST /ui/api/search/reindex`). | reindex job, resync |
| **Pointed job status** | `queued` → `running` → `awaiting_review` → `merged` \| `abandoned`. Only the allowed transitions (`TRANSITIONS`): running back to queued, awaiting review back to running, anything but an end to abandoned; merged and abandoned are ends. Each move is a `pointed_job.status_changed`. | state, phase |
| **Priority** | `0..100` on the job, higher first. Default 50. | rank |
| **Backburner** | The `hopper:backburner` label on an issue: the GitHub sources never pick it up while it is set, and cancel a waiting job whose issue gets it. Not a priority. **Take off the backburner** (Intake; issue #798) removes the label, `source.backburner_lifted`. The UI calls it **Later** (issue #2375): the Intake group Later, its tooltip names the label, and the action is Take out of Later. An issue on it is never shown as gone (issue #808): its intake outcome says since when and by whom (`backburner`, the label's newest `labeled` event), and the Intake nav entry and the Queue count them. | paused (*parked* is a job a person took out of its lane) |
| **Effective priority** | Priority after the router's boost (cheap advice), which applies only in active mode, or the resume boost of a job resuming with an answer. The decider's notion (`effectivePriority`, `src/decider/assign.ts`); the queue sorter is given it. | score |
| **Queue sorter** | The role that orders the waiting jobs (`QueueSorter` port: `sort(entries) → job ids`). Live, exactly one instance (the plugins config's `queueSorter`; absent → the built-in `priority`). Built-in plugins `priority` (effective priority, then oldest, then id — the decider's own rule), `oldest-first`, `newest-first`. It orders, and it is the **pre-sort**; it never holds. One that cannot run, throws, or returns anything but distinct waiting job ids → `priority` answers, `fallback` in `/api/plugins`. | scheduler, ranker |
| **Queue gate** | What a new job passes before it may run (issue #159): every job a source creates waits **unaccepted** until the pre-sort or the user **accepts** it, or is **rejected**. A user's setting (`QueueGate`): mode `auto-accept` (default: the pre-sort is applied before each Decision as jobs arrive) or `review` (each job waits for the user), and `autoAcceptPerHour`, the throttle (null: no limit; past it a new job waits for the user). The Queue view; `POST /ui/api/queue-gate`. | intake filter, approval (*approve* is the router's) |
| **Pre-sort** | The queue sorter over the unaccepted jobs (`PreSort`): their order (the Queue view's Pre-sorted column) and the ones it rejects, each with a reason (the sorter's optional `reject`). **Accept pre-sort** applies it at once: rejects what it rejects, accepts the rest. The UI says **waiting to be ordered** (issue #2375): the Queue badge and column, and Accept this order. | triage, presort ranking |
| **Accepted** (job) | Let through the queue gate (`job.accepted`, `by` `pre-sort` or `user`). An **unaccepted** job (`accepted: false`) is waiting, held `awaiting acceptance`; a job from before the gate is accepted. Not *approved*: approving overrides router holds and never passes the gate. | admitted, approved |
| **Rejected** | Status `rejected`: a waiting job turned away at the queue gate by the user (UI Reject) or the pre-sort. Ended, kept, never run — neutralized, not deleted; its reason is its `error` — the user may give one (UI Reject's reason field, issue #387), else `rejected by the user`. On GitHub nothing is written (issue #387): the issue is not taken again until it is **assigned** to the user again after the rejection, or the user runs it again. A `hopper:rejected` label from before still keeps an issue out while set; removing it offers the issue again (a re-run). | declined, dropped, deleted |
| **User order** | The order the user gives the waiting jobs in the Queue view (`POST /ui/api/queue/order`): each job's `userRank`. It wins over priority (issue #1776): the ranked jobs run in the user's order, whatever their effective priority; a job with no rank goes ahead of the next ranked job only when its effective priority is higher (issue #461), and a new priority ends a job's rank. Moving an unaccepted job into it accepts the job. | manual order, pins |
| **Job view** | One job's page in the UI, at `#job/<id>` (issue #433): its title, state, lane, times and error; since issue #935 its **parts list** — a plain-words header (what was asked, where it stands, what is next) with what waits on the person pinned, the job's own graph with a node per part, the timeline newest first, and the parts as a filterable table — read again on each new event of the job. The job comes from the job store, else from `GET /api/jobs/:id`. Every timeline entry about a job links to it — a lane span or question wait on the lane timeline, an event line with a job in Live activity and Events; an entry with no job has no link. The timeline is a shadcn `Trace` (issue #1169): a line per event, and one line for what belongs together — a sandbox step's start and end (one `step` id), a fork's spin-off and decision (one `forkId`, with a link to the fork job) — that opens to its events (`ui/src/model/timeline-steps.ts`). An operator opens the job's **transcript** there (issue #1169). No nav entry. `ui/src/views/job.tsx`, `jobHref` in `ui/src/model/job.ts`. | job page, job detail, drill-down |
| **Parts list** (BOM) | Everything one job touched or made, its bill of materials (issue #935). The UI and help say parts list; code and the API say BOM (`JobBom`, `BomPart`, `jobBom` in `src/domain/job-bom.ts`, `GET /api/jobs/:id/bom`). Grouped: what was asked, work, code, outputs, infra, decisions, machines and sandboxes. Each **part** has its status (`waiting_on_you`, `running`, `open`, `done`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `recorded`: a fact, not work) and its page. With it: a header in plain words, a one-line summary, and the job's events. Read from the job's record, events and rows on each read; never kept. Since part 2 also its usage (its job runs), its pushes, every commit and changed file of its pull request (kept on the followed pull request as the mirror said), its help pages and What's new lines, and its place in the merge order. Since issue #975 also its **test databases**. | inventory, manifest, contents |
| **Test database** (of a job) | A container of a database image (Postgres, MySQL, Redis and the like) that a job's tests started on its machine, in the job's own container runtime or with its label (issue #975). The job's reap says each container's name, image, and when it started and stopped before it removes it (`details` on `job.containers_removed`); the job's **parts list** shows each as the part `container:<id>` in the machines group, kind `test_database`, and any other such container as kind `container`. A database a job copies for an ad-hoc daemon is not one. | test container, test DB, scratch database |
| **Secret canary** | A distinct random value a test gives the hopper as one of its secrets — the master key, a vault secret, a connected account's token, a webhook signing secret — or reads from what it mints (a job's proxy token), so that the **canary scan** can prove the value is nowhere it should not be (issue #1957, `test/support/secret-canary.ts`). Tests only. Not the **canary** step of a release. | sentinel, honeytoken, marker |
| **Canary scan** | The check that reads every place a test can see after a job ran — every table of the instance's and each user's schema, files under a directory, captured logs, the event log, an SSE replay, webhook bodies, a box's environment and argv, the WAL written since the run began, the file, argv and environment of each tool the process spawned — for each **secret canary** in its plain, base64, base64url, hex and URL-encoded forms, a gzip value (bytes, a bytea, or base64 text) opened first, and names each place and form it finds (issue #1957, `test/integration/secret-canary.test.ts`; after a crash, also each crashed host's output, `test/crash/secret-canary-kill.test.ts`; a vault secret delivered to a job, in its checkpoint, `test/integration/secret-canary-vault-checkpoint.test.ts`; a Windows step's vault secret after a kill mid-step, `test/crash/secret-canary-windows.test.ts`; a box step's vault secret after a kill mid-step, `test/crash/secret-canary-box-step.test.ts`; its e2e mode, the same on a real container runtime, also each container's layers and home, each volume and image, and the runtime's storage, `test/crash/secret-canary-real-runtime.test.ts`, nightly in `.github/workflows/secret-canary.yml`). | leak scan, secret sweep |
| **Jobs view** | The UI's view at `#jobs` (issues #434, #900): where every job stands, at a glance, and how the jobs connect. It reads the **job graph** (`GET /api/jobs/graph`) when it opens and again when the store's jobs change. Above both modes: the count of jobs in each **job stage** (a count is also its stage's filter) and the filters by stage and by repository (kept in the page only). **List** mode (the default since issue #2372): a table, a **job row** per job, the latest stage change first, 30 to a page (issue #2353); a click on a row opens its **run graph** under it. **Graph** mode: a section per **job tree**, laid out from its root, what blocks the root ringed, a node's details on select. The header, its filters and the counts stay in view; the mode and each tree folded or open are **page choices**. Not the Dashboard, which shows health. `ui/src/views/jobs.tsx`, `ui/src/model/jobs-view.ts`. | job board, kanban, dashboard, tracker |
| **Job graph** | Where every job stands and how the jobs hang together (issue #900, `src/domain/job-graph.ts`, `GET /api/jobs/graph`): **nodes** — jobs, issues, pull requests, and open questions and hand-offs (each `waiting`), each with its job stage — and **typed edges**: `parent` (an issue to its sub-issue: GitHub's, or a required child a job filed), `follow_up` (an issue to a follow-up a job filed), `works` (an issue to a job of it), `next_part` (a partly done job to the job that runs the rest), `rerun` (a job to the job that runs its issue again), `spawned` (a job to a fork from its question), `plan_apply` (a proposal's job to its follow-on), `opened` (a job to its pull request), `waits_on_merge` (a partly done job to its unmerged pull request), `closes` (a pull request to the issue its merge closes), `blocked_by` (an issue to an issue that blocks it: GitHub's issue dependencies), `stacked_on` (a pull request to the open one whose head branch is its base), `asks` (a job to its open question), `hands_off` (a job to its open hand-off). An issue that **waits on its batch** is `done`, with `awaitsBatch` (issue #1337). Made on each read from the jobs, the lineage links, the issue graph and the open questions and hand-offs; never kept. | dependency graph, DAG, job map |
| **Job stage** | Where a job is in its life, as the Jobs view and the job graph say it (issue #900, `stageOf`): `queued` (queued, held, claimed), `running`, `waiting` (on a question, an operator, parked, its own wait), `pr_open` (finished, its pull request open), `partly_done` (finished partly done, its part not merged), `done`, `failed`, `cancelled` (cancelled or rejected), `superseded` (failed or cancelled, then a later job of its issue, its issue's close, a merge, or a person's dismissal took its place: settled, and no failure, issue #1036). Whether a failed or cancelled job is still a failure, and an issue node's stage, are its **work item**'s, as the **work-item ledger** has it (issue #1067): a failed job is `failed` only while its item is, and `waiting` while its item is handed to a person on it. An issue node no job here works takes its own state first (issue #1895): closed as completed is `done`, closed as not planned or as a duplicate is `cancelled` ("Closed"); open, its work item's stage, else `no_job` ("Not started"). Not the job's status, which it is read from. Each node also says its stage in **stage words** (issue #935, `stageText`): Queued, Building it (attempt 2 of 3), Plan, Waiting on you: approve the plan, PR open: checks running, PR open: fixing checks, PR open: waiting for the merge (never "ready for review", issue #1067), Part shipped, Merged, Superseded, Dismissed…, made by `src/domain/job-words.ts`. **Time in stage** counts from the moment the job's record names for it (`stageSince`). | phase, state, column |
| **Work item** | One item of a source, by its source key, whichever of its jobs works it (issue #1067): what the **work-item ledger** keeps one row of. Its **work item state** says where it stands for every view. A job's status says only what its own run did. | job, card, entry |
| **Work-item ledger** | The `work_items` table (issue #1067, design.md "The work-item ledger"): one row per **work item**, written only by its pure reducer (`src/domain/work-items.ts`), in the transaction of each event that moves it and each read of the issue graph. Rebuilt from every event and the issue graph by tenant migrations 55, 56, 57, 58, 82 and 83 and `hopper ledger rebuild`. Each row keeps its **item owner**, next check and deadline (issue #1891). `GET /api/work-items` reads it; so does the sync's **closed as complete** rule (part 4). The Intake badge counts the request cards Intake shows (and the issues not assigned to the user, issue #1614), not the ledger (issue #1193). | work table, status table, projection |
| **Work item state** | Where a **work item** stands (issue #1067): `waiting_for_approval`, `queued`, `working`, `needs_you`, `waiting_outside`, `pr_open`, `partly_done`, `done`, `failed` (the newest attempt failed, nothing replaced it, nobody dismissed it: the only failure counted), `blocked` (it broke its state's promise and waits on a person, issue #1891: `no_pr`, no pull request to wait on; `stale`, past its deadline; or `pr_closed`, its pull request closed without a merge on GitHub — the first time in a row the **item reconciler** runs it again by itself) or `dropped` (dismissed, declined, cancelled, rejected, its pull request closed by a person's Close PR, its issue closed as not planned, or blocked and a person's **Won't do** in Intake, `work_item.dropped`, which swaps its end label for `hopper:rejected` through the **GitHub outbox**). Not the **job stage**, which is a job's. | status, stage, phase |
| **Item lifecycle** | What each **work item state** promises (issue #1891, `src/domain/item-lifecycle.ts`, design.md "The item lifecycle"): its **item owner**, how often it is checked, and its deadline (PR open, queued, working and waiting outside: an hour; partly done: a day). The next check and the deadline run from the item's last move, a move within its state too (issue #1892 part 18). A state that waits on a pull request (`pr_open`) is never written with none named. | item saga, state machine |
| **Item owner** | Who moves a **work item** out of its state (issue #1891): `scheduler`, `lane_worker`, `github_observer`, `assessor`, `reconciler` or `person`. Kept as the ledger row's `owner`; each kind of move names its owner too (`transitionOwner`), and the item keeps the owner of its last move (`movedBy`, issue #1892 part 18). | assignee, responsible |
| **Item reconciler** | The timed pass that corrects the **work-item ledger** (issue #1891, `src/work-items/reconcile.ts`): every 5 minutes, under its sweep lease, each item whose next check is due and broke its state's promise — queued, working or waiting outside though its job ended, blocked for want of a pull request its job's report named since, in PR open past its deadline, or past its deadline in another state no person holds (queued, working or waiting outside with no job of it found, partly done with no next part) — is moved by one `work_item.reconciled` event. An item blocked as `pr_closed` on its first close in a row runs again: Run again on its job, `by: reconciler`. A blocked item that is **stuck work** gets one notice, `work_item.person_asked`, once per block. A block of an item with a GitHub job keeps its issue's label change as GitHub outbox rows in the block's transaction. Database only: it calls no GitHub itself. Not the **fallback read**, which reads GitHub, and not a **component watcher**, which only reports. | reconcile loop, fixer, janitor |
| **Label mirror** | The check, at each discover of a GitHub source, of each listed issue's end labels against its **work item**'s state (issue #1891, `src/sources/github/label-mirror.ts`): the labels a state wants are a pure function of it (`labelsFor`, `src/sources/github/item-labels.ts`) — none for an item a job works, `hopper:pr-ready` or `hopper:partly-done` in PR open, `hopper:blocked` or `hopper:pr-closed` when blocked, `hopper:failed` of a failure reported as failed (a failed item, or a hand-off), `hopper:done` of a done item whose pull request merged as the whole item, that a person did by hand (`doneByHand`: **Done** on its hand-off) or whose job's final report found its issue closed as complete (`doneReported`, part 24), none of a partly done item whose merged part leaves parts to run, none of an item whose job waits on a person but a hand-off (which carries `hopper:failed` as reported), none of a request card, `hopper:rejected` of an item a person dropped by **Won't do** (`wontDo`: a blocked item in Intake, or a hand-off) (issue #1892); the other states are not judged. A **label drift** is logged and counted once, and corrected through the **GitHub outbox** (act `mirror`), coalesced, once (issue #1933): a blocked item's, an item a job works and an item in PR open, which gets `hopper:partly-done` for a part, else `hopper:pr-ready`. Reads only what the discover listed and the ledger. Of the hoppers on one database, only the holder of its sweep lease (`label-mirror/<source>`) corrects and records (issue #1892). Not the **repository mirror**. | label sync, relabeler |
| **Label edit** | An end label a person took off an issue by hand on GitHub (issue #1892, `work_item.label_edited`), as the **label mirror** found it: the label the **work item**'s state wants, seen at the last discover (after a start, in the listing cache) and gone now, with the item not moved since and no outbox row of the hopper's own unsent: `hopper:pr-ready` or `hopper:partly-done` of PR open, `hopper:blocked` or `hopper:pr-closed` of blocked, `hopper:failed` of a failure reported as failed (a failed item, or a hand-off), `hopper:done` of a done item whose pull request merged as the whole item, that a person did by hand (`doneByHand`: **Done** on its hand-off) or whose report found its issue closed as complete (`doneReported`), `hopper:rejected` of an item dropped by **Won't do**. It means Run again: the item keeps the ask (`runAgainAsked`) and Intake takes it again; the label is not put back. A label not on the issue at its first sight is no label edit. An end label put on a request card by hand withdraws the card (`source.request_withdrawn` with `label`); the label stays. An end label put on by hand on an item a cancelled or rejected job dropped, after its report (part 19), keeps the issue out (`means: keep_out`): the item keeps the label (`keptOut`), which decides Intake's reason; taken off again, it is a Run again. | manual relabel, label override |
| **Label drift** | Where the end labels a discover saw on an issue differ from what its **work item**'s state wants, as the **label mirror** found it (issue #1891): the end labels to take off, and the one missing. Counted as `hopper.work_items.label_drift`; each correction as `hopper.work_items.label_corrections`. Not a **drift**, which a **component watcher** finds. | label mismatch |
| **Pull request wait** | What a **work item**'s open pull request waits on first (issue #1067, part 3; `PullRequestWait`, `src/domain/work-items.ts`): `draft` (to be marked ready), `conflicts` (a fix of its merge conflicts), `fixing_checks` (a fix of its failed checks), `checks_running` (its checks to pass) or `merge` (the merge). The follow records it as `job.pull_request_seen` when it changes; the item's `waitingOn` says it in plain words. Never "ready for review". | review state, PR status |
| **In flight** | A **work item** that a job works now, or whose pull request is open and its checks run, its checks are being fixed, or it waits on the merge (issue #1113, `isInFlight`, `src/domain/work-items.ts`): its state `working`, or `pr_open` with its **pull request wait** `checks_running`, `fixing_checks` or `merge`. Not a draft, merge conflicts, or a wait the follow has not read. Each is in one part (issue #1164, `inFlightPartOf`): **running**, **on its own command** (an **own command wait**: it holds its lane), or **pull requests** in checks or the merge. The header's badge and the dashboard's **In flight** card show the same count and parts (`GET /api/queue` `inFlight`: `{ total, running, ownCommand, pullRequests }`); the badge's link opens the Jobs view filtered to them (`#jobs/in-flight`). Not the users' `IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES`, which are job statuses. | active, running, busy |
| **Fallback read** | The **work-item ledger**'s batched read of GitHub (issue #1067, part 3; `src/work-items/fallback-read.ts`): every 5 minutes, the issues of up to 50 items no job or person acts on and not done (`pr_open`, `partly_done`, `failed`, `dropped`, issue #1105) with no news for 30 minutes, in one GraphQL call, with the user's own connection; the first read after the start reads every such item. A close or a reopen it finds is recorded as a **GitHub delivery** would record it (`work_item.issue_closed`, `work_item.issue_reopened`). Not a **component watcher**, which only reports drift. | poll, refresh, sync |
| **Withdrawn request** | A **request card** that left Intake with no person's approval or decline (issue #1067, part 4): a sync of its source that listed every repository of the item no longer offers it — its issue closed, its label or assignee gone, or it is of a trusted origin now. Recorded as `source.request_withdrawn`; its **work item**, waiting for approval, is dropped. Offered again, it opens again (`source.request_opened`). Not a **declined** request, which is a person's act. | dismissed request, expired card |
| **Component watcher** | A check of one component of the hopper against its source of truth, on its own interval (issue #1077, `src/component-watchers/`): the **work-item ledger** against GitHub, the ledger against the job records, the **lanes** against the jobs they run, intake against the jobs and the sync timers, the job repositories' default branches against their checks on GitHub, and the hopper's stack against a healthy stack (the instance's, for its admin). It reports **drift**. Not an **environment watcher** (people told of an infra plan) and not a **GitHub watch** (a poll of one item). | monitor, probe, checker |
| **Drift** | Where a component of the hopper disagrees with its source of truth, as a **component watcher** found it (issue #1077), by kind: `failed_but_finished`, `pr_open_but_ended`, `pull_request_state`, `issue_state` (ledger vs GitHub); `active_but_job_ended`, `ended_but_job_active`, `state_differs` (ledger vs jobs); `lane_busy_job_not_running`, `job_running_no_lane` (lanes vs jobs); `taken_no_job`, `sync_overdue`, `sync_stuck` (intake vs jobs); `checks_failing`, `checks_stuck` (default branches vs checks); `service_down`, `part_unreadable`, `database_strain` (stack health; not **stack drift**, which compares the stack with its compose file). Each item says what the component says and what its source of truth says. A change newer than the grace is no drift yet. | mismatch, divergence, inconsistency |
| **Drift issue** | The one issue per kind of **drift** a **component watcher** files on the hopper's own repository with the user's own GitHub connection, while the user's setting is on (issue #1077): labelled `user-report`, each item a short hash of its key, edited while the drift lasts, found again by its **drift key**, never filed twice. | drift report, alert |
| **Drift key** | `<watcher>/<kind>`, the last line of a **drift issue**'s body (`Drift key: …`): how the next check finds the issue to update it (issue #1077). | drift id |
| **Job tree** | One group of connected nodes of the job graph (issue #900, `JobTree`): its **root** (a node no edge leads into, an issue first), its nodes, how many jobs are in each stage, what is **not settled** under the root (`blocking`) — each node not done or cancelled that the root leads to, and every node on the way —, and its **summary** (issue #935): one line, "3 of 4 pieces shipped, 1 being redone, nothing waiting on you." Only a `blocked_by` edge says that something blocks. | component, cluster, epic |
| **Job row** (Jobs view) | One job in the Jobs view's list mode, a table row (issue #2372; one line since issue #2353, a card before): its job stage, issue as `owner/repo#number` linked to it, goal (linked to its job view) and task count (`x/y`), lane (`no lane` without one), time in stage, pull request with its checks, for a partly done job the pull request it waits on and what runs after the merge, and **last event**. A click on the row, but on a link, opens its **run graph** under it; a second click folds it. A narrow screen drops the issue, the lane, the time in stage and the pull request; only a wide screen shows the last event. | job card (that is the Queue view's), tile |
| **Run graph** (Jobs view) | The **job tree** a **job row**'s job is in, drawn as Graph mode draws it, under the row (issue #2372): it opens on a click on the row, and a node opens its details. Not kept: a reload shows every row folded. | job graph (that is the API's whole graph), expansion |
| **Last event** | The newest event of a job (issue #434): `GET /api/jobs/last-events` answers it for each job `/api/queue` holds, and a newer one the stream brings replaces it. Its time, type and detail show on the job row. | latest activity, last update |
| **Job card** (Queue view) | One waiting job in the Queue view's columns (issue #737). Collapsed by default: its issue, state, lane, pull request state, parts done, its repository's yolo mode and why it is held (issue #874), to scan and compare. **Open**: its detail too — priority, advice, how long it waits, the pre-sort's rejection. Opened alone by a click, or every card by **Expand all**; **Collapse all** closes every card. Its actions show either way. | row, tile, accordion |
| **Hopper name** | A GitHub source's `hopperName` option: which hopper this is when several watch one GitHub (issue #159). An issue labelled `hopper@<name>` is **addressed** to the hopper of that name: only that one takes it; an issue addressed to no hopper goes to any (the first claim wins). A source with no name takes no addressed issue. | instance name, hopper id |
| **Job tracking** | The layer above the lanes that owns the job queue and each job's pointer (issue #426, design.md "Job tracking above lanes"): the `jobs` table, the job's `source` (what it points at; its source key is its identity in the queue), intake (`SourceHost.ingest`, the only way an item becomes a job), the queue gate, the queue order, and the Decision that hands a waiting job to a free lane. A source never gives work to a lane. Not a separate module or service. | tracker, dispatcher, job service |
| **Queue order** | The queue sorter's order of the waiting jobs for one Decision (`DecisionInputs.queueOrder`: sorter instance + job ids). Step 6 orders admissible jobs by it; jobs it leaves out follow by the decider's own rule. `/api/queue` `waiting` is in the same order. | ranking |
| **Machine** | A host that can run jobs: this laptop (`local`) and every attached machine. Each is a `machine-source` instance in the plugins config's `machines` (issue #74), named after the instance (the machine id): this one of the built-in plugin `local` (its `lanes` option is the lane count, its optional `executors` option narrows what runs here, its optional `session` names the herdr session its jobs run in, which the hopper starts — issue #260), an attached one of `ssh`, `container` or `client`. One that cannot run lists no machine; the others still run what they list. The hopper does not register its own host by default: a fresh plugins config lists no machine, and this one is added like any other (issue #259). The container is not a machine: with `HOPPER_LOCAL_MACHINE=false` there is no `local` instance (issue #141). | node, worker |
| **Attached machine** | A target beside this machine: a machine-source instance of the plugin named by its **connection** — `ssh` (an **ssh target**'s host, running jobs in its own herdr session, or with `herdr: false` none: Cursor and commands only, online while it answers over ssh, issue #142), `container` (a **container target**) or `client` (a **client target**) — followed without a restart (issue #74). Online while that herdr session answers, or while a **command box** can start for it. A client target joins itself with a **join line** (issue #308); an ssh target is attached from the UI by a **machine edit**; its name, lanes, executors, label and connection details edited, and it removed, like any plugin instance (a new name: the options edit's `rename`, issue #205). | remote, worker, consumer |
| **Target** | The role of an attached machine: a machine the hopper connects to and runs jobs on. "Backend" is the same role; code, docs and UI say target. | consumer, client |
| **Connection** | How the hopper reaches a machine to run a job there: none (this machine, plugin `local`), ssh (plugin `ssh`), a **command box** for each command (plugin `container`, issue #865), or a client's tunnel (plugin `client`). One per attached machine, which names its machine-source plugin; each authenticated as **target authentication** says. The machines API (`GET /api/machines/config`) gives each machine's `connection`, never `plugin` (issue #206). | protocol, transport |
| **Target authentication** | How the hopper proves itself to each kind of target, and the target to it (issue #59, design.md "Target authentication"): an ssh target by the **hopper's ssh key** alone to a **pinned host key**; a container target through the sandbox module's container runtime, as every sandbox box (issue #865); a client target by its **client token**, derived from the **link keys**. Never a password. | target auth, credentials |
| **Hopper's ssh key** | The key the hopper offers an ssh target: the mounted secret file `HOPPER_SSH_KEY_FILE` names, alone; none mounted, the hopper's own (issue #293) — minted once, kept in the user's store, written to `<workdir>/ssh/hopper_ed25519` at each start, its public half shown in the Add machine form —, then the key files `ssh -G` names that exist. Installed on a target with `restrict`. Never the user's agent. | identity, deploy key |
| **Pinned host key** | An `ssh` instance's `hostKey` option in the plugins config (`<type> <base64>`): the only host key the hopper accepts from it, written to its own known_hosts under the target's name. Taken from the user's own `~/.ssh/known_hosts`, or the **host key offer** the person confirmed (issue #293); never learned from a connection unseen. Absent → the hopper does not connect. | known host |
| **Host key offer** | The host key a new ssh target would be pinned to, with its SHA256 fingerprint (`POST /ui/api/machines/host-key`, issue #293): the one `~/.ssh/known_hosts` holds (`known`), else the one the target presents now, which the person checks on the machine and confirms; only then is it sent with the machine edit and pinned. | fingerprint prompt, TOFU |
| **ssh target** | The ssh destination of an attached machine: a `~/.ssh/config` alias or a plain `user@host` — typed in the UI too (issue #293), so a hopper with no `~/.ssh/config` can attach one. | host |
| **Container target** | An attached machine with no agent and no herdr, plugin `container` (issues #58, #865): it runs commands only, each in a **command box** of its `image`. Online while a command box can start for the user. | sandbox, docker host |
| **Command box** | The one-shot sandbox box a container target's command runs in (issue #865, `CommandBoxes`): a need box of the target's image, with no network and no credential, started by the sandbox module and burned when the command ends; kept in the sandbox runs ledger, the image as its template. | container, exec target |
| **Comment command** | A comment on a GitHub issue whose first line is `/hopper run`, `/hopper research` or `/hopper propose` (issue #655 part 21, `src/domain/comment-command.ts`). Written by the source's connected account on a labelled issue, its newest one, not edited since, **nominates** the issue in place of the assignment: the origin has it as the act `nominated`. `research` and `propose` ask the job for research or a proposal. Anyone else's command nominates nothing; one through an app makes a **request card**; a job writes none (the GitHub proxy refuses it). | slash command, /hopper |
| **Job shape** | How a job's work is done (issue #316, design.md "Job shapes"): **sandboxed** — an executor the hopper runs, in a **container sandbox**; the primary shape — or **operator-led** — by hand in an IDE such as Cursor, no executor. Both are claimed once per issue and checked in by the closing pull request. | job type, job kind |
| **Cluster install** | A hopper image run as a Kubernetes workload, a Helm release (issue #1285, `src/update/cluster.ts`), found by `KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST`. Its update is `helm upgrade` of the release to a pinned image tag (`sha-<commit>`); its **stack drift** is the image it runs against the update channel. No compose file, compose tool or container runtime command applies to it. Its pods have no WSL, systemd or container runtime socket, and no part asks for one (issue #1289, `src/client/cluster.ts` `inCluster`, design.md "Host-only parts in a cluster"). | Kubernetes install, k8s mode, Helm install |
| **Container tool** | Podman or Docker: the tool whose commands the update notice and Settings → Version show for a container install's update (issue #521). Chosen per browser, Podman until Docker is chosen; the hopper does not detect it. Not the engine. | engine, runtime |
| **Container sandbox** | A container, on Podman or Docker (a pod on Kubernetes), that confines the work the hopper runs for a sandboxed job: the primary, enforced sandbox path (issue #316). The hopper starts and stops each one itself, through the **sandbox module**: a **need box** for each **need step** or **box step**, burned when the step ends (issue #1349), and a **check box** for the read-only check. A **template** scopes it: the box gets only what a person approved for that template (design.md "Templates", "A template's blast radius"). An older build's **sandbox box** joined as a client target; since issue #1349 no sandbox joins as a machine. The jobs directory confines the work tree on every machine (issue #314). Not built yet: the box's network still reaches the internet (#315; a read-only box's egress allowlist: #849). Neither a container target (commands only) nor an agent box (a test machine, not locked down). | jail, VM |
| **Agent box** | Gone (issue #865): a test container with one agent CLI in it, an ssh target made by `scripts/agent-boxes.sh`. A **sandbox box** image of each agent (`box-<agent>`, `BOX_AGENTS`) took its place. | test container, sandbox |
| **Pickup** | Gone with the agent boxes (issue #865): an issue taken up by hand at an agent box, reported in the box's pickup record (issue #319, a spike). Not a claim: a claim is the hopper's. | session, check-in, lease |
| **Pickup record** | One JSON file per issue in a box's pickup dir (`HOPPER_PICKUP_DIR`, else `~/.hopper/pickups/`): the pickup's state, its heartbeat (`updatedAt`) and its history, each entry with a `seq` that only grows. Written by the box (`hopper-pickup`), read by the hopper side over ssh; the box sends nothing. | status file, report |
| **Operator-led** | The job shape beside sandboxed (design.md "Job shapes"), and the mode of a pickup: work done by hand — a person at a terminal, or an IDE or its agent the hopper has no pane in — not by an executor the hopper runs. Status `operator_led` (issue #318): a waiting job an operator claimed in the UI (`POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/operator-led`, event `job.claimed_by_operator`). It holds no lane and the decider never runs it; its issue keeps `hopper:claimed`; its source finishes it when the work is complete (`JobSource.notComplete`: its closing pull request reached the completion), and it is cancelled as any job is. Shown in the Dashboard's Operator-led list and on the lane timeline's own `operator-led` row. | handjammed, manual, IDE job, interactive |
| **Operator-led claim** | Claiming a waiting job as operator-led (`job.claimed_by_operator`): the hopper's record that someone took its work by hand. Distinct from a lane claim (`job.claimed`, a Decision's) and from the claim of an issue (the label). | handjam, check-in |
| **Client target** | An attached machine that runs the **hopper client** and dials in to this hopper's own URL (issue #308): herdr calls go down its **link** over HTTP/2, signed with its **client token** (`client: { key }`, its **machine key**, issue #59). Added by **joining** with a **join code**. Its herdr binary and session are its own. Online while its herdr session answers the hopper's signed calls. | agent, remote client, backend |
| **Job pod** | A pod the hopper starts through the Kubernetes API for one job (issue #1283, design.md "Job pods on Kubernetes"), named `hopper-job-<job id>`: it joins once with a join code that names it, as a **client target** only its job runs on, dials out to the hopper, and is deleted with its machine when the job ends. Started by the **kubernetes executor**. The sweep deletes one whose job ended and whose cleanup no **cleanup record** still owes (issue #1570, `job.pod_removed`). | worker pod, runner |
| **Kubernetes executor** | The executor plugin `kubernetes` (issue #1283): each job in a **job pod**, run there by herdr-claude. Its jobs go to a **kubernetes cluster** machine. | k8s runner |
| **Kubernetes cluster** (machine) | The machine-source plugin `kubernetes-cluster` (issue #1283): the cluster as a machine whose lanes are how many job pods run at once; online while the Kubernetes API answers in its namespace. Nothing runs on it. | node, cluster target |
| **Client door** | A client target's listener on loopback (issue #695, `src/client/door.ts`): it passes a job's `/job/` requests on to the hopper at the URL the client joined at. The hopper gives its URL to each job there as `HOPPER_URL`, so the job needs no name of the hopper's resolved. Only `/job/` paths, only its own Host; it adds no right: every route still asks for the job's proxy token. A request it cannot connect with while the hopper is **out of reach** is held and tried again, up to two minutes from when the hopper went away; then 503 with Retry-After, saying since when (issue #752). | relay, tunnel, local proxy |
| **Reach check** | Before a job starts on a machine, the machine's check that it reaches the hopper at the URL the job is given (issue #695, `src/client/reach.ts`): curl there, through the machine's own connection. Not reached at a fresh start, the job is queued again, never failed, and the machine gets an **address problem** for a while (issue #1920); a resuming job fails at once with the reason and what to do. | preflight, health check |
| **Hopper client** | The program a client target runs (`src/client/`, installed as plain files by the hopper's install script `/client/install`, unit `hopper-client`, or baked into a **sandbox box**): joins with a **join line**, dials in, serves `POST /herdr` (and, for `claude-plan`'s usage readings, two read-only `claude` calls, `POST /claude`, issue #366; for an escalation level, one locked-down print-mode `claude` run, `POST /level`, issue #482; a print-mode agent's turn the hopper built, `POST /turn`, issue #865) for requests signed with its token, signs its answers, dials again when its link ends. It runs a **client release** the hopper loads onto it. | agent, daemon |
| **Client release** | The hopper client as one versioned unit (issue #70, `src/client/release.ts`): its files, its **manifest** (each file's SHA-256) and an id from the manifest; the manifest, not a list in the client, names its files (issue #545). Released from the hopper — the client files of the install it runs — and loaded onto each client target whose client runs another (`POST /load`), never while a job runs there. | client version, client build, client update |
| **Bridge** (release) | The release the hopper loads onto a client released before manifests, which checks a fixed list of file names (issue #545, `src/machines/client-bridge.ts`): exactly that list's names, the id that client computes; its `main.ts` writes the hopper's client release over the install dir and restarts. | shim, stub release, upgrader |
| **Link** | A client target's connection to the hopper (issue #308): the socket its client dialled in on, an HTTP/1.1 upgrade to `hopper-client/1` at `/client/connect` on the hopper's own URL, signed with the client token; HTTP/2 flows on it, the hopper's calls going down it. One per machine; dialling in again replaces it. | tunnel, reverse tunnel, websocket |
| **Join code** | A one-time code that adds one machine (issue #308): minted by an admin's **Add machine** (`POST /ui/api/machines/join`) or `hopper join-code`, for 10 minutes, kept only hashed; spent by the machine's join (`POST /client/join`). A spent code is kept with the **machine key** that spent it until it expires (`join_codes_spent`, issue #1628): that machine's join sent again is answered as the first; another machine's is refused. | pairing code, registration token, invite |
| **Join line** | `<hopper URL>#<join code>`: what a machine joins with. Add machine shows it inside the one line to run: the install script's pipe for a computer. A sandbox never joins as a machine (issue #1349). | join URL, invite link |
| **Cluster bootstrap** | `hopper-cluster.sh bootstrap` (issue #1373, `docs/kubernetes.md`): a small Kubernetes cluster on the host of a compose install — k3s, rootless, under the user unit `hopper-k3s.service`, with its own container store, so no Podman container, image or volume changes. It stops when a cluster is there already, unless told to use it. | cluster install (that is the hopper in a cluster), cluster setup |
| **Cutover** | The step of a **cluster migration** where the compose hopper stops and the cluster hopper starts on the moved data (issue #1373): one writer of the database at a time. It rolls back by itself when a check after it fails. The compose stack is stopped, never removed. | switchover, failover |
| **Cluster migration** | `hopper-cluster.sh migrate` (issue #1373, `docs/kubernetes.md`): a compose install moved into the Helm chart's release — its database (OpenFGA's store with it), its **master key** on a pipe into the chart's Secret (or, never read by the script, checked in a one-off pod in a Secret the person made or an outside store fills), its own launch settings and its home —, then the **cutover**. **Rollback** (`hopper-cluster.sh rollback`) starts compose again; nothing is deleted. | cluster import, move to k8s |
| **Lane pod** | A long-lived pod of a Kubernetes StatefulSet that is one machine (issue #1284, design.md "Lane pods"): the sandbox box image, its client dialling out, no sshd, no inbound port. It joins with the **lane pool key** under its pod's name at each start (`pool: true` on its `client` instance), so a restart is the same machine; told to stop, it is **leaving**, and with no job left it is **retired**. | worker pod, runner, agent pod |
| **Lane pool key** | The preshared key lane pods join with (issue #1284): 64 hex, made by the install, given to the hopper as `HOPPER_LANE_POOL_KEY` (or `_FILE`) and to the pods' join step as a Secret. Never spent, unlike a **join code**. Its pods are the machines of the hopper's first user. The hopper also issues one per user (issue #1454: Add machine → Pods on Kubernetes, `hopper pool-key`), kept hashed, replaced or revoked with no restart, for a **runner-only release**; its pods are that user's machines. `src/machines/lane-pool.ts`. |
| **Runner-only release** | A release of the Helm chart with `hopper.enabled=false` (issue #1454): its **lane pods** alone, which join a hopper outside the release at `lanes.hopperUrl` with a **lane pool key** that hopper issued, in `lanes.existingSecret`. | registration token, bootstrap token, join secret |
| **Leaving** (machine) | A client target that said it leaves (`POST /client/leave`, issue #1284), until it dials in again: no new job is placed on it, the jobs on it run to their end; the Machines view shows *leaving*. Not a **lane**'s `draining`, which is one lane's. | draining, cordoned |
| **Drained** (machine) | A machine a person drained (issue #1511, design.md "Draining a machine"): no new job is placed on it, the jobs on it run to their end, until a person undrains it or it ends by itself. **Drain** and **Undrain** are an admin's (`POST /ui/api/machines/drain`, the Machines card, `hopper machine drain\|undrain`), each recorded (`machine.drained`, `machine.undrained`); kept in the user's settings, so a restart keeps it. A drain names its reason and ends by itself after its hours, a day when it names none (issue #2499, `machine.drain_expired`); a drained machine idle with a backlog is told (`machine.drained_idle`). `MachineSnapshot.drained`: who, the way, when, why, until when. Any machine. Not **leaving**, which a client target says of itself, and not a **lane**'s `draining`. | cordoned, paused, maintenance mode |
| **Sandbox host** | The machines a user's sandboxes start on, first first (issue #2272, design.md "The sandbox host"): `local` (the runtime beside the hopper), a machine with no coding lanes, or a **Podman host**, at most 5. While it is set, a sandbox starts only on the first of them online, never on another machine; none online, a step waits and says `sandbox host <name> is offline`. Set by an admin (`POST /ui/api/machines/sandbox-host`, the Machines card, `hopper machine sandbox-host`), recorded (`machine.sandbox_host_set`), kept in the user's settings. `MachineSnapshot.sandboxHost`: a machine's place in it. Not set: sandboxes start where they did before. | sandbox machine, sandbox node, box host |
| **Podman host** | A Linux computer with rootless Podman a person set up for sandboxes (issue #2272, design.md "Podman hosts"): a name, an ssh destination, its pinned host key, and the most CPUs and memory a box gets there, kept in the user's config record `podman-hosts` (Settings → Podman hosts, `hopper machine podman-host`). The hopper reaches it over ssh with its own key and runs each call of the Podman adapter through `podman system dial-stdio`; its `authorized_keys` line forces that command. Used once the **sandbox host** names it. A box there may take vault secrets, by a copy. Not a machine: no hopper client, lane or job runs there. | sandbox VM, remote Podman, sandbox machine |
| **Retired** (lane pod) | A **lane pod** that left with no job on it, removed from the plugins config by the hopper as a person removes a machine (issue #1284). Its next start joins it again, the same machine. | deleted, deregistered |
| **Link key** | An X25519 key each end of a link holds (issue #308): the machine's in its client dir (`link-key.pem`, mode 600), the hopper's for each user in that user's schema. The client token is derived from one end's private half and the other's public half, the same on both ends. Never sent but its public half. | keypair, device key |
| **hopper.io** | The hosted front door for self-hosted hoppers (tracking issue #469; only its GitHub sign-in, account deletion, the **hopper.io records**, the **privacy note**, and the **hopper.io registrations** with their **registration tombstones** are built, in `cloud/`, issue #463, and its end of the **cloud link**'s connection, issue #2039): a person signs in there with GitHub and sees the instances and machines their **hopper.io account** registered, each by its **cloud link**. It runs nothing on an instance and holds none of its credentials (`docs/hopper-io-security.md`, issue #468). | the cloud, the hub, SaaS |
| **hopper.io account** | Who signs in at **hopper.io**, keyed by a GitHub account (issue #463), and the one isolation unit there: every record at hopper.io carries one, every query is scoped to the signed-in one (issue #468). Its owner deletes it after a fresh GitHub sign-in as the same account, which also deletes the hosted app's grant at GitHub (issue #463). Not an **account**, which is who a part acts as on an outside service. | tenant, account (alone), user |
| **hopper.io session** | A person signed in at **hopper.io** (issue #463): opened when GitHub's sign-in through hopper.io's own GitHub App comes back, a random token in an HttpOnly cookie, kept at hopper.io only as its SHA-256, for 7 days; sign-out ends it and nothing else. It is no **UI session** of any instance, and no instance accepts it. | a hopper.io login, an instance session |
| **hopper.io record** | What **hopper.io** keeps of one act on a **hopper.io account** for its owner to read (issue #463, `docs/hopper-io-security.md` R6): a sign-in, a sign-out, a deletion confirmed as another GitHub account, an instance registered, named, removed, unlinked or moved to another account, or of an instance's connection (issue #2039): connected, disconnected, replaced by a second connection, refused, its key rotated, or a message refused; its kind and its time only, for 90 days, and gone with the account. Not an **event**, which is the instance's. | audit log, hopper.io event, activity |
| **Privacy note** | The page that lists each field **hopper.io** keeps, why and for how long (issue #463, `cloud/privacy.ts`, `GET /privacy`; `docs/hopper-io-security.md` R5): one line per column of its store, held to the store by a test. | privacy policy, data sheet |
| **hopper.io registration** | One instance under one **hopper.io account** at **hopper.io** (issues #463, #465, `cloud/store.ts` `registration`): its **instance id** and when, the name its owner gave it at hopper.io, the instance's last hello or heartbeat, and the public half of the **cloud link key** it connects with (bound at registration, issue #2039), kept when the instance answers `registered` to the **registration code** its account owner entered; one per instance id, so an instance registered under another account moves there (issue #465 part 6); gone when the owner removes it, when the instance unlinks, or with the account. | install registration, linked install |
| **Registration request** | The **registration code** an account owner entered at **hopper.io** for one **instance id** (issue #465 part 7, `cloud/requests.ts`): kept in memory only, never in hopper.io's store, for 10 minutes, at most 10 open per account. hopper.io gives it as `register` only to that instance while its link names the owner's account; only the instance's `registered` answer to it makes a **hopper.io registration**. A refusal drops the code and keeps only why, which the owner sees. | pending registration, code entry |
| **Instance state** | The last **hopper.io state snapshot** an instance sent, and when **hopper.io** got it (issue #465 part 8, `cloud/states.ts`): kept in memory only, never in hopper.io's store, under the account the instance is registered to, and shown to that account only; dropped with its **hopper.io registration** and at a restart. | dashboard data, cached state, instance status |
| **hopper.io machine name** | The name an account owner gave one machine of a registered instance at **hopper.io** (issue #465 part 9, `cloud/store.ts` `machine_name`): kept by the **instance id** and the machine's opaque key in the **hopper.io state snapshot**, never sent by the instance; at most 500 per instance; gone with its **hopper.io registration** and at a move to another account. Not the machine's name on the instance. | machine label, machine alias, device name |
| **Registration tombstone** | What an account deletion at **hopper.io**, or the owner's removal of an instance there, leaves for each **hopper.io registration** it removes (issue #463, `cloud/store.ts` `registration_tombstone`): the **instance id**, the time and the public half of the **cloud link key** it revoked, nothing of the account, for 90 days. hopper.io cannot reach an instance, so the instance's next hello is answered `unregister` (`cloud/link.ts`), and that key connects no more (issue #2039). | deletion marker, removed registration |
| **Cloud link key** | The Ed25519 key an instance holds for its **cloud link** (issues #468, #467, #2039; X25519 before #2039, which cannot sign): one per instance, minted at the link, its private half the system secret `cloud-link.key` that the link record names, only its public half (in the record) at hopper.io, bound to the **hopper.io registration**; it signs the **cloud link hello**. The instance admin rotates it in Settings → hopper.io or with `hopper cloud-link rotate-key` — the key before stays, the system secret `cloud-link.previous-key`, until the new one connected — and revokes it (`revoke-key`), which keeps the link with no key until a rotation; an unlink drops it; at hopper.io a removal or an account deletion revokes it. Not a machine's **link key**. | link key (alone), hopper.io link key, API key, install token |
| **Cloud link hello** | The first message an instance sends on its **cloud link**'s connection (issue #2039, `src/http/cloud-link-hello.ts`): its **instance id**, the account its link names and the public half of its **cloud link key**, with an Ed25519 signature over **hopper.io**'s challenge (a fresh nonce), those three and a fixed context, so it proves the key for that one connection; after a rotation, also the key before, over the same text. hopper.io checks it before any other message (`cloud/hello.ts`). | handshake, login, auth message |
| **Cloud link agent** | The instance's end of its **cloud link**'s connection (issue #2039, `src/http/cloud-link-agent.ts`): the one WebSocket the instance opens to **hopper.io**, only while the link record exists, after TLS and the pin of hopper.io's key shipped in the code. It sends the **cloud link hello**, a heartbeat and, while registered, the **hopper.io state snapshot**; it hands each message it takes to `receive`, scans each it sends for secrets, and tries again with backoff. Its state (connected or not, and why) shows in Settings → hopper.io. | cloud client, link daemon, tunnel |
| **hopper.io state snapshot** | What an instance may send on its **cloud link** for the dashboard (issue #466, design.md "The hopper.io state snapshot"): one closed, versioned schema of counts, closed reasons, and per machine an opaque key and the kinds of its executors — never a machine's name, a path, a host name, a person or job content. Sent whole, never as a change. | heartbeat (alone), telemetry, sync payload, status report |
| **Machine key** | The public half of a machine's link key: who a client target is (`client: { key }`), recorded when it joins. A removed machine's key dials in no more. | device id, node key |
| **Sandbox box** | A locked-down container, from the published image `ghcr.io/henningfutrell/hopper:box-<agent>` (`scripts/box/`; an image of each agent in `BOX_AGENTS`, issue #865), that an older build started and that joined as a client target, named `hopper-sandbox-<name>` and labelled `io.hopper.box` (issues #308, #603, #708). Since issue #1349 no sandbox joins as a machine: the hopper launches none, and the **burn pass** burns each one left. A job's step runs in a **need box**. Not an **agent box** (an ssh target, for testing). | sandbox, container machine |
| **Container runtime** | What the hopper starts, inspects, stops and removes sandbox boxes through (issue #708; issue #603's *sandbox engine*): the `ContainerRuntime` port, with an adapter per kind — **Podman** (rootless, its libpod API over `HOPPER_PODMAN_SOCKET`; the primary and the default whenever it is reachable) and **Docker** (rootless, the Docker Engine API over `HOPPER_DOCKER_SOCKET`, never the CLI), and **Kubernetes** (issue #1286: a box is a pod in a namespace, through the Kubernetes API, with no socket; only when `HOPPER_SANDBOX_RUNTIME=kubernetes`). Selected once, in one place (`src/sandbox/runtime.ts`). Never a rootful daemon. A box on a client target goes through the **remote runtime**. | sandbox engine, engine socket, container engine |
| **Remote runtime** | A client target's own rootless Podman (else Docker), driven by the hopper through that machine's client (issue #708, `src/sandbox/remote.ts`; the client's end `POST /runtime`, `src/client/runtime.ts`): the Podman or Docker adapter's calls, carried down the machine's link and checked again by the client. Its kind is `remote`; a sandbox record of it names the machine. | relay runtime, machine runtime, proxy runtime |
| **Sandbox module** | The one owner of the life of the sandboxes the hopper starts (issues #708, #1349, `src/sandbox/`): need steps and box steps in **need boxes**, the read-only check in **check boxes**, the sweep, the **burn pass** and the view all go through it, over one container runtime. | sandbox service, box manager |
| **Sandbox record** | One row of the instance's `sandboxes` table per **sandbox box** an older build launched (issue #708): its user, name, image and machine, with what that build wrote. The hopper writes no new one (issue #1349): the **burn pass** removes each once its box is gone. | sandbox row, box record |
| **Burn pass** | The sandbox module's pass, every 10 s and at start (issue #1349, `src/sandbox/burn-launched.ts`), that burns each **sandbox box** an older build launched: stopped and removed with its home volume, beside the hopper and on each client target dialled in or that a **sandbox record** names, through its client; the record goes once the box is gone. A box it cannot remove is a **cleanup problem**. | reconcile pass, cleanup loop |
| **Template limits** | The defaults and maximums a template gives its sandboxes (issue #708, `TemplateLimits`): `cpus` and `memoryMb`. No time limit and no kept home (issue #1349): a sandbox does one step and is burned with its home; migration 71 removed both from the stored templates. Absent: the hopper's defaults. Not part of the template's approval: limits narrow what a box may use. A need box and a check box run with the defaults (issue #1349, `boxLimits`). | quotas, resources |
| **Box limits** | On Kubernetes, the most CPU and memory any one sandbox box gets (issue #1422): the chart's `sandbox.limits`, given as `HOPPER_KUBERNETES_BOX_CPUS` and `HOPPER_KUBERNETES_BOX_MEMORY_MB`, and the Kubernetes runtime's `most`. A box that asks for more gets these; one that asks for less keeps what it asks. They are its requests too, so a box with no room on a node waits for the scheduler. | box quota, pod resources |
| **Sandbox label** | A label that marks a container, a volume, a pod or a claim as a sandbox resource the hopper may stop or remove (issue #1371, `src/sandbox/owned.ts`): `io.hopper.box`, `io.hopper.need`, `io.hopper.check` or `io.hopper.playground` (issue #1410), and on Kubernetes the runtime's `io.hopper.sandbox-pod`. The container runtimes refuse to stop or remove anything without one, so a hopper stack container is never touched. | sandbox tag, hopper label |
| **Sandbox playground** | Where a person starts many **playground sandboxes** at once, lists them and destroys one, a selection or all (issue #1410, `src/sandbox/playground.ts`, design.md "The sandbox playground"): the panel on Machines, `GET /api/sandbox-playground`, `POST /ui/api/sandbox-playground`. Its limits are the hopper's settings (`HOPPER_SANDBOX_MAX_CONCURRENT`, `HOPPER_SANDBOX_MAX_PER_USER`, `HOPPER_SANDBOX_DEFAULT_*`, `HOPPER_SANDBOX_MAX_*`; the chart's `sandbox.*`). | sandbox pool, sandbox farm |
| **Playground sandbox** | One sandbox of the sandbox playground (issue #1410): an idle container (a pod on Kubernetes) of an agent's box image or a template's approved image, with a fresh home, no secret and no join code. It is no machine and takes no lane. It is kept in memory only: destroyed on request, at its time limit, and at the hopper's next start. States `starting`, `running`, `failed` (nothing of it is left), `destroying`. Not a **need box** (which runs one job's step). | playground box, scratch sandbox |
| **Sandbox flags** | The locked-down settings of every sandbox box (issue #708, `src/sandbox/flags.ts`, built once): every capability dropped, no new privileges, a read-only root, `/tmp` a tmpfs, its home a volume, the image's own non-root user, and the template's CPU and memory limits. | security flags, hardening |
| **Cleanup problem** | A **sandbox box** an older build launched that the **burn pass** could not remove (issues #603, #1349): shown on Machines with the reason (`GET /api/sandboxes` `problems`), tried again every 10 seconds, gone when the box is. | orphan, leak |
| **Client token** | What a client target and the hopper sign with (issue #59): derived on each end from its **link key** and the other end's public half (issue #308), so neither stores it and it never crosses the wire: each request carries an HMAC of it, and each answer too. | API key, bearer token, password, shared secret |
| **Print-mode agent executor** | An executor that runs an agent CLI in print mode on the job's machine — this one, an ssh target or a client target (issue #865) —, one run per turn, reading the hopper protocol's marker from its last message (issue #307): `cursor-agent`, `codex`, `opencode`, `omp`, and `claude` (issue #533: Claude Code in print mode, which shows no startup screen). A question pauses the job with the agent's session id, the answer resumes that session. Never idempotent; nothing to reattach. | headless agent, batch agent |
| **Cursor executor** | The built-in `cursor-agent` executor plugin (issue #142), a print-mode agent executor: one job is Cursor's CLI agent (`cursor-agent -p`) on the job's machine — this one, an ssh target or a client target (issue #865), never a container target — in the job's work tree, one print-mode run per turn. The job prompt carries the hopper protocol; a question pauses the job with its **Cursor chat** id, and the answer resumes that chat (`--resume`). Never idempotent; nothing to reattach. Cursor signs in on each machine; the hopper holds no Cursor credential. | cursor runner |
| **Shipped plugin** | A built-in plugin (`src/plugins/builtin.ts`). One of a list role other than `machine-source` and `escalation-level` (edited in Question gates alone, issue #444) is **enabled** while an instance names it and is switched on or off in the Plugins view: on adds one instance under the plugin's id with its defaults, off removes its one instance (issue #142). A switch, not an options form (owner decision); installing other plugins from a store URL is a later issue. | built-in, bundled plugin |
| **Cursor chat** | Cursor's session of one job (`session_id` of its JSON result): what a question's answer resumes. Kept in the job's executor state. | chat id, conversation |
| **Command executor** | The built-in `command` executor plugin: runs a job's `body` (its first fenced code block, else all of it) with `sh -c` on the job's machine, through that machine's connection, and finishes with the exit code and output; a non-zero exit fails the job. Never idempotent, never asks. | shell executor, runner |
| **Detected ssh target** | A `Host` alias of `~/.ssh/config` (patterns excluded, `Include` followed): offered by the Add form; a machine edit also takes a typed plain `[user@]host` (issue #293). | known host |
| **Machine edit** | Attaching an ssh target from the UI (`POST /ui/api/machines`): a new `ssh` instance in the plugins config's `machines`, against its `version`. Without an ssh target it adds this machine (issue #260): a `local` instance under the name given, with the herdr session named (default `hopper`), started by the hopper first; refused while a machine is this one already, and in a container, which is not a machine (issue #275). An ssh target that is this machine — ssh would reach this host as the hopper's own user on port 22 — is added as this machine too, with no ssh (issue #275). Sets `ssh` to a detected ssh target or a plain `[user@]host`, `hostKey` only to a confirmed **host key offer** (else the daemon pins the one known_hosts holds, issue #293), never `session`; herdr is called there as the **owned herdr** when it is linked, else by name, from `~/.local/bin` then its PATH (issue #741), so no herdr path is stored (issue #311). Lanes and executors left out are the **machine defaults**; it runs herdr (`herdr`) only when one of its executors is a herdr-claude instance (issue #142). Editing (its name too: `rename`, issue #205) or removing a machine is a plugins edit (`POST /ui/api/plugins`, issue #74). | machine config |
| **Machine defaults** | What a machine attached from the UI starts with: its lanes and the executors it runs — the plugins config's `machineDefaults` (`lanes`, `executors`), edited in the Machines view (`POST /ui/api/machines/defaults`, issue #142). A field left out is the `ssh` plugin's own default: one lane, `herdr-claude`. Never changes a machine already attached. | default machine, template |
| **Lane** | One concurrent job slot on a machine. Opened and closed by Decisions. `idle`, `busy`, `draining`. | slot, worker, thread |
| **Unhealthy lane** | A lane whose last 3 starts failed with the same start error, its lane's fault (issue #2234, `src/engine/lane-health.ts`): for 30 minutes no new job is placed on it, its machine counts it as one lane fewer, and a new lane there gets another number. Then one job is placed there again: a start that fails the same way puts it out again at once, one that gets under way makes it healthy. Its machine carries it (`unhealthyLanes`), Machines shows it with the last error, and `lane.unhealthy` records each time it goes out. Kept in memory: a restart forgets it. | broken lane, dead lane, quarantined lane |
| **Avoided lane** | The lane a rerun of a job that failed for its lane's fault avoids (issue #2234, `avoidLanes` in the Decision's inputs): it goes to another machine when one may take it, else to another lane of that machine, and to that lane only when there is no other. | blocked lane, excluded lane |
| **Draining** | A busy lane the Decision wants gone; it closes when its job ends. Its job runs on untouched: the UI shows it busy, "closes after this job" (issue #381). | |
| **Usage reading** | One budget measurement: `used` of `limit` in a `unit`, optionally for one machine (a machine with throttling readings of its own is capped by those alone: its own account, e.g. claude-plan's `machine` option, issue #139; every claude-plan reading has one, this machine's as `local`, issue #174), optionally limiting the jobs of some executors only (`executors`; absent: every job), optionally of one usage window, optionally informational. Supplied by usage sources: the `usage-source` role's 0..n instances (the plugins config's `usageSources`; an absent section → the built-in `claude` instance of `claude-plan`; `command-usage` reads any agent framework's; the fake is a test double). | quota |
| **Agent framework** | The agent CLI a job runs in: Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and others. One executor plugin per framework (`herdr-claude` and `claude` are Claude Code's, `cursor-agent` Cursor's, `codex`, `opencode` and `omp` theirs); a usage reading limits the jobs of one framework's executors, never another's (issue #140). | provider, model, backend |
| **Token ledger** | What each job's agent spent, per agent session and model (issue #2302, design.md "The token ledger"): input, output, cache-read and cache-write tokens and the assistant messages (`turns`), read from the session's own log, with its subagents' logs, at each **checkpoint** and once more as each run ends — for a print-mode Claude job, from each turn's own JSON result (part 6) —; with the machine, lane and **last account** it was read on. Counts and metadata only, never the session's text. Its **estimated cost** is at the model's list price. `GET /api/jobs/:id/tokens`, `GET /api/usage/tokens`, `hopper usage tokens`, the job view's Tokens panel, the Dashboard's Tokens over time (by day, stacked by repository or account). | token log, spend tracker, cost tracker |
| **Token budget** | The user's setting (issue #2302, part 3, design.md "The token ledger", `TokenBudgetSettings`): the most one job may spend before it is paused — `jobTokens` (every token: input, output, cache read and cache write) and `jobCostUsd` (its **estimated cost**) —, and the **day budgets** (part 5); 0 turns a cap off, and all are off by default. An admin's to set (Settings → Token budget, `POST /ui/api/token-budget`, `hopper usage budget set`). | spend limit, quota, cost cap |
| **Day budget** | A cap of the **token budget** on what the jobs spend in one UTC day (issue #2302, part 5): `dayTokens` and `dayCostUsd` for all the user's jobs, `repoDayTokens` and `repoDayCostUsd` for the jobs of each repository. While one is reached, each waiting job it covers is held (`budgetHeld` in the Decision's inputs, with why), new or going on, until the next UTC day or a raise; a running job runs on. A day counts as the token ledger's `day` group counts it. | daily quota, spend limit, rate limit |
| **Budget pause** | A running job parked because its **token ledger** reached the **token budget** at a checkpoint (issue #2302, `budgetPaused` on the job, with why): its lane freed, its pane and agent ended, its work tree and agent session kept — never killed. Needs you's **Budgets** row lists it. A person's pick up lets it go on with a whole budget again, counted from what it had spent then (`budgetFrom`). | throttle, kill, budget stop |
| **Waste signal** | A flag on a job that spends tokens with no progress (issue #2302, part 4, design.md "The token ledger", `src/domain/token-waste.ts`): `no_progress` (many turns with no commit and no push), `failing_tests` (repeated **failed test runs**), `resume_loop` (started again and again), `cache_churn` (much written to the cache, little read back); each with why.
