A signal only: it pauses nothing. The job view's Tokens panel and the Dashboard's Tokens over time show them. | waste flag, warning, alert |
| **Failed test run** | A shell command of a job's agent that runs a test suite and whose tool result is an error, counted from the agent session's log into the **token ledger** (issue #2302, part 4). Only the count is kept, never the command or its output. | test failure, red run |
| **Tokens per merged pull request** | The efficiency number (issue #2302, part 4): every token and the estimated cost of a range of the **token ledger**, divided by the pull requests merged in it (`perMerged` on `GET /api/usage/tokens`). Every job's spend counts, a failed job's too. | cost per PR, efficiency ratio |
| **Usage window** | The period a usage reading measures, as its source names it (`window`): claude-plan's `session` (5 h), `week` (all models), `week (Fable)`. | period, bucket |
| **Informational reading** | A usage reading that is shown but never throttles lanes (`informational: true`): its budget limits one model, not every job — claude-plan's `week (Fable)`, or a window it does not know. The decider's step 1 skips it. | advisory |
| **Usage sample** | A usage reading as the **usage history** keeps it (issue #385): its time (`at`, when the source read it), source, machine, usage window, used, limit, unit, `resetsAt`, informational, and the account's identity. Kept once per source, machine, usage window and time, so once per read. `UsageSample`. | data point, record |
| **Usage history** | Every usage sample of one user, in their user schema (`usage_samples`), recorded from every usage source each minute (`src/usage/history.ts`); the usage graph reads it. Survives restarts and container recreates; pruned past the history retention. | metrics, time series, log |
| **Machine sample** | One machine's resources at one time, as the resource recorder keeps them (issue #560): CPU busy, load, cores, memory total and available, swap, disk free and total, a WSL machine's memory held and host memory (issue #868), its lanes in use and its lane cap. Kept once per machine and time, every minute while the machine is online with a reading. `MachineSample`. | metric, data point |
| **Memory held** | What a machine in WSL holds of its Windows host's memory (issue #868, `vmHeldBytes`): MemTotal less MemFree, the page cache included, which Linux counts as available but the VM keeps. A line of the resource graph. | VM-resident memory, committed memory |
| **Host memory** | The Windows host's own physical memory, free and total, as a WSL machine reads it through interop (issue #868, `hostMemFreeBytes`, `hostMemTotalBytes`). Host memory used is a line of the resource graph. | Windows RAM, vmmem |
| **Patched Node floor** | The lowest Node version of each line with Node's October 21, 2026 security release: 24.22.1, 26.12.1 (issue #2479, `src/client/node-floor.ts`). Below it the daemon and the client warn at start, and the Machines page shows **Node unpatched**. | minimum Node, safe Node |
| **Memory alert** | The user's setting that tells a person when a machine's **free memory** stays under a threshold for some minutes (issue #868, `MemoryAlert`): `machine.memory_low`, then `machine.memory_recovered` when it is back over. Off by default; an admin's to set (the Usage page). | memory alarm, low-memory notification |
| **Joined container** | A container a person started and joined to the **hopper's network** (`podman network connect hopper_default <container>`), an ssh target's machine for example (issue #1497). After the stack is up, the user service starts each one that is stopped: a host restart stops it, and nothing else starts it. A container of the hopper's compose project, or a sandbox (a label `io.hopper.*`), is never one. | seat, fleet container |
| **Offline watch** | What tells a person that a machine stayed offline (issue #1497, `src/machines/offline-watch.ts`): at each record of the resource recorder, a machine offline for two minutes is told once (`machine.offline`), and once more when it is online again (`machine.back_online`). Kept in memory: after a restart of the hopper a machine still offline is told again. Nothing to set. | down alert, seat monitor, heartbeat |
| **Hopper's disk use** | The disk the hopper's own files take on a machine (issue #1120, `HopperDisk`): each work tree's **`.worktrees`** (issue #1188: its job worktrees, scratch dirs and shared dependencies; and what the layout before left) and each repository checkout in its workspace (`workspace/<name>` with a `.git`, part 5), of each work tree the sweep's survey names, measured at each sweep (one `du` per work tree, with a 20 s limit; none where `timeout` is missing). Shown on each Machines card (`hopperDisk` on `GET /api/machines`), against the **disk budget**. | footprint, storage usage |
| **Cache use** | What the caches the tools keep in a machine's home take there (issue #2494, `HopperDisk.caches`): per kind — `nuget` (`~/.nuget/packages`), `gradle` (`~/.gradle/caches`, `~/.gradle/wrapper/dists`), `go` (`~/go/pkg/mod`, `~/.cache/go-build`), `npm` (`~/.npm`), `pnpm` (`~/.local/share/pnpm/store`), `yarn` (`~/.cache/yarn`, `~/.yarn/berry/cache`), `pip` (`~/.cache/pip`) and `cache`, the rest of `~/.cache` —, measured by the sweep's survey with **container storage**, shown on each Machines card. Not counted against the **disk budget**. | cache size, package cache usage |
| **Container storage** | What each container runtime on a machine holds on its disk, as its own `system df` says (issue #2494, `HopperDisk.storage`): images, containers, volumes and build cache, each with the bytes it could reclaim. Measured by the sweep's survey, shown on each Machines card. | docker disk usage, image storage |
| **Disk measure** | What the sweep last measured of one machine's disk (issue #2494, `DiskMeasure`): when, each place of the **hopper's disk use**, the **cache use** and the **container storage**. Kept in the store, one per machine (`machine_disk_measures`), so a restart still shows it; the **disk budget** is applied at each read. | disk snapshot, saved disk use |
| **Disk budget** | The user's setting (issue #1120, `DiskBudget`): the most the **hopper's disk use** may be on each machine. Over it, the sweep reaps every ended job's scratch dir at once, whatever its age (work not pushed is still kept), and measures again; still over, `machine.disk_over_budget`, then `machine.disk_within_budget` once it is back within it. None by default; an admin's to set (Machines). A machine's `diskBudgetGiB` option is its own, in place of it (issue #2494). | disk quota, disk limit |
| **Disk defaults** | The user's setting (issue #2494, `DiskDefaults`): the disk thresholds (`diskLow`) and the sweep settings (`sweep`: the sweep interval, the scratch age, the retention per job status, the storage prune's age, the trim threshold, the cache caps) every machine takes where its own machine options are silent. A machine's own option wins, field by field. None by default; an admin's to set (Machines, `POST /ui/api/machines/disk-defaults`). Not the **machine defaults**, which a new machine starts with. | global disk settings, disk config |
| **Leftover** | A dir the layout before issue #1188 left in a work tree's `.hopper-scratch` that the sweep does not judge (issue #1116, `Leftover`): one whose name is no job the hopper knows (another hopper's, a test's), or a repository mirror there while one of those holds the layout. The sweep lists each machine's leftovers with size, age and whether each holds work not pushed; its Machines card shows them, and a person keeps or removes each one (`machine.leftover_kept`, `machine.leftover_removed`). The sweep removes none by itself. | orphan, stray dir, junk |
| **Storage prune** | The sweep's removal of a machine's old container storage (issue #2494, `src/client/prune.ts`, `src/engine/sweep-prune.ts`), on a machine whose `pruneAfterDays` option is set, off where it is absent: once a day, or once an hour while its disk is low, the exited containers stopped more than that many days ago with no label it keeps (the stack's, an `io.hopper.` one, a kind node's, a **job container**'s), the anonymous volumes no container uses, and the images no container uses built more than that many days ago, never one titled `hopper`. Never forced; a named volume (a seat's home, the stack's) is never removed. Recorded as `machine.storage_pruned`, with the bytes. | image prune, container cleanup, docker prune |
| **Cache cap** | A machine's limit in GiB on one kind of the tools' caches in its home (issue #2494, `cacheCap<Kind>GiB`, `src/domain/cache-caps.ts`, `src/engine/sweep-caches.ts`): NuGet, Gradle, Go, npm, pnpm, Yarn or pip. Over it — or at all while the disk is low —, the sweep empties that kind, only while no job is on the machine. Recorded as `machine.caches_cleared`. | cache limit, cache quota |
| **Trim** | The sweep's run of `fstrim` on a machine (issue #2494, `src/client/trim.ts`, `src/engine/sweep-trim.ts`), on the filesystem its home is on, once the sweep's cleanups there freed the GiB its `trimAboveGiB` option names, off where it is absent; by `sudo -n` when only root may. Recorded as `machine.disk_trim_ran`, with its outcome; in a WSL distro, it also says that the host's virtual disk shrinks only once compacted there. | trim pass, fstrim run |
| **Job container** | A container or a volume labelled `hopper.job=<job id>` on a job's machine (issue #1120): the job's, whoever started it (the hopper's own tests label theirs so). The job's **reap** removes it, a container with its anonymous volumes (`job.containers_removed`), and the sweep reaps an ended job that still has one. Not removed at a reap with keep, nor at a client's isolation stop. | job resource, orphan container |
| **Job limits** | A machine's limits for each job (issue #2498, `JobLimits`): its `jobMemoryMaxGiB` and `jobCpuPercent` options, set as `MemoryMax` and `CPUQuota` on the **job's scope** as it is made. Off by default. Past the memory limit, the OOM killer stops a process of that job only. | quota, cap (a lane cap is a count of jobs), memory floor (that is the machine's headroom) |
| **Resource limit** (a known cause) | A failure where Claude exited after the kernel's OOM killer stopped processes in the job's scope (issue #2498, `resource-limit`): the job's memory, not its code. Its text says how many. Transient, retry: the job runs again by itself. | OOM failure, crash, code failure |
| **Resources in use** | What a running job's scope uses now on its machine (issue #2498 part 3, `JobUseNow`, `ResourceReading.jobUse`): its memory now, its **peak memory**, its CPU time so far, its **OOM kills**, and the CPU cores it kept busy since the meter's read before. Read by the machine's resource meter; a part of the job's parts list while it runs, and its machine's card on Machines says its jobs' total. | live metrics, current usage, load |
| **Resources used** | What a job's scope used on its machine (issue #2498, `ResourceUse`, `job.resources_used`): its **peak memory** (cgroup `memory.peak`), its CPU time, and its **OOM kills** (processes in it the kernel killed for want of memory). Read by the job's reap from the scope's cgroup before it stops the scope; with them the disk its scratch dir and worktrees held at its end (part 4, `diskBytes`), read before the reap removes them; a part of the job's parts list. | footprint, usage (that is token or plan usage), metrics |
| **Leftover box build** | An image the hopper built for **box tools** (`localhost/hopper-box-tools:<hash>`), or one such a build kept of its fetch stage (untagged, labelled `io.hopper.tools-stage`), that no container uses, that no sandbox record names, and that was built more than 7 days ago (issue #1120, `src/sandbox/leftover-builds.ts`). The sandbox module's reconcile pass removes them beside the hopper, at most once an hour, never forced. | dangling image, orphan image, unused image |
| **Free memory** | What the memory alert watches (issue #868): host memory free for a WSL machine that reads it, else memory available. A container and a WSL machine with no host memory have none. | free RAM |
| **Windows link** | Whether Windows on the same PC reaches a hopper that runs in WSL at `http://localhost:<port>` (issue #712, `WindowsLink`, `windowsLink` in a machine's resources): a client in WSL that dials the hopper on loopback tests it through PowerShell, with WSL's **networking mode** (`nat`, `mirrored`). The keep-alive loop on Windows reports it too (`hopper windows-link report`, `POST /ui/api/windows-link`): the **Windows link report**, kept in memory, told as the machine `windows`. Broken, it is told (`machine.windows_link_broken`, then `machine.windows_link_restored`) and shown with its fix on Machines and in a banner. | host link (that is the **host link base**), localhost forwarding (one of its fixes) |
| **Resource recorder** | The part of a user runtime that keeps a machine sample of each of the user's machines every minute (`src/machines/history.ts`, `machine_samples`), pruned past the history retention. | collector, agent, exporter |
| **Resource graph** | A graph of machine samples over the graph range (issue #560): per machine and resource step, the peak share of CPU busy, memory used, memory held and host memory used (a WSL machine, issue #868), swap used, disk used and lanes in use, and its memory pressure and CPU throttle (issue #2499), with a gap where the machine gave no sample. One machine's on its Machines card (*Resources, lanes and usage*), with the usage that limits it and its lane changes marked (issue #688); every machine's on the Usage page. `GET /api/machines/history`, `GET /api/machines/:id/history`. | dashboard, monitoring |
| **Stack resources** | What every container of the hopper's own stack and each of its sandbox boxes uses now, and what its Postgres does (issue #947, `StackResources`, `GET /api/resources`): **container readings**, a **Postgres reading**, an **OpenFGA reading** and a **Solr reading**, each part with the `problem` that kept it from being read. Read when asked; the **stack recorder** keeps some of it over time as **stack samples**. The **Resources** page shows it. Not the machine resources, which are each machine's (issue #560). | stack metrics, service metrics, telemetry |
| **Container meter** | What reads the containers of the hopper's compose project and its sandbox boxes from the container runtime, through the socket sandbox boxes start through (issue #947, `src/stack-resources/containers.ts`). | exporter, collector, sidecar |
| **Container reading** | One container now, as the container meter reads it (`ContainerReading`): its **service** (the label: the compose service, or a box's container name), `group` (`stack` or `sandbox`), state, health, start time, restarts, CPU cores busy since the read before, memory used against its limit, the cores it sees and the most memory it may use (its limit, else the computer's), network and disk bytes since it started, its layer and volumes. | container stats, sample |
| **Stack sample** | One service's metric at one time, a share 0..1, kept by the stack recorder (issue #947, `StackSample`, `stack_samples` in the instance schema): a running container's `cpu` (of its CPU quota, else of the cores it sees) and `memory` (of the most it may use), Postgres's `connections` (of `max_connections`), and OpenFGA's `check-errors` (the share of its checks that failed since the record before). Kept 30 days (`STACK_HISTORY_RETENTION_DAYS`). | stack metric, data point |
| **Remote box meter** | What reads the sandbox boxes on each client target that holds one of the hopper's sandbox records, for the stack resources (issue #947, `src/stack-resources/remote.ts`): the **container meter** through that machine's **remote runtime**, only the hopper's boxes there, no disk use. Its reading per machine (`MachineBoxes`): the boxes, or the `problem` that kept that machine from being read. | remote exporter, agent metrics |
| **Stack recorder** | What keeps the stack samples (issue #947, `src/stack-resources/history.ts`): each minute it reads the stack resources, on the usage recorder's loop, and every hour prunes the samples past 30 days. One for the whole hopper, not one per user. | collector, agent, exporter |
| **Stack resource graph** | A graph of stack samples over the graph range (issue #947, `StackHistory`, `GET /api/resources/history`): per service, metric and resource step the peak share, with a gap where the service gave no sample. On the Resources page, *Stack resources over time*: a colour per service, a dash per metric, a sandbox box's lines hidden until shown. | dashboard, monitoring |
| **OpenFGA meter** | What reads OpenFGA's checks from OpenFGA's own metrics endpoint (`:2112/metrics`, the Prometheus text format), for the stack resources (issue #947, `src/stack-resources/openfga.ts`). Its reading, the **OpenFGA reading** (`OpenFgaReading`): checks and failed checks since OpenFGA started, and the mean check time. | exporter, scraper, collector |
| **Solr meter** | What reads the search index's Solr from Solr's own metrics API (`/solr/admin/metrics`, JSON), for the stack resources (issue #947, `src/stack-resources/solr.ts`). Its reading, the **Solr reading** (`SolrReading`): queries and failed queries since the core was loaded, the mean and 95th percentile query time, the documents in the index and its size. `GET /api/resources` gives it with the documents waiting to be indexed (the pending index updates). | exporter, scraper, collector |
| **Postgres reading** | The hopper's Postgres now (issue #947, `PostgresReading`, the store's `databaseStats`): connections against its maximum, database size, cache hit rate, locks waiting, long transactions, and statement latency from pg_stat_statements. | database metrics, db stats |
| **Resource step** | The time one point of the resource graph stands for; finer than the graph step: 15 minutes up to a day, an hour up to 4, 6 hours up to 14, a day up to 60, else a week. | bucket, resolution |
| **Lane tuning** | Recommending how many lanes a machine runs from its own history (issue #688): its resource history and the usage it burns. The hopper shows and records each machine's lane recommendation and changes no lanes by itself: an admin applies it (the machine's configured lanes, set as an edit of the machine sets them), dismisses it, or undoes the last one applied. Per machine an admin turns auto-tune on or off and sets the least and most lanes (`minLanes`, `maxLanes`, default 1 and 8). `src/decider/lane-tuning.ts`, `src/engine/lane-tuning.ts`. | autoscaling, lane scaling, active mode |
| **Lane load** | What a machine's samples with one number of lanes in use show over the lane tuning window (7 days): how many, how many at or above each resource pressure, and the average CPU busy and memory used. `LaneLoad`. | |
| **Apply (a lane recommendation)** | An admin's act: the machine's configured lanes become the recommended lanes, through the plugins config edit of the machine, with one `lanes.applied` and its reason (`apply` on a lane recommendation when it can be applied). The last one applied can be undone while the configured lanes are still its lanes. | auto-apply, override |
| **Dismissed recommendation** | A lane recommendation an admin dismissed: not offered again until the recommended or the configured lanes move (`dismissed` on a lane recommendation, `LaneDismissal`, the `laneDismissals` setting). | snoozed, ignored |
| **Resource pressure** | A machine sample at or above a resource's limit: CPU busy 95%, memory used 90%, disk used 95% (`RESOURCE_PRESSURE`). A lane count shows it when more than 5% of its samples do. | saturation, contention |
| **Lane headroom** | The most lanes a machine runs without resource pressure or slow progress, from its lane load and lane progress: one less than the first lane count with either; with none, the most lanes in use seen (10 samples or more), plus as many as each lane's measured cost leaves room for up to 85% memory and 90% CPU, at most 2 more. | capacity, safe concurrency |
| **Lane progress** | The job runs on a machine that ended `finished` or `failed` at one number of lanes in use (a run's average lanes in use, rounded, at least 1), over the lane tuning window, and how many of them failed (issue #688, `LaneProgress`). | throughput |
| **Slow progress** | A lane count whose runs fail far more often than the runs at fewer lanes: more than 25 points more of them failed, each on 4 runs or more (`FAILURE_RISE`, `MIN_PROGRESS_RUNS`). A stall, a timeout or an out-of-memory kill counts as the failed run it ends in; the hopper records none of them on their own. | degradation, thrashing |
| **Lane recommendation** | The lanes a machine can run now (`LaneRecommendation`): its lane headroom below the soft limit; never more than its configured lanes within 10% of the soft limit or past it; never more than its **memory lanes** (issue #2499); within its bounds. The configured lanes when auto-tune is off, offline, or with no lane headroom yet. With a reason and a confidence (0..1, its samples per hour). When the `lane-count` decision point is active and Jev was sure, Jev's pick from the lanes the rules allow, `decidedBy: jev`, the rules' own beside it. `GET /api/lanes/plan`, `hopper lanes plan`. | suggestion, autoscale target |
| **Job run** | One claim of a job on a machine, to the event after it that ended it: `job.finished`, `job.failed`, `job.cancelled`, `job.requeued` or `job.parked` (issue #688, `JobRun`). A job resumed after it was parked has more than one. | execution |
| **Machine baseline** | What a machine uses with no lane in use: the average CPU busy, memory used and disk used of its samples with no lane in use over the job cost window (30 days). A job run's cost is its use above it (issue #688). | idle load |
| **Job cost profile** | What a finished job cost its machine over its job runs (issue #688, `JobCostProfile`): one lane's share of CPU, memory and disk above the machine baseline, average and peak, its duration and the usage it burned per usage window, with its repository, job cost kind and template. Read on demand from the events and the samples, never kept. Grouped by each (`JobCostGroup`): the mean, what a job of the group is expected to cost. | job footprint, cost estimate |
| **Expected cost** | What a waiting job is expected to cost its machine (issue #688, `ExpectedCost`): the job cost group of its repository, else of its job cost kind, with 3 jobs or more. In step 7 the decider passes over a machine where its memory peak or average CPU would reach resource pressure, when another one fits; it never holds a job for it. | estimated cost, predicted load |
| **Placement pick** | Jev's applied pick of a waiting job's machine at the `job-machine` decision point (issue #688, `PlacementPick`): in step 7, of the machines the job may go to, it goes first. A preference, never a hold: when it is not one of them, the job is placed as before. | machine choice, routing hint |
| **Job cost kind** | What a job was asked to do, for its job cost profile: `research`, `proposal`, else `code` (`JobCostKind`). Not the router's kind hint (`spec.kind`). | job type |
| **History retention** | How many days usage samples and machine samples are kept (default 90): the user's setting, an admin's to change (the Usage view). Older samples are deleted every hour, and at once when it is lowered. | TTL, expiry |
| **Usage graph** | The dashboard panel *Usage over time*: one line per account and usage window — one line however many machines read the account (issue #502), per graph step the highest of their readings, since they read the same limit — named by the account and the window, one colour per account, session solid, week dashed, informational dotted (hidden until shown from the legend) — over the graph range at the graph step, drawn like the KPI cards' sparklines. Zoomed by its buttons, a pinch, Ctrl/⌘ + scroll or a drag across; Reset goes back to the graph range. A gap where no source of the account gave anything, a tick at the axis foot where a window reset. Redrawn as new usage samples are kept (SSE `usage.recorded`). `GET /api/usage/history`. The instance admin's, summed over every user, is in Users & access → Users (`GET /api/instance/usage-history`). | chart, dashboard |
| **Graph range** | The stretch of time the usage graph shows: a preset ending now (`24h`, `3d`, `7d`, `30d`; default `7d`), the user's choice, kept by the hopper (`usageGraphView` setting); or a stretch zoomed to, for that page view only. | window (a usage window is a reading's), period |
| **Graph step** | The time one point of the usage graph stands for: the highest share of the limit used in it. It follows the graph range (issue #502): an hour up to 2 days, 6 hours up to 4, a day up to 60 (the default week is per day), else a week. Day and week steps start at the viewer's midnight, weeks on Monday. | bucket, resolution, period, grain |
| **Account** | Who a part acts as on an outside service: the Claude account a usage source reads usage for (email, plan), the **connected account** a `github-account` source acts as. `GET /api/accounts`. Facts only, never a token. | identity (in UI copy), credentials |
| **Tool account** | The account a machine's agent tool (Claude) is signed in as, with every machine signed in as it (issue #1128): one per service and identity, gathered from the usage sources' **accounts** — a usage source reads one machine's tool. Each machine says its **sign-in**: `signed-in`, `signed-out` (the tool read that it is signed in as nobody; those machines are one entry with no identity) or `unknown` (not read, or the read failed), when it was read, and when a job of the source's executors last started there (last used). Its spend is its week windows. `GET /api/tool-accounts`, the Accounts view, `hopper accounts`. Facts only, never a token. | seat, tool login, CLI account |
| **Last account** | Who a machine's agent tool (Claude) was last read signed in as (issue #1269): the identity and organization its usage source's last signed-in read gave, and when. Kept per machine and service in the database, the label only, never a token; a signed-out read keeps it, so it outlives the session and a restart. Every request to sign that tool in on that machine shows it, or that none is known: a login (`LoginView.lastAccount`), a not-signed-in question (`QuestionView.lastAccount`) and a machine not signed in on Accounts (`ToolAccountMachine.lastAccount`). In code `LastAccount`, `src/logins/last-accounts.ts`. | previous account, prior login, remembered account |
| **Tool sign-in** | A machine's agent tool signed in again from the hopper, with no terminal there (issue #1128): the usage source that reads the tool on that machine starts its sign-in (`claude auth login`, through the machine's client, ssh or container), and the hopper opens a **login** of kind `paste_code` on that machine with no job (`run` `sign-in`). A person opens its URL, signs in, and pastes the code the sign-in page shows; the hopper gives it to the tool, whose end completes or fails the login. The tool keeps its own token on its machine; the hopper never holds one. One per machine and tool. When it completes, it answers the **not-signed-in questions** on that machine. Accounts → Sign in, `POST /ui/api/tool-accounts/sign-in`, `hopper accounts sign-in`. | re-login, remote login |
| **Claude sign-in for pods** | The **tool sign-in** the hopper runs in itself, on no machine, for the agent of its **job pods** and **lane pods** (issue #1287, design.md "The hopper's Claude sign-in for pods"): an admin names the Claude account on Logins, the hopper runs `claude setup-token` in a pseudo-terminal, and its `paste_code` **login** (`tool` `claude setup-token`) names that account. One paste of the code its page shows, and the token the CLI printed is the user's vault secret `CLAUDE_TOKEN` (the **agent credential** pods start with), its scope `Claude account <email>`. The token is never answered, logged or in an event. | pod login, setup-token login |
| **Not-signed-in question** | A job's question that Claude has no credential on its machine (issue #533): its text starts with `NOT_SIGNED_IN` (`src/domain/questions.ts`). A **tool sign-in** that completes on the question's machine answers one of Claude's prompt (`sign-in`, `SIGNED_IN_ANSWER`; nothing is typed, and the job's text goes once Claude is signed in), issue #1128. One of Claude's login screen stays a person's. | signed-out question, login question |
| **Job's terminal** | A running job's herdr pane, viewed and typed into by a person from the hopper (issue #1128): its screen read, text written or keys pressed through the job's executor, so over the job's own machine connection (this machine, ssh, or the machine's client). Only a job at work or waiting holds its pane. Access decides each use (`terminal#can_attach`, the job's owner). A **terminal session** is one person's on one job: it opens at their first use (`terminal.opened`) and lasts while they use it at least every two minutes; each input is `terminal.typed`, with how many characters or which keys, never the text. The job view's Terminal, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/terminal/screen` and `/terminal/input`, `hopper terminal job`. | web terminal, pane view, attach |
| **Infra credentials** | The credentials a machine's job user holds, by the well-known place each is in (issues #1128, #1248): cloud CLI config (`.aws`, `.azure`, `.config/gcloud`), kube config (`.kube`), `.keycloak`, `.terraform.d`, git credentials (`.git-credentials`, `.config/git/credentials`, `.netrc`, the GitHub CLI's `.config/gh/hosts.yml`, a **git credential helper** in the global git config), ssh private keys (`.ssh/id_*`), and token variables (`$AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `$GH_TOKEN`, `$KUBECONFIG`, …). Each probe reads which the job user's home and environment hold, by name only, never a value. A `.terraform.d` folder that holds only terraform's checkpoint files (`checkpoint_cache`, `checkpoint_signature`) is no credential (issue #1502). A machine holding any takes jobs with a warning; the **credential check** decides when it is **not ready** (issue #1467). | infra creds, credential dir, infra credential folder |
| **Not ready** | A machine that takes no new job until a person fixes it (issue #1248): the **credential check** holds it for the **infra credentials** its job user holds, or it has an **address problem**. Its jobs are held with the reason (`… is not ready: …`, `… is off-network: …`), never failed; Machines shows it with the guided fix (the commands that make a **dedicated job user**), and Needs you lists it under Machines. It becomes ready at its next probe that finds nothing wrong, with no restart. | blocked machine, unready |
| **Credential check** | The rule that decides whether a machine's **infra credentials** hold it (issue #1467, `credentialHeld`): a warning by default, and the machine takes jobs; a hold when an admin stopped it (**Stop taking jobs**), or when **strict mode** is on and the machine has no **credential acceptance**. The decider, the placement picks and the Machines card all read it. | readiness gate, credential gate |
| **Strict mode** | The admin's setting of the **credential check** (issue #1467, off by default): on, a machine whose job user holds **infra credentials** takes no new job until it has a **credential acceptance** (Use anyway). For a shared or hosted hopper. | strict credentials |
| **Credential acceptance** | An admin's act on a machine with **infra credentials** (issues #1459, #1467): Use anyway on a held one, or Acknowledge on a warning. It is kept with the credentials it named; the machine takes jobs, its warning folds, and a credential found later that it did not name raises the warning again. Revocable by Stop taking jobs, which keeps the row as a stop. | credential acknowledgement, credential approval |
| **Dedicated job user** | A login on a machine made only to run the hopper's jobs (issue #1248, `hopper-job` in the guided fix): no password, no credentials in its home or environment. A credential a job needs comes from the hopper's vault, through a **vault grant**, never from the host's own user. | job account, service user |
| **Machine's terminal** | The panes of the hopper's herdr session on a machine, listed, viewed and typed into by an admin from the hopper (issue #1128): the ones no job holds too, each with the job that holds it, if any. Over the machine's own connection, through the first of its executors that reaches it. Never herdr's `default` session. Access decides each use (`machine_terminal#can_attach`, the machine's owner). Its **terminal sessions** are the job's terminal's, naming the machine and the pane. Machines → Terminal, `POST /ui/api/machines/:id/terminal/panes`, `/terminal/screen` and `/terminal/input`, `hopper terminal machine`. | remote shell, herdr access |
| **Signed-out move** | A job held on a **not-signed-in question** on a machine with a **signed-out tool** goes on at another machine whose usage source reads the tool signed in, with no person (issue #1211): the hopper parks it, answers its question as `signed-out-move`, and picks it up to resume elsewhere from its checkpoint, or queues it as a new start when Claude never got its task. A pinned job does not move. `src/engine/signed-out-move.ts`. | auto-failover, job migration |
| **Busy-machine move** | A job queued to resume on its own machine (an answer pending, `resumeOn`: picked up, **preempted**, or answered in its pane) waits there at most 5 minutes (`RESUME_WAIT_MS`) while every lane of that machine is in use and another online machine that runs its executor has room (issue #1977, `busyMove` in `src/decider/busy-move.ts`, `src/engine/busy-move.ts`). Then the hopper parks it (auto, with why) and picks it up to resume elsewhere from its checkpoint, or queues it as a new start when its agent never got its task. A job pinned by its spec, a **continued** job, or one whose checkpoint cannot be taken or holds no agent session stays. | seat fallback, resume timeout, overflow |
| **Park to move** (a job) | A park the hopper makes to take a job off a machine with no person: the **signed-out move**, the **busy-machine move** or the **lost-machine move** (issue #1892, `ParkedMove`). The job keeps `parked.move` — the move, how it goes on (from its checkpoint or as a new start), whether its live pane's end was confirmed — until its **pick up**, and `job.parked` names it (`move`). Its work item stays queued, waiting on a move to another machine, not on a person. A stop between the park and the pick up leaves the move to that move's next pass (`src/engine/move-finish.ts`). | auto-park (that waits on a person), migration |
| **Lost-machine move** | A running job whose machine was lost resumes, with no person, in place of failing (issue #1569, design.md "The lost-machine move", `src/engine/lost-move.ts`): its machine answers but its work is gone (`gone`) — it resumes its agent session in its kept work tree there —, or its machine stays away past the reconnect or link grace (`away`) — it resumes on another machine from its last checkpoint, and a **job-move saga** ends the agent it left. A park and a pick up with `auto` and `why`. A job with no agent session, or for `away` no checkpoint that holds one, fails as before. | relocation, failover, migration |
| **No-way move** | A job picked up to resume elsewhere from its checkpoint that gets no way to the hopper on the machine it was placed on goes back to the queue, its answer, agent session and checkpoint kept, and that machine is held for ten minutes, so the decider places it on another (issue #2355, `src/engine/no-way-move.ts`): its pane's restore found no `HOPPER_URL` or no readable token file (`noWayToHopper` on the executor's failure), the reach check found the machine out of reach, or the machine gives its jobs no hopper URL or has no shell. Nothing of the job ran there yet. After 3 such moves (`MAX_NO_WAY_MOVES`) it fails as before. | re-place, requeue elsewhere, failover |
| **Signed-out tool** | A machine's agent tool that its usage source reads as signed in as nobody (issue #1128 part 5): `signedOut` on the source's **account**, which names the machine. The decider places no new job of the source's executors (every job, when it names none) on that machine, so the job goes to a machine on another account, or is held with why (`… is signed in: … sign it in from Accounts`). A job resuming in its pane there is not kept off. A read that failed is not one: only a read that says so. `DecisionInputs.signedOut`. | failover, signed-out seat |
| **Lane effect** | What usage does to one machine's lanes now: per executor it runs, the used fraction, lane cap and band (`free`, `soft`, `hard`, `offline`) — the decider's steps 1-2 over the readings that limit that executor — and the machine's own, its least limited executor's, each with its **throttle**. `GET /api/usage` `machines`. | |
| **Throttle** (of a lane effect) | The usage window that sets a lane effect's band (issue #2169, `UsageThrottle`): of the readings in the highest band, the most used. The Usage view says it (`97% weekly`). `burning`: a week window in its burn window. | cause, driver |
| **Soft limit / hard limit** | Usage fractions. From soft, the lane cap is the **soft lanes**; at hard, lanes stop. Together the **usage limits**: the user's, set on the Usage view and stored (issue #522), else `HOPPER_SOFT_LIMIT` / `HOPPER_HARD_LIMIT`. A machine with **its own usage limits** (its `usageLimits` option, issue #1860) goes by those instead: on two machines of one account, the secondary's lower limits make its lanes give way first. Below soft is the **free** band, from soft to hard the **soft** band, at hard and above the **hard** band. | thresholds, throttle bands |
| **Soft lanes** | The fixed number of lanes a machine keeps from the soft limit to the hard limit, at most its own lanes (issue #2091): a setting of the usage limits (`softLanes`), the user's (default 1) or a machine's own. No scale-down between the limits. | lanes past soft, fixed lane count, soft cap |
| **Lane cap** | The most lanes a machine may run given its usage; an executor's lane cap, the most its jobs may hold there. | |
| **Usage pacing** | How the decider spends each account's usage against the time left before its usage windows reset (issue #373): the burn window, placement pressure and the critical priority. `DeciderPolicy.pacing`. | |
| **Week window** | A usage window whose name starts with `week` (claude-plan's `week`; `weekly`): the one the burn window applies to. A session window never burns. | |
| **Burn window** | The hours before a week window's `resetsAt` (`HOPPER_BURN_WINDOW_HOURS`, default 18; 0: off) in which that window's hard limit is 100%: what is left would be lost at the reset. It still throttles at the soft limit (issue #2169). The window is *burning*. Session windows never burn. | burn-down |
| **Placement pressure** | Of a machine, for an executor's jobs: the headroom of its binding week window — to the hard limit, or to 100% while burning — per hour left before that window resets. With reset-aware placement (`HOPPER_RESET_AWARE_PLACEMENT`, default on) a job goes to the machine with the most, then the most **memory room** (issue #2499), then the most room, then the lowest id. 0 without a week window that names its reset. | urgency |
| **Critical priority** | `HOPPER_CRITICAL_PRIORITY` (default 100; 0: off): a job at or above it that fits on no machine may take a lane past its caps — the machine's lane cap, its executor's, or the reserved lanes — on a machine that is online, runs its executor, matches its pin, is not at its hard limit and is not over its cap already — so at most one lane over it. The lane drains when its job ends. It preempts no job (**preemption** does, issue #1525). A job at or above it is a **critical job** (issue #2203). `GET /api/queue` answers it as `criticalPriority`. | urgent, express |
| **Critical job** | A job whose live priority is at or above the **critical priority**, or that has the **critical flag** (issue #2203): the **critical job tracker** shows it on every page. | urgent job, P0, emergency |
| **Critical flag** | A person's mark on one job (issue #2203 part 2, `critical` on the job, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/critical`, `job.critical_flag_changed`): the job is a **critical job** whatever its priority, also while the critical priority is off. Set on a job that has not ended; cleared on any. It changes only what the tracker shows: the lane over the cap stays a rule of the **critical priority**. A new job of the same item does not take it. | critical label, pin, urgent mark |
| **Critical job tracker** | The widget pinned under the header on every page while a **critical job** is tracked (issue #2203, `ui/src/app/critical-tracker.tsx`): each job's status, lane and sandboxes, its current step and for how long, its last actions, and each wait that is a person's with its action in place, by the card or route of its one home. Red and pulsing while a job needs a person, with a browser notification once per new wait. An ended job stays until a person dismisses it, per browser. | critical widget, pager, alert bar |
| **Specialty job** | A job designated to elbow in (issue #762, `spec.specialty`): its item carries the label `hopper:specialty`, read at intake. Only a specialty job or a **tend job** may start a lane past a machine's lane cap, and only when it fits on no machine inside the caps. Its priority does not make it one, and no job becomes one implicitly. | elbow-in job, oversubscribed job, special job |
| **Elbow-in** | A lane a specialty job or a **tend job** (issue #967) starts past a machine's lane cap (issue #762), and the act of starting it. The machine's `elbowIns` option (default 1; 0: none) bounds how many lanes go past its cap at once. Never at the machine's or the executor's usage hard limit, never under resource pressure (CPU at least 95% busy or memory at least 90% used, lane tuning's thresholds), and never on a machine the job may not run on (its pin, a low disk, a problem, the gate). The elbow-in lane drains first and closes when its job ends, so the machine is back at its cap. The lanes panel shows it: `elbow-in · over the cap` on the lane, and the machine's lanes in use over its lanes (e.g. 7/6). | oversubscription, overflow lane, squeeze |
| **High priority** | A job whose live priority is at or above the **high-priority threshold** (issue #535): the user's setting `highPriority`, default 75 — what `hopper:high` gives. Tagged (`high`) and listed first wherever jobs are listed or wait — questions, logins, lanes, Needs a person, parked jobs, the queue, the nav badges —, its runs again made first, its lane drained last under a usage limit, and it takes a free **priority lane** first. The job's *live* priority: a label change at the next sync moves it everywhere, a started job's too. A **low** job is one below the default priority (50). `priority`, `high` on its question, login, failure and hand-off in the API and in their events. | urgent, critical (*critical priority* is the over-the-cap rule) |
| **Priority lane** | One of the lanes a high-priority job gets first (issue #535): a machine's lane number (`desk/lane-2`), chosen by **lane reliability** — the most reliable, as many as the setting `count` (default 1) — or by an admin (`manual`). It is held only on demand (issue #788): while a high-priority job still to be placed could run on its machine, every other job leaves it free (`keep-free`, the default) or only low jobs do (`share`). While none waits, any job takes it, after the machine's other lanes. A running job is never stopped for one. The decider names the lane it opens (`StartPlan.opens`). `GET /api/priority-lanes`; the settings `POST /ui/api/priority-lanes/settings`. | fast lane, reserved lane (*reserved lanes* keep room for pinned jobs) |
| **Discovery** | What the hopper reads of a machine (issue #542): the PATH and its executables, the versions of the known tools (aws, terraform, tofu, kubectl), each AWS identity (a profile, or the environment's own) and what IAM policy simulation allows it of a curated list of write actions, each kubectl context and what `auth can-i` says there across all namespaces, and the credential sources present — names, identities and scopes only, never a secret. A fixed read-only script (`src/client/discover.ts`) run through the machine's own connection, when it comes online, every `everyMinutes` and on demand. Its record (`DiscoveryRecord`) keeps when, the facts, what changed since the one before, and the level it gave. | scan, probe (*probe* is the online check) |
| **Reach** | One thing a machine's discovery says it can affect: an AWS identity, a kubectl context, or a credential source no identity accounts for. Its access is `read`, `write`, `admin` (it can grant itself more: IAM writes, every verb on every resource, an account's root) or `unconfirmed` (discovery could not tell); it is **prod** when a name holds one of the rules' prod patterns or its account is a prod account. | access, permission |
| **Blast radius** | How badly a job on a machine could do damage, rated from its reach (issue #542): `high` for admin reach or write reach to prod, `medium` for other write reach, `low` otherwise; unconfirmed counts as a write unless the rules say `read`. Each level carries its evidence. Rated with the rules now, so a rule change applies at once. A box's blast radius is the higher of its reach and its template's rating (issue #605), with its source (`RadiusSource`): `reach`, `template` or `both`. In code `rate` and `machineRadius`, `src/blast-radius/rate.ts`. | risk, exposure |
| **Blast-radius gate** | What keeps jobs off a machine rated at or above `gateAt` (default `high`), and off every actor machine: such a machine is **gated**, and takes only a job that **passes** — by one of the pass labels or repos, or at or above the pass priority — or one a person **let through** (`gatePass`, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/gate-pass`, admin). A job no other machine may take is held `held at the blast-radius gate: …`. A job resuming returns to its pane. Pure, in the decider (`src/decider/gate.ts`). | queue gate (the *queue gate* holds new jobs for acceptance), sandbox |
| **Actor machine** | A machine set up on purpose for high-radius work, declared in the blast-radius settings with its purpose and the level it is expected to rate (issue #542). Always gated. A rating other than the level declared is a **mismatch**, flagged and told once (`machine.actor_mismatch`). | privileged machine, jump box |
| **Lane reliability** | How a lane has done over the window (`windowDays`, default 14), from the event log (`src/reliability/`): its runs — a claim on it and how it ended there: finished, failed or on a question; cancelled runs do not count —, its **lane faults**, success, median time to start and lane faults in the last day. Ranked by its share of runs without a lane fault, each run weighing half as much per quarter of the window it is old; a lane needs `minRuns` (default 5) runs to be ranked. A priority lane is replaced only by a lane more reliable by more than the switch margin (0.1), so one failure does not move it. | lane health, score |
| **Lane fault** | A failure that was not the job's fault, but its lane's: the machine offline or not dialled in, its link closed, a start or a dialog the agent could not get past, a pane lost (`isLaneFault`, `src/reliability/fault.ts`). | infra failure |
| **Exit at start** | The agent CLI exited as it started, or in the task's turn of a new session before it showed anything (issue #2432, `src/executors/herdr/exited.ts`). Its failure says so, with its exit code (and the signal, above 128) and what it printed after its command line, never the setup before it. The failure assessor's known cause **Exited at start** (`exited-at-start`): a **lane fault**, so it runs again with backoff and the rerun avoids that lane. A resumed session's turn is never one. | crash at launch, seat crash, early exit |
| **Reserved lanes** | A machine's `reservedLanes` option (issue #372): lanes it keeps for jobs pinned to it. A job not pinned to it holds at most its lane cap less these; jobs pinned to it may use every lane. At or above the cap, only pinned jobs run there. Never moves a running job. | kept lanes, held lanes, lane quota |
| **Retired machine** | A machine gone from the plugins config, that no machine source lists (issue #919). A waiting job pinned to it, or resuming there, fails in the next scheduler pass: `pinned machine <m> is retired: …`; a run again places it afresh. A machine still in the config whose instance did not build is not retired, and none is while the plugins config has an error. A told **cleanup leftover**'s entry on it is closed `retired` (issue #2504 part 12). | removed machine, gone machine, dead seat |
| **Decider** | The pure function `decide()`: inputs in, one Decision out. In the UI, **Decider** is also the Settings section of the decider calls (issue #661): Jev's bounded choices, made through TypeSafe. The UI never shows `decide()`, so a person meets only that one. | scheduler |
| **Engine** | The loop that gathers inputs, calls the decider, applies the Decision. | daemon (the daemon is the whole process) |
| **Trigger** | What woke the engine: `tick` or an event type. | |
| **Decision** | The decider's single answer over all inputs: lane plans, starts, holds, waits, divergences (`advice`), reasons, and the inputs themselves. | plan |
| **Admission** | Whether a waiting job may start now. | |
| **Overlap** | Two jobs whose work touches the same thing. The jobs settle it, never the hopper: a job states the assumptions it made about the other work, or makes the needed fix in the other project, annotated with which way the dependency runs. Nothing holds a job for an overlap. | dependency hold, blocked-by |
| **Hold** | A Decision keeping a waiting job out, with a reason: the router, the queue gate, or a native hold (no machine able to run it). Status `held`. Its **hold reason** (`holdReason`) shows on its job card, on the job view and in `hopper queue list` and `hopper job show` (issue #874). Never for want of a lane — that is a **wait** (issue #381). | block, defer |
| **Wait** (for a lane) | A Decision leaving an admitted job queued because every lane it may use is in use. Status stays `queued`; the job's **wait reason** (`waitReason`) names the lane cap that binds — the machine's or its executor's — with its number, or the usage hard limit. No `job.held`. The UI reads it *waiting for a lane*; a job that has a lane but whose memory no machine holds reads *waiting for memory* (the **memory floor**). | held (for capacity), blocked |
| **Memory floor** | The least memory a machine keeps available (issue #2291): its `memoryFloorGiB` option, else 10% of its memory and at least 1 GiB; 0 turns it off. A job starts or resumes there only while its memory available, less a **job's memory** for each job that started there in the last 10 minutes and for each job the same Decision places there, less the new job's own, stays at or above it; else it **waits** for memory and preempts nothing. On WSL, Windows' own free memory counts when lower. `src/decider/memory-floor.ts`. | memory limit, OOM guard, memory threshold |
| **Job's memory** | What a new job is taken to need over the **memory floor** (issue #2291): the machine's `memoryPerJobGiB` option (default 2 GiB), or the job's expected memory peak when that is more. | memory budget, reservation |
| **Memory pause** | The decider's park of a running job because its machine's memory available is below its **memory floor** (issue #2291), its **memory pressure** is at or above 40%, or its **host pool** is below the pool's floor (issue #2499): the job that started there last (on any machine of the pool), whose executor parks and that has an agent session; one per machine every 2 minutes. Parked on the preemption's path, its work kept, and queued again at once to resume there when the memory holds it. `job.memory_paused`; the job carries `memoryPaused` until it runs again. | memory preemption, OOM kill, eviction |
| **Host pool** | The machines whose memory readings name one kernel, by its boot id (issue #2499, `ResourceReading.bootId`): seats in containers on one VM, with the hopper's own machine there. Two machines or more, or one in a container, each with a **memory floor** on. A job there needs room in its machine's memory and in the pool's: the kernel's memory available (`kernelMemAvailableBytes` under a cgroup limit, else the reading's own), or Windows' free memory when one machine of it reads that lower, less a **job's memory** for each job any of its machines keeps, over the kernel's floor. `poolHosts` in `src/decider/memory-floor.ts`. | shared host, VM pool, host group |
| **Memory pressure** | The share of the last 10 s that some task (`someAvg10`) and every task (`fullAvg10`) of a machine waited on memory, as Linux's PSI says it (issue #2499, `ResourceReading.memPressure`): `memory.pressure` of the cgroup whose limit the machine reads, else `/proc/pressure/memory`. A machine whose `someAvg10` is at or above 20% takes no new job while its **memory floor** is on, and its wait for memory says so. | memory stall, PSI score, memory thrash |
| **CPU throttle** | The share of a machine's CPU periods that a CPU quota (`cpu.max`) of its own cgroup or one above it held its tasks back in, since its read before (issue #2499, `ResourceReading.cpuThrottledFrac`): `cpu.stat` `nr_throttled` of `nr_periods`, the most of those cgroups. An ssh target's is that of the one second its probe reads CPU. A machine whose CPU throttle is 50% or more takes no new job, and its wait for CPU says so. | CPU pressure (PSI's), CPU starvation, CPU cap hit |
| **Last OOM kill** | When a machine's meter last saw a process killed for memory (issue #2499, `ResourceReading.memOomKillAt`): the count `oom_kill` in `memory.events` of the cgroup whose limit the machine reads, else in `/proc/vmstat`, grew since its read before. A machine whose last OOM kill is less than 10 minutes old takes no new job while its **memory floor** is on, and its wait for memory says so. | OOM event, memory crash, kill time |
| **Memory lanes** | How many jobs a machine's memory holds in all, for its **lane recommendation** (issue #2499, `memoryLaneCaps` in `src/decider/memory-floor.ts`): its memory less its **memory floor**, divided by a **job's memory**; on a **host pool**, the lower of that and the kernel's memory less the pool's floor and a job's memory for each configured lane of the pool's other machines. From the memory total, not from what is available now, so it does not move with each reading. | memory cap, memory capacity (**memory room** is how many more jobs fit now) |
| **Memory room** | How many more jobs a machine's memory holds now (issue #2499, `memoryLanes` in `src/decider/memory-floor.ts`): its memory over its **memory floor**, the lower of its own and its **host pool**'s, less a **job's memory** for each job it keeps, divided by a job's memory; none under **memory pressure**. Its **real room** is its lane room, only as far as its memory room holds jobs: a job goes to the machine with the most, then the most memory over its floor. Each Decision names it per machine. | memory capacity, memory headroom (**lane headroom** is a count of lanes from samples) |
| **Waiting memory** | The memory a machine's job scopes (`hopper-job-<id>.scope`) hold for jobs with no lane in use there: a job on its own wait, parked, or queued to resume, whose builds still run (issue #2499). The meter reads each scope's memory in use (`ResourceReading.jobMemoryBytes`); a scope outside the cgroup whose limit the machine reads is taken off its memory available. A machine sample keeps it (`waitingMemBytes`), and a job's cost leaves it out; a wait for memory names it. Read only where a systemd user manager runs the scopes. | idle memory, leftover memory, held memory (that is a WSL VM's) |
| **Wait release** | The decider's end of the agent of a job on its own wait (issues #2303, #2326), once its wait is 5 seconds old, memory short or not: every job on its own wait but one on its own command (a build or a test run of the job, in its pane), whatever it waits on (an outside event the hopper watches nothing of — gated, not managed or with no reason given — stays released until a person ends the wait, or its **recheck** when the job named a check-in, issues #2335, #2338), with an agent session and an executor that parks, the oldest wait first. Its pane and agent end on the Park button's path and its checkpoint is taken (`waitReleased.checkpointAt` once it holds the session); it stays `waiting_on`, its wait, work tree and agent session kept, and carries `waitReleased`. The UI shows it as `parked (waiting on …)`. When its wait ends, with that checkpoint it is queued to resume elsewhere: on any machine with room, its checkpoint restored and its session resumed (`parked.elsewhere`); without it, its claim resumes its session in a new pane on its machine. `job.wait_released`. | eviction, suspend, wait park |
| **Waiting** | Status `queued` or `held`. | pending |
| **Ended** | Status `finished`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `rejected` (`TERMINAL_STATUSES`). `/api/queue` `ended` lists those that ended in the last 24 hours, newest end first; the UI's Finished and Failed cards count the same jobs. *Finished* is only the success status, and only a job whose work is **complete** (its **completion** reached) reaches it. | done, completed, terminal (in UI copy) |
| **Job group** | Which of *waiting*, *waiting answer*, *running* (`claimed` or `running`) and *ended* a job's status puts it in. One map in the UI (`ui/src/model/board.ts` `GROUP`); every job list and job count reads it. | phase, bucket, category |
| **Lane span** | One run of one job on one lane: from `job.started` (or `job.reattached`) to the event that ended it — `finished`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `requeued`, `question` — or still `running`; a run started again with no end logged ends at the new start, as `requeued`. Derived in the UI from the event log, with the job store's word on which jobs run now; drawn as a bar on the lane timeline. An operator-led job's span runs from `job.claimed_by_operator` to its end, on the timeline's `operator-led` row, not a lane (issue #318). | slot, run |
| **Question wait** | One stretch a job sat on a question (*waiting answer*): from `question.asked` to the event that ended it — `question.answered` (or the job running again, answered in its pane), `closed`, `dismissed`, `expired` — or still `waiting`. Drawn on the lane timeline as a hatched strip at the foot of the lane the job asked from, which another job may run on meanwhile. Derived in the UI from the event log, with the job store's word on which jobs still wait (`ui/src/model/history.ts` `questionWaits`). | pause, blocked time, idle |
| **Idle lane** | On the lane timeline, a lane with no lane span and no question wait in the window shown. **Hide idle lanes** drops them from the timeline; with more than 12 lanes the timeline draws the lanes that ran first, until **Show all** (issue #2354). Not an **idle reason**, which says why a machine leaves lanes unused. | empty lane, unused lane |
| **Attention** | The UI list of what wants a human now: open questions, a router in fallback, sources in error, recent failures. Derived; nothing stored. | alerts, notifications |
| **Notice** | One thing the UI holds up to the viewer until dismissed: an Attention item or the update notice. Its **notice key** names one occurrence — a question, a failed job, a router in fallback, a source's error text, an update's state and target (`ui/src/model/dismissed.ts` `noticeKey`). A toast is not a notice: it closes, or goes by itself. | alert, notification |
| **Dismissed** (notice) | The viewer hid a notice in this browser (issue #83; the X on it). Kept per browser (`localStorage` `jh_dismissed`), not by the hopper: nothing goes to the daemon, and a dismissed question stays open. A new occurrence shows again; a router, source or update notice seen cleared is forgotten, so its next occurrence shows too. Attention's *show* brings its dismissed items back. | hidden, muted, acknowledged (*dismissed* question is something else) |
| **Claim** | A Decision assigning a job to a lane, before the executor runs. | |
| **Executor** | Runs one job on one lane. The role whose 1..n instances are named in the plugins config's `executors`; a job names an executor instance (`spec.executor`). Built-in plugins `herdr-claude` (Claude Code in a herdr pane), `command` (the command executor) and `test`. One that cannot run holds the jobs naming it (`executor <name> unavailable: …`); never fails or re-routes them. | runner, task (issue #6's "task" is an executor instance) |
| **Plugin** | One module implementing one role: built in (`src/plugins/<role>/<id>/`) or custom (one directory under the plugin dir). Default export a `PluginDefinition`. | extension, addon, adapter (an adapter is the code behind a port; a plugin is the swappable unit) |
| **Role** | A slot the engine calls through one port. Today: `router`, `queue-sorter`, `escalation-level`, `executor`, `job-source`, `machine-source`, `usage-source`, `notifier`. Every role is **live** (since issue #356): it follows the plugins config when it changes, an unchanged instance kept, a new or changed one built, a removed one retired; nothing waits for a restart. A removed job source still reports its own jobs until they end. A **list role** (`escalation-level`, `executor`, `job-source`, `machine-source`, `usage-source`, `notifier`) holds 0..n instances (executors 1..n), each added or removed from the UI under its own name; escalation levels are also moved, since their order is the climb. The others hold one instance. | slot type, kind |
| **Plugin instance** | A plugin plus validated options, under a name (`gate-router`), chosen in the plugins config. | config, profile |
| **Command-bearing option** | A plugin option naming a program, its arguments, a working directory, an interpreter or a sourced file (`bin`, `args`, `cwd`, `workTree`, `python`, `jevPath`, …), or the endpoint a credential is sent to (`apiUrl`), or an identity (`appId`, `slug`), or the environment variable a secret is read from (`privateKeyEnv`, `urlEnv`, `keyEnv`). Marked `.meta({ commandBearing: true })`, carried into its JSON Schema; the UI edits it like any option (admin only) and marks it "runs a command" (issue #198, design.md "UI and mutation"). | |
| **Machine option** | A plugin option naming the machine a part runs on (`.meta({ machine: true })`, issue #174): claude-cli's and claude-plan's `machine`. No default — not even this machine, which is the `local` machine in the list like any other — and picked from the configured machines, never typed. An edit naming none, or one not configured, is refused; a machine one names is not removed. A config stored with none (issue #259) picks one as the part runs, or says plainly that none can (issue #442): see **default escalation machine**. | machine field, host field |
| **Default escalation machine** | The plugins config's `escalationMachine` (issue #442): where a claude-cli escalation level that names no machine runs when the job's machine cannot run claude and more than one machine can. Before it: the job's machine, then the only machine that can. Set in the Question gates panel; a configured machine, followed by a rename, and that machine not removed while it is named. The trail says which machine a level ran on and why. | fallback machine, escalation host |
| **Option choices** | The values a plugin option may take, read from the system by the plugin's `choices(sys)` (the models the `claude` CLI offers), reported in `/api/plugins` `plugins[].choices`. The UI offers them as a select instead of a typed value (issue #151). | suggestions, presets, model list |
| **Settings** | The UI's configuration plane (issue #151): one view, a section per part, routed by hash (`#settings/routing`), listed in a left **sub-nav** of six groups (issue #2373). Configuration only: Plugins, Vault and Users & access are views of their own. | preferences, config page, admin |
| **Users & access** | The UI view (`#users-access`, issue #2373) where a person says who may do what: Users, Access, the permission matrix and API tokens, each a section. It left Settings. | admin page, permissions page |
| **Sub-nav** | A view's own list of its sections (issue #2373): Settings' left column of groups, Users & access's row of four links. | tab strip |
| **Detection** | A plugin's cheap check that it can run here: `available`, `unavailable` + reason, or `needs-setup` + the command to run. A job source or notifier that needs setup still runs (it waits, then works without a restart); any other role's does not. | health check |
| **Plugin dir** | `HOPPER_PLUGIN_DIR`: custom plugins, one directory each. No default: unset, there are none. Code, not config — the one thing a deploy still mounts as files, and only for plugins put there by hand: store installs need none. | |
| **Plugin store** | A git repository whose root holds a **store catalogue**, named by the **plugin store setting**; the UI installs plugins from it (`POST /ui/api/plugin-store`, issue #75). Always "plugin store", never "store" alone: the store is the database. This repository is one (its `plugin-store.yaml` lists `examples/plugins/`). | marketplace, registry, plugin repo |
| **Plugin store setting** | Where the plugin store is (issue #445): an instance setting in the database (`PluginStoreSource`), the **default plugin store**, a git repository an instance admin named on the Plugin store card, or none. Never set: the default. `HOPPER_PLUGIN_STORE` only seeds it while it was never set. Applies without a restart. | store URL, store config |
| **Default plugin store** | The plugin store a hopper reads when nothing else is set: this repository's `plugin-store.yaml` and `examples/plugins/`, built into a git repository the Pages site serves as plain files (`https://henningfutrell.github.io/hopper/plugin-store.git`, `scripts/build-plugin-store.sh`). | official store, built-in store |
| **Store catalogue** | `plugin-store.yaml` at a plugin store's root: `version: 1` and `plugins:`, each `{ id, role, describe, path }` — what the store offers and where in the repository each plugin's directory is. The UI offers only what it lists. | index, manifest |
| **Store install** | A plugin installed from the plugin store, kept in the database (`PluginInstall`: `{ id, role, describe, commit, tree, installedAt }`, issue #93); its code is unpacked into `<work dir>/plugin-store/installed/<id>` and restored from the plugin store at start. Only a store install is updated or removed from the UI; a plugin in the plugin dir is the operator's. **Current** while its `tree` equals the catalogue's directory at the store's head. | store plugin, installed plugin |
| **Plugins config** | The config record `plugins`: which instance fills which role, and every part's options — the only configuration of a part (env holds process settings only). Re-read on change. Always present: the boot that finds none writes the built-in instances; it never replaces one. A section left out means the built-in instances. Written by the UI's **options edit** (one instance's options, every one of them), **select** (a plugin for a one-instance role), **add** and **remove** (an instance of a list role), **move** (an escalation level to another place) and **machine edit**, each against its `version`. | plugins.yaml, plugins document, plugins file, config file |
| **Config record** | One named JSON value the store holds and the parts read (table `config`, issue #198): a user's `plugins`, `rules` and `job-rules` (in the user schema), the instance's `sign-in`. Replaced whole against its `version` (sha-256 of its JSON, or `missing`): from the UI, or with `hopper config set`. Never a file, never YAML. | config document, config file |
| **Database URL** | `HOPPER_DATABASE_URL` (or the mounted file `HOPPER_DATABASE_URL_FILE` names): the one Postgres database the daemon keeps everything in — `postgres://…`, the only store. Required; never assumed. A secret: it carries the password. | data dir, db path |
| **Secret** | Two kinds (issue #451). A credential the hopper is *given* for an outside service: the runtime gives it by name, the environment variable `NAME`, or the **mounted secret file** the variable `NAME_FILE` names (`runtimeSecrets`, issue #56), named by a command-bearing option (`GROKBOT_WEBHOOK_KEY`). A secret the hopper *owns*: a **sealed secret** in the store (a **signing secret**; a **connected account**'s token, by the token box; a **system secret**). A **realm secret** is kept in the store too (issue #216). | key file, stored secret |
| **Mounted secret file** | A file the runtime provides holding one secret (container or orchestrator secrets, systemd credentials), named by `<NAME>_FILE`; read at each use. The runtime's, never the hopper's. | secret file (the hopper's own, gone) |
| **Vault** | A user's write-only secrets for jobs (issue #558, design.md "The vault"), kept in the user's store: set in Vault and never answered back, to any role. In code `src/vault/`. Needs no outside key service; it runs in the hopper, or in the **vault container**. | secret store, keychain, KMS |
| **Vault secret** | One secret of the **vault**: a name, an optional **scope** (one line saying what it reaches), and a value **sealed** under the master key, bound to `vault:<id>/value`. Only its metadata is ever shown: who set it and when, who last changed it and when. | credential, env secret, stored secret |
| **Infra plan** | The plan of an infrastructure change a job recorded (issue #870, design.md "Infra plans: no apply without a plan"): its tool's raw plan (`terraform plan` text and saved plan file, `helm diff`, a GitOps diff), the asset an apply of it is minted a credential for, the target, the repository, branch and commit it was made at, its job and issue. A record of its own, never changed once kept; stale an hour after it was made. Shown on **Infra** (a top-level view, issue #896), where an admin approves it. | plan artifact, changeset, diff (for the record) |
| **Apply** (of a plan) | A credential the vault minted for an approved, current **Infra plan** (issue #896): the operation, the asset, the job that asked, when, until when it is valid, and whether it renewed one. Read from its `vault.minted` event; never the credential. Listed under its plan, and on the applies and drift timeline, on **Infra**. | run, deployment, execution |
| **Infra** (the view) | The top-level view for plans and applies of infrastructure changes (issue #896): Plans (each **Infra plan**, its state, who it waits for, Approve, Reject (issue #2368), its applies), Applies and drift (one timeline, newest first), Out-of-band changes, and a link to **Topology** (issue #1943). Every panel shows always: loading, an error with Try again, or an empty list. | Infra changes (the old Vault panel), plans page |
| **Out-of-band change** | An infrastructure change that did not come through IaC or GitOps (issue #871, design.md "Out-of-band changes"): drift (a GitOps app out of sync, a non-empty `terraform plan` on unchanged code), or an audit-log write by a principal that is no **controller identity** and no **plan-bound apply credential**. Reported by a read-only check — a job's, or an outside collector's with an API token —, kept, sent to the notifiers, and shown on Infra until a person marks it adopted, reverted, or accepted with a reason. | drift (for the record), shadow change, manual change |
| **Out-of-band collector** | A read-only check outside the hopper that reports signals of **out-of-band changes** with a grant's API token (issue #871, design.md "Out-of-band changes" parts 2 and 3); the ready-made one is `scripts/out-of-band-collector.ts` (Argo CD, Flux, `terraform plan`, CloudTrail, the Kubernetes audit log), run on a schedule of the owner's. Its **state file** keeps the grant's refresh token and when its last whole run started. | drift scanner, drift detector |
| **Controller identity** | A principal whose writes are GitOps's (issue #871): an Argo CD or Flux controller's service account, an Atlantis role; a trailing `*` names a group. Set by an admin on Infra. | GitOps identity, trusted principal |
| **Topology** (the view) | The top-level view of the **infrastructure topology** (`#topology`, issue #1943), in the Infra **nav group** beside **Infra**, which links to it and holds no copy: the graph and the **topology globe**, each **topology source**'s state and its last read error, and, with no source, why and what fills it (add an AWS account or a cluster, Discover now). `#topology/<node id>` picks a node. | topology panel, map page |
| **Infrastructure topology** | The graph of the services in a user's AWS accounts and Kubernetes clusters, and what is linked to what (issue #1781, design.md "The infrastructure topology"): **topology nodes** and **topology links**, joined from each **topology source**'s last good **topology snapshot**. Read-only. Shown on **Topology**, its own top-level view (`#topology`, issue #1943); `GET /api/topology`. Each entry of Applies and drift **touches** the nodes of its asset or its target, and links to them; a node lists the entries that touched it. | topology map, service map, infra graph, inventory |
| **Topology source** | One AWS account in one region (`aws/<account>/<region>`), or one Kubernetes cluster (`kubernetes/<cluster>`), that a read-only check reports as a whole: what it found, or why it could not read it (`POST /ui/api/topology/report`, scope `topology:report`). | target, scan target, discovery source |
| **Topology snapshot** | What a **topology source** held at its last good read, with when. A failed read keeps it and makes the source **stale**, with why, so a source out of reach leaves the rest of the graph as it was. | cache, last scan |
| **Topology node** | One thing in the **infrastructure topology**: an AWS account, a VPC, a cluster (EKS or its own Kubernetes API: one node, by its ARN), a load balancer, a database, a queue, an IAM role, an S3 bucket, a namespace, a deployment, a service, an ingress, a service account. Its id is an ARN, `aws-account:<id>`, or `k8s:<cluster>/<namespace>[/<kind>/<name>]`; an **alias** (`dns:<host>`, `url:<queue URL>`) is another name a link may use. A node only a link names (an IAM role of an account no source read) is **not seen**. | vertex, asset (an asset is the vault's) |
| **Topology link** | How two **topology nodes** are linked, from one to the other: `in`, `routes-to`, `selects`, `runs-as`, `assumes` (IRSA), `can-assume` (an IAM role's trust), `uses` (a database or a queue a workload names). | edge, relation, dependency |
| **Topology discovery** | The hopper's own read of a user's **infrastructure topology** (issue #1781 part 3, design.md "The infrastructure topology"): each AWS account an `aws-account` minting credential mints for, with a read session of its read role `hopper-read`, in every region it has enabled (issue #1864), then each cluster EKS lists there and each cluster with a minting credential of its own or a cluster override that the vault mints for, with a read token; reported as **topology sources** by `topology discovery`. The saga `topology-discovery`, every 6 hours and on **Discover now** (`POST /ui/api/topology/discover`). Read-only. Not **cluster discovery** (issue #1766), which finds one cluster's account for a mint. | scan, crawl, sync, inventory run |
| **Topology globe** | The Globe view of the **infrastructure topology** on Topology (issue #1893, design.md "The Topology globe"), beside the graph and with its filters: each **topology node** at the AWS region it lives in (a Kubernetes object at its cluster's), one marker per region with its counts by kind, and an **arc** for the **topology links** between two regions. A node with **no known place** (no region, or a region the globe does not know) is listed apart. Drawn with three.js; with no WebGL the panel shows the graph. | map, world map, geo view |
| **Plan-bound apply credential** | A credential the vault minted for an approved **infra plan** (issue #871): its `principal`, from its mint to its expiry. A write it made then is not an **out-of-band change**. | apply identity, plan credential |
| **Apply gate** | The vault's check before it mints a credential for any operation but read (issue #870): the ask names an **infra plan** of that asset, approved, not stale, made at the commit its branch is at now. No apply without a plan. | plan check, change gate |
| **Infra applies** | The instance setting (issue #1722, design.md "Infra applies off: changes through CI only") that says whether this hopper applies infrastructure changes. On by default. Off, the vault mints only read credentials and no need or box step applies: an infrastructure change goes through CI. The hopper's admin turns it on or off on **Infra**. | applies mode, apply switch, read-only mode |
| **Gated app** | An Argo CD Application that syncs only through the **apply gate** (issue #959, design.md "Infra plans: Argo CD syncs through the plan gate"): no automated sync, labelled `io.hopper.plan-gate=true`, its sync started only with a `hopper-sync` token the vault mints for an approved GitOps **infra plan** of `argocd-app/<cluster>/<app>`. One that syncs by itself is reported as an **out-of-band change**. | gated application, plan-gated app, managed app |
| **Plan summary** | What an **infra plan** changes in plain words, made once when the plan is kept and kept with it (issue #870, design.md "Infra plans: the summary and the MAP tag check"): one line ("Adds 2 security group rules in uat; changes nothing in prod; destroys nothing"), its level (`low` or `high`), its **risk callouts** (destroy, IAM and identity, prod, data, network, each with its resources), its blast radius, and its checks. | plan description, change summary |
| **Approval by risk** | Who approves an **infra plan**, read from its **plan summary** (issue #870, design.md "Infra plans: approval by risk"): a low-risk plan whose every environment named is uat, an operator or an admin; any other plan (prod, IAM and identity, destroys, data, a diff the hopper cannot read, another environment or none named, a plan kept before summaries), an admin only; a plan that failed a check, nobody. | approval tier, risk routing |
| **Environment watchers** | Who is told of each **infra plan**, its approval and its apply (issue #870 part 4, design.md "Infra plans: environment watchers"): for each environment a plan can name (prod, uat, staging, dev, qa), and for every plan (`*`), names as the notifier's receiver knows them. An admin names them on Infra. A notification of a plan names the watchers of `*` and of each environment the plan names. | subscribers, owners, recipients |
| **Rejection** (of a plan) | A person's no to an **infra plan** that waits (issue #2368, design.md "Rejecting an infra plan"), with a note: who, when, and why, a row of its own. Terminal: the plan is never approved after, and the **apply gate** refuses it. A job that waits on the plan is woken with the note. Any operator or admin rejects one. | decline, cancel, withdraw |
