| **Auto-approve** | An admin's setting of **infra plans** (issue #870): on, the hopper approves a plan an operator may approve (**approval by risk**) when the job records it. The approval names the hopper and is shown and on the timeline as any other. Off by default. | auto-apply, silent approval |
| **MAP tag check** | A plan's check that each new AWS resource that takes tags carries `map-migrated=migXWT2TUR4TJ` (issue #870). A resource that exists already is not checked. A plan that fails it is kept and shown, but nobody approves it and the **apply gate** refuses it. | tag policy, tag lint |
| **Minting credential** | A **vault secret** marked with the AWS account or Kubernetes cluster it mints for (issue #580, `VaultSecret.mints`, *Mints for* on Vault): the hopper mints **minted credentials** from it, after Access allows each one. Never delivered to a box and in no template's scope. | issuer, root credential, parent credential |
| **Mint test** | A person's check of a **minting credential** (issue #781, `hopper vault mint-test aws-role/<account>/<role>`): the vault mints a read session from it, as for a job, and asks STS `GetCallerIdentity` with it; the answer is the assumed role's ARN and the **AWS session name**, never a key. For a cluster (issue #854, `hopper vault mint-test cluster/<name>`): the vault mints a read token, as for a job, and asks the cluster who the token is and which namespaces it lists (`kubectl auth whoami`, `kubectl get ns`). Audited as `vault.mint_tested`. | credential test, dry run, test mint |
| **EKS sign-in** | A cluster's **minting credential** of type `eks` (issue #854): it names an IAM role, and the vault mints an EKS token for the cluster from a read session of that role, minted from the AWS **minting credential** of the role's account. The token is STS `GetCallerIdentity` presigned with the session; the role's access entry (or aws-auth mapping) in the cluster decides what it may do. No service account and no long-lived cluster token. | EKS auth, IAM auth, aws-iam-authenticator |
| **Cluster discovery** | How the vault finds a cluster that has no **minting credential** of its own (issue #1766), for a read only: in every AWS account an `aws-account` **minting credential** mints for, a read session of the account's **read role** asks EKS `DescribeCluster` in every region the account has enabled (issue #1845, EC2 `DescribeRegions`); the one account and region that has the cluster signs the EKS token, as an **EKS sign-in** does. None or several mint nothing. A cluster's `eks` minting credential whose kubeconfig secret is gone is passed over for it, with a warning. No vault secret names the cluster. `mintSourceFor`, `mintDiscovered`. | cluster lookup, auto-registration, cluster scan |
| **Read role** | The IAM role `hopper-read` in each AWS account the vault mints for (issue #1766, `EKS_READ_ROLE`): **cluster discovery** signs in to a cluster as it, and EKS maps it to view access. A write on a cluster is no read role's: it needs the cluster's own **minting credential**, or a **cluster override** that names the role its write is granted to. | viewer role, discovery role |
| **Cluster override** | One entry of the user's config record `clusters` (issue #1766, Settings → Clusters): how **cluster discovery** finds one `cluster/<name>` — its name in EKS (`eksName`, an alias), the one AWS account and the region to look in, and the IAM role a write signs in as (`writeRole`). Config, never a vault secret: a cluster holds no secret. A cluster's own **minting credential** wins over it. | cluster alias record, cluster secret, cluster registration |
| **Unknown asset** | A need's cluster that **cluster discovery** would look for — no **minting credential** of its own, no **cluster override** — and that no AWS source of the **infrastructure topology** lists (issue #2067, `unknownAssets`): the vault would never find it. A template request that names one is refused at once with the clusters the topology lists, nearest first; a template that has one shows `unknown asset` in place of its approval, in `hopper template list` and in Needs you, and Access requests lists it with what fixes it (issue #2123). A **short cluster name** of more than one cluster is one too, with its candidates (issue #2168). It waits for no approval: the Access requests badge never counts it; Needs you counts it in its own row, **Unknown clusters**. | missing cluster, bad asset, unresolved name |
| **Short cluster name** | A need's cluster that is the start of the names of clusters the **infrastructure topology** lists, not a full name (issue #2168, `matchCluster`): `uat` for `uat-eks`. A short name of one cluster resolves to it — no **unknown asset**, and **cluster discovery** finds it by that cluster's name; a short name of several is ambiguous, an **unknown asset** with its candidates, never a guess. A **cluster override** of the name wins over it. **Point at** on the template card or Access requests saves the template with the full name. | nickname, partial name |
| **AWS session name** | The role session name an AWS **minted credential** is assumed with (issue #781, `awsSessionName`): `hopper-<job id>`, or the **minting credential**'s own `SessionName` (fixed) or `SessionNamePrefix` (before the job id), for a role whose trust policy allows only some names. STS's `RoleSessionName`. | role session name, session id |
| **Minted credential** | A short-lived credential the vault mints for one job and one operation profile (issue #580): an AWS STS role session (15 minutes) or a Kubernetes TokenRequest token (10 minutes), asked through `hopper-secret` and minted again at each renewal, each time after `decideMint`. Audited as `vault.minted` with Access's decision id. A **check box** gets one for each read need too (issue #797, `decideCheckMint`, `vault.check_minted`). | session credential, temporary token |
| **Vault backend** | Where a **vault secret**'s value may be kept outside the hopper (issue #585, design.md "Vault backends"): a plugin of the role `vault-backend` — built in: `hashicorp-vault`, `1password`, `bitwarden`. Always optional: the vault keeps its secrets itself without one. Only where the value is kept changes; the vault still decides who gets it, and reads the value there at each delivery, keeping no copy. Its own token comes from the runtime, as for any outside service. | secret manager, secret store, provider |
| **Reference** (vault) | Where a **vault backend** keeps one value, in the backend's own form: `path#key` (HashiCorp Vault, KV version 2), `op://vault/item/field` (1Password), the secret's id (Bitwarden). A **vault secret** kept in a backend holds its backend's name and its reference, never a value. | path, pointer, locator, URI |
| **hopper-secret** | The vault's helper on a box (issue #558): written by the hopper client into its client dir, its path given to each job there as `HOPPER_SECRET`. It asks the client for one **vault secret** with the job's proxy token and prints it as a tool wants it (`get`, `kube`, `git`, `aws`), or asks for a **minted credential** (`kube|aws OPERATION ASSET`, issue #580); nothing in the environment or on disk. | credential helper (one of its forms), secret CLI |
| **Key provider** | The vault's one seam to its key (issue #558): the `Sealer` interface (`src/secrets/sealer.ts`). Its default is local: the sealer under the **master key**. The other, optional, is the sealer under a **data key** a **KMS** opens (issue #586). | key manager |
| **KMS** | An outside key service the vault's **data key** is made and wrapped by (issue #586, design.md "The KMS: an optional key provider"): AWS KMS's API: local-kms at `HOPPER_KMS_URL` (the compose profile `kms`), or AWS KMS itself in the region `HOPPER_KMS_REGION` names, with a key an operator made (issue #1288). Always optional, never required. In code `src/vault/kms.ts`. | key service, HSM, Vault Transit |
| **Data key** | A user's vault key when a **KMS** is the **key provider** (issue #586): made by the KMS, kept only wrapped by it in the user's store, opened by it at start; every **vault secret** is then sealed under it. | DEK, envelope key, master key |
| **Log mask** | The secret mask every line the daemon and the vault container write to stdout and stderr goes through (issue #685, `src/secrets/log-mask.ts`): the **master key** and its previous keys, held by value; GitHub tokens; and 32-byte keys as hex, base64 or base64url. The master key never goes to a log, an event, an error or the API: the log names only its source and fingerprint. | log filter, redaction |
| **Vault container** | The vault in a container of its own (issue #586, design.md "The vault in a container of its own"): the compose service `hopper-vault` (profile `hopper-vault`), `node src/vault/main.ts` from the hopper's image. It holds the vault's key and runs the vault; the hopper asks it at `HOPPER_VAULT_URL` with the preshared key `HOPPER_VAULT_KEY`. | sidecar, vault service (the vault service is its code, `service.ts`) |
| **Work dir** | `HOPPER_WORK_DIR`: scratch only (claude's working directory, ssh sockets, the update mirror, the store installs); losing it loses nothing. Each user's parts use their **user work dir** in it. | data dir |
| **Operator CLI** | `hopper` (`src/cli.ts`): `config`, `users`, `user add`, `user transfer`, `join-code`, `ssh-key`, `ledger rebuild` and `local-account` (issue #1380), against the daemon's database (`--user <id>` for one user's records; without it, the one user); `update`, `service script` and `cluster script`, which need no database. Holds the database's credentials, so it may set what the UI may not. It signs nobody in (issue #238). Its operator actions (`OPERATOR_COMMANDS`, `src/cli-operator.ts`): `job`, `queue` and `question` (issue #374), `problem`, `handoff` and `failure` (issue #623, the Failures actions), and every other command from `prs` to `windows-link` (`prs`, `pr`, `repo`, `yolo`, `backfill`, `proposal`, `typesafe-key`, `artifact`, `master-key`, `lanes`, `accounts`, `terminal`, `vault`, `grant`, `template`, `sandbox`, `token`, `report`, `search`, `windows-link`), act on the running daemon: each is the UI's own route, under a UI session minted for that one call and dropped after it, or under a grant's API token when `HOPPER_REFRESH_TOKEN_FILE` is set; the events name it as the way (`via: "cli"`). | admin tool |
| **Login code** | A one-time code that starts a local UI session for one user, role admin: minted into the database only from a signed-in session — a device link, or a new user's login link — only its SHA-256 kept, good once for 10 minutes. Nothing on the host mints one (issue #238). | login file |
| **No bootstrap login** | The rule of issue #238: a new hopper creates no user, no account, no password and no login code, and nothing on the host signs anyone in. The way in is a realm — every hopper offers GitHub — and the **first GitHub admin** is the admin. Each identity's first sign-in makes a user of its own. Replaces issue #237's first sign-in by login code. | bootstrap user, initial admin, first sign-in, first-start login |
| **Rescan** | Re-read the plugin dir and re-run every detection, from the UI (`POST /ui/api/plugins` `rescan`). | refresh |
| **Router** | The role that manages the queue: its advice per job is always applied to admission and order (`Router` port; issue #211 removed the router mode). A role, not a model: what answers it is a plugin. Built-in plugins `gate-router` and `pass-through`. When the one named in the plugins config cannot run, `pass-through` answers and its advice is `source: fallback`. Not an escalation level: the router never sees a question. | advisor, classifier |
| **Router selection** | How the router instance was chosen: `file` (named in the plugins config) or `detected` (none named: the first router plugin that can run here, built-ins first, then custom; `pass-through` when none can — chosen, not a fallback). | auto, default router |
| **Advice** | A router's action + reason + details for one job; `source` names the plugin, or `fallback`. | classification, verdict |
| **Jev** | grok-bot-jev's usage router; the `gate-router` router plugin runs it from the checkout at `jevPath`. | |
| **Jev gate** | One named judgement Jev's router asks about a job (`intent`, `reuse_cache`, `needs_subagent`, `stop_retry`, `complexity`), answered by Jev through TypeSafe or by the Claude model; `details.gatesBy` says which (`jev` | `claude`). Jev's own code calls them questions; here **question** is a job's question to a human. | question, classification |
| **GitHub App private key** | Gone (issue #1377): the hopper keeps no GitHub App private key and asks for none. Every GitHub read and write uses the signed-in user's token of the **hopper's app**. Tenant migration 69 removed the vault row `github-app-private-key`. | app key, app PEM, `privateKeyEnv` (gone) |
| **TypeSafe** | Jev's own gate service (`typesafe_sdk`); answers Jev's gates `intent`, `reuse_cache` and `stop_retry` (`JEV_GATES`, fixed), and the decider calls, when the user's **TypeSafe API key** is set (read per call). Makes the **decider calls** (Settings → Decider); in code, **Decider** is `decide()`. | decider |
| **TypeSafe API key** | The key Jev asks TypeSafe with, one per user (issue #657): set on the Jev page (Settings → Decider) or by `hopper typesafe-key set` from stdin, checked once against TypeSafe, kept as a **system secret**, read at each decision; shown only as set or not, its last 4 characters and when. Without it Jev is off. The variable `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is only imported once. | TypeSafe key, TYPESAFE_API_KEY |
| **System secret** | A secret the hopper keeps for itself, in the vault's **system scope** (issues #657, #658): a row of a vault named `system/<name>` — the user's vault for their **TypeSafe API key**, their GitHub connection's access and refresh tokens (`connected-account.github.*`) and each webhook signing secret (`webhook.<id>.signing-secret`); the **instance's vault** for each sign-in realm's secret (`sign-in.<realm>.<setting>`) and the **GitHub webhook secret** (`github-webhook-secret`, issue #1079) —, sealed under the master key, never in clear; listed only in the system scope on Vault (its last 4 characters, never its value), in no template, never given to a job, machine or box, never minted from. Written only through `keep`; each set, replace, rotation, removal, move and read by the hopper is an event (`vault.secret_*`), its **audit trail**. Access (`system_secret`) decides a person's change, the user or an admin, and denies every job's read. A user's TypeSafe API key or webhook signing secret may instead be kept in a **vault backend** (issue #658): its row holds only the backend and **reference**, and the hopper holds the value it read there in memory only. | internal secret, hopper secret |
| **Shared secret** | A vault secret the hopper's admin shares with the agents of every user, or of the users named (its **readers**), in place of a copy in each user's vault (issue #718): a row of the **instance's vault** named `shared/<name>`, sealed under the master key, set and removed only by the instance admin (`POST /ui/api/vault/shared`, `hopper vault set <name> --shared`). Every user's vault lists it, never its value. A template names it as `shared/<name>`; a job on a box of the template gets it only once a person approved that, and Access (`shared_secret#can_read`, the readers told at each ask) allows the job's user. A vault secret is the user's by default. Not a **system secret**: those are the hopper's own and never given to a job. | server secret, global secret, system-scope secret |
| **Instance's vault** | The vault table of the instance schema (issue #658, instance migration 33): the system secrets of the whole hopper, its sign-in realms' secrets, and the **shared secrets** (issue #718). It holds no user's vault secret and no template. | instance vault |
| **Divergence** | A job where the advice's verdict differs from the native one: the advice held it, or changed its order. | |
| **Waiting answer** | Status `waiting_answer`: a job stopped on a question. Holds no lane; its pane stays open. | blocked, paused (a *paused* source is something else) |
| **Own wait** (a job's) | Status `waiting_on` (issue #483): a job blocked on something only a person or the outside world can do ended its turn with `HOPPER_WAITING`, naming what it waits **for** and, if it can, **until** — how it will know (a background poll it started). No question opens; it holds no lane; its pane and agent stay; nothing is typed into it. It goes on when its agent goes on by itself (its poll woke it), a person **ends the wait** (UI End the wait, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/end-wait`), or the hopper ends it with the answer to a **watch** of the job's (issue #998). `job.waiting`, `job.wait_ended`. UI "On its own wait". | parked (a person's act, holds nothing live), blocked, sleeping, a question only to wait |
| **Waits on** (a job's own wait) | What a job's **own wait** waits on, typed (issue #1245, `WaitOn`, `wait.on`): a pull request (its number, and `checks`, `merge` or `review`), a job, an item, a person's question, a sandbox or template approval, an **infra plan**'s approval (issue #2205, `infra plan <id>`), a vault grant, its own command, or an outside event. The job names it on an `on:` line after `HOPPER_WAITING`; else it is read from its `for:` and `until:`, and an outside event at last. A job never enters `waiting_on` without it. The hopper ends the wait on the event that settles it: a pull request merged or closed, a job ended, an item's issue closed, a question answered or ended, a grant given or denied, an infra plan approved (`settledBy`); an approval through the skill request's watch. A wait from before, with none, is stuck work: "waiting on nothing recorded". | dependency, blocker, waits for (that is the `for:` text) |
| **Short id** (a job's) | The first 8 characters of a job's id, as the views show a job (issue #1780, `SHORT_ID_LENGTH`, `src/domain/job-id-start.ts`). `GET /api/jobs/:id`, and so `hopper job show`, take one, or any longer start of the id; a start more than one job's id has is refused (409). | job prefix, abbreviated id |
| **Watched item** (a job's own wait) | An issue, a pull request or a workflow run that a job's own wait on an item or on an outside event names in its text (issue #1508, `WatchedRef`, `src/domain/watched-items.ts`): `owner/name#N`, `name#N`, `issue N`, `<repo> run N`, a link. The hopper reads each at every sync of the job's source and wakes the job once every one ended (`job.waited_items_ended`); an outside wait leaves out one that ended before the wait began (issue #2219, `endedBeforeWait`). A wait that names none and only a background poll in its pane can end is one its **pane carries**: lost with its pane, the hopper wakes it. | dependency, tracked item |
| **Comment wake** (a job's own wait) | A comment that ends a job's **own wait** (issue #2159, `src/domain/comment-wake.ts`): while the job waits, a **GitHub watch** follows its issue and its own pull request; the first new comment there since the wait began by a trusted account — the user's connected account or a maintainer of the repository, as for a **thread ask**'s reply — and not one a job posted ends the wait. The job is told the comment between untrusted-text markers and decides whether its wait still holds. `job.wait_ended { by: hopper, comment }`; the job view shows "woken by a comment". A comment by anyone else wakes nothing. | comment trigger, ping, nudge |
| **Own command wait** (a job's) | An **own wait** whose `until:` names the job's own background command (issue #1037): `until: own command <id> ends`, or `background command <id> ends` (`namesOwnCommand`, `src/domain/own-command.ts`; a poll is not one). Marked `ownCommand` on the wait and on `job.waiting`. Nothing outside the job is involved, so it is running work: stage *running*, "Running: waiting on its own command", its runtime from when it began to wait; not in the Waiting count nor waiting on you. When its pane shows no background work for the **own command grace** (`HOPPER_OWN_COMMAND_GRACE_MS`, default 5 min) and its agent did not go on, the hopper types one nudge into its pane (`job.own_command_nudged`); a grace after that its **wait check** ends the wait (issue #1694); a wait flagged **stuck** before (`stuckAt`, `job.own_command_stuck`) is ended so too. | own job wait, self wait, background wait |
| **Wait check** (a job's own wait) | What the hopper tests of a job's **own wait**, with no model, and when (issue #1694, `WaitCheck`, `wait.check`, `src/domain/wait-checks.ts`): its **condition** (`items_ended`, `job_ended`, `question_settled`, `command_exited`, `pr_checks_ended`, `pr_review_ended`, `time_passed`, `recheck`, or `none`), its next check (`nextCheckAt`), the time between checks (doubling), its **deadline**, and what the last check found. Kept with the wait, so any hopper goes on from it. The **wait sweeper** tests each check due under a lease and ends the wait when it is met; past its deadline the wait is **overdue** (`job.wait_overdue`), once. | wait condition, poll, timer |
| **Recheck** (a job's own wait) | The **wait check** of a wait on an outside event the hopper watches nothing of (issue #2335, `recheck`, `src/domain/wait-checks.ts`): once a background poll in the job's pane watched it; now the agent ends with every other wait (**wait release**), and at the check-in the job named on its `check:` line the hopper ends the wait and tells the job it cannot see what it waits on, to look again and to wait again if it did not happen yet. With no check-in there is no recheck (issue #2338): the wait check is `none`, and only a person (End the wait) or a time the job named wakes it; its lost pane does not. | poll, timer, wake-up |
| **Check line** (a job's own wait) | The `check:` line a job may write after `HOPPER_WAITING` (issue #1694): `in <time>` or `every <time>`, when the hopper checks its wait next; `after <time>` or `at <ISO time>`, a time the hopper wakes it at. Kept with the wait (`wait.checkLine`); its **wait check** reads it (`checkAskOf`). | check-in line, wake time |
| **Question** | What a running job needs answered before it continues, with its escalation trail. | prompt, query |
| **Question form** | The one UI form a person answers a question or a decision in (issue #1169, `ui/src/components/question-form.tsx`): a shadcn `Questionnaire` whose title names what is asked, with the options as choices and a free answer as a text box; Ctrl/Cmd+Enter submits. An open question's and a parked job's answer, a correction of an auto-answer and a decider pick's override use it. Beside it, the **answer source**: a marker that says whether a person answered, or a level or Jev with no person (`ui/src/model/answer-source.ts`). | answer box, reply form |
| **Raising machine** | The machine a question was asked on (issue #485): its id, its name (label) then, and the lane, recorded on the question as `raisedBy` — a snapshot taken when it is asked, kept as it was after the job moves on or the machine is renamed or removed. Every `question.*` event carries it (the `machineId` subject, `laneId` where known, and `raisedBy` in its data); the Questions view, the question history, event lines, the Grok Bot routine body and webhooks show it; a question with none reads *machine unknown*. A login prompt a machine raises names it the same way. Not the machine an escalation level ran on (`QuestionAttempt.machine`). | origin machine, source host, asking node |
| **Login** | A sign-in a machine or agent waits on (issue #476): a CLI in a job, or an escalation level's run, showing a device code to enter at a URL. Handled by its **login kind**, never as a question: it opens none and never reaches an escalation level. Kept in `logins` without its URL or code — those stay in the daemon's memory only, answered only to a UI session of the user whose role is operator or admin (issue #477), and dropped when it ends. Shown in the **Logins** view, never in Questions; it warns before its code runs out (the logins setting `warnSec`). `pending`, then `completed` (a **login signal** or its **login outcome** said it went through), `expired`, `cancelled`, `failed` (what waited on it ended first, the user denied it, or its login outcome said it did not go through) or `dismissed` (a person put it away, issue #1110: **dismiss**). A completed or dismissed login whose requester then says it does not work is **reopened**: `pending` again, with the reason (`auth.reopened`). One open login per job (or question) and prompt — the same tool, code or URL (issue #567): the same code again is the same login, a new code updates it. Its tool, the host of its URL, and, when the tool shows them, the account and scopes say what asks (issue #698); a device code on a herdr job's screen that the job did not report is a login too. When its code expires, the logins setting `onExpiry` says what follows: `renew` (the default, issue #1016), the hopper asks the job for a new code by itself, each time, until the login is completed or cancelled; `hold`, a person asks for one; `fail`, the job fails. Not the *login code* (a one-time code that signs a browser into the hopper's UI) and not *sign-in* (signing in to the hopper). In code `Login`, `src/logins/`; its events are `auth.*`. | auth request, login lane, credential prompt |
| **Proposal** | What a job asked for one writes instead of doing the work (issue #537): since issue #651 a set of **paths** — a TL;DR, a short problem statement, 0..N paths, the recommended path or combination and why, and the context it relied on — ended with the marker HOPPER_PROPOSAL. Zero paths is valid with the reason (no change is needed, or no viable path was found). Never a question: its own routes, view, badge and events (`proposal.*`). Its job waits on it (`waiting_answer`, `proposalId`). `open` (in review, at its **stage**: a reviewer level or `human`), `revising` (sent back; its job writes the next version), `accepted`, `rejected`, `cancelled` (its job ended first). A person accepts it by **selecting** paths, or **rejects** it whole (issue #1659), with paths or none, in review or sent back. In code `ReviewItem` of kind `proposal`, `src/domain/review.ts`, `src/domain/proposal-paths.ts`. | plan, RFC, design doc |
| **Path** (proposal) | One alternative strategy of a proposal (issue #651): its number (`id`, kept from one version to the next), title, TL;DR (`summary`), **tradeoffs** (security, effort, risk, friction for the person), what continuing with it creates (jobs, issues, research), any labelled lines the format does not name (`otherFields`, such as `Role:`, issue #755), and its full Markdown. Its card shows the TL;DR as Markdown and each tradeoff and other field as its own labelled row. The agent marks one or several **recommended**. A reviewer level may add a path (`addedBy`) or mark one **not viable** (`notViable`, with why), which cannot be selected. A proposal written before paths reads as one path (`single`). `ProposalPath`, `ProposalPathSet`. | option, alternative, choice |
| **Selection** (proposal) | The paths a person continues with, each with an optional note (`PathPick`). Taken with **Continue with selected** (the `accept` decision); kept on the proposal as `selection` when the person asks for more paths or steers, so the next version starts with it checked; recorded at sign-off as `signOff.selected`, each with its follow-on job. | choice, vote |
| **Follow-on** | A job that continues one selected path of an accepted proposal (issue #651): the parent job's spec, priority and work tree, accepted at once, told its path, the note and the other selected paths (`followOn` names the parent job, the proposal and the path). It has the parent's source as its own (issue #859): it ships on the parent's repository, and only a pull request it opens makes the parent's source item done; the parent's job, finished when the proposal was accepted, leaves that item to it. The proposal shows each follow-on's live status (`ReviewItemView.followOns`). | child job, sub-job, spin-off |
| **Proposal version** | One proposal as the agent wrote it: version 1, then one per revision. Every version is kept, each with its **parts** (`sections`) read from its labels and the parts it left out (`missing`). | revision (a *revision* is the act of writing the next version) |
| **Asked for a proposal** | A job whose agent is told to write a proposal instead of doing the work (`spec.proposal`): its item is labelled `hopper:proposal` or has a Proposal heading in its body, or a person asked it before it started (Propose). A special job. | |
| **Reviewer level** | An escalation level named in a review section's settings' `reviewers`, reviewing a review item — a proposal or a research report (`EscalationLevel.review`): its **verdict** — `approve`, `request_changes` or `escalate` — with notes. A level that fails, times out, replies malformed or cannot review escalates; it never decides. | reviewer model, approver |
| **Pre-check** | The structural check of a review item's newest version before the reviewer levels, with no model call (issue #631, `src/review/pre-check.ts`); it runs only where the section's settings name reviewer levels. Its gaps, each one fixed line: a part left out, no summary (the section's first part: a proposal's TL;DR, a report's Question), a proposal's path that leaves out a part or a set with no recommendation (issue #651), a broken link (no target, an anchor that names no heading, a URL that does not parse), a next version the same as the one sent back. An item with gaps goes back to its job with the gap list (stage `pre-check`, role `check` on the trail); after `PRE_CHECK_RETURNS` send-backs, to a person. Not a reviewer level: its send-backs do not count against `levelRevisions`. | lint, validation, gate |
| **Sign-off** | A review item's acceptance or rejection (a research report is only ever accepted): who (a person, by name, or the top reviewer level where the settings let it), when, which version, and the notes. Recorded on the item (`signOff`) and sent as `<prefix>.accepted` / `<prefix>.rejected`; its job then moves on to the next section it asks for, goes on with its own work (accepted research it was not asked for, issue #538), or ends finished. | approval (a level's `approve` is a verdict, not a sign-off) |
| **Proposal settings**, **research settings** | A review section's settings, the user's (`ReviewSettings`, one per section): `reviewers` (the reviewer levels, lowest first), `signOff` (`owner`: only a person accepts; `top-level`: the top reviewer level's approval accepts too), `levelRevisions` (how often the reviewer levels may send one back before a person decides), and a proposal's `autoContinue` (**continued by the hopper**). In the database, read at each review. | |
| **Continued by the hopper** | A proposal the hopper signed off itself (issue #1059), at the stage `hopper`, with its one recommended path selected, where a person would have decided: no `Needs a person:` line, no reviewer level escalated it, no gaps, one viable path recommended, no risk rule on it. In code `AUTO_CONTINUE`, `autoContinue`. | auto-accepted, auto-approved |
| **Needs a person** | The line a job writes in a proposal when a person must decide (issue #1059): the case (security or cost the rules do not settle, a credential or outside access, a destructive change) and which rule does not settle it. In code `needsAPerson`. | |
| **Decisions line** | The fixed protocol line that tells every job to decide and go on when a path is clear inside the standing rules, and when to ask a person instead (issue #1059). In code `DECIDE_LINE`. | |
| **Decisions made** | The heading in a job's pull request body that lists each decision the job made itself, so a person can change it later (issue #1059). | |
| **Section** | A category of things that wait on or involve a person, with its nav entry (or its tab of a **merged page**, issue #2374), list, badge and events: Questions, Proposals, Research, Logins, Failures, Parked (issues #543, #565; `SECTIONS`, `src/domain/sections.ts`). Its badge counts what is open and waits on a person, seen or not, and stays up while anything is. | area, inbox, category |
| **Page choice** | How a long UI page shows its items, kept per browser so a reload keeps it (issue #2353, `localStorage` `jh_page_prefs`, `ui/src/model/page-prefs.ts` pure, `ui/src/hooks/use-page-pref.ts`): a page's mode (Cards or **Rows**; the Jobs view's Graph or List) and each group or section folded or open. One key each, `<page>.<what>`; the newest 400 are kept. Nothing goes to the daemon. Which page of a list shows is not one: a reload starts at the first. | layout setting, preference (a setting is the daemon's) |
| **Rows** (mode) | A page's mode that shows each item on one line, beside **Cards**, which shows it whole (issue #2353): Pull requests, Failures, Questions, Proposals and Research. A row keeps the item's facts that fit, each in full in its title; its acts and details are in Cards. On Questions, Proposals and Research a row opens its card in place. A page choice. | compact, condensed, dense, list view |
| **Pager** | Back and More under a long list, and where it is (`1–30 of 250`) (issue #2353, `ui/src/components/pager.tsx`): 30 rows or 6 cards to a page. A new filter starts again at the first page. None when the list fits one page. Search pages by 20 on the daemon. | pagination bar, infinite scroll |
| **Jump list** | Settings' list that names every section by its group and opens the one picked (issue #2353, `ui/src/views/settings.tsx`), above the section links, which are in groups that fold. | section picker |
| **Nav group** | A group of nav entries in the UI's main menu, with a heading a person collapses (issue #906): Waiting on you, Work, Fleet, Infra, Admin (`NAV_GROUPS`, issue #2374; none holds one entry only, issue #1943, `ui/src/app/routes.ts`). The **Dashboard** and Needs you are pinned above them, in no group (`NAV_PINNED`): the Dashboard covers the whole system; Needs you's badge already sums other entries'. A collapsed one shows its entries' badges as one — counts summed, the most urgent colour — so no badge is hidden (`groupBadges`, `ui/src/model/nav-groups.ts`). Which are collapsed is kept per browser. Not a section. | menu section, category, folder |
| **Merged page** | One nav entry whose **tabs** filter one workflow, each tab a view that was its own entry before (issue #2374, `PAGE_TABS`, `ui/src/app/routes.ts`, `ui/src/app/page-tabs.tsx`): **Approvals**, Failures (Failures with Stuck work, Parked), Jobs (Jobs, Queue order), **Ask hopper**, **Activity**, Machines (Machines, Accounts, Logins), Usage (Usage, Resources). The hash names the tab (`#<page>/<tab>`), the first by default; the old view's hash opens it. Its entry's badge sums its tabs' badges (`mergedBadge`), and each tab shows its own. A tab is never a second home of another workflow. | hub, combined view, super page |
| **Approvals** (view) | The **merged page** of what waits on a person's approval (`#approvals`, issue #2374): the tabs **Access requests** and Pending approvals (each **pending approval**). | permissions page, requests page |
| **Activity** (view) | The **merged page** of what the hopper did and decided (`#activity`, issue #2374): the tabs Events (every event, newest first) and Decisions (each decision, with its verdicts). | event log page, audit page |
| **Review section** | A section whose items a job writes instead of doing the work, reviewed by reviewer levels and a person, versions kept: Proposals and Research. One model; each is declared once by its type (`ReviewSectionType`, `REVIEW_SECTIONS`): its marker, label, heading, parts, decisions, events. | |
| **Review item** | One item of a review section: a proposal or a research report (`ReviewItem`, its `kind`). | document, report (alone) |
| **Research report** | What a job asked to research writes instead of doing the work (issues #538, #543): the question, findings, sources and evidence, confidence, open threads and a next step, ended with HOPPER_RESEARCH_REPORT. Its versions are its **rounds**. A person accepts it, asks to **dig deeper** (on the whole report or on chosen open threads) or **steers** it (a new direction): the last two start the next round in the same job. | finding, study |
| **Phase** | What a job is doing (issue #548): `work`, `research` or `proposal`. Set by a **phase shift** (`phase`), else read from what its spec asks for. Shown wherever the job is named, unless it is `work`; `job.phase_changed` records every move. | stage (*stage* is a question's or review item's holder), mode |
| **Phase shift** | Answering a job's question with **Research this** or **Propose this** (issue #548), with an optional note scoping the aspect: a **fork** or a **switch**. Made by a person, or by an escalation level the phase-shift settings name; a job or a level may **suggest** one, which the question card offers as one click. | redirect, escalation |
| **Fork** (phase shift) | A separate job asked for research or a proposal about the aspect (`forkOf` names the job and question it came from; the parent lists its `forks`). It has no source of its own. The parent is parked while it runs (issue #2335; where its executor can park); the fork's accepted result answers the question. It shows on its question (`QuestionView.forks`, issue #570); one of each kind runs at a time. A question answered without it keeps its answer on the fork (`forkOf.answered`), and its acceptance then delivers nothing. | child job, sub-job, spin-off |
| **Switch** (phase shift) | The same job and session move into research or a proposal (`shift`). At Accept the person picks what it does next (`then`): back to `work` with the document as context, `end`, or from research on to a `proposal`. | phase change (that is the event), mode switch |
| **Asked to research** | A job whose agent is told to research and write a research report instead of doing the work (`spec.research`): its item is labelled `hopper:research` or has a Research heading in its body, or a person asked it before it started (Research). Asked for a proposal too, it researches first; accepted, it writes the proposal in the same session. A special job. | |
| **Special job** | A job asked for a review section's item — a research job or a proposal job —, inferred from the item's structure (its labels and headings) or asked by a person. | |
| **Browser profile** | The browser profile a person opens a **login**'s page in (issue #1249, `ui/src/model/browser-profile.ts`): one per machine and account, named `hopper-<machine>-<account>` (`hopper-<machine>` while no account is known there; `self` for a login on no machine), so one machine's sign-in cookies never sign another machine's tool in, nor as another account, and a sign-in again on the same machine and account opens the same one. The account is the one the login names, else the machine's **last account**. The Logins card shows the name; a page cannot pick the browser's profile, so the card gives a command that starts Chrome or Edge with the profile's own data dir (`--user-data-dir`), and a Firefox container link (`ext+container:`, the add-on Open external links in a container). Only in the browser: the hopper keeps nothing of it. | seat profile, sign-in profile |
| **Login kind** | How the user completes a login (`LOGIN_KINDS`, `src/logins/kinds.ts`): `device_code` (RFC 8628: open the URL, enter the code before it expires) first; `paste_code` (issue #1128, a **tool sign-in**: open the URL, sign in, paste the code the page shows back to the hopper, which gives it to the tool); a later kind is one more entry there. The issue's "login lane" is a login kind: named apart from a **lane**, which is a job slot. | login lane, auth type |
| **Login outcome** | What the requester of a login — the job that asked for it — says came of it (issue #1110): `completed` or `failed` with a reason. Only the requester knows whether a login worked, so its word ends the login with no person: `sh "$HOPPER_LOGIN" completed|failed` (`hopper-login`, `POST /job/login`, with the job's proxy token). `completed` completes a login that failed too; `failed` fails an open one, or reopens a completed or dismissed one. In code `LoginOutcome`, `Logins.outcome`. | login result, auth status |
| **Dismiss (a login)** | A person puts an open login away (issue #1110, `POST /ui/api/logins/:id/dismiss`): `dismissed`, whatever its job does, and it leaves the active list; the run that waited on it stops waiting. The fallback when the requester never gives its login outcome. Not *cancel*, which also tells a herdr job to go on without it. | clear, acknowledge |
| **Login signal** | What a run shows that ends the login it waits on (issue #567, `src/logins/signals.ts`): a token obtained, the CLI or Claude saying it is logged in (`completed`); the code expired (`expired`); the user denied it (`failed`). Read only after the login's report. A device-flow script polling (`authorization_pending`, `slow_down`, the same code again), Claude going on by itself, or the screen changing is none. | success signal, login detection |
| **Decider call** | A bounded choice with a known option set and a low blast radius, made at a **decision point**; Jev picks first (issue #550, `src/minor-decisions/`). Never one that deletes, sends, publishes, pays, changes permissions or runs on a gated machine: that is **consequential**, and never decided by Jev. Settings → Decider holds their configuration (issue #661); Decisions holds the picks, with Override (issue #897). | small decision, micro decision |
| **Access** | The decision before every credential the vault (issue #558) mints or renews (issue #559, `src/authz/`): OpenFGA asked, at request time, whether the **requester** reaches a **template** approved for that **operation** on that **asset**. Fails closed. Users & access → Access. | authorization, authz, permissions |
| **Requester** | Who asks in an Access check (issue #581): a job, a machine (a box) or a user, each of one user. A user owns their live jobs, a job runs on its machine, a box is an instance of the template its join line named: tuples the hopper writes from who is live and removes when a job ends or a box leaves. Each is a row of the permission matrix. The issue's *identity*: **identity** is who signed in. | identity, principal, subject (the subject is a tuple's first part) |
| **Mint decision** | Access's answer to one request (`decideMint`, `MintDecision`): allowed or denied, why, and on an allow the **relationship path**; every one recorded, a check tried in Users & access → Access as a **trial**. | authz check, permission check |
| **Template** | A kind of machine a job runs on, by name (issue #559): what an approval names — a job gets what its template is approved for. For the vault (issue #558, design.md "Templates"): an image and its scope, the **vault secrets** its boxes may ask for, saved on Vault and approved once by a person, and again when its scope widens or its image changes; until then its boxes are given only what was approved. The approval gates job placement too (issue #602): a box of a template not approved — never, revoked, or its image changed — takes no new job and shows on Machines as *waiting for template approval*; a person approves it and placement starts, revokes it and new placement stops at once. A sandbox box joins as an instance of one when its Add machine line names it. A template is removed only when no box of it is attached (issue #604): the removal is refused and names the boxes. The scope is the template's, never a job's. | box template, kill box template, machine type |
| **Agent credential** | One of a **template**'s vault secrets that its boxes' agent signs in with (issue #708, part 10, design.md "The agent credential"): a Claude token, what `claude setup-token` prints, good for a year and never renewed by a box. A launch whose scope holds it gives it to the box at its launch, in its environment as `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, so the box starts signed in; the **sandbox record** keeps its name, never its value. Given only as far as the template's secrets are approved; a value of another form (a `/login`'s credentials file, whose refresh token rotates and would sign other boxes out) refuses the launch. Not a *sign-in* (signing in to the hopper) and not a *login* (a device code a machine waits on). The sign-in in the box (`/login`, kept in its home volume) is the fallback. A **job pod** takes it too (issue #1287): the job's user's own vault secret the kubernetes executor names (`CLAUDE_TOKEN`), given to each pane in the pod as `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, never in the pod's spec; and so does each job on a **lane pod**, from the same `CLAUDE_TOKEN`, so a lane pod started again asks for no sign-in. The hopper makes that `CLAUDE_TOKEN` itself with the **Claude sign-in for pods**. | agent token, inherited login, shared sign-in |
| **Waiting for template approval** | What a sandbox box is while its **template** is not approved (issue #602): it joined and shows on Machines, but takes no new job — `template.waiting` on its machine snapshot says why. A job resuming in its pane there returns to it. Ends when a person approves the template; begins again when a person revokes it (`revoke-template`, `vault.revoked`). | pending box, held box, unapproved machine |
| **Operation** | What a credential lets a job do on its asset: `read`, `write`, `sync`, `apply` (issue #559), `observe` (issue #2146). Write, sync and apply **change the asset**: only an approved infra plan mints them. **Observe** reads a cluster or a namespace and port-forwards to its pods: a **bastion**'s credential, the service account or IAM role `hopper-observe`; it changes no asset, so it needs no plan. | verb, permission |
| **Asset** | What a credential is for: a cluster, a namespace, an Argo CD app, a Terraform workspace, an AWS account or an AWS role, by kind and name (issue #559). The issue's *target*: **target** is an attached machine. | resource, target |
| **Operation profile** | An operation on an asset (`read/cluster/x`, issue #559): what an approval lets a template's jobs do. A template declares the ones its boxes may ask for (issue #584). A **high-radius profile** is one of `write`, `sync`, `apply` or `observe`: it changes the asset, or (observe) port-forwards to its pods, and only its own explicit approval (`approve-profile`) approves it; approving the template approves its read profiles alone. | scope, policy |
| **Template rating** | A template's blast radius (issue #584, `TemplateRadius`, `src/blast-radius/template.ts`): rated from its operation profiles — declared or approved, approved or waiting — and its vault secrets. A read profile is `low`, a high-radius profile `high`; a vault secret counts as an unconfirmed reach, by the blast-radius rules (`medium` by default, `high` when its name or scope is prod). What the template's boxes *may* do, beside a machine's blast radius, what a box *could* do; a box is rated for the higher of the two (issue #605). Shown next to the template on Vault and Users & access → Access, and next to each box on Machines. | template risk, template radius |
| **Template request** | A running job's ask for the sandbox template its work needs (issue #716, `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" sandbox-template --needs …`, `src/skills/template-request.ts`), or an admin's or an API token's same ask (issue #718, `POST /ui/api/vault` `request-template`, `hopper template request`), or an admin's on Vault, **Template request**, which shows the template before it is made (issue #708, `preview-template`): the hopper writes the template from the job's **needs** — the box image with the **box tools** its needs use, no vault secret, one operation profile per need — named `asked-<hash>` from the needs alone, and approves it with no person only when it makes it now and every need is a read the **read policy** holds, or **skip template approval** is on (issue #991). Anything else waits for a person. A job never widens, approves or re-approves a template (design.md "Templates on request"). Issue #1875: a request may also name vault secrets (`--secrets`), **egress hosts** (`--egress`) and one of the hopper's own box images by its short name (`--image box|test`, never a reference of its own); with a secret or an egress host it may name no need, its name hashes them too, and only a person approves it. Issue #2146: it may also name **box tools** beside the ones its needs use (`--tools "jq promtool logcli"`); its name hashes them too, and a policy approves them as it approves the needs' tools. | recipe, template on demand |
| **Test image** | The hopper's box image for end-to-end tests (issue #1875, `TEST_IMAGE`, `ghcr.io/henningfutrell/hopper:box-test`, `scripts/box/test.Dockerfile`): the claude box image with Python 3, Playwright for Python with Chromium's headless shell, its system libraries and fonts, and `requests` and `pytest`, prebuilt so a box behind the egress proxy downloads nothing. Non-root (`agent`). A **template request** names it `--image test`. A box pulls the tag of the hopper's own branch: `box-test-dev` on dev (issue #1976). A request is refused while that tag is not published (issue #1977, `src/sandbox/image-published.ts`). | browser image, e2e image, playwright image |
| **Bastion** | A job's one sandbox that outlives a step (issue #2146, design.md "Bastions", `src/sandbox/bastions.ts`): a need box of a template with an **observe** on each of its **targets** (`<cluster>/<namespace>/<svc\|pod\|deploy>/<name>:<port>`), which holds `kubectl port-forward` to each on 127.0.0.1 inside it. Its kube tokens are files in it that the hopper renews, never the job's. Only the **relay** joins a job to them. It ends with its TTL (an hour with no use; never while a relay connection is open), its job, the job's stop (`sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" bastion stop`), a person's **Stop** on Access requests, or a refused renewal. | jump box, port-forward box |
| **Bastion read** | One HTTP read of a **target** of a job's **bastion**, run in the bastion's box (issue #2146 part 6, design.md "Bastions", `src/sandbox/bastion-read.ts`): `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" bastion read --targets T --path P [--secrets S] [--data JSON]`. The hopper starts curl in the box over a **relay** connection, with the Authorization header of a vault secret the bastion holds (a Grafana Viewer token) from its file there, and answers the target's body. A GET, or a POST to Grafana's `/api/ds/query` only. The token never leaves the box. | Grafana proxy, token proxy |
| **Bastion target** | A target a template names for its bastions (issue #2146 part 3, design.md "Bastions", `Template.bastionTargets`): `<cluster>/<namespace>/<svc\|pod\|deploy>/<name>:<port>`, on a namespace or cluster the template has an **observe** on. A person approves it with the template; a bastion forwards only to approved ones, and Access decides each **relay** connection to one (`bastion#can_relay`). | allowed service, forward target |
| **Relay** (bastion) | The hopper's way from a job to a **target** of its **bastion** (issue #2146 part 2, design.md "Bastions"): `GET /job/relay`, an HTTP/1.1 upgrade with the job's proxy token, becomes a byte pipe to a command the hopper starts in the bastion's box for that one connection, which connects to the target's forward on 127.0.0.1 there. A job uses it through `hopper-relay` (`sh "$HOPPER_RELAY" TARGET [LOCAL] [-- COMMAND]`), a port on 127.0.0.1 where the job runs. | tunnel, port-forward (that is the bastion's), proxy |
| **Need** | One operation profile a **template request** names, written `<operation>/<kind>/<asset>` (`read/cluster/lab`), or read from a line of plain words (`get pods on cluster lab`, issue #708, design.md "Needs in words"). | requirement, capability |
| **Read policy** | The assets a user lets the hopper approve read on with no person, for a template a job asks for (issue #716, `ReadPolicy`, `src/domain/read-policy.ts`): each an asset as Access names it, or one ending in `*` for every asset of that kind whose name starts so. Never write, sync or apply. Set by an admin on Vault or with `hopper template read-policy set`; empty by default. It approves first; the **auto-approve policy** then decides by blast radius (issue #1001). | standing read policy, auto-approve list, allowlist |
| **Run card** | One job's run that waits for a person (issue #2197, `src/domain/run-cards.ts`, `GET /api/vault` `runs`): the job, why it asks, its plan and undo, and the access it needs — its template's image, tools, profiles, vault secrets and egress hosts. One per job and template: a waiting **sandbox request**, an ask of the job that waits on a **template** for a person (`sandbox`, `sandbox-template`, a **need step**), or a waiting template a live job asked for (`askedBy`). Decided once on Access requests (Sandbox runs): **Approve** (`POST /ui/api/sandbox-requests` `approve-run`) approves the template as it is, each write, sync or apply profile it lists and the run, so the job's step goes on with no other approval; **Decline** (`decline-run`) declines the job's run of the template, asked or not yet, and the job is told no. A template a job asked for has no card of its own: it is the hopper's cache of the access. Each new card is one `vault.run_card_opened` on the job's timeline, which the notifiers send (part 2). Its approval is for its job's runs only (the template's `approval.jobs`), unless the person turns on **allow again** (part 3). A job's ask for an **action grant** on a role the template changes is on the card (`actions`, issue #2206 part 3): Approve grants it, and Decline declines it. | approval card, run approval |
| **Allow again** | The choice on a **run card** (issue #2197 part 3, `allowAgain` of `approve-run`, `hopper sandbox approve-run --allow-again`): on, the card's approval approves the template for every job's runs and remembers each write, sync or apply approval, so a later job with the same needs runs with no new card; off (the default), the template's approval names the card's job (`approval.jobs`, `approvalCovers`), and another job that asks for the same access gets a run card of its own. An approval with no `jobs` — allow again, a person's own template approval, a policy's, or one made before — covers every job. | reuse approval, standing approval |
| **Sandbox request** | A job's ask for **sandbox runs** of the template its needs make (issue #991, `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" sandbox --needs …`, `src/sandbox/requests.ts`), or of a template it names (issue #1471, `--template NAME`, and **sandboxes before the start**): one per job and template, kept in memory until the job ends. It waits for a person on Access requests (**Sandbox runs**) unless **skip sandbox approval** is on, the **auto-approve policy** holds its template's blast radius, **remembered write approvals** cover its changes, or a person approved the template the job asked for (issue #1001). Approved, it starts nothing (issue #1349): each of the job's **box steps** of that template then runs in a sandbox of its own, burned after the step. Its state: `waiting`, `approved`, `declined`. Events `vault.sandbox_requested`, `vault.sandbox_approved`, `vault.sandbox_declined`, on the job's timeline (`vault.sandbox_started` and `vault.sandbox_failed` are kept from before issue #1349). Not a **template request**, which asks for the template only. | sandbox ask, box request, launch request |
| **Event loop stall** | A time the hopper's event loop was held for more than 2 s (issue #1002, `src/http/event-loop.ts`): the UI, the API and the engine stop answering while it lasts. Logged with the routes of the requests in flight and the store calls in it (issue #1154, by statement: `SELECT jobs`); `/api/health` shows the last one for 10 minutes (`eventLoopStall`), and the top bar a **stalled** badge. Raised as `hopper.event_loop_stalled`, at most one a minute. Not a **Problem**. | freeze, hang, lag |
| **Health port** | The port (`HOPPER_HEALTH_PORT`; the image sets 4791) where the thread that reads a held event loop answers `GET /api/health` on `127.0.0.1` (issue #1383, `src/http/event-loop-held.ts`): the instance's version and uptime, 200 while the loop runs, 503 and `eventLoopHeldMs` while it is held. It answers while the daemon's port cannot. Loopback only, a loopback Host only, nothing else served. | health endpoint, sidecar port, metrics port |
| **Process reading** | `GET /api/process` (issue #1383): the hopper's own process now — its memory in MiB and its event loop over the last minute (timer delay `mean`/`p50`/`p99`/`max`, use 0..1). An instance read. Not the **stack resources**, which are the containers. | metrics, process stats, heap stats |
| **Read pool** | The async Postgres pool the HTTP read routes read through, off the event loop (issue #2392, `src/store/read-pool.ts`, design.md "Database": "Reads off the loop"): one per process and database server, at most 4 connections, read only, each user's schema set on a connection before its query. A read through it that waits on the database holds no timer and no other request. Its reads are a user store's `reads` (`src/domain/store-reads.ts`); every write stays on the store's own connection. | async store, replica, second database |
| **Skip template approval** | A user's toggle (issue #991, `SandboxApprovals`): a template the hopper makes now for a request, and the read policy does not approve, is approved at once with its write, sync and apply profiles, `by` **skip template approval**. Off by default; an admin's, on Vault, **Sandbox approvals**. | auto-approve templates |
| **Sandboxes before the start** | The hopper's ask for the sandbox runs a job will need, before the job starts (issue #1471, `src/domain/sandboxes-before-start.ts`, `sandboxesHeld` in `src/users/sandbox-requests.ts`): at each Decision, a waiting job's prompt that names a template of its user's — `box <name>`, `template <name>`, quoted or not — gets a **sandbox request** for each such template, all at once, and the job is held `awaiting its sandboxes` until each request is approved or declined and each approved template is ready. A job resuming after a question is never held for them. A running job names a template itself with `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" sandbox --template NAME`. | pre-flight check, sandbox preflight |
| **Skip sandbox approval** | A user's toggle (issue #991): a **sandbox request** is approved the moment a job asks, a high-blast-radius one too, `by` **skip sandbox approval**. Off by default; an admin's. | auto-start, auto-approve boxes |
| **Remembered write approval** | A person's approval of a write, sync or apply on an asset, remembered for that asset and access (issue #1001, `RememberedWrite`, `src/domain/remembered-writes.ts`; the user's settings, key `rememberedWrites`): a later template the hopper makes for a job, or a **sandbox request**, whose every change is remembered — no vault secret, each read approved with no person — is approved `by` **a remembered write approval**. It ends after the admin's time limit (`rememberWritesHours`, default 168; 0: never) or when an admin forgets it (`vault.write_forgotten`). An approval no person gave is never remembered. | standing approval, sticky approval, write grant |
| **Action grant** | A person's grant of one AWS action on one resource for an aws-role (issue #2206, `ActionGrant`, `src/domain/action-grants.ts`; the user's settings, key `actionGrants`): a **need step**'s change on that role, which names the action (`--action <service:Action>=<resource ARN>`), is minted narrowed to `ReadOnlyAccess` and the granted actions on their resources, with no infra plan: the grant approves the change (issue #2370). It covers a later ask of the same role, action and resource until the time limit of **remembered write approvals**, or until a person revokes it (`vault.action_revoked`). Granted on Access requests, AWS actions (`grant-action`, `vault.action_granted`), by the job's **run card**'s Approve when the ask is on it, or by `hopper sandbox grant-action`; a person's act only, never an API token's. A session policy only narrows, so the role must allow the action: that is the **action role**'s job. | permission grant, IAM grant, policy grant |
| **Action ask** | A job's need step's ask for an AWS action no **action grant** covers (issue #2206, `vault.action_asked`, `ActionAskView`, `GET /api/vault` `actionAsks`): its job, the role, the action, the resource and why. Read from the event log while the job is at work, so it outlives a restart. The step waits until a person grants it, or declines it (`vault.action_declined`, the step told no). With **infra applies** off, none is made. | permission request, grant request |
| **Action role** | The IAM role `hopper-action` (`ACTION_ROLE`, issue #2206) an operator makes in an AWS account for **action grants**: wide in IAM, and minted by the hopper only narrowed to the actions a person granted. A change on it with no granted action is refused at every mint. | grant role, break-glass role, admin role |
| **Action check** | The check of the AWS actions a template request's change names (issue #2206 part 2, `sandbox-template --actions`, `checkTemplateActions`, `src/domain/action-check.ts`), before the template is made: each is ready, waits for an **action grant** (asked now), cannot (the role does not allow it, by IAM's policy simulation: the request is refused, with the need to ask for in its place; or the role does not exist in its account, by IAM's `GetRole`: refused too, issue #2387), or not checked. | permission check, IAM check, preflight |
| **Purpose** | Why a job asks for a sandbox, in its own words (issue #1001, `--why`, required): kept on the **sandbox request** and on a template the ask made, shown first on their cards with the job and its **plan**. A template's readable name (`label`) comes from it. | reason, justification |
| **Plan** | What a sandbox's box will do, in the job's words (issue #1001, `--plan`, required when a need changes an asset): the commands, and for each change what it changes and whether it can be undone. Kept and shown as the **purpose** is. Not an **infra plan** (issue #870). | intent, steps |
| **Auto-approve policy** | A user's setting (issue #1001, `SandboxApprovals.autoApprove`, `src/domain/sandbox-approvals.ts`): for each blast radius level, whether a template the hopper makes for a job, and a **sandbox request** for a template, of that level is approved with no person, `by` **the auto-approve policy**. Default: low on (read only, no vault secret), medium and high off. Set by an admin on Vault. Not the **read policy**, which names assets, not levels, and not **auto-approve** of infra plans. | radius policy, auto-approval rules |
| **Direct push** | A person's setting, with **yolo mode** (issue #1806, `push` in the user's `yoloMode`): the repositories where a job may push straight to the **target branch** — the repository's landing branch, else its default branch — with no pull request, within the user's own GitHub write access. Off unless a person names the repository; any repository, a job repository or not. Only a fast-forward, as the user's mirror reads it; a push the hopper cannot check is refused. Every other kept branch, a tag and a delete stay refused. Each such push is a `github_proxy.done` with `direct`. `src/domain/yolo-mode.ts`, `src/github-proxy/git-broker.ts`. | force push, push to main, yolo push |
| **Sandbox YOLO** | Both sandbox approval toggles on (issue #991, `sandboxYolo`): a job gets its sandbox with no person. Not **yolo mode**, which merges pull requests. | YOLO, sandbox autopilot |
| **Sandbox strictness** | Brake 2 of issue #655: which **sandbox runs** gate 2 may approve with no person — `relaxed` (the toggles and the auto-approve policy decide), `standard` (the default; the same, but **Jev's sandbox check** first for a medium or high blast radius template, or one with no rating), `strict` (a person for each run of a medium or high blast radius template, or one with no rating), `locked` (a person for each run) —, set by default, per blast radius level and per template; the template wins over its radius level, that over the default (`sandboxBrakeOf`). Where it asks a person, a sandbox request waits, a box step counts only a person's approval, a need step is refused and the standing read grant grants nothing. An admin's, in Vault (`/api/sandbox-strictness`, `sandbox.strictness_changed`); read at each ask, so a change applies at once. `src/domain/sandbox-strictness.ts`. | sandbox policy, sandbox brake |
| **Held by trust** | Intake's list of the waiting jobs a **trust stamp** holds (issue #655, `ui/src/views/intake-trust.tsx`, `heldByTrust` in `ui/src/model/trust.ts`): no stamp, one that does not verify, a **quarantine**, a level below the dispatch minimum or the **intake strictness**, or Jev's trust check due. Each with its hold reason, its trust, origin acts and links, Approve trust or Lift quarantine, and Cancel (issue #1974); the jobs that wait on a person come first, and Intake's nav badge and Needs you count them. | trust queue, blocked jobs |
| **Hold timeout** | Issue #655 part 9: a **sandbox request** that waits 24 hours with no person's answer (`SANDBOX_HOLD_MS`) is declined by the hopper, `by` the hold timeout (`HOLD_TIMEOUT`, the request's `declinedBy`), with the reason `no person answered it within 24 hours`: `vault.sandbox_declined`, which wakes a job that waits on it. A deny of this request only: no person said no, so the job's **trust stamp** stays. Looked for at each ask and list, and once a minute (`holdSweep`). `src/sandbox/requests.ts`. | request expiry, auto-decline |
| **Read-only check** | The check a template a job asked for with read needs only passes before its first box (issue #716, `ReadOnlyCheck`, `src/sandbox/check.ts`): the hopper runs the probes for its needs in a check box of the template, and every write must be refused, every read must work. Observe needs count as reads here (issue #2146, part 5): each is minted for the check box, and its probes also refuse `pods/exec` and a Secret read. Until the check of its current version passed, the template takes no box and its boxes take no job. A person runs it again on Vault or with `hopper template check` (design.md "The read-only check"). A passed check runs again once a day (issue #715); the template stays open while it runs, and a failure blocks it. A `terraform-workspace` need runs its probes with the read session of the template's one `aws-role` read need (issue #715, `workspaceRole`). Each check that ends keeps a **check report**. A check that failed only on a network error (a connection reset, a timeout) is **transient** and runs again after 5 minutes with no person (issue #1468). A failed check keeps the read needs the vault minted nothing for (issue #1779, `unminted`): setting the minting credential for their account or cluster runs it again, and a job that asks for the template is refused with why, not left waiting. | negative suite, smoke test, verification, read-only test |
| **Cloud acceptance run** | A run of `scripts/cloud-acceptance.ts` by a tester in their own environment, with their own minting credentials (issue #866, `docs/cloud-acceptance.md`): through the operator CLI it mint-tests each asset, asks for a template in plain words and waits for its **read-only check**, then writes a masked **cloud acceptance report**. | smoke test, e2e test, live test |
| **Cloud acceptance report** | What a cloud acceptance run writes (`report.json`, `report.md`): each step and each tool (`aws`, `kubectl`, `helm`, `terraform`) `passed`, `failed` or `skipped`, the tester's assets masked. Filed with the issue form `cloud-acceptance`; `collect` sums the reports up. | test results, run log |
| **Check report** | The page a **read-only check** that ended keeps (issue #715, `checkReport`, `src/sandbox/check-report.ts`): each probe, what it expected and what it did, on an HTML artifact of the job that asked for the template (`Template.askedBy`). One artifact per template; each later check is a new revision of it. A template a person asked for has no job and keeps none. Not a **report** to a source, and not a **bug report**. | check result page, suite artifact |
| **Read-only wrapper** | The `terraform` a read-only template's box runs first on PATH (issue #715, `TERRAFORM_WRAPPER`, `src/sandbox/terraform-wrapper.ts`): it runs only the commands that read (init, plan, show, validate, output, providers and providers schema, version, state list and show, workspace list and show) and refuses every other one with `refused by the read-only wrapper`. Each command runs with the image's CLI config, so terraform installs providers only from the **provider mirror**. One layer: the real binary stays in `$HOPPER_TOOLS`, and the **deny policy** refuses its writes (design.md "A read-only infra box"). | terraform guard, shim |
| **Apply wrapper** | The `terraform` a write box runs first on PATH (issue #870, `TERRAFORM_APPLY_WRAPPER`, `src/sandbox/terraform-wrapper.ts`): every tools image with terraform that is not read-only. `apply` runs only the saved plan file of the **infra plan** `HOPPER_PLAN` names, fetched from the hopper with the job's token; a plan file the caller names must be that file by its SHA-256, and a flag that changes what the plan does is refused. Each other command that writes infrastructure or state with no plan (destroy, import, refresh, taint, state rm and mv, …) is refused with `refused by the apply wrapper`. One layer: the **apply gate** is the boundary (design.md "Infra plans: the apply wrapper on write boxes"). | plan gate, apply guard, shim |
| **Provider mirror** | The terraform providers a read-only template's image holds, in `/opt/hopper-tools/providers` (issue #715, `PROVIDER_MIRROR`, `src/sandbox/terraform-wrapper.ts`): a filesystem mirror with the AWS provider and HashiCorp's utility providers (null, random, local, time, archive, tls; issue #1977, `MIRRORED_PROVIDERS`, `src/sandbox/tools.ts`), each pinned and checked by its SHA-256. The image's CLI config (`TF_CLI_CONFIG_FILE`) names it as the only place to install from, so a box downloads no provider and `init` of one it does not hold fails (design.md "A read-only infra box", part 3). | plugin cache, provider cache |
| **Deny policy** | The inline session policy every read session the vault mints from STS carries beside `ReadOnlyAccess` (issue #715, `awsReadDenyPolicy`, `src/domain/minting.ts`): explicit denies only, of the `Create`, `Delete`, `Put`, `Update` and `Modify` actions of twelve named services (IAM takes no wildcard in a service prefix, issue #853), IAM's other writes, and assuming any role but its own. An explicit deny wins over any allow, so the role's own policy cannot widen a read session. | deny list, write block |
| **Probe** | One write or read the read-only check tries on one asset (issue #716, `Probe`, `src/sandbox/probes.ts`): a shell snippet, the asset kinds it applies to, and whether it must be refused (`denied`) or work (`allowed`). Its outcome is `allowed`, `denied` or `error`, `skipped` when a **probe mark** says so, or `not-run` when the check lacks what it needs (issue #715: a workspace with no **state backend**): unproven, never a pass. The hopper's probes are a catalog that #715 adds to or replaces. | test, case, check |
| **Probe mark** | A person's mark on one read **probe** of a template (issue #916, `Template.probeMarks`, `ProbeMark`): `expect-denied`, the read must be refused, as a right credential refuses it (`helm.list` on a view role); or `skip`, the check does not run it (outcome `skipped`, a pass). A write probe takes none. Set only by a person's save of the template (design.md "Probe marks"). | probe override, waiver, exemption, allow-list |
| **Right refusal** | The words of a refusal a right read credential gives for one read **probe** (issue #1736, `Probe.rightRefusal`): an `allowed` probe with no **probe mark** that is refused in those words passes, and its result carries its note. `helm.list` on a view role, which cannot list the secrets helm keeps its releases in. A failed check that only such a refusal failed runs again by itself (`judgedAgain`). | expected denial, informational probe, waiver |
| **State backend** | Where a terraform workspace keeps its state: an S3 bucket, its region, and the key prefix of its states (issue #715, `StateBackend`, `src/domain/state-backend.ts`), set by a person on the template's `terraform-workspace` profile (`backend`). The read-only check pushes a state of its own past the wrapper to `<key prefix>/hopper-read-only-check/terraform.tfstate` there, never to the workspace's state, and the account must refuse it. No approval covers it. Not a **vault backend**. | backend config, state bucket |
| **Check box** | The one-shot container the read-only check runs in (issue #716): the template's image with its tools, under the sandbox flags, its probe script in place of the entrypoint, labelled `io.hopper.check`; removed when the check ends. Not a sandbox box: it joins no hopper and takes no job. It gets the **minted credential** of each read need in its environment (issue #797), minted after Access allowed the template it. | probe container, test box |
| **Egress proxy** | The one way out of a read-only box beside the hopper (issue #849, `src/sandbox/egress.ts`, the compose service `egress`): tinyproxy on the internal network `hopper_egress`, which a need box of a read-only template and the **check box** join. It lets a box reach only the **egress allowlist**. Not the hopper's **GitHub proxy**, and not a reverse proxy in front of the hopper. | egress gateway, firewall, forward proxy |
| **Egress allowlist** | The hosts the **egress proxy** lets a read-only box reach (issue #849): AWS's endpoints (`*.amazonaws.com`), the API server of each cluster the vault minted for a read-only box, and the **egress hosts** of the templates whose boxes started (issue #1875). The hopper writes it before each such box starts, and the box waits until the proxy loaded it. It only grows while the hopper runs. | allow list (two words), whitelist |
| **Egress host** | One `host:port` a **template** names (issue #1875, `Template.egress`): a host its boxes may reach through the **egress proxy**, beside AWS's endpoints and the minted clusters. A DNS name or an address, no scheme, no wildcard. Approved with the template's image: a host added waits for a person, and the template gives nothing meanwhile. Counted in its blast radius (medium). A box of a template that names any runs behind the egress proxy, a write box too. | egress rule, allowed domain, firewall rule |
| **Box tools** | The tools the hopper adds to a box's image for a run (issue #716, `BOX_TOOLS`, `src/sandbox/tools.ts`): `aws`, `helm`, `jq`, `kubectl`, `logcli`, `promtool`, `terraform` (the observability tools `jq`, `logcli` and `promtool` since issue #2146), each pinned and checked by its SHA-256. An image with `kubectl` also has the **port-forward helper**. A template names its tools, approved with its image; a launch gets them or names fewer. The hopper builds the image `localhost/hopper-box-tools:<hash>` once per base image and set of tools (design.md "Tools assembled per run"). | recipe, toolbox, tools image, tool set |
| **Port-forward helper** | `hopper-port-forward` (issue #2146, `src/sandbox/port-forward-helper.ts`), on `PATH` in every box image with `kubectl`: runs one command while `kubectl port-forward` holds a port on `127.0.0.1`, waits for the port first, and stops the forward when the command ends (`hopper-port-forward [-n NS] svc/NAME LOCAL[:REMOTE] -- COMMAND`). No boundary: what the forward reaches is the cluster's RBAC for the box's credential. The **observability** skill says how a job uses it for PromQL, LogQL and Grafana queries. | tunnel, proxy, port mapping |
| **Warm pass** | The pass that builds the image with a template's **box tools** ahead of its first need step (issue #1737, `createWarmPass`, `src/sandbox/warm.ts`): with the check pass, for each template with tools whose image is approved, where the user's steps run; a template a **read-only check** is due for is left to the check. Each image is looked at again an hour after; a build that fails is tried again 5 minutes after (design.md "Tools assembled per run"). | prebuild, preheat, prefetch |
| **Approval** (access) | A template approved for an operation profile (the `approved_for` tuple): who and when; **revoked** from Users & access → Access, after which the next request is denied. Written by the vault's gate (issue #558). | grant (a *grant* is GitHub's, issue #514), permission |
| **Access model** | The OpenFGA authorization model, in its DSL, kept in the hopper's database and edited in Users & access → Access; an edit keeps every relation the hopper writes or asks (issue #559). | policy, schema |
| **Relationship path** | Why an allow was given: the job runs from its template → the template is approved for the operation profile → the profile grants the operation on the asset (issue #559). | trace |
| **Permission matrix** | Users & access → Permission matrix (issue #559): who may do what on which asset. Rows are the templates, each followed by its boxes, and the users, or, switched, the live jobs — the rows besides the templates are the **requesters**; columns the assets, grouped by kind; a cell the approved operations, and apart those of the row's template that wait for approval. A click on a cell shows the relationship path, who approved it and when, and revokes. | access table, ACL view, identity (an *identity* is a sign-in's) |
| **Decision point** | Where the hopper makes a decider call, with the options it declares: `question-risk` (the **risk call** on a level's answer, issue #679; `question-answer`, a question's listed options, was removed then and stays only in old events), `failure-assessment` (a failure no known cause explains: `retry` or `person`), `lane-count` (a machine's lanes, from the lanes the rules allow, issue #688), `job-machine` (a waiting job's machine: a **placement pick**), `intake-order` (the new item of a sync that enters the queue first, issue #796), `merge-order` (the next pull request of a **tending queue**, issue #796), `intake-trust` (**Jev's trust check** of a job's **trust stamp**, issue #655), `intake-kind` (**Jev's kind check** of a work job before it starts, issue #655), `sandbox-request` (**Jev's sandbox check** of a sandbox request, issue #655 part 13). Its **mode**: `off`, `shadow` (Jev's pick only recorded), `active` (a pick at or above its `threshold` applied). | decision tier, gate |
| **Jev pick** | Jev's choice of one option of a decider call, with a confidence (`minor_decision.picked`); **applied**, or not and why. **Jev first** (`JevFirst`) asks for it before anything else at a decision point. | verdict, advice (advice is the router's) |
| **Agreement rate** | Of a decision point's picks compared with what was decided after them (`minor_decision.compared`) or overridden by a person (`minor_decision.overridden`), the share where Jev's pick was what was decided. | accuracy |
| **Failure assessor** | What judges every failed job (issue #509, `src/failures/`), rules first: its error normalised to a **signature**, matched to a **known cause**, and decided — **retry**, **hold** or **redirect** into a **problem**, **continue** (a timed-out job still at work, issue #630; a job whose own pull request's checks are red, issue #1230), or **needs a person**.
