One **failure record** per failed job; the job carries its **assessment**. Never a model, never a GitHub issue (both open decisions, taken conservatively). | failure classifier, triage bot |
| **Signature** | A failure's error text with ids, paths, numbers, times and URLs stripped, case and spaces folded (`normalise`), and hashed: 12 hex digits. One cause on many jobs is one signature. | fingerprint, error hash |
| **Known cause** | A cause the assessor recognises: built in, matched by its text (`BUILTIN_CAUSES`: done check unread — first, issue #2235 —, socket path too long, login expired, disk full, machine offline — hopper's own `is not dialled in` among them; daemon restart, link closed, network blip, start race, timed out, own PR checks red, no way to the hopper (issue #2233: a session with no `HOPPER_URL` or `HOPPER_GH` runs again as a new job; issue #2316: a checkpoint restore with no `HOPPER_URL` or no readable token file too, once its no-way moves are spent, issue #2355); question unanswered, invalid job, not complete), or **named** by a person for a signature, with its default decision — a named cause wins. Its class: `transient`, `shared` or `job`. | error class, rule |
| **Own PR checks red** | The built-in **known cause** of a job that ended done while the checks of its own pull request were red (issue #1230, `own-pr-checks-red`, `src/failures/checks-red.ts`): its miss names the pull request and its failed checks (`PR #N: checks failed: <names>`). Class `transient`, decision **continue**: the hopper runs the failed checks again once for each head commit (`job.checks_rerun`), and red again the job goes on in its own session told the **checks-red brief** — the failed checks and their logs — to fix them on the same branch. Never grouped. Only once its chain was sent back `maxAttempts` times: a person. No `hopper:failed` while it is pending. | red CI, failing build, CI flake |
| **Done check unread** | The built-in **known cause** of a job that ended done whose source the hopper could not read to check its **Completion** (issue #2235, `done-check-unread`): a connect timeout, a busy service, a rate limit, also after two reads again with backoff (`HOPPER_DONE_READ_RETRY_MS`). Its text: `could not confirm the work is complete: <the read error>`. Transient, decision `retry`, matched first: the read is the hopper's own. Nothing showed the work incomplete, so it is never **Not complete**. | done-check timeout, unreadable completion |
| **Own PR unfinished** | The built-in **known cause** of a job that ended done while its own pull request had merge conflicts with its base, or was a draft (issue #1265, `own-pr-unfinished`, `src/failures/unfinished.ts`): its miss names the pull request and its step (`PR #N: merge conflicts with its base`, `PR #N: a draft`). Class `transient`, decision **continue**: the job goes on in its own session (else a new job of its item) told the **unfinished brief** — rebase and resolve the conflicts, or mark it ready, and make the checks pass. Never grouped. Only once its chain was sent back `maxAttempts` times: a person. No `hopper:failed` while it is pending. | conflicting PR, merge conflicts hand-off |
| **Checks-red brief** | The fixed text a job is told when the hopper sends it back for its own pull request's red checks (issue #1230, `checksRedBrief`): work on that pull request's branch, the failed checks with their links, what they reported and their logs' excerpts, fix the cause, open no new pull request, end done; and that the hopper ran the checks again already, or, when it could not, that the job may once. No model call. | fix brief |
| **Problem** | A shared cause, shown once with the failed jobs it hit (`jobIds`), not as N failures: one per signature while open, scoped to a machine, an executor on it, or every machine. While open, a new job it covers is held (`held by problem: <title>`) or placed on another machine; its failed jobs are **held** (wait on its release) or **redirected** (run again at once, elsewhere). **General** (Recurring) when no known cause matched but the signature failed enough items within the grouping window; a general problem holds only its own jobs, never new jobs of its scope, and a job-specific cause never joins any problem (issue #637). Resolved by a person or its check (the machine back, the disk free again): its held jobs run again through the normal queue. | incident, outage, group |
| **Hopper bug problem** | The **problem** of a **bug report** a job filed on the hopper about itself while it ran (issue #1147, `src/failures/hopper-bug.ts`, cause `hopper-bug`): a failure of that job the rules hand to a person is held on it instead — the hopper failed it, not its work — so it is not handed off and is not in Needs a person. One per report's issue (`Hopper bug #N`); it holds only its own jobs. Its check resolves it once the **fix landed**: the issue closed as completed, and this hopper's build made after that close (the close alone when the build's time is not known); its jobs then run again. Closed any other way, it stays open for a person. | bug hold, wait on fix |
| **Hand-off** | A failed job automatic handling has ended for, handed to a person (issue #516): its retries used up, a job-specific failure, its decision's automatic action off, a run again the assessor decided refused, or its locked entry dismissed with nothing else to end it. Shown in Failures under **Needs a person** with its own count, and counted in the Failures badge, while **open**; never dropped by age, a restart or the recent failures' window. It **closes** when its item runs again (`run_again`), a person **resolves** it (issue #551: a **resolution**), or its job ends finished; `cleared` is a close by the build before #551. A closed one is deleted after `handoffRetentionDays`. Counted with the failed jobs not assessed yet at the top of Failures and in `GET /api/failures` (`counts`, issue #517): both zero, nothing is left. A failure whose item ran again is **superseded** and hands nothing off; its open hand-off closes (`run_again`). Stale ones close by themselves, at start and on every sweep (issue #529): a newer job of its item (`superseded`), its job gone (`job_gone`), its job live again (`resumed`, issue #1777: a Continue, Push it or a resume took it back to the queue; no hand-off opens for a live job, and its card offers no resolution), or its item closed at its source (`item_closed`). | dead letter, dead-letter queue, escalation |
| **Resolution** (of a hand-off) | What a person did about an open **hand-off** (issue #551), with a note, from its card in Needs a person (`POST /ui/api/failures/handoffs/:id/resolve`): **Continue** — the job goes on with the note: when its own **agent session** can resume (`continueResumes`: its executor parks and it recorded one), the same job is queued again pinned to its machine and resumes that session in its work tree, told the failure and the note (**continued**, `job.continued`, end `continued`); else a new job of its item, told the earlier error, the assessment and the note (end `run_again`). **Run again** (`fixed`; *I fixed it* before issue #621) — the cause is fixed outside the job; a new job of its item runs, told the note. **Done** (`done_by_hand`; *Done by hand* before issue #621) — the work is done, by the job's pull request or by the person; its job ends finished, with a link. **Won't do** (`wont_do`) — not to be done, or no real failure; the note says why; its locked entry is dismissed. Kept on the hand-off with who (the signed-in person), when, the note and the link, and told to its job's item at its source (**write-back**: on GitHub one comment naming no person, and the end label `hopper:done` or `hopper:rejected`, never left `hopper:failed`; tried again at each sync until the source takes it). Its failure record turns `retried` or `resolved` (settled). A refused resolution is shown with why, never offered. | clear, acknowledge, dismiss (that is the locked entry's) |
| **Work state** (of a hand-off) | What a failed job's work shows at its source (issue #621, `JobSource.workState`): its item — `open`, `done` (closed as completed), `closed` (any other way), `gone` — and the pull requests the job opened or pushed to (an **updated pull request**), each with its state. Asked by the failure sweep's **work check** for each open **hand-off**, at once and then every 10 minutes, and kept on it (`work`). The work **shipped** when a pull request of the job's merged or its item closed as completed: the hopper closes the hand-off (`finished`) and the job ends finished. | outcome, real state, landed |
| **Card** (of a hand-off) | What a hand-off says first in Needs a person (issue #621, `HandoffView.card`): **what happened**, short plain sentences from its **work state**, never the raw error; the **recommended** resolution, shown as the main button, and why, in a few words — always one the hopper takes now (issue #1010: refused for good, it is the other run again, else Done, with why and what a person does); for Done, the link to the work. The assessment and the error are behind Details. | next step, headline, TL;DR |
| **Acting person** | Who did a person's action on Failures, and the way (issue #623, `ActingPerson`): `person`, the verified email of the session's user, else the name of the identity the session signed in (issue #1380; `operator CLI` for the CLI), and `via`, `ui` or `cli` (the operator CLI's session, realm `cli`). Kept in the action's event: `failure.resolved`, `failure.released`, `handoff.closed`, `job.rerun`. | actor (an *actor machine* is a machine), operator |
| **Acting identity** | The GitHub account that acts for a job (issue #1380): its own user's **connected account** — the one whose **job repositories** hold the repository it acts on, else the one of its own repository (issue #1448) —, for its **GitHub proxy** requests and its git. Each `github_proxy.*` event names its login (`as`), and so does each answer the proxy gives the job. The job page shows it as **GitHub as**: the login the job's newest request named; before the job acted, the connected account that will act; with none, how to connect one. Never another user's connection. | bot account, service account, hopper's identity |
| **Continued** (a job) | A failed job a person resolved with **Continue** whose agent session can resume (issue #551), or a timed-out job still at work the assessor continued (issue #630): queued again (`job.continued`, `handoffId` or `recordId`), its end to be reported again, its brief pending — the failure and the note, or that it timed out while at work; its claim reopens its session in its work tree as a parked job's re-queue does. The same job, not a new one: `nextJobId` on its hand-off or record is its own id. | resumed, restarted, run again (a new job) |
| **Liveness** | What a timed-out job showed of its progress at its timeout (issue #630, `JobLiveness`): when its pane output last changed (`outputAt`), whether it pushed commits (`pushed`, from the reap), whether a pull request of its own is open (`pullRequest`, from its source). A fact absent is not known. **Active**: output within `activeWindowMin` minutes before the timeout, commits pushed, or a pull request open — it continues, at most twice in a row. **Silent**: none of them — it runs again once, then a person. | heartbeat, progress (*progress* is the job's progress line) |
| **Repeated failure** | A failure whose signature is the same as the failure of its item's run before it (issue #1461, `repeatedOf` in `src/failures/chain.ts`): the same job's earlier record when it was continued, else the record of the job it ran again. A run again did not help, so the assessor stops the item: a person, raised once as one **hand-off**, and nothing runs it again by itself — no retry, no Continue, no Jev pick. Not a daemon restart, a timeout or a `shared` cause, which say nothing of the item's work. | loop, flapping failure, crash loop |
| **Poison failure** | A failed job whose own data makes its assessment throw (issue #1961, `src/failures/poison.ts`). The **failure assessor** hands it to a person with the reason (`the hopper could not assess this failure: …`), once, and goes on to the other failures. | poison message, poison pill |
| **Failure record** | What the assessor keeps of one failed job (`failures` table): signature, class, decision, reasons, summary, evidence (error, output tail, machine, executor, model, repo, source, attempt, how long it ran, the same signature on other items), its problem, and what became of the decision (`outcome`). A run again it decided is **pending** on it until made, so it outlives a restart. **Superseded** (issue #517): a newer job of its item exists — a person's Run again or anything else — so nothing is left to do for it; it neither waits on a person nor holds anything. **Item closed** (issue #529): its item is closed at its source, so nothing waits on it either. Both are **settled**: out of Recent failures, still counted in the profile. A record **ended** (issue #618) when its job ran again, it was settled or resolved, its hand-off closed, or its job ended finished: it waits on nobody, so it is out of Recent failures and in **Ended** (Failures, the last day) — history, never counted. | failure log entry |
| **Assessment** | The failure assessor's judgement as a failed job carries it (`Job.assessment`): decision, class, summary, reasons, problem. Shown where the job's error shows. | diagnosis, verdict |
| **Failure profile** | Counts and a daily trend per signature over 14 days, and breakdowns by machine, repo and executor; a signature on at least the grouping threshold of jobs is flagged recurring. In the **Failures** view. | failure stats |
| **Task count** | A running job's progress (issue #794, `Job.tasks`, `TaskCount`): how many tasks of its own task list are done, out of how many, as `x/y`. The job keeps a task list (the job rules ask it) and writes a line `HOPPER_TASKS <done>/<total>` when it makes the list and each time a task completes; the executor reads the last one. The card's bar steps with it; before the first count the bar is indeterminate. Never a clock. A task here is a step the job set itself, never a job. | percent, ETA, progress fraction |
| **Status note** | A turn of a job that ends without a marker (issue #163): progress, not a question. It opens no question and fires no question webhook; its text is the job's progress, and the executor sends the job a **nudge**. Only `HOPPER_QUESTION` or Claude's own dialog opens a question. | idle question, update |
| **Nudge** | What the hopper types into a job after a status note (`STATUS_NOTE_NUDGE`): go on, ask with the marker if an answer is needed, end with a marker. herdr-claude waits `idleNudgeMs`, then 1, 5, 15 and 30 minutes for further status notes in a row, then sends none until Claude works again by itself, and none while the job waits on **background work** (issue #491); cursor-agent nudges at most three times in a row. Before each nudge herdr-claude asks the **check before a nudge** (issue #627). | reminder, ping |
| **Check before a nudge** | What herdr-claude asks before it nudges a job (issue #627, `ExecutionContext.beforeNudge`): the job's source first — its pull request ready for review, or its issue closed → the job ends done, not nudged —; then whether a person is awaited — an open watch on its job stream, or a credential request that names it → no nudge —; else the nudge. A check that cannot tell → the nudge. | source check, pre-nudge |
| **Background work** | Work a job's Claude started to run on without it — a background shell command, task, monitor or agent — named in the footer under its input box (`· 1 shell ·`). Claude Code wakes the job when it ends, so a turn that ends without a marker while it runs is not a status note and gets no nudge (issue #491). | background task, wait |
| **Lost send** | A send to a job's Claude (its prompt, an answer, a nudge) that never reached it (issue #278): Claude sat waiting with its state unmoved since the send for `idleNudgeMs`, the turn anchor nowhere on screen or the text unsent in its input box. herdr-claude submits the input box with Enter, else sends the same text again, at most three sends in all, then fails the job: a job never stays running on a waiting Claude. | dropped prompt, stuck job |
| **Escalation level** | One rung a question climbs (role `escalation-level`, `EscalationLevel` port; the plugins config's `escalationLevels`, lowest first). A level answers the question, or escalates it to the next level up; above the top level is the owner. A simple question is settled low; the harder it is, the higher it climbs. A level that fails, times out, replies malformed or cannot run escalates. 0..n instances; built-in plugins `claude-cli` (the claude CLI on its `machine`, a machine option) and `anthropic-api` (the Claude API with a runtime API key); the built-in level is `level-1` (model `fable`, issue #632). A level's name names the level, never a model (issue #209): the model it uses is only its `model` option; a level named after a model is refused. | answerer, assessor, tier, gate, a model name (`opus`, `fable`, `sonnet` are models) |
| **Auto-answer** | An escalation level's answer goes into the job with no person (issue #632): auto-answer is on, the level does not escalate, its **confidence** meets the threshold, and nothing holds it — a risk rule, the consequential guard, or a high-priority job with **hold high-priority questions** on (`holdHighPriority`, issue #679, default off). Below the threshold a lower level's question climbs; the top level's goes to a person. The user's settings `autoAnswer` (`enabled`, `threshold`, `holdHighPriority`; default on at `high`, high priority not held). `question.answered` carries `auto: true`. | auto-approve, auto-accept (those are the queue gate's) |
| **Confidence** | How sure a level says it is of its answer: `low`, `medium` or `high` (`LevelReply.confidence`, on the trail). An answer goes into the job only at or above the auto-answer threshold; a level that gives none meets no threshold. Jev's pick has its own numeric confidence. | certainty, score |
| **Correction** | A person's answer in place of a level's auto-answer (issue #632, `POST /ui/api/questions/:id/correct`): the question keeps what it replaced (`corrected`), and the job gets the correction ahead of its next answer (`pendingCorrection`). Counted in the **agreement stats** — the auto-answers over 30 days, the corrected ones, the share kept — that the threshold is tuned on. | override (Jev's picks are overridden), undo |
| **Level reply** | What one level returns: `{ answer?, escalate, reason }` (`LevelReply`). `escalate: false` with an `answer` answers: that answer is typed into the job unless a risk rule matches. `escalate: true` sends the question up; its `answer` is then a **recommendation** — the next level sees it on the trail, and the owner sends it as their answer in one click with **Use answer** (labelled with the level: Use Fable's answer). Fails closed: anything but a schema-valid `escalate: false` with an answer escalates. | draft, assessment, verdict |
| **Stage** | Where an open question is: the instance name of the escalation level holding it, or `human`. Stored as the question's `tier`; `question.escalated.target` names the stage entered. | tier, level (a *level* is the rung, the *stage* is where the question is) |
| **Owner** | The user a job, question or config belongs to: answers the questions the gates escalate, in the UI or in the pane, and closes them. The last stage of the question gates; code and stored rows call that stage `human` (`tier`, `answerByHuman`). A connected account's source acts as the user; the App acts as its bot. A stage, not an account: there is no user `owner` (issue #220); the first user of every hopper is the **default admin account**. | operator |
| **Default admin account** | The user `admin` (issue #220) of a hopper from before issue #238, with the work dir itself, an empty secret prefix and the herdr session `hopper`; instance migration 21 gave it everything the built-in user `owner` held. A new hopper has none (**no bootstrap login**); on a hopper from before it stays, with its work, and no sign-in signs in as it — until the first GitHub admin signs in as a user of their own: then the next start **folds** it into that user and it is gone (issue #265). |
| **Fold** (a user) | One user into another, at start (issue #265, `UserRepository.fold`, `src/store/fold-user.ts`): the **default admin account** into the first GitHub admin's user, the real account. That user keeps its id and name on the folded user's record — its place, work dir, secret prefix and user schema, so its work runs on untouched — and the two users' work, sign-ins, sessions and login codes are one. A row whose key both hold (a lane, a webhook's name, a setting, a config record) is the folded user's; the plugins config gains the instances only the real user named; the connected account is the real user's. | merge, absorb, transfer (that is the operator CLI's, which refuses a user with work) | owner (the user), root, superuser |
| **User** | One person the hopper works for, with everything of theirs kept apart from every other user (issue #158): jobs, questions, events, decisions, lanes, job sources, machines, executors, escalation levels, rules document, job rules, routing, usage sources, webhook subscriptions and credentials. A row of the instance schema's `users`: an id (`admin`, else a slug of the name), a unique name, a **user schema**, a **user work dir**, a **secret prefix**, and the verified **email** it is known by (issue #1380, unique, lowercase; absent until a sign-in gave one). Never visible to or touchable by another user. | tenant, account (an account is who a part acts as), customer |
| **Instance** | What every user of one hopper shares: the daemon process and its port, sign-in (the config record `sign-in`), the plugin store and its installs, self-update, the instance schema. | server, deployment, tenant |
| **Instance id** | This hopper's id (issue #603, `instanceId()` in `src/store/settings.ts`): 16 hex characters, made at its first ask and kept in the instance's settings, so a restart, a new container or an update on the same database keeps it. It labels the sandbox boxes the hopper launches; the **instance tag** is a hash of it. Settings → Version shows it to the instance admin (issue #465). | install id, hopper id |
| **Display name** (of the instance) | What the instance admin calls this hopper (issue #465): one line, 80 characters at most, empty when none; the instance's settings key `displayName`, set in Settings → Version → This hopper. A change is `display_name.changed`. Not the **hopper name**, a GitHub source's option. | instance name, install name |
| **Instance totals** | What the **instance admin** reads of the users' work (issues #221, #241): users, jobs not ended by status, open questions, lanes open and busy, jobs ended in the last 24 hours by how they ended, and usage readings summed per unit and usage window (**usage total**, `UsageTotal`) — summed over every user, never one user's share, nothing named (`GET /api/instance`, `InstanceTotals`). | stats, metrics, usage (that is a usage source's reading) |
| **Instance schema** | The Postgres schema the database URL names (`public`, or its `?schema=`): `users`, `user_identities`, UI sessions, login codes, the config record `sign-in`, the instance settings (update channel, auto-update, store installs), its own `schema_version`. | |
| **User schema** | The Postgres schema holding one user's tables: `u_<id>` (`<instance schema>_u_<id>` when the instance schema is not `public`): jobs, lanes, decisions, events, webhooks, deliveries, settings (the queue gate), questions, config records (`plugins`, `rules`, `job-rules`), and its own `schema_version` on the **tenant track** of migrations. | tenant schema |
| **User runtime** | One user's running parts (`src/users/runtime.ts`): user store, plugin host, executors, question service, engine, job source sync, webhook dispatcher, notifiers. One per user, started at boot and when a user is added. | worker, tenant process |
| **User work dir** | A user's scratch: `HOPPER_WORK_DIR` joined with the user's `work_dir` — the work dir itself for `admin`, `users/<id>` for a user added later. Holds the user's ssh sockets, plugin scratch, and the `gh` and `claude` config dirs of the CLIs their parts run (`GH_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`). | home |
| **Secret prefix** | What a user's secret names start with (issue #158): the user's runtime reads secret `NAME` as `<prefix>NAME` (and `<prefix>NAME_FILE`). Empty for `admin`; `HOPPER_USER_<ID>_` for a user added later, so one user can never name another's variable. | |
| **Identity link** | A row of `user_identities`: an identity (provider, subject) and the user it signs in as. An identity with none that a role rule grants a role signs in as the user known by its verified email (issue #1380, identity keyed by email), else gets a new user; either way it is linked. | |
| **Escalation** | A question going up: from one escalation level to the next, or past the top level to the owner. It reaches the owner when every level escalates, when there are no levels, or when a risk rule matches an answer. `question.escalated` announces each stage entered; `question.escalated_to_human` announces only the human stage, once. | |
| **Escalation reason** | Why a question came to a person (issue #679, `Question.escalation`): `guard`, `low_confidence`, `no_answer`, `frontier_escalated`, `high_priority`, `auto_answer_off` or `fork`; with a guard, the **guards** that held the answer back; every **hold**; and the trail's **recommendation**. The sentence at the top of the question card; the Questions list filters by it. | escalation cause, why-sent |
| **Hold** | One thing that kept a level's answer out of the job (issue #679, `AnswerHold`): `guard` (a risk, `by: risk` — the risk rules' names that matched, or the Decider's **risk call** as `decider` —, or the guards, `by: guard`, with the names that matched), `high_priority`, `auto_answer_off` or `low_confidence` (the confidence and the threshold). Every hold is recorded, strongest first, on the attempt (`holds`), the question's escalation and `question.escalated_to_human`. | block, stop |
| **Closed** (question) | Status `closed`: the owner ended an open question without answering (UI Close). The **close text** ("The owner closed this question without answering. Continue on your own judgement; if you cannot, end with HOPPER_FAILED and say why.") is typed into the job in place of an answer; any stage in flight is aborted. `question.closed`. | skipped (*dismissed* is something else) |
| **Dismissed** (question) | Status `dismissed`: the owner dropped an open question that needs no action any more (UI Dismiss). Nothing is typed into the job; a job still waiting on it is cancelled (`question dismissed`), a job that moved on is left alone. `question.dismissed`. | closed (Close lets the job go on), archived, hidden |
| **Seen** (question) | The owner had the question in front of them in the Questions view: `seenAt`, set once. Part of the question's record only: the nav badge counts every open question at the human stage, seen or not (issue #499). | read, acknowledged |
| **Lapsed** (question) | Status `lapsed` (issue #376): nobody answered a dialog before its **countdown** ran out, so the agent denied it by itself (Claude Code's auto-deny on an unattended session) and went on. Not an answer: nobody answered it, nothing was typed, any stage in flight is aborted, and the job is reattached (`reason: "the dialog lapsed"`). `question.lapsed`. The question's `lapsesAt` is when the countdown ends, read from the dialog when the turn stops on the question; the owner is shown it. | auto-denied, timed out (*expired* is the human stage running out, which fails the job) |
| **Handled** (question) | Any question no longer open: answered, closed, dismissed, expired, lapsed or cancelled. Settings lists them as the **question history**, kept in the hopper's database and never sent to GitHub. | archived, done, resolved |
| **Attempt** | One entry in a question's trail: an escalation level's reply, or the human's answer. `tier` = who, `role` `level` / `human`, `outcome` `accepted` / `escalated`, or, for a level's **call key** (issue #1608), `started` while its call is in flight and `interrupted` when the daemon stopped during it. Rows stored before escalation levels may carry `confident` (and before slice 2, `risky`). | try, draft |
| **Call key** | The key of one escalation level's call on a question (issue #1608, design.md "Question pipeline", `callKey`): `<question id>:<n>`. The `started` **attempt** is written with it before the model is asked, and the reply is kept under it, in its place; a review item's call in flight (`call`) and its review (`callKey`) carry one too. Recovery goes on at the level of a call in flight, not from the first. Not a **pane key** or a **step ask key**. | request id, call id |
| **Risk rule** | A named pattern (delete, deploy, force-push, spend, credentials, send-message) over the question and the answer to be typed; a hit on an answer a level gave sends the question to the owner, past every level above, unless the **risk call** is active (issue #679): then only delete, deploy, spend and send-message hold, as guards. A plain negation ("uses no secrets") is no hit. Code, not configuration. | keyword rule (in UI text: "keyword rules") |
| **Risk call** | The Decider's typed risk decision on a level's answer (issue #679, decision point `question-risk`, `src/minor-decisions/risk.ts`): `{ risk: none \| low \| real, categories, confidence }`, over the question, the answer and the job's goal. Active, it replaces the risk rules (not the guards): `real`, too little confidence, or a failure holds the question. In shadow or with no TypeSafe API key, the risk rules decide, and the attempt's `riskCheck` says which did. | risk classifier, risk score |
| **Confidence spread** | Per escalation level, the confidence of the answers it did not escalate over the last 30 days, and how many fall below the auto-answer threshold (issue #679, `autoAnswer.calibration`); **flagged** when most do. | calibration report, histogram |
| **Fixed answer** | The first step of the question pipeline (issue #629, `src/questions/fixed-answers.ts`): an answer the facts fix, given before the escalation levels and without a model — an **allowed dialog**'s Yes, or a **reused answer**. The risk rules run on it: a hit sends the question to the owner. On the trail and as `answeredBy`: `fixed`. | rules tier (the **Rules** are the owner's text), policy, auto-answer |
| **Allowed dialog** | A permission dialog of Claude's the fixed answers answer Yes (`ALLOWED_DIALOGS`): an edit (`Edit file`, `Create file`, `Write file`, `Overwrite file`) or a read (`Read file`) of a file inside the job's **work tree**, read off the screen (`detectedBy: blocked`). Code, not configuration. Any other dialog goes on to Jev and the levels. | allow list, permission table (the **Permission matrix** is Access's) |
| **Reused answer** | A fixed answer: the answer a person gave to the same question on the same item — the job and the jobs it runs again (`rerunOf`) —, compared case and spacing aside and a dialog's countdown aside. Only a person's answer (`answeredBy: human`), never a level's, Jev's or a close. | cached answer |
| **Rules** | The config record `rules`: the owner's standing rules, a text given to every escalation level, read on every ask. Edited whole from the UI (`POST /ui/api/rules`, against its `version`). | rules.md, rules document, rules file, policy |
| **Job rules** | The config record `job-rules` (issue #172): a text every job's prompt carries first in its footer, before the fixed lines — the work tree and the protocol (markers the hopper reads back), which are not edited. Read when each job starts (`ExecutionContext.jobRules`), so an edit reaches the next job. While none is saved, a job gets the **default job rules**: the publishing rule, the parallel-work rule and the **writing style**; a saved empty text gives none. Edited whole in Settings → Job rules (`POST /ui/api/job-rules`, against its `version`) or with `hopper config set job-rules`. Not the **Rules**, which go to escalation levels. | prompt rules, job policy, footer rules |
| **Repository job rules** | The config record `repo-job-rules` (issue #1472): a text per repository (`owner/name`) or per owner (`owner/*`), told to every job of it after the job rules — the owner's first, then the repository's, each after a line `[hopper repository rules]`. Read when each job starts. Edited whole in Settings → Job rules → Per repository (`POST /ui/api/repo-job-rules`, against its `version`) or with `hopper config set repo-job-rules`. What done means there, which tools a job may use, the standing context. | repo rules, project rules, repo context |
| **Writing style** | What a job writes for people — questions, research reports, proposals, hand-off notes, summaries, pull request and issue text — is in Simplified Technical English (ASD-STE100) (issue #571). One line in the default job rules (`STE_RULE`), a short clause in the question, research and proposal protocol lines and in their asks. A style only: nothing checks it. | STE rule, plain English |
| **Agent text** | What an agent writes for a person in the hopper — a question, a research report or proposal and its parts, a level's answer or notes —, and a person's note beside it. **Markdown** source (issue #569): kept, and sent to webhooks and the Grok Bot routine, as written; the UI renders it sanitized (`ui/src/lib/markdown.ts`): raw HTML shows as text, no image loads (an image is a link to it), a link goes only to http(s), mailto or the UI and opens in a new tab. The formatting job rule and the protocol lines ask for it. | rich text, HTML |
| **Summary** (of agent text) | What a card shows first of a long **agent text** (issue #569, `summaryOf`): its first paragraph that is not a heading; a review item's first part (a proposal's Goal, a report's Question, as the **pre-check** reads it). The rest is behind Show all. A text of at most 600 characters and 8 lines shows whole. | excerpt, lead, preview |
| **TL;DR** | One or two plain sentences on top of a long card (issue #569): what is asked or proposed and what the person must decide. A proposal's is its own first part, the agent's (issue #651); for a long question, research report or hand-off a cheap model (Claude Haiku) writes it — plain text, never Markdown or HTML, written once per text and stored with the card (`tldr`), written again when the text changes, with a setting that turns it off. Without one, the card shows the **summary**; a notification carries the TL;DR, else the summary. | abstract, digest, synopsis |
| **Question gates** | The chain an open question goes through: the fixed answers, the escalation levels, lowest first → the risk check (the **risk call**, else the risk rules) and the guards → owner. The UI's panel of that name (first section of Settings) shows each level's state, adds, removes, moves and tunes the levels — their one editor; Plugins shows them read-only (issue #444) — and edits the rules; `GET /api/question-gates` reads the rules and the risk rules. | escalation chain, pipeline settings |
| **Reattach** | The executor watching a job's live pane again without sending anything: restart recovery keeping a running job running (its pane and Claude outlived the daemon), or a job on a question *answered in the pane*. Never a re-run. With no lane idle it opens one past the lane cap, marked `pastCap` (issue #1181): that lane drains first. | resume (that delivers an answer), restart |
| **Reconnect grace** | After a daemon restart, how long a running job whose machine does not answer yet (a client target not dialled in, an ssh target not replying) stays running on its lane before it fails as `machine <id> did not reconnect within <n> s after the daemon restart` (issue #368, `HOPPER_RECONNECT_GRACE_MS`, default 120 s). The machine answering in time reattaches the job, or fails it as `interrupted by daemon restart` when its pane is gone. A **link-lost hold** has its own, longer **link grace**. | reconnect timeout, startup grace |
| **Link-lost hold** | A running job whose machine's link dropped while its work was watched (issue #752): it is not failed, it stays `running` on its lane (`linkLost` on the job, `job.link_lost`). The hopper asks every second whether its pane is alive again; the link back and the pane alive → `job.reattached` (reason `link restored`) and the watch goes on from the saved turn anchor; the pane gone → failed `interrupted while the link to machine <id> was down`; no link within the **link grace** (`HOPPER_LINK_GRACE_MS`, default 10 min) → failed `machine <id> did not reconnect within <n> s`, its cleanup deferred. Only a machine with a link (a client target) is held: a herdr error while its link is up counts toward the three in a row that fail the job, as before. Shown on its lane and its job page as *held: link lost*, and on its machine's card as *out of reach since <time>, <n> job held*. | link outage, offline hold, disconnect grace |
| **Link grace** | How long a **link-lost hold** lasts before the job fails as `machine <id> did not reconnect within <n> s` (issue #752, `HOPPER_LINK_GRACE_MS`, default 600 s). Longer than the reconnect grace: a box on a flaky network can be gone for minutes. | link timeout, hold timeout |
| **Max isolation** | How long a client target may go without hearing the hopper — no request the hopper signed, no keepalive PING answered on its link — before its **isolation stop** (issue #752; the client's `HOPPER_CLIENT_MAX_ISOLATION_MS`, default 30 min, `0`: never). Longer than the **link grace** (10 min), so in the normal case the hopper decides first. | TTL, offline timeout |
| **Isolation stop** | A client target cut off from the hopper for its **max isolation** stops the jobs the hopper last named in its `/release` probe: their scopes and processes, by the reap with no scratch dir, so their work stays (issue #752, `src/client/isolation.ts`). Its next `/release` answer says each stop once (`isolationStops`), and the hopper logs it. The named jobs and the last hearing are kept in the client dir's `isolation.json` (issue #1627), so a client restarted while cut off still stops them; a file older than a day is not read. | watchdog, kill switch |
| **Answered in the pane** | The owner typed the answer straight into a waiting pane. The hopper sees Claude working again, marks the question answered by `human` (`question.answered { via: "pane" }`) with the typed text, or `(answered in the pane)` when it cannot be read, and reattaches the job on a lane. Nothing typed and the dialog's countdown run out is no answer: the question *lapsed*. | |
| **Resume** | Delivering an accepted answer to a job's waiting pane and continuing it; for a **parked** job **picked up**, its agent session resumed in a new pane first. | restart |
| **herdr session** | The named herdr server that hosts job panes: the supervised `hopper` for every user, unless an executor's `session` option names another. No user gets a session of its own (never `hopper-<id>`). Never the user's default session. | |
| **Pane** | The herdr terminal a herdr-claude job runs in; one tab per job run. | window |
| **Restored pane** | A job's pane herdr restored after a restart of its server (issue #1465): the same pane id in a new terminal (`terminal_id`), its shell without the tab's variables and outside the job's scope and sandbox, Claude resumed in it by herdr. The hopper knows it by the terminal it recorded at the pane's start, stops that Claude, and resumes the agent session in a new pane. Claude that herdr resumed in another pane than the one the hopper recorded is taken the same way, and that pane is closed too (issue #2320). | respawned pane |
| **Work tree** | A herdr-claude job's cwd, and everything under it: where all of the job's work happens — clones, git worktrees, builds, temporary files. Work done in a copy outside it is a job that drifted (design.md "Work tree"). Its executor reports it once resolved (`job.workTree`), and the lane running the job shows it (issue #166). A `~` in it is the home of the machine the job runs on: this process's for this machine, an attached machine's as its probe found it (issue #323) — a Windows client's as a drive-letter path with `/` (`C:/Users/<user>`, issue #365); no job goes to an ssh or client target before its home is found. Which applies (issues #324, #361, #691): on the machine a routing rule pinned the job to, the rule's `workTree`; anywhere else the machine's **work tree per repository** for the job's repository, else its **machine work tree**. A path set for one machine never reaches another. | checkout, job dir |
| **Yolo** | A herdr-claude executor instance's choice (option `yolo`, default on, issue #267) that Claude runs with every permission granted (`--dangerously-skip-permissions`) and its warning about that accepted, so it never stops to ask. Off, Claude asks before it acts and each permission dialog pauses the job on a question for the escalation levels or the owner; an answer naming one of the dialog's options picks it. Picked by picking the instance (design.md "Yolo"). | bypass mode, skip-permissions, auto mode |
| **Startup screen** | A screen Claude Code shows as it starts, before its prompt: folder trust, external CLAUDE.md imports, the bypass permissions warning, the first-run theme, the machine's API key, a notice, the login method, or any other (issue #533). Decided on by its text: Claude is up only when its input box is on screen. One with a default (design.md "Startup screens") is answered; any other is asked of a person as a dialog before the send (issue #534). | startup dialog, onboarding prompt, first-run prompt |
| **Unattended** | A herdr-claude executor instance's choice (option `unattended`, default on, issue #533) that Claude starts with no key press: its config seeded where the machine has none, its first-run startup screens answered with their defaults, no self-update in a job's pane. Off, every first-run screen is asked of a person. Either way no startup screen is a wait. | non-interactive, headless, auto-accept |
| **Disk** (of a machine) | The filesystem its home is on, where the jobs directory and the scratch dirs live (issue #401): bytes free and in all, and **low** below the machine's thresholds (issue #410; default 5 GiB or a tenth free). Read by its probe (`MachineSnapshot.disk`); shown on Machines, low ones in the Dashboard's Attention and the header. A machine whose disk is low takes no new job. **Critical** (issue #2494) below its critical thresholds (default 1 GiB or 2% free): it takes no job at all, a job back to its pane included. A disk that turns low or critical is told as `machine.disk_low`, and as `machine.disk_ok` once it is no longer low. | storage, volume |
| **Machine work tree** | A machine's `workTree` option (`local`, `ssh`, `client`; issues #324, #361), set in the Machines view: the work tree of every job there but one a routing rule pinned there with its own; absent, the **jobs directory**. `~` in it is that machine's home. The hopper makes it there when it probes the machine (at attach, then every 30 s) — this machine in the background, an ssh target over ssh, a client target through its client — and says its **work tree problem** when it cannot. Never on a container target. | machine cwd, machine default dir, workspace |
| **Work tree per repository** | A machine's `workTrees` option (`local`, `ssh`, `client`; issue #691): `{"owner/name": "<path>"}`, the repository matched in any case. A job from a repository it names works in that path on the machine, made there and the repository cloned in it when missing — never in the **machine work tree**, which takes the jobs of every other repository. `~` is that machine's home. Edited in Plugins (the machine's instance), shown on the machine's panel. A path that is the home or above it fails the job, naming it. Never on a container target. | repo map, repo workspace |
| **Hopper address** | The hopper's URL as a job on a machine reaches it, given to the job as `HOPPER_URL` (issues #563, #698): a client target, where it dialled in; this machine, loopback; an ssh target, the first of the hopper's addresses — the public URL, then each LAN name with the port — that its probe found it reaches, asked from the machine at `GET /job/address` (204) — and, when the hopper runs in WSL and none answered, a WSL machine is asked the **host link base** (default `http://localhost:<port>`) last, since WSL distros on one computer share one network (issue #1248 part 4) —; a container target, the public URL, else the first LAN name. An ssh target that reaches none has an **address problem**, and its jobs get no hopper address. | hopper reach URL, callback URL |
| **Address problem** | Why a machine's jobs cannot reach the hopper: an ssh target's, as its last probe found (`MachineSnapshot.addressProblem`, issue #698); any machine's, as the **reach check** at a job's start found, for ten minutes (`src/engine/reach-holds.ts`, issue #1920): no address answered as the hopper there (no connection, refused by the Host guard, another server), the hopper has no address, or the machine has no curl; a WSL machine asked the host link base is told that address too, as the one it reaches the hopper at from the hopper's computer (issue #1248 part 4). A machine with one is **off-network** and takes no new job: its jobs are held with the reason (`no machine running executor <x> reaches the hopper: <m> is off-network: …`, `pinned machine <m> is off-network: …`), never failed, and the Machines view and the log say it. | unreachable hopper |
| **Off-network** | A machine with an **address problem** (issue #711): its jobs cannot reach the hopper. The Machines view shows it as off-network, with why and, for an ssh target that names a container, the command that joins that container to the **hopper's network** (`MachineSnapshot.addressFix`, `podman network connect hopper_default <container>`). Its jobs are held with the reason and that command (`… is off-network: … Fix: …`). | unreachable machine, disconnected |
| **Hopper's network** | The network the release `compose.yaml` makes and names, `hopper_default` (issue #711): every service is on it, and each sandbox box the hopper starts joins it (`hopperNetwork`, `HOPPER_SANDBOX_NETWORK`). On it, `http://hopper:<port>` reaches the hopper. `host` for a hopper on the host. | compose network, shared network |
| **Herdr problem** | Why an ssh target's herdr client cannot work with its herdr server, as its last probe found (`MachineSnapshot.herdrProblem`, issue #741): the server says the client is not compatible, the client's protocol is older than the server's, or herdr's own protocol error. Every job start there would fail. A machine with one takes no new job: its jobs are held with the reason (`no machine running executor <x> has a working herdr: …`, `pinned machine <m>: …`), never failed, and Machines shows it with both versions (badge `herdr outdated`). A job start that fails on the protocol opens the problem **Herdr outdated** on that machine at once. | herdr mismatch, outdated client |
| **Owned herdr** | The herdr the hopper owns on an ssh target (issue #741): the hopper's own herdr (the one on its PATH), copied to `~/.local/share/hopper/herdr/<version>/herdr` there by each probe when missing, only to a machine of the same platform (`uname -sm`). The link `current` names it while the session server takes it: then every herdr call there runs it (`REMOTE_HERDR`), and `systemd/hopper-herdr.service` starts the session server from it. Else the calls find herdr by name as before, and the hopper **switches** the session server to it — restarts the server from that path — only with no job running and no pane open there, never when the server is a container's main process (that is a herdr problem: the image must change), and once per version per machine (`switch-tried` beside it). Machines shows its version and whether it is in use, or why not. | managed herdr, bundled herdr, herdr pin |
| **Work tree problem** | Why a machine's work tree cannot be made usable, as its last probe found (`MachineSnapshot.workTreeProblem`, issue #361): it is the home or above it, or it cannot be made or written. A machine with one takes no new job: its jobs are held with the reason (`no machine running executor <x> has a usable work tree: …`, `pinned machine <m>: …`), never failed, and the Machines view shows it. | unusable workspace |
| **Jobs directory** | `~/hopper-jobs` on the job's machine (`JOBS_DIR`, issue #314): the work tree of a machine that names none (issue #361). A machine's work tree is made when missing; a work tree that is the machine's home, above it, or `/` fails the job at start. | default cwd, jobs folder |
| **Checkout** (of a job's repository) | `<work tree>/workspace/<name>` (issues #361, #1103, `checkoutOf`): the clone of a job's repository in its work tree's **workspace**, made when the work tree is not itself the top of the job's repository — a plain directory (the jobs directory by default) or another repository's top (issue #431: one lane runs jobs of any repository one after another). The one the workspace already tends is used as it is; else the hopper clones it there once. Its origin must be that repository. A start fetches only the remote's default branch into `refs/remotes/origin`, so its own branch and files stay as a person left them. Made in the pane's shell before the agent starts, with the job's credentials by name, never on a command line; its **job worktree** is made of it. With job worktrees off, the job runs in the work tree beside it. A work tree that is the job's own repository is used as it is and never gets one; a job that names no repository uses any repository work tree as it is. | provisioning, clone step, mirror |
| **Scratch dir** | `<work tree>/.worktrees/@scratch/<job id>` (issue #1188, `jobScratchOf`), for herdr-claude and print-agent jobs alike (issue #410): a job's temporary files, its credentials dir, Claude's scratchpad (`TMPDIR`, `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`, through its **temp link**) and the clones a job makes only for itself, git-ignored by `.worktrees/.gitignore`. Never a **job worktree** (issue #1103). The reap removes it at the job's end. Not the work dir, which is the daemon's. | tmp dir, scratchpad dir |
| **Temp link** | `/tmp/hopper-<job id>` on the job's machine, a symbolic link to its scratch dir (issue #506): the job's `TMPDIR` and `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`. Short, so a Unix socket a tool binds under `TMPDIR` fits in 108 bytes (Chromium's did not under the scratch dir's own path). Made by the scratch command, removed by the reap while it still points at the scratch dir. | tmp link, short tmpdir |
| **Workspace** (of a work tree) | `<work tree>/workspace` (issue #1103, `WORKSPACE_DIR`): where a work tree keeps its repositories' **checkouts**, `workspace/<name>`, the layout the workflow repos tend; the **job worktrees** made of them are in **`.worktrees`** (issue #1188). One the hopper makes ignores itself (`.gitignore` of `*`). Not the **work dir**, the daemon's, nor a herdr workspace. | worktree root |
| **`.worktrees`** (of a work tree) | `<work tree>/.worktrees` (issue #1188, `WORKTREES_DIR`, `src/domain/job-layout.ts`): the one place a job's files are in its work tree, git-ignored by its own `.gitignore` of `*`: each **job worktree** `.worktrees/<name>/<job id>`, each **scratch dir** `.worktrees/@scratch/<job id>`, and the **shared dependencies** `.worktrees/@deps/<lockfile hash>`. A name that starts with `@` is the hopper's own. The layout before (`.hopper-scratch`, `workspace/worktrees`) is only cleaned up by the **reap**. | worktree root, job dir |
| **Start lock** | `hopper-start.lock` in the git common dir of the repository a **job worktree** is made of — the **checkout** in the workspace, or the work tree's own repository (issue #1075, `JOB_WORKTREE_LOCK`): a start takes it around its fetch and its worktree add, so starts on one machine use one repository one at a time, and git's automatic gc runs inside it. A directory with its holder's pid; one whose holder ended, or held past 300 seconds, is taken over. | mirror lock, fetch lock, mutex |
| **Pass traffic** | What one sync pass of a GitHub source asked of GitHub (issue #858, `src/github-limiter/traffic.ts`): its REST calls per route, its GraphQL calls per read, its git fetches per repository and by who made them (a job, or the **hopper's mirror**), and what went between passes; logged as one line when the pass ends, never kept. | API stats, usage |
| **Hopper's mirror** | `<user's work dir>/git-mirrors/<owner>/<name>.git` (issue #858 part 2, `src/git-mirror/`): one bare, blobless git repository per job repository per user on the daemon's machine, fetched at most once per repository per sync pass — the pull request's base and every `refs/pull/*/head` — with the source's own credential. Whether a pull request has merge conflicts comes from it (`git merge-tree --write-tree`), GitHub's `mergeable` only where it cannot say; so does whether two pull requests conflict with each other (issue #939, `conflictBetween`: `git merge-tree` of their two heads). Not a **Checkout**, which is on a job's machine. | daemon clone, merge cache |
| **Job worktree** | A herdr-claude job's own git worktree of its work tree, or of its repository's **checkout** in it (issues #379, #361, #1188): `<work tree>/.worktrees/<name>/<job id>` (`jobWorktreeOf`), in the work tree's **`.worktrees`**, `<name>` the job's repository's or the work tree's own. Made when the executor's `jobWorktrees` option is on (the default): of the work tree when it is the top of the job's git repository (or of any, for a job that names none); else, when the job has a repository, of its checkout (issue #431: also when the work tree is another repository's top); after a fetch, detached at the remote's default branch (else the current branch's upstream, else HEAD), and Claude starts there. The job reports it as where it works, so its lane shows it; the work tree it was made from is shared and left alone. Never in the scratch dir; the reap removes it with the scratch dir unless it holds uncommitted or unpushed work, and the sweep reaps one an ended job left. | job clone, job checkout, per-job tree |
| **Cleanup record** | The record that an ended job's cleanup (closing its pane, the reap, deleting its **job pod**) is still owed (issue #1570, `Job.cleanupDue`): written in the transaction that records the job's end, removed once the cleanup went through. A hopper that stops before then does the cleanup from it at its next start, and `job.cleaned_up` says so with `dueAt`. | pending cleanup, cleanup flag |
| **Resource ledger** | The `job_resource_ledger` table (issue #2504, design.md "The resource ledger", `src/domain/resource-ledger.ts`): one **ledger entry** per thing the hopper made for a job on a machine. Written when the job's executor saves its state, in the same transaction; closed by the job's cleanup, the sweep and a discard, as each **reap** says. A closed entry stays as the record. | resource registry, inventory, job resources |
| **Ledger entry** | One row of the **resource ledger**: the job, its machine, its kind (`scratch_dir`, `job_worktree`, `job_scope`, `container_runtime`: the **job's container runtime**, issue #2504 part 4; `job_processes`, `job_containers`: the processes carrying its id and the containers and volumes labelled with it, part 9; `agent_session`: the session its agent runs in, part 11; `herdr_pane`: the pane it works in, which only its executor's cleanup closes, part 13), its locator (a path, a unit name, the variable, the label, the session id or the pane id), when it was made, and its state — `live`, `reaped`, `kept` (with work not pushed; a later reap may still remove it) `failed` with why (the reap could not remove it), or `retired` (left on a **retired machine**, which no reap reaches, part 12; a reap there should the machine come back still judges it). | resource record, ledger row |
| **Cleanup status** | Where a job's cleanup stands, read from its **ledger entries** and its **cleanup record** (issue #2504, `cleanupStatusOf`): `none` (no entry), `in_use` (the job has not ended), `pending` (its cleanup could not reach its machine, or an entry is still live), `in_progress`, `done`, `kept_work`, `retained` (every live entry kept for its machine's retention, issue #2504 part 5), `machine_retired` (an entry left on a **retired machine**, part 12) or `failed`. The job's **parts list** shows it as the part `cleanup`, and each entry beside it. | cleanup state, reap status |
| **Cleanup item** | A **ledger entry** still `live` or `failed` of an ended job whose own cleanup is over, past its retention and its next attempt (issue #2504 part 14, `maidWorkOf`): the **maids** — the pass with each sweep check (`src/engine/maids.ts`) — reap it through its machine's own shell, and one the reap does not close is an attempt, kept on the entry (`attempts`, `nextAttemptAt`) and tried again after a backoff (1 minute, doubled each time, 1 hour at most). Never a herdr pane. | cleanup job, retry task |
| **Maids** | The pass that reaps **cleanup items**, with each sweep check on the sweep lease's holder (issue #2504 part 14, `createMaids`): one reap per job and machine, through that machine's own shell. | cleanup worker, janitor |
| **Cleanup leftover** | An ended job's **ledger entry** still `live` or `failed` once the grace period (`CLEANUP_GRACE_MINUTES`, 60 minutes) after the job's end passed (issue #2504, `cleanupLeftoversOf`), or after its retention ends when the reap kept it for one (`retainedUntil`, part 5). The leftover check, with each sweep pass, records it once per job (`job.cleanup_leftover`); Failures lists the job as stuck work (`cleanup_leftover`) while it stays, and Machines counts them on each machine (`cleanupLeftovers`, part 3), with the bytes the last disk measure says they hold (part 10). Once told, an entry of one on a **retired machine** is closed `retired` (part 12): it is then no leftover. Not a **leftover** (issue #1116), which is a dir of the layout before issue #1188 that no ledger names. | orphan |
| **Reconciler** | The sweep's step that compares what a machine's survey found with the **resource ledger** (issue #2504 part 3, `goneClosures`, `closeGone`): an ended job's entry still `live` or `failed` on that machine whose thing the survey no longer found — a scratch dir in a work tree the survey looked in, a job worktree, a scope that no longer runs — is closed `reaped`, with the reason "not found on the machine". It removes nothing: what the survey still finds, the sweep's reaps remove. It also records what the survey found of an ended job the store knows that no entry names (part 6, `unledgeredEntriesOf`): a scratch dir or job worktree, kept until the sweep's reap of it, and a running scope; a job the store does not know is never recorded. The sweep surveys the work tree of each open entry too (part 7, `ledgerRootsOf`), also one no job in the job list used, and of each `kept` entry (part 8); a `kept` entry whose thing is gone is closed `reaped` the same way (part 8); an ended job's processes and labelled containers are recorded and closed gone the same way (part 9). | ledger sync, garbage collector |
| **Deferred cleanup** | A job's cleanup (closing its pane, the reap) that could not reach the job's machine (issue #371): the job ended, but its pane and agent may still run there. Recorded on the job (`cleanupDeferred`, `job.cleanup_deferred`), tried again on every tick until it goes through (`job.cleaned_up`), or until the user marks it closed. Meanwhile a waiting job of the same item is held. Shown as *pane may still be open*. | orphan pane, leaked pane, pending cleanup |
| **Job's container runtime** | A job's own podman (issue #1295, `src/client/job-runtime.ts`): its runtime dir `<the user's runtime dir>/hopper-runtime-<job id>`, its storage in its scratch dir, its socket, its environment in `<scratch dir>/containers/env.sh`, and the guards `podman`, `docker` and `systemd-run` first on its PATH. Never the user's runtime or socket; the reap resets and removes it. With no podman on the machine, none: DOCKER_HOST names a socket that is not there. | job runtime, nested podman, job socket |
| **Job's sandbox** | The bubblewrap that Claude, and all it runs, runs in (issue #1295, `src/client/job-sandbox.ts`): its own PID and IPC namespaces, the user's runtime dir empty but for the job's own runtime (its bus and systemd's socket masked), the user's container storage, the host's container sockets and herdr's sockets masked. Put around Claude by the sandbox's `claude` first on the pane's PATH, and around a print-mode agent's turn by its runner; podman in it is a client of the job's socket. With no bubblewrap that runs, none: Claude runs as the user does. | agent sandbox, bwrap jail, Claude Code's sandbox (Claude's own, inside it) |
| **Instance tag** | The name of the hopper that gave an answer (issue #1295, `src/http/instance-tag.ts`): 16 hex characters, a hash of the instance id, in the `x-hopper-instance` header of every answer and as the event stream's first event (`hopper.instance`). The page keeps its hopper's tag (`jh_instance` in the browser) and drops an answer with another tag, or with none once it knows one: **another hopper** answers at its address (a job's test hopper on the address of the user's hopper while that one was down). No proof: a hopper a job changed can send any tag. `ui/src/lib/instance.ts`. | instance id (what it hashes), server id, fingerprint (the master key's) |
| **Reap** | What a job's machine runs as the job ends (issues #401, #410), through the machine's own connection (this machine, ssh, a client target's `/reap`), never in a pane: the job's scope stopped, every process still carrying its `HOPPER_JOB_ID` stopped, and its temp link removed, and its scratch dir and its **job worktrees** in `.worktrees` (issue #1188; and what the layout before left) removed unless a repository in them holds uncommitted or unpushed work — then kept, and flagged as `job.work_kept`. | purge |
| **Job scope** | The transient systemd user scope `hopper-job-<job id>` a herdr-claude job's pane shell, or a print-agent job's turn, runs in where the machine has a systemd user manager (issue #410): every process the job starts is in its cgroup, whatever it does to its environment or session, and the reap stops it. | job cgroup, unit |
| **Sweep** | The engine's pass over a machine (issue #410), at startup and every `reapEveryMinutes` (default 10): from the machine's **survey** (the job scopes, the job ids of its processes, the scratch dirs and the job worktrees in each `.worktrees`, with their ages) it reaps the scopes and processes of known jobs that are not live, and an ended job's scratch dir and job worktrees once `scratchMaxAgeHours` old (default 24), or past their **retention**, or while its work was kept; never a job it does not know. | reaper, janitor, GC |
| **Retention** (job work) | How many hours an ended job's scratch dir and job worktrees stay on its machine, counted from the job's end, for the status it ended in (issue #2494): the machine options `retainFinishedHours`, `retainFailedHours`, `retainCancelledHours`, `retainRejectedHours`. Above 0, the reap at the job's end keeps the scratch dir, and the sweep removes it once the retention is over; work not pushed is kept whatever it says. Not set for a status: reaped at the job's end, as before. Not the **history retention**. | TTL, keep time, grace period |
| **Shared dependencies** | `<work tree>/.worktrees/@deps/<lockfile hash>/node_modules` (issue #1188): the dependencies installed once for a lockfile and linked as `node_modules` into each job worktree with that lockfile (issue #410, herdr-claude option `sharedDependencies`). Read-only to a job; a job that changes dependencies replaces the link with its own install. | deps cache, shared node_modules |
| **UI bundle check** | `scripts/check-ui-bundle.ts`, the last step of `npm run build:ui` (issue #274): the stylesheet `ui/dist/index.html` links must be a real Tailwind bundle — at least 40 KB, with a rule for each of a few layout utilities — else the build fails, and with it the install, a self-update's build of the next install, and the image. | CSS check, style check |
| **Waiting pane** | The pane of a job waiting on an answer (`waiting_answer`): it stays open, Claude in it, while the job holds no lane. | parked pane (a *parked* job has no pane) |
| **Parked** (a job) | Status `parked` (issue #501): a person took a running job, or one on a question, a proposal or a research report (issue #2167), out of its lane for an open-ended time (UI Park, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/park`, `hopper job park`). Who parked it (`parked.by`, `via`) and the reason they gave, if any (`parked.reason`), are kept on it (issue #2167). Its lane frees at once; its pane and agent end and its processes stop; its work tree, branch, **agent session**, machine (`resumeOn`) and open question are kept — the sweep, the reap and the scratch dir's age never remove them while it is parked. Its question stays answerable and never expires; an answer is kept (`pendingAnswer`) until it is **picked up**. Listed in the **Parked section** with its open question, which is out of Questions, its badge and Attention meanwhile (issue #565). Never running, never waiting, on no lane, never counted against its machine. `job.parked`. Any job whose executor can park (`parkingExecutors`); one with no agent session **starts fresh** when picked up (issue #530). A cancel releases what it kept, through the normal reap. | shelved, paused, suspended, backburner (an issue label) |
| **Preemption** | Issue #1525: a job of strictly higher priority that waits for a lane, with every lane it may use busy, takes the lane of a running job of lower priority — the lowest priority, then the one started last — that has run at least the **minimum run time**, whose executor parks and that has an **agent session**. That job is parked on the Park button's path and queued again at once (**preempted**). Step 7b of the decider (`Decision.preempt`, `src/decider/preempt.ts`); `job.preempted`. The admin's setting turns it off (Settings → Preemption, `GET /api/preemption`). | bump, evict, kick out, steal a lane |
| **Preempted** (a job) | A running job a **preemption** parked (`job.preempted`, `preempted: { by, byPriority, priority }`): its work tree, agent session and checkpoint kept, queued again pinned to its machine at its own priority with no resume boost, ahead of the other jobs of that priority; its claim resumes its agent session where it stopped (on another machine, from its checkpoint, after the **busy-machine move**). Shown as `Queued: preempted by #12 …` until it runs again, which clears the mark. | bumped, evicted, restarted (it resumes) |
| **Minimum run time** | How long a job runs, from its start or resume, before a **preemption** may take its lane (issue #1525): the user's setting `minRunMinutes`, default 10, 0 to a day. So no job is preempted again and again. | grace period, cooldown |
| **Auto-park** | A job that waits on a person parked by the hopper, not a person (issues #650, #2335): its question a few seconds (`WAIT_RELEASE_AFTER_MS`) after it reached a person, or after it began to wait on one again (issue #692), its proposal or research report as long after it reached the human stage; a question a risk rule or the consequential guard sent to a person too. The Park button's path; `parked.auto`, and `why` on the card and in `job.parked` ("Parked automatically: a job that waits on a person holds no agent."). Its checkpoint, taken after its agent ended, is marked on the park (`parked.checkpointAt`): the answer to its question, or the decision on its item, picks it up to resume on any machine. No setting. | auto-pause, timeout, expiry (an *expired* question fails its job), park timeout (gone, issue #2335) |
| **Thread ask** | A GitHub job's question asked on its issue thread (issue #435): once it reaches a person, the hopper posts it as one marked comment under the source's identity (policy `post`), or holds it for a person's approval first (`review`); the job parks at once (`why` "Parked: its question goes on the issue thread, and a reply there resumes it."). A **GitHub watch** on its issue sees each new comment (issue #427). The first reply after it by the connected account or a maintainer of the repository (`author_association` owner, member or collaborator) answers it (`answeredBy` `thread:<login>`) and resumes the job; a maintainer's reply reaches the job fenced as data. `Question.thread` (`review` → `approved` → `posted`). Never for a question kept for the owner (`keptBy`). | post, comment (the hopper's other issue writes) |
| **Thread policy** | What the hopper does with a thread ask, per kind of repository (issue #435): `ownRepos` (the connected account owns it) and `otherRepos`, each `post`, `review` or `off`; default `post` and `review`. The user's setting: Settings → Issue thread, `GET /api/thread-ask`, `POST /ui/api/thread-ask`. Read at each sync. | |
| **Parked item** | A proposal or research report whose job a person, or a running job through the `park` skill (`/job/park`, `via` `job`), parked on it (issue #2167, `parkedItemOf`, `src/domain/review.ts`): out of its section's open list, counts and badge, listed apart (`GET /api/<section>?status=parked`, each with `parked`: since when, by whom, why) and folded under its section and in the **Parked section** with its job. No reviewer level reviews it and no decision is taken on it (409) until its job is **picked up**, which puts it back where it was: the same version at the same stage, a reviewer level the park stopped asking again. A job picks up only an item a job parked: a person's park stays until a person picks it up. | shelved proposal, snoozed research |
| **Parked section** | The **section** of the **parked** jobs (issue #565, UI Parked): one compact row per job — issue title and number, machine, how long parked, its open question's first line, whether its agent session resumes —, high priority first, then the longest parked; expanded, the full question and **Answer and pick up** (the answer, then the **pick up**, in one step). Its badge counts the parked jobs, quiet, marked when high priority. | shelf, backlog, snoozed |
| **Pick up** (a parked job) | Putting a **parked** job back (UI Pick up, in Parked and wherever parked jobs are listed; `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/requeue` and `job.unparked`, whose names predate the word, issue #565; `hopper job pick-up`, issue #2167): it waits pinned to its machine — held while that machine is offline, never started elsewhere but by the **busy-machine move** — and its claim opens a new pane in its kept work tree and resumes its agent session there, with the answer it was given, else a note that it was parked. With its question still open, it waits on the question again instead, and the human timeout starts again. Refused (409) for a job **auto-park** parked on a person, with no answer kept (issue #2415): picked up, it would only park again; the answer, or the decision on its item, picks it up. A job with no agent session **starts fresh** instead, only once the person confirms it (`freshSession`). | re-queue (only the route's name), unpark, resume (that is the executor's part), run again (a new job) |
| **Start fresh** (a parked job) | Issue #530: the **pick up** of a **parked** job with no **agent session** (one started before sessions were recorded). Confirmed by the person (`freshSession: true`, never silent), its claim starts a new agent session in the kept work tree, told its task, then that it has none of the earlier session's history, and its question and the answer. | restart, run again (a new job) |
| **Agent session** | The id of the conversation a job's agent runs in (`job.agentSession`, issue #501): the herdr-claude executor starts Claude with one it chose (`--session-id`) and reports it once Claude is up; a **parked** job resumes it (`--resume`). The agent keeps the session's data on the machine; the hopper never removes it. | conversation id, chat id (a Cursor chat is a print-mode agent's) |
| **Give-back** | What a job's source does when a person's act takes its item back (issue #1194, `SourceRegistry.giveBack` and `afterAnswer`) — Run again, Rework, Failures' Retry, a hand-off's Continue and I fixed it, Push it, Tend again: on GitHub the issue assigned to the connected account, reopened and its end labels gone; for Rework the pull request made a draft first. It runs after the answer, on the job's report chain, and the new job follows, or the job goes on; one that fails records `job.give_back_failed` with why and makes no job. The button waits from the click until the stream says how it ended. | retry, pending rerun, background task |
| **Adopt** (a job) | What a running job source does for a job whose own source no longer runs (issue #1352, `JobSource.adopts`): it takes the job's item back, as Run again, Retry, Continue and Push it need, when the job's repository is one of its job repositories. An item belongs to its repository and issue, not to the source that first saw it. The new job is the adopting source's; the ended job's end counts as told, since its removed source is told nothing more. | re-home, migrate, transfer |
| **After-answer timing** | How the work after a person's act spent its time (issue #1194, `src/sources/after-answer-timings.ts`): per act, the time each run waited on its queue — the job's report chain, or the source's sync chain — apart from the time it ran, as p50, p95 and max of its last 100 runs. `act` is a give-back act, `merge`, `close`, or `sync:<act>` for the sync an act asks for; with `source`, an intake action's **write-behind** in that source, its rows' wait in the **GitHub outbox** apart from their call (part 8, issue #1238). Kept in memory; Sources shows it as Work after an act; `GET /api/sources` answers it as `afterAnswer`. A long wait says the act's work stood behind other work; a long run says it was slow itself. | queue time, latency, histogram |
| **Write-behind** | The GitHub work a person's intake action leaves after its answer (issue #1194): Approve's comments and `hopper:done`, Assign to me's assignment, Release claim's and Take off the backburner's labels, Take into Intake's label and assignment. Its rows in the **GitHub outbox** (issue #1238). The action answers first; its item shows the write-behind (`writeBehind`) `running` — with its `attempt` and the `error` of the try before while the outbox tries again by itself (part 8) —, then `failed` with why until the same act sent again goes through. Its event is recorded once GitHub took it. Not a **follow-up**, which is an issue a job filed. | follow-up, background task, pending write |
| **GitHub outbox** | The GitHub changes people's actions asked for, kept in the user's store until sent (issue #1238, `github_outbox`, `src/sources/github/outbox.ts`): an intake action's **write-behind**, a hand-off resolution's end labels and comment (act `resolve`), a person's Merge now or Close PR (acts `merge`, `close`, part 3), and a person's act that gives an ended job's item back — Run again, Rework (acts `rerun`, `rework`, part 4), Failures' Retry, a hand-off's Continue and I fixed it, Push it, Tend again (`retry`, `continue`, `fixed`, `push`, `tend`, part 5: the row names the record, hand-off or job and the person's input, and its run is checked again at the send) — those sent at once, with no batch window —, the **label mirror**'s change of a blocked item's end labels (act `mirror`, issue #1891), and the end labels of the state a followed pull request's end moves its item to (act `follow`, issue #1892 part 14, sent in the follow's own pass), and a job's claim and the end labels of the state its report's end moves its item to (act `report`, issue #1892 part 15, sent in the report's own pass; an end drops its claim's rows not sent yet). One row per change — its issue, act, change, the event to record, state `pending`, `sending`, `sent` or `failed`, attempts, last error, when it is due —, kept in the transaction of the action's local change, sent in batches after the answer and coalesced per issue, tried again after a backoff; a failed row is listed on Sources with a Retry (`retry_outbox`). A send **claims** its rows (issue #1959, `OUTBOX_CLAIM_MS`, 2 minutes, renewed before each call): one statement makes them `sending` until the claim ends, so a second hopper on the database sends none of them; a row a killed hopper left `sending` is sent again once its claim ends; a clean stop releases its rows at once. While its connected account is **expired** (reconnect needed, issue #1891), nothing is sent, and a call that fails for it leaves its rows `pending` with no attempt counted, never `failed`. A sync never waits for it, and does not take an issue with rows not sent. A person's act on one job shows on its pull request's card and on the job while its row is not sent (`jobActs`, the act's marker): on its way, or failed with why. Not a **pending index update**, which waits for Solr. | write queue, job queue, outbound send |
| **Work not pushed** | An ended job whose **reap** kept its scratch dir because a repository in it holds uncommitted or unpushed work (`job.work_kept`), until the sweep removes it once pushed or a person discards it (`job.work_removed`; issue #1010). Listed in Failures with two ways out: **Push it** — the failed, finished or cancelled job goes on in its own agent session and kept work tree, told to push the work and open the pull request (a draft when not finished); through its open **hand-off** as Continue when it has one — and **Discard** — the scratch dir removed on its machine whatever it holds. Listed and counted in the Failures badge only while its **work item** is not done or closed, its job is the item's newest and no person dismissed it, as the **work-item ledger** has it (issues #1067, #1457); once its item is done, the sweep discards it (issue #1105). Once its scratch dir and job worktree are gone from its machine (removed outside the hopper), the sweep resolves it (`job.work_removed` with `gone`, issue #1120). Discard answers before the removal on the machine, which the entry follows as **discarding** — removing, or failed with why (`job.work_discard_failed`), issue #1194; kept on the job (`Job.discard`) until the removal ends, so a removal a stop cut short is done again by the next sweep of its machine (issue #1570). Once the job went on in its work tree and its reap kept the same work again (`keptAgainOf`, issue #1470: a clone of another repository, whose push is refused), Push it is refused and only Discard moves it: no person pushes on the machine, which holds no GitHub credentials (issue #2087). Work whose every commit the reap names (its **kept tips**) the hopper pushed for the job is not work not pushed (issue #2087). A finished job's work not pushed is pushed by itself: **Push it by itself**. | kept work, leftover, orphaned work |
