| **Stranded wait** (a job's) | An **own wait** the hopper watches (not one its pane carries) whose agent session ended — Claude exited and its pane shows a bare shell, or the pane is gone (issue #1779). Marked `strandedAt` on the wait by the pane sweep, unmarked when its agent is seen again; never ended by the hopper. What it waits on still wakes it, resumed in a new pane. **Stuck work**, cause `stranded` (Wake all); the Dashboard shows "stranded: its agent session ended"; `hopper failure stuck`, `hopper job end-wait <id>`. | dead wait, orphaned wait, zombie wait |
| **Stuck work** | Every item that waits on a person, counted by its **stuck cause** first in Failures (issue #1010, `stuck` in `GET /api/failures`, `src/domain/stuck.ts`): an open **hand-off**, a failure Run again takes, **work not pushed**, a pull request the hopper gave up on or cannot tend, a job stuck on its **own command wait**. Each item is in one cause only. A **parked** job is not: it waits by design, in Parked only (issue #2414). | stuck list, stuck jobs, backlog |
| **Kept tips** | The commits a clean repository the **reap** keeps holds that no remote has (issue #2087, `hopper-kept-tip`, `Reaped.tips`): a worktree's `HEAD`, a clone's `HEAD` and branches. When the hopper pushed each of them for the job (`heads` on its git push events), the repository is no **work not pushed**: its remote branch may be gone or never moved. | unpushed tips |
| **Push it by itself** | The failure sweep's Push it with no person (issue #1472 part 2, `autoPush`, `src/failures/kept-work.ts`): a finished job with **work not pushed**, no open **hand-off**, and Push it taken now goes on in its own agent session and kept work tree, told to push the work and open the pull request. Once for each job (`job.continued` with `keptWork` and `auto`); after that the work waits for a person. Not for a failed job (the failure assessor and its hand-off decide) or a cancelled one (a person stopped it). The failures setting `auto.push`, default on, turns it off. | auto-push, automatic push |
| **Stuck cause** | Why a piece of **stuck work** waits, and what moves it (issue #1010, `STUCK_CAUSES`): a hand-off's by its card's recommended resolution (`continue`, `run_again`, `wont_do`, `done_by_hand`, or `taken_soon` while it is refused for a short time), `retry`, `push` or `kept_work` (Push it taken or not), `tend`, `checks` or `tend_refused` (a pull request), `wait_unnamed` (a wait that names nothing it **waits on**, issue #1245; bulk action Resume all), `stranded` (a **stranded wait**, issue #1779; bulk action Wake all), `own_wait`, `pr_open_stale` (an item blocked with no pull request to wait on, issues #1231, #1891), `cleanup_leftover` (an ended job with a **cleanup leftover**, issue #2504). A cause the hopper acts on for each item (`STUCK_BULK`) has one **bulk action**; every other says what a person does. A parked job has none: it is no stuck work (issue #2414). | stuck reason, stuck kind |
| **Bulk action** (a stuck cause's) | One press that runs a **stuck cause**'s action on each of its items now, through the same path as the item's own button, with who pressed it (issue #1010, `POST /ui/api/failures/stuck/:cause`, a box action): Continue, Run again or Won't do on each hand-off (Won't do says its card's why), Run again on each failure, Push it, Tend again. An item refused meanwhile is skipped with why. | mass action, batch action |
| **Checkpoint** | A copy of a job's agent session and its work not pushed, taken on its machine while it is at work (issue #921): every ten minutes, once when it is parked, and at a **missed heartbeat** (issue #1569). The machine's fixed script reads the job's worktree — the patch from a commit on the remote (the job's branch as pushed, else where it left the default branch) to the work tree as it is, and the commits not pushed —, and the agent session's file; the hopper scrubs both of secrets and keeps only the latest (`job.checkpoint`, the parts in the store's checkpoints). `job.checkpointed`. Kept a week after its job ends. | snapshot (an *item snapshot* is an issue's text), backup, save point |
| **Missed heartbeat** | The first sign that a running job's machine may be lost (issue #1569): a poll of the job's watch that failed while the link is up, or a keepalive PING of a client target's link not answered when the next one is due, before the PING timeout drops the link. The hopper takes the job's **checkpoint** at once, at most once a minute, while the machine may still answer. | late PING, heartbeat timeout, link loss (that is the drop) |
| **Transcript** | A job's agent session as its latest **checkpoint** keeps it, read as turns (issue #1169): each **turn** a prompt and the agent's reply after it — its text, reasoning, tool calls with their results, plan and subagents. Operators read it on the **job view** (Open transcript), a page of turns at a time, through `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/transcript`; Access decides as for the job's terminal. While the job is at work it follows the job: each read asks for it fresh, so the hopper takes a checkpoint first, at most once a minute (issue #1169 part 7). `src/domain/transcript.ts`, `ui/src/views/job-transcript.tsx`. | conversation, chat log, session log |
| **Resume elsewhere** | Pick up a **parked** job with a **checkpoint** that holds its agent session on any machine, not pinned to its own (issue #921; Parked's Resume elsewhere, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/requeue` `{elsewhere: true}`, `job.parked.elsewhere`). Its claim makes a new job worktree on the machine it gets, restores the checkpoint there (the base checked out on its branch, the patch applied as uncommitted changes, the session put where the agent finds it), resumes the session and tells the agent it moved. Its question must be answered first. | move, migrate, relocate, fresh start (that is a new session) |
| **Job source** | Where the hopper pulls jobs from: the `job-source` role's 0..n instances (the plugins config's `jobSources`). Built-in plugin `github-account` (a **connected account**'s, the one built-in instance, of the same name, issue #214): the one way the hopper reads and writes GitHub; the gh CLI source `github-gh` is removed (issue #359), the App source `github-app` too (issue #1377); the instance name keys its jobs and sync state. Nothing pushes jobs. | inbox, feed |
| **Stalled** (a job source) | In error longer than the **stall threshold** (30 minutes, `STALL_AFTER_MS`): nothing new is pulled from it. The sync loop records `source.stalled` once per run of failures, which the notifiers send (issue #358). | stuck, dead, down |
| **Source item** | One eligible thing a source offers — for GitHub, an open issue labelled `hopper` and assigned to the user's connected account (the **assignee**), whoever filed it, not addressed to another hopper (issue #387). | |
| **Assignee** | The GitHub login an issue must be assigned to for a user's source to take it: the user's connected GitHub account (issue #387). A job records it (`JobSourceRef.assignee`); jobs taken before issue #387 have none. Its comments are the only ones that reach the job's context. Replaces the author allowlist (`authors`, removed). | author allowlist, owner |
| **Unassigned** (a job) | A started job whose issue is no longer assigned to its **assignee** (issue #387): it runs on, flagged (`sourceState.sync.unassignedAt`, `job.unassigned`), and the user decides whether to stop it; assigned again, the flag goes (`job.reassigned`). A waiting job is cancelled instead, reason `unassigned`. | drifted, orphaned |
| **Routing rule** | One entry of the plugins config's `routing`, an ordered list (absent: none): a `name`, a `match` (any of `source` — the job-source instance —, `repo` with `*` globs, `label`, `author`, `title` substring; all case-insensitive; every field given must match, none given matches every item) and a `set` (any of `machine` — the job's machine pin —, `executor`, `priority` 0..100, `workTree` — the job's own work tree, issue #324, only with `machine`: a path is one machine's, issue #361). Applied at intake, when a source item becomes a job: the **first** matching rule wins. A matching rule naming a machine or executor that is not configured is skipped with a warning, never failing intake. A change applies to new jobs, and to a waiting job that has not started on the next sync of its source (issue #375). Never a lane. Never the issue text: a machine named in the body pins nothing (issue #917). Edited in the UI (`POST /ui/api/routing`). | route, filter, lane rule |
| **Routed by** | `spec.routedBy` `{ rule, set }`: the routing rule that set a job's machine, executor, priority or work tree at intake or, while the job has not started, a later sync (issue #375), and what it set. | |
| **From config** (a job) | `fromConfig`, a `SpecFromConfig`: what a job's source and routing rules last gave its spec — executor, model, work tree, machine pin, routing rule (issue #375). Worked out at intake and again on each sync while the job has not started; a part of the spec that differs from it was changed by hand and keeps the hand value. A change records **JobRespecified**. | derived spec, defaults |
| **Source key** | The id of a source item (the issue URL). Many jobs may share one (see re-run); the newest is the key's job. | |
| **Re-run** | A new job for a source key whose newest job ended (finished, failed, cancelled or rejected) and whose end the source already reported — offered again because a human cleared the marker (`hopper:done` on an open issue — reopened, when a merge closed it; an unmerged pull request leaves it open — `hopper:failed`, or `hopper:rejected`), or — for a failed, finished or rejected job (issue #387) — because the user asked for it with **Run again** (`POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/rerun`, issues #313, #354): its source gives the item back (`JobSource.rerun`: on GitHub it reopens a closed issue and clears the marker) and the new job is queued. A person's Run again is answered first (issue #1194, the **give-back**), and the new job follows. The ended job is kept; the new job's `rerunOf` names it. A source may refuse to give the item back when its new job would not run (`RerunRefused`, issue #527): `job.give_back_failed` says why. | retry, resubmit, reprocess |
| **Issue graph** | The local graph of the issues a GitHub source sees (issue #437): each issue with its repository, that repository's **owner** (a GitHub organization or user), the Projects it is an item of, and the issues and pull requests linked to it (`owned_by`, `in_repo`, `in_project`, `closes`, `references`, `sub_issue_of`). Kept in the user's store; read by the job graph and by search (issue #945). An issue's **place** is what one read of it on GitHub says of these. A GitHub source's options `issueGraph` (on or off) and `graphRefreshMinutes` (how old a read may get before the issue is read again). | issue map, link graph, knowledge graph |
| **Item snapshot** | The text an item's first job ran with (issue #662): its title, body and the assignee comments the job saw — the **item text** — and its SHA-256 hash, kept per item key. Every later job of the item runs it; only the owner's **Accept the new text** replaces it. | approved spec, baseline, frozen issue |
| **Text change** | A job held because its item's live title or body differs from its **item snapshot**'s — new comments alone are none (issue #747) (`Job.textChange`, issue #662): the snapshot's text, the live text, who edited it, and the job's text with the new text. It ends by **Rerun the original**, **Accept the new text**, or Cancel. | drift, edit hold, diff |
| **Rerun the original** | A person's choice on a **text change** (issue #662): the job runs the snapshot's text, and that live text holds it no more (`Job.originalKept`). | keep, ignore the edit |
| **Accept the new text** | The owner's choice on a **text change** (issue #662), allowed by Access (`item#can_accept_text`): the live text is the item's new snapshot, and the job runs it. An agent cannot. | approve the edit, take the new spec |
| **Locked entry** | A failed job that stays in the queue (issue #355): a job of a source whose failure is still its **work item**'s — the item `failed` on it, or handed to a person on it, as the **work-item ledger** has it (issue #1067): not run again, not dismissed, its issue not closed (`isLocked`, `/api/queue` `locked`, highest priority first). Never run by itself, holds no lane and no budget; it leaves the queue when it is run again (a re-run, its new job's `rerunOf` names it) or **dismissed** (`POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/dismiss`, `job.dismissed`: it stays failed, and can still be run again; its **hand-off** stays open, or opens if nothing else ends it — issue #516). Its issue keeps `hopper:failed` while locked. A failed job nothing waits on any more is **dismissed by itself** (issue #1123): a newer job of its item exists (`job.dismissed` `by` `superseded`), or its item is closed at its source, done or dropped (`item_closed`); only a person's dismissal (`by` `user`) opens a hand-off. | parked job, dead letter, retry queue |
| **Claim** (of an issue) | Labelling it `hopper:claimed` when the hopper takes it (no comment), with `hopper:held-by:<id>` naming its **claim holder** (issue #440). Distinct from a lane claim. A **stale claim** is the user's own with no job here: released and taken again. | |
| **Claim holder** | Who holds an issue's claim (issue #440): one user of one hopper, by a random id made on first use and kept in the user's settings (`claimHolder`), written as the label `hopper:held-by:<id>`. Names no person or machine. A claim with another holder is respected; the user may release it from Sources. | owner, claimant, install id |
| **Intake outcome** | What a job source's sync did with one open item in its scope (issue #440): taken (with its job), or the one reason it was not (not assigned to you, on the backburner, claimed by another hopper, …), with its **not-taken group** and the action the user can take (`assign`, `release`, `approve` on a request card, `unbackburner` on the backburner). A taken one carries its job and that job's status now (`jobStatus`, issue #798). `JobSource.intake()`, `SourceStatus.detail.intake`, shown in Intake. | skip reason, filter result |
| **Arrival** (intake) | When the hopper first listed an item in a job source's intake (issue #2228): `IntakeOutcome.arrivedAt`, ISO 8601 in UTC, on each `detail.intake[]` entry of `GET /api/sources`. Kept per source and item in the user's store (`intake_arrivals`); a row is never changed or removed, so no sync and no restart resets it. Intake shows it on each row as an age ("arrived 3h ago"), with the time in the viewer's time zone on hover. | created at (GitHub's own time for the issue), first seen, age |
| **Not-taken group** | Why an intake outcome was not taken, as one of seven groups (issue #798, `IntakeOutcome.group`, `NOT_TAKEN_GROUPS`): `request` (a request card waiting for approval), `not-assigned`, `backburner`, `pr-waiting` (its pull request waits for review), `partly-done`, `declined` (a request card a person declined, issue #1035), `other`. The UI groups and counts the outcomes by it, in that order. | category, bucket, status |
| **Intake** (view) | The UI view of the open labelled issues a source listed that wait on an intake decision (issue #897; it was **Not taken**, issue #798, and the intake list in Sources), per source, grouped by not-taken group with a count per group: Approve and Decline (issue #1035) on a request card, Take off the backburner on a backburner issue, Assign to me (one, or all), Release claim. An issue that passed intake (taken, partly done, its pull request waits for review) is on Jobs, and a declined request is no row: one line counts each (issue #1035). The one home of the request cards: Sources links to it with its counts. Its nav badge counts the request cards and the labelled issues not assigned to the user (issue #1614), as Needs you's Intake row does; `#not-taken` opens it. Not **Settings → Intake**, where an admin names the trusted actors. | Not taken, inbox, backlog, approvals |
| **Tagged item** | An open issue or pull request of a **job repository** that waits on the user on GitHub (issue #1141): a review is requested from the user's connected account (`review`), it @mentions the account (`mention`), or it is assigned to the account (`assign`) — its **why**, one or more. An item with the source's label is intake's, and a pull request the user wrote is the user's own: neither is tagged. Read by a connected account's GitHub source each intake sync interval, from GitHub's issue search; each new review request records `source.review_requested`. It leaves by itself once it is handled on GitHub. `src/domain/tagged.ts`. | mention, notification, inbox item, review request (GitHub's word for one reason) |
| **Tagged** (view) | The UI view of the **tagged items** (`#tagged`, issue #1141), per source: repository and number, title, author, why, age, checks, a link, **Dismiss**, and the **tagged starts** (issue #2342). A Needs you row and the nav badge count them, but not one a job was started on. | Mentions, Inbox, Notifications |
| **Tagged start** | A person's one click on a **tagged item** that starts a job on it (issue #2342, `src/domain/tagged-jobs.ts`): **Research** (`research`, a research job), **Propose** (`propose`, a proposal) or **Start work** (`work`): the item's **work**, `review` for a pull request (a draft review in the job's result) or `fix` for an issue (a pull request that closes it); **Fix its branch** is `work` `fix` on a pull request (a push to its own branch). It submits a **direct job** made from the item, with no request card: the click is the approval. Kept on the item while it is tagged (`started`), so the item is started once. | Dive in, quick job, one-click job |
| **Dismiss** (tagged item) | A person takes a **tagged item** off Tagged (`dismiss`, an intake action; issue #1141), for the whys it has then: it shows again only when it is tagged for a new why. Nothing is written to GitHub. Kept in the user's settings until the item is no longer tagged for those whys. | hide, mute, snooze, decline (a request card's) |
| **Needs you** (view) | The nav entry after the **Dashboard** (issue #897): one row per kind of decision that waits on a person — Intake, Access requests, Infra plans, Questions, Proposals, Research, Logins, Accounts (the machines whose agent tool is signed in as nobody, each named, issue #1128), Machines (the machines **not ready**, each named with why, issue #1248), Failures, Pull requests (the ones the hopper gave up on, each with why, issue #927), Checks (the **checks read problems**, each with the fix, issue #1244), Tagged (issue #1141), the pre-sort — each with its count and a link to its one home, where the person acts. Its badge is the sum, marked when a high-priority job waits (#535). Parked jobs have no row: they wait to be picked up, not for a decision. Always rendered, every row with its count, 0 too. | inbox, attention, to-do, dashboard |
| **Dashboard** | The first nav entry, and the view the UI opens on (`#dashboard`, issue #951): the whole system at a glance — the numbers (running, lanes, waiting, ended, failed), the lane timeline, attention, lanes, waiting, ended, usage and live activity, as the **dashboard layout** places its **dashboard panels**. Pinned above the nav groups, in no group: it covers more than jobs. It was the Overview; `#overview` still opens it. Not the **Jobs view**, which tracks each job. | overview, home, summary |
| **Access requests** (view) | The one home of what jobs on boxes wait on a person for (issue #897): **credential requests** (Asked for), **vault grant** asks, **run cards** (Sandbox runs, issues #991, #2197), and the approvals of templates a person saved (Template approvals), each a panel that always renders. Vault keeps the configuration and links a waiting template here. Its nav badge counts the credential requests, the grant asks, the run cards and a person's own templates that wait, a run card only while its job has not ended (issue #2196), and marks a run card of a high-priority job. **Approve selected** (issue #1018) approves every run and template card an admin selected in one action, each approval sent at once and shown on its own card. Not **Access**, the OpenFGA decision. A tab of **Approvals** (`#approvals/access-requests`, issue #2374). | pending, vault requests |
| **Trusted actor** | A GitHub account whose acts start the user's jobs (issue #652): one the user picked in Settings → Intake, kept by its GitHub user id so a rename keeps it (issue #930), else the user's connected login. Access decides each (OpenFGA `intake#can_start_jobs`, the list told at the check by the login each account has now). An app, a bot or an organization never is one. | allowed actor, whitelisted user |
| **Actor candidate** | An account the trusted actor picker offers (issue #930): the connected account, a job repository's collaborator, its organization's member, an author of its newest issues, or a hopper user or API token holder by their connected GitHub; or one looked up by login. Only an account GitHub shows can be picked. | suggestion, typed login |
| **Trusted origin** | An issue a trusted actor wrote, labelled with the source label and assigned to the user, not through an app or as a bot, whose title and body only trusted actors edited (issue #655 part 17), and no job filed (issue #652). Only such an issue, or an approved request card, becomes a job. | trusted issue, safe issue |
| **Request card** | An open labelled issue assigned to the user that is not of a trusted origin (issue #652): its intake outcome with `action: 'approve'` and `request` — the hash of its title and body, who wrote, labelled and assigned it, and why. Shown in Intake (its nav badge counts them; Sources links there); a new one records `source.request_opened` once per text (issue #798). **Approve** records its text as the item's snapshot (reason `approved`), and the next sync takes it. A GitLab issue of another origin is a request card too (issue #1642): the same Approve and Decline, nothing written to GitLab. **Decline** (issue #1035) records `source.request_declined` for its text's hash: no job. On GitHub (issue #1710) the person picks a **decline choice**: `close` (close the issue as not planned and take off the source's label, its priority labels and `hopper:done`) or `open` (leave it open and take off the source's label); one comment says what the hopper did. These are GitHub outbox rows; until `source.request_decline_written`, the item is in the not-taken group `declined`, and then GitHub's labels and state are the record: the issue comes back only when a person adds the label again. Settings → Intake sets the choice offered first (**decline defaults**). Approve and Decline are operator CLI commands too (issue #2220): `hopper intake list|approve|decline`, on the same route. A GitLab card, and a decline before issue #1710, write nothing and hold until an edit to its title or body. | approval request, pending issue |
| **Reopened card** | A **request card** of an issue reopened after its job was done (issue #1126): open, with `hopper:done`, and its job here ended, unless it **waits on its batch** (issue #1175). Reason `reopened after its job was done: approve it to run it again`; it names no acts and carries the item's history (`IntakeRequest.reopened`: its jobs, newest first, and its pull request). A card whatever the issue's origin. Its work item waits for approval again. **Approve** takes `hopper:done` off and records the text, and the next sync makes a new job of the item. | reopen request, rerun card |
| **Auto-approval** | The hopper's own approval of a **request card** the user's own act already asks for (issue #1268): reopened by, written by, or labelled `hopper:high` by a **trusted actor**, or written by a **listed app** — an app or bot the user's setting trusts to act for them. Recorded as `source.request_auto_approved` with its rule and actor; the item's snapshot is its text, as for Approve. A guard keeps a card waiting: a job filed it, someone else edited it, its text changed since an approval, or a person took it into Intake. On by default; Settings → Intake. A GitLab card (issue #1642) is approved by `author` or `high-label` only. |
| **Job origin** | Where a job's item came from (issue #652, `JobSourceRef.origin`): `trusted` (a trusted origin) or `approved` (an approved request card). Absent on a job of a source that judges none, or taken before. It gates the job, not its merge: yolo mode merges a job with none too (issue #805). | provenance, source trust |
| **Origin pending** | A job of a GitHub source queued before origins were judged, waiting for its job origin (issue #652, the backfill; `Job.originPending`, set by tenant migration 39). Held until a sync finds its issue of a trusted origin or a person approves its request card; then it gets that origin (`job.origin_judged`) and runs. | unjudged, legacy job, quarantined |
| **Trust level** | How far the hopper trusts a job's work by where it came from (issue #655), lowest first: `quarantined`, `untrusted`, `screened`, `approved`, `trusted`, `owner`. The rules give it (`ruleCeiling`): `owner` — a trusted origin the user wrote and was assigned; `trusted` — a trusted origin another trusted actor wrote; `approved` — a request card a person approved, a direct job, or a job source an admin configured that judges no origin; `untrusted` — a GitHub item with no job origin, or no source. A job a job made gets at most its parent's level, never `owner`. `src/domain/trust-stamp.ts`. | trust score, risk, origin (the **job origin** is one fact the level reads) |
| **Trust stamp** | A job's **trust level**, why, who stamped it (`intake`, `job`, `respecify`, `backfill`, `person`, `jev`, `edit`) and when — for a **quarantine**, the level before it (`was`) —, signed with an HMAC under the user's **stamp key** over these, the job's id and its spec (issue #655, `Job.stamp`). The job store stamps each job it makes, and each stamp from where the job came is an `intake.stamped` event, with no MAC (part 11); a raw change of the row, a raised level or a changed spec does not verify. `src/domain/trust-stamp.ts`, `src/store/jobs.ts`. | signature, badge, label |
| **Origin acts** | Who did each act a job's **job origin** was judged from (issue #655 part 14, `JobSourceRef.originActs`): its item's author, who added the label, who assigned it and who edited its title or body (on GitHub since part 17, one act per editor), each with its login and user id when known, the app or bot it was done through, and whether a **trusted actor** did it. Kept as read when the origin was judged, on a job of a trusted origin or an approved **request card**; shown in the stamp view and on the job view, and given to **Jev's trust check**. No rule reads them. | provenance, timeline, audit trail |
| **Linked pull request** | A pull request a job's item's text names (issue #655 part 19, `JobSourceRef.linkedPulls`): `#N`, `owner/name#N` or its URL, read when the item's **job origin** was judged, with its author, whether its head is a fork, and whether a **trusted actor** wrote it; `unread` when the read failed. Kept with the repository's visibility on a job of a trusted origin or an approved **request card**; shown in the stamp view and on the job view, and given to **Jev's trust check**. No rule reads it; a job's **pr edit** reads the same names, from its item's approved text (issues #2151, #2227). | referenced PR, linked PR, provenance |
| **Minimum trust** | The lowest **trust level** a job needs to do an operation on an asset (issue #655 part 15): an admin sets one per operation profile in Users & access → Access. No live job below it is that profile's requester in OpenFGA, whatever its template's approvals say; a box and a person are no job, so it does not apply to them. Each live job holds its level and each level below it (`trust:<level>#holder`). A need of a profile above the job's level is **outside the stamp** (part 16): the skill broker refuses it, and declines a sandbox request of it by `the minimum trust`, with no Jev check and no lower trust. `src/authz/trust.ts`, `src/domain/stamp-capabilities.ts`. | trust floor, min level, required trust |
| **Stamp key** | The key a user's **trust stamps** are signed with: HKDF of the hopper's **link key** for that user, label `hopper-job-stamp/1` (issue #655), as a **proxy token**'s key is. Never stored, never sent. | signing secret |
| **Quarantine** | A person's act on a job that has not ended (issue #655): its **trust stamp** is signed `quarantined`, its level before kept as `was`. The decider holds it, and its **proxy token** opens no door of the hopper (the job's doors refuse it, as a token that is no job's). A running job keeps its pane but reaches nothing through the hopper; its steps stop, and its item's **vault grant** is revoked (part 6). **Lift quarantine** signs the level it had again, never higher — a box action; the grant stays revoked. `job.quarantined`, `job.unquarantined`. | ban, block, suspend, freeze |
| **Deny** (at gate 2) | A person's no to a job's privileged request (issue #655 part 5): a declined **sandbox request**. It lowers the job's **trust stamp** one level (`deny:sandbox`, `by: person`, `job.trust_lowered`); the second deny in the job, or a deny of a job at `untrusted`, **quarantines** it (`job.quarantined` with the reason). Below `approved`, a person approves its trust. The denies stay on the stamp. `deniedStamp`, `src/domain/trust-stamp.ts`. | refusal, rejection, block |
| **Edit after the start** | An edit of a started job's item — its title or body changed since the **item snapshot** — that a sync sees (issue #655 part 7): the job goes on with the text it started with, and its **trust stamp** drops to `untrusted` (`edit:text`, `by: edit`, `job.trust_lowered`), once for each text (`Job.editSeen`). A person approves its trust. `editedStamp`, `refreshEdit`. | rewrite, tamper |
| **Automatic approval** (at gate 2) | An approval of a job's privileged request that no person gives: skip sandbox approval, the auto-approve policy, a remembered write approval, a person's approval of the template, the **standing read grant** (issue #655 part 7). | **Dispatch minimum** | The lowest **trust level** a job starts at when no **intake strictness** says otherwise: `approved` (issue #655), the level of `standard`. A job with no **trust stamp**, one that does not verify, or one below the level its brake needs is held (`stampHeld`). | threshold |
| **Stamp view** | What the hopper says of one job's **trust stamp** (issue #655 part 10): the stamp with no MAC, whether it verifies (`yes`, `no`, `missing`, `unknown` with no stamp key), the level as verified, the **intake strictness** on the job and why the stamp holds its start, whether gate 2 approves its requests with no person (`gate2`: `auto` or `person`), whether its proxy token opens the hopper's doors, and whether Jev's trust check is due. Read only: `GET /api/jobs/:id/stamp`, `hopper job stamp <id>`. `src/domain/stamp-view.ts`. | stamp status, trust report |
| **Intake strictness** | Brake 1 of issue #655: the **trust level** a job's stamp needs before the job starts — `open` (`screened`), `standard` (`approved`, the default), `strict` (`trusted`), `closed` (`owner`) —, set by default, per job source and per job repository; the repository wins over the source, the source over the default (`brakeOf`). An admin's, in Settings → Intake (`/api/intake-strictness`, `intake.strictness_changed`); read at each Decision, so a change applies at the next one, to waiting jobs; and at each ask of gate 2 (part 12), so a started job below it gets a person's approval for each privileged request. `src/domain/intake-strictness.ts`. | threshold, trust policy, gate (the **blast-radius gate** is another thing) |
| **Jev's kind check** | The decider call `intake-kind` on a waiting work job from a job source before its first start (issue #655, `src/domain/kind-check.ts`): Jev reads its text, marked untrusted, and picks `keep` (the work), `research` or `proposal`. It can only ask for research or a proposal, which change nothing, never for more. Active, the job waits for it; research or a proposal is asked as a person's ask is (`research.asked`, `proposal.asked`, `by: jev`); no sure pick keeps the work. Kept on the job (`kindCheck`). | intake kind, job kind pick |
| **Jev's sandbox check** | The decider call `sandbox-request` at gate 2 (issue #655 part 13, `src/domain/sandbox-check.ts`): for a **sandbox run** the sandbox approvals would approve with no person, under **sandbox strictness** `standard`, of a template not of low blast radius. Jev reads the request, its template and the job, the job's words marked untrusted, and picks `allow`, `hold:<reason>` or `deny:<reason>`; it never sees a run the approvals would not approve, so it approves nothing they would not. Active, the request waits for it: allow approves it by `Jev`, hold leaves it for a person, deny declines it and lowers the job's trust as a person's deny does (`deny:jev`); no sure pick holds it (fail closed). In shadow (the default) the pick is only recorded. | sandbox screening, request review |
| **Jev's trust check** | The decider call `intake-trust` on a waiting job's **trust stamp** (issue #655 part 4, `src/domain/trust-check.ts`): due for a job at `approved` or `trusted` that no person stamped. Jev reads its text, marked untrusted, and where it came from, and keeps the level or picks a lower one with a reason — never `quarantined`, never higher. Active, the job waits for it, and a check with no sure pick lowers it to `untrusted` (fail closed); a person then approves its trust (`approved`, never more). Its pick is on the stamp (`check`). | trust analysis, screening |
| **Intake migration** | The one-time move of a source's existing items to the current intake rules (issue #440), on its first sync that lists every repo: claims with no holder and no job in any user of this hopper are released; labelled issues not assigned to the user are listed. Recorded per source (`intakeMigration`, `source.intake_migrated`); a second run does nothing. | |
| **Idle reason** | Why a machine leaves lanes unused after a Decision (issue #440): the lane plan's `idle` — lane count 0, offline, home not known, disk low, usage pacing, reserved lanes, waiting jobs that cannot run there, every waiting job held, or no job waiting. | |
| **Sync** | One pass of a source: discover, check active jobs, retry reports. | poll |
| **Setup problem** | Gone (issue #1377): only the App source had one. A source the plugin host cannot build shows with its error (state `error`); a plugin's detection still says `needs-setup`. | |
| **Duplicate job source** | A job source that takes the same work as one configured before it (issue #1264): for GitHub, both on, the same label and hopper name, through the same connected account. Its plugin says what the same work is (`PluginDefinition.sameWork`). Adding one, or turning one on, is refused, naming the first (409); one configured already shows as `SourceStatus.duplicateOf`, in a banner on Sources with **Remove**. `src/plugins/duplicates.ts`. | copy, clone, twin |
| **Poll backoff** | A configured job source's timed syncs coming further apart while they find nothing changed (issue #1264): each such sync doubles the interval to the next, up to 8 times its **poll interval** (`pollSeconds`). A change — its discover answered otherwise, a job made, a job of its own not ended, an error, a pull request merged or closed — or a sync a person or a GitHub delivery asked for brings the poll interval back. A 304 to a conditional request is no change: the rate limiter answers it with the answer it kept. `SourceStatus.pollMs` and `nextPollMs`. `src/sources/poll-backoff.ts`. | throttle, slow mode |
| **Call log** | The calls to its service each job source's **sync** passes made (issue #1264): a row per source and minute in the database (`source_calls`), kept a day. Sources shows each source's calls in the last hour and day from it (`SourceStatus.calls`). Calls made outside a pass (a job's own requests, git fetches) are not in it. `src/store/source-calls.ts`. | traffic log, call history |
| **In-motion cycle** | How the sync loop follows the pull requests its jobs follow (issue #985, `src/sources/follow-rounds.ts`): in rounds, not at each **Sync**. Every `prCycleMinutes` (default 3) a round follows only the pull requests **in motion**; every **intake sync interval** a round follows every followed pull request and reconciles; between rounds a sync follows none. The first sync, a sync a person asked for (Sync now) and the sync at once after a merge or close follow every one. Each round reads its pull requests and their issues in one batched read first (`JobSource.readFollowed`, `pullRequestStates`), and each follow reads GitHub only for what changed, or before the hopper's merge. Replaces the sync watch (issue #967). | sync watch, event poll, webhook |
| **In motion** | A followed pull request the **in-motion cycle** reads every cycle (issue #985, `inMotion`): not seen yet, its checks still running, no checks within the grace window after its last push, or ready while **Yolo mode** merges it. Every other followed pull request is idle — checks finished and not ready, a draft, merge conflicts or failing checks (tended from what was seen) — and is read only on the reconcile round. A merged or closed one is never read. | active, hot |
| **Completion** | Where a job's work must get before the job is done (issues #187, #579, #637), decided from what its source shows, not from what the job did: its issue **closed as complete**; a pull request of its repo that closes or references the issue, open and ready for review — not a draft, no merge conflicts and no failing checks (issue #736) — or merged since the job began, whoever opened it, unless it only mentions the issue and closes or ships part of another issue (issue #786: that issue's work); an open one on a branch named for the issue (`issue-N-…`, `N-…`), ready for review; or, for an issue that asks to update the pull requests it names, each of them merged or free of merge conflicts; or, for an **artifact-only issue** (issue #673), an artifact of the job linked to the issue and named by a comment on it. One rule for every issue: a merge is never needed (a pull request is done; a merge is only allowed, by **Yolo mode**). The job's prompt states it (the `done` line). A job that ends done is recorded *finished* only when its work is **complete** — the job's source says so (`JobSource.notComplete`), asked again once after `HOPPER_DONE_RECHECK_MS` for GitHub's lag; otherwise it ends *failed* with the reason, naming what was looked at. A source that cannot be read is read again after `HOPPER_DONE_READ_RETRY_MS`, then after twice as long (issue #2235); when it still cannot be read, the job ends *failed* with the read error, and the transient cause **Done check unread** runs it again, not a person. A failed job whose work is complete is never run again: it ends finished. A source that does not judge completion takes every done job as complete. Not *Ended*: completion is the target, *finished* the status. | definition of done, done mode, stop point, done-check |
| **Closing reference** | A closing keyword — `close`, `closes`, `closed`, `fix`, `fixes`, `fixed`, `resolve`, `resolves`, `resolved`, any case, an optional colon — then one issue: `#N`, `owner/repo#N` or the issue's URL (issue #430, `closingReferences`, `src/domain/cross-references.ts`). Each issue needs its own keyword. Code and HTML comments do not count. A text proposes it; only GitHub decides that it closes the issue. | closing link, fix reference |
| **Cross-reference** | A pull request or a commit, of the job's repository or another, that GitHub confirms closes the job's issue (issue #430): a pull request it links as closing the issue, in any state, or a commit that closed it. Kept on the job while it follows its pull request (`sourceState.source.crossReferences`: kind, repository, URL, and the closing keyword its text used — absent when GitHub links it without one, by hand). A closing reference GitHub does not confirm, a mention or a part is no cross-reference. | link (a client target's, a skill's), linked item |
| **Change kind** | What kind of change a followed pull request is (issue #1076, `src/domain/change-kinds.ts`): `docs` (docs or comments only), `tests`, `low_risk`, `code` or `sensitive`, read from the files and lines it changes by its repository's **change rule**; the most severe file decides. It names the pull request's **merge gate**. Recorded as `job.pull_request_classified`, shown on its **Pull requests list** card. | change class, risk class, change type |
| **Change rule** | The globs and content expressions that give a file its **change kind**, and each kind's **merge gate** (issue #1076): a built-in rule, an admin's `default` over it, and one per repository, saved with **Yolo mode** (`changeKinds`). | approval rule, path rule |
| **Merge gate** | What a **change kind** needs before the hopper merges its ready pull request (issue #1076): `ready`, no person, yolo mode on or off; `yolo`, only with **Yolo mode** on; `person`, only a person's **Merge now**. | approval gate, merge policy |
| **Yolo mode** | Whether the hopper merges a **PR waiting** job's ready pull request itself (issue #637: not a draft, no merge conflicts, its checks passed — or it has none, and none started within the grace window after its last push, issue #677; its merge of a pull request with no closing keyword that is no part closes the issue, with one comment that links the pull request); a job never merges (issue #652: it holds no token; issue #579 let it): off unless an admin turns it on, for every job repository or per repository (`owner/repo`), which wins; on a repository the hopper does not work on (issue #1806), only a person naming it on turns it on, and no **change kind** merges there with no person until then; a setting of the user's, read each time a job's prompt is built and at each follow. It adds the merge and never changes what done is: a merge that waits or is refused never fails the job. It never merges while the job's **done criteria** are not met, or one says a person merges (issue #1924 part 3). It is the `yolo` **merge gate**: a **change kind** whose gate is `ready` merges with it off, one whose gate is `person` never by it (issue #1076). Not **Yolo**, the herdr-claude executor's every-permission choice. | auto-merge, self-merge, merge mode |
| **Contribution** | Whether a job of a repository its user cannot push to may work through the **job's fork** (issue #429, Settings → Contribution, `GET /api/contribution`, `POST /ui/api/contribution`, admin): `hopper-gh repo fork` forks the repository into the user's account, the job follows the repository's contributing rules, pushes its branch to the fork and opens its pull request on the repository from the fork (`--head <login>:<branch>`); when the repository takes no issues, it files its issue on the fork. Off unless an admin turns it on: all of it goes out under the user's account. A setting of the user's, read at each request a job makes. Off, a push to a repository the user cannot push to is refused, saying so. | contribution mode, fork mode, outside contribution |
| **Job's fork** | GitHub's fork of a job's own repository in its user's account, as GitHub says (`fork`, its `parent` or `source` the job's repository; issue #429): with **Contribution** on, the job pushes to it as to its own repository — the same ref check, never its default or a release branch — and files, reads and comments on issues there; its pull request goes on the job's repository, never on the fork. Not a job repository. Not a **Fork** (phase shift), which is a job. | fork (a phase shift's job), copy, mirror |
| **Updated pull request** | A pull request the job did not open but pushed to (issue #618): its head commit made at or after the job's `createdAt`. A job whose issue asks to update an existing pull request — one the issue names, or an older one that closes the issue — is done when that pull request has no merge conflicts, or was merged after the job began, with no new pull request of its own. | maintained PR, refreshed PR |
| **Partly done** | A job that shipped part of its item (issue #579): its own pull request says `Part of #N` and lists what is left, open and ready for review or merged. The job ends *finished* with `partlyDone` (that pull request's URL), never *failed*; on GitHub its issue gets `hopper:partly-done`, and the next part runs once that pull request is merged. | half done, partial, slice |
| **Declared parts** | The parts an issue says it has (issue #729): the checklist under its heading `Parts`, and its GitHub sub-issues that no item names (with no checklist, only these). Done: a ticked box, or a merged `Part of #N` pull request each, the larger count (`Parts`, `done` of `total`); a **required child** — a part that starts with an issue (`#N`, `owner/repo#N`, its URL) — and a sub-issue only when that issue is closed. The prompt and the job card say `x/y`; the merge of the part that makes them all done marks the issue done. A checklist anywhere else is not parts. | sub-tasks, steps, checklist (any checklist), milestones |
| **Decline choice** | What a person's Decline of a GitHub request card does to the issue (issue #1710, `DeclineChoice`): `close` — **Close the issue**: closed as not planned, without the source's label, its priority labels and `hopper:done` — or `open` — **Leave it open**: open, without the source's label. Either way one comment says what the hopper did. The confirm step says the effect of each before the click. | decline mode, decline action |
| **Decline defaults** | The decline choice the confirm step offers first, and what an API decline with no choice does (issue #1710, `IntakeDeclineDefaults`): one for a request card (`open` by default), one for a reopened card (`close` by default). An admin sets them in Settings → Intake. | decline setting |
| **Delivery path** | How a job's change reaches people (issue #1458): it lands in the job's own repository through its pull request; a change another repository needs goes there as a **required child** a job of that repository delivers; it is **delivered** when merged on that repository's landing branch with its checks passed there and through the next stages the repository names; production waits for a person's approval. Every job is told it (`DELIVERY_LINE`). | delivery route, release path, rollout |
| **Routed child** | A required child a job filed on one of its user's job repositories (issue #1510): the hopper labels it with the job sources' label and assigns it to the job's user, so Intake lists it as a request card — a job filed it, so a person approves it. The job is answered `routed` and the `on:` line `issue OWNER/NAME#N`, and waits on it as an item. A follow-up is never routed. | handed-off issue, delegated issue, cross-repo job |
| **Outside reason** | Why a person acts on an outside wait (issue #1510, `OutsideWhy`): `not managed` (the repository is not one the hopper works on) or `gated` (an infrastructure apply, a gitops apply, a production approval), from `on: outside: not managed: <what>` or `on: outside: gated: <what>`. An outside wait with neither is shown `reason not given`. | blocker type, wait reason |
| **Issue lineage** | The parent ↔ child links of issues (issue #729): an issue a job files through the hopper is a **child** of the job's own issue, its **parent**. Recorded both ways — the child's text names its parent (GitHub links the mention back), and `job.child_issue_filed` on the job's timeline — and shown on the job card (`child of #N`, `children n`, descendants on hover). A **follow-up** by default; a **required child** when the job marks it `--required`: then also a GitHub sub-issue of the parent, a part it waits on. A sub-issue link GitHub refused is kept and tried again until it is made (`job.sub_issue_linked`, issue #1607). | spawned issue, sub-task, related issue |
| **Merge re-check** | The one check of a finished job's merge recorded as the whole item before issue #721 (`rechecksMerge`): whether that merge closed the item. Closed: `mergeChecked`. Not: it was a part — `hopper:done` comes off and the item is offered again. Only the newest job of an item is checked. | backfill, re-scan |
| **Finish brief** | The fixed text a job is told when it ended done but left its own pull request with merge conflicts, failing checks or as a draft (issues #626, #736): rebase it onto its base branch (`rebase`), fix its checks (`fix_checks`), or mark it ready for review (`mark_ready`). The job goes on in its own session with it, in place of a failure and a hand-off (`job.finish_briefed`); at most three per run. No model writes it. | nudge, reminder, follow-up |
| **Rework** | Sending a done job's open pull request back with notes (issue #724, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/rework`, `hopper job rework`, Send back for rework on a Pull requests card): the pull request becomes a draft and stays open on its branch, the ended job stops following it (`follow: reworked`), and a new job of its item (`rework` on the job: the pull request, the notes, who) reworks it, told the **rework brief**. That job is done only when it pushed to the pull request after it began and marked it ready. `job.rework_requested`. | reject, send back, bounce, request changes |
| **Rework brief** | The fixed text, with the person's notes quoted, a job that reworks a pull request is told after its item's prompt (issue #724, `reworkBrief`): the pull request, the notes, push to its branch, mark it ready, open no new one. | review comments, feedback |
| **Note** (to a job) | What a person, or an agent over the API, sends a job that may still run (issue #997, `POST /ui/api/jobs/:id/notes`, **Add note** on the job view): its text, who sent it and how, and its state — **queued** until the job's session gets it, **delivered** (at a **turn boundary**, in its task as it starts, or after the answer it resumes with), or **carried** to a new job of its item. Kept on the job (`notes`). `job.note_added`, `job.note_delivered`, `job.note_carried`. A note ends no wait and lifts no hold. | message, comment, hint, instruction |
| **Turn boundary** | Where the hopper may type into a running herdr-claude job (issue #997): Claude ready and its turn over — not a lost send, not a question, a proposal, a research report or a login it ended on, never over a dialog —, or Claude sitting ready while the watch waits for it to work by itself. The **notes** go in there as the next turn. | idle point, pause |
| **Tending** | The hopper's care of a **PR waiting** job's pull request that got merge conflicts with its base branch, or failing checks, after its job ended (issue #736): its item runs again (`job.rerun`, `by: "tending"`) with the **tend brief** — the fixed text that names the pull request and the step (`rebase`, `fix_checks`), and for `fix_checks` the checks that failed, with what each reported (its failure annotations) and, for a GitHub Actions job, its **check log**'s excerpt or why the hopper could not read it (issue #993), and the one **rerun** of a flaky check the first fix of a row may make, by an empty commit (issue #927) —, and the new job brings the same pull request back to ready or fails saying why. The tended job follows it no more (`follow: tended`, `job.pull_request_tended`). A rebase is never capped (issue #786); at most three `fix_checks` in a row of one pull request. A failed job's pull request is tended too, but a failed tend job's waits on a person: the hopper gave up, with that job's reason (issue #927). A tend job cancelled by no person and no source — the sweep, a restart — is tended again after a wait that doubles, at most three times in a row (issue #1010). A failed rebase tend is tended again once the hopper's mirror sees its base branch at another commit than when it first saw the failure (`tended.baseAt`, `baseMoved`), at most three times in a row (issue #1143 part 3). After the hopper gave up, a person's **Tend again** tends the same step at once (issue #1010). A rebase resolves small conflicts itself and fails only on a design-level one, stating the choice. After a tend job, a closed work item is tended no more (issue #2127). Each tend shows on its pull request's node of the **job graph** (`tends`). Never a draft. | babysitting, auto-rebase, PR maintenance |
| **After done** | Following a finished job's pull request after its end was reported (issue #579, `JobSource.follow`): merged, its item is done (`job.pull_request_merged`, `hopper:done`; a part's next part runs); closed without a merge, its item is flagged (`job.pull_request_closed`, `hopper:pr-closed`); its item closed as not planned upstream, **Abandoned**; still open, what is seen of it is kept (`seen`, issue #637) and, with **Yolo mode** on, the hopper merges it once it is ready (`byHopper`). | tracking, watching |
| **Abandoned** | The end of **After done** when a finished job's item is closed as not planned upstream while its pull request waits, or before one is found (issue #428): followed no more, never merged, the pull request left open; no end label. Recorded on `job.pull_request_abandoned`. Not a **Close PR**: nobody closed the pull request. | not planned, dropped |
| **PR waiting** | A finished job — or a failed one, reconciled (issue #786) — whose pull request is open and followed (issue #637): done, not failed, not forgotten. It uses no lane and is no failure; it shows in the **Pull requests list** until its pull request merges (then it drops off) or closes without a merge (then it waits on a person, `hopper:pr-closed`). Not a job status: a finished job with `follow: open` in its source state. A done job that follows none becomes one through **Reconciling**. | awaiting merge, pending PR |
| **Tend job** | The job **tending** makes (issue #967, `spec.tend`): its item run again by `tending` to rebase its pull request or fix its checks. It may **elbow in** as a **specialty job** does, and shares the machine's `elbowIns` with them, so a pull request's conflict never waits behind feature work for a lane. Never at a usage hard limit. A person's Run again or Rework makes no tend job. | rework lane, reserved lane, tending lane |
| **Tending queue** | The tends of one job repository, in order (issue #796, `src/domain/tending-queue.ts`), one queue per set of pull requests that conflict (issue #939): a pull request that needs tending is not tended while a **head** that conflicts with it holds it — a tend job at work on a pull request, or a pull request on its way to the hopper's merge (yolo mode on, no conflicts, its checks passed or none, or running within 30 minutes of its last push; issue #927: nothing holds it for good) — and two that do not conflict with each other are tended at the same time. Whether two conflict is the **hopper's mirror**'s answer; where it cannot say, they conflict, so one tend at a time. What holds one is kept on its job (`heldBehind`), and since when (`heldSince`): held an hour, it is tended anyway (issue #1143). A tend job holds it only while it works on the pull request, or finished with its end still to report: a merged pull request holds nothing (issue #1162). The order: Jev's pick first (`merge-order`), then the rules' (highest priority, then the oldest pull request). One rebase per merge. | merge queue (GitHub's own), merge train, rebase queue |
| **Additions file** | A file a repository marks `hopper-merge=additions` in its base branch's `.gitattributes` (issue #967): the **base merge** resolves a conflict in it where both sides only added lines, as in a Markdown file, the base branch's lines first. The base branch decides: a pull request cannot mark its own. The hopper's own migration lists are additions files (`src/store/migrations-recent.ts`, `src/store/tenant-migrations-recent.ts`), so two pull requests that each add a migration merge with no job, and the one merged second takes the next number. | union merge, `merge=union` (git's own driver, which also keeps both sides of a changed line) |
| **Base merge** | The hopper's own merge of a pull request's base branch into it, in the **hopper's mirror**, pushed to the pull request's branch with no job and no lane (issue #967, `baseMerge`, `job.pull_request_base_merged`): asked before a `rebase` **tending** job. It resolves only a conflict in a Markdown file, or an **additions file**, where both sides only added lines (both kept, the base branch's first); any other conflict goes to the tend job. The push leases the branch at the head as fetched. | auto-rebase, update branch (GitHub's own button), auto-merge |
| **Shrink guard** | The git proxy's check of a job's push that brings in its base branch — a rebase, or a merge of the base, as a **tend job**'s conflict resolution makes (issue #1096, `src/domain/shrink-guard.ts`, `src/git-mirror/push-lines.ts`): before GitHub gets it, the push is refused when it empties or removes a file, or takes more than the guard's **percent** of its lines (Settings → Shrink guard, admin; default 50), against the base branch as brought in or the branch's head before the push — the base: the branch's open pull request's base, else the landing branch, else the default branch (issue #1147) —, and the branch's own change did not empty or shrink that file so; a file the branch only shrank may still not be emptied, nor lose more than the percent of what the branch kept (issue #1218). The hopper's own **base merge** is judged the same way before it pushes, at the default percent, and a resolution it refuses goes to a tend job (issue #1218). The job sees git's refusal with the files named and redoes the resolution; `github_proxy.refused` carries them (`shrunk`). A job whose last push it refused ends failed (tending failed), not done. | size check, blanking check, file-loss guard |
| **Quarantine** (a push's) | The object directory of its own, beside the user's mirror, where the **shrink guard** indexes a job's push pack to count its lines (issue #1096); the mirror's objects are its alternate, and it is removed after, so nothing a job pushes enters the mirror. | staging area, temp repo |
| **Push requirement** | A rule a repository keeps for a job's push (issue #2555, design.md "Push requirements", `src/domain/push-requirements.ts`): an `id`, a **requirement condition** (`when`; none: it always applies), the **checks** to run and their pass criteria. A check is a shell command (`run`), run in the job's work tree, that passes when it exits 0 and each of its **thresholds** (`min`, `max`) holds on what it **measured** (a line `hopper-measure <name>=<number>` of its output). Its **level**: `default` (the hopper's), `repo` (the **requirements file**), `path` (a folder's requirements file), `job` (the **job's push requirements**); one of the same id at a higher level replaces a lower one. | pre-push hook, gate, rule (a **job rule** is the job's text) |
| **Default push requirements** | The hopper's own **push requirements** (issue #2555, level `default`): a user setting an admin edits on Settings → Push requirements (`POST /ui/api/push-requirements`), in the shape of a **requirements file**, as JSON; none by default. They apply to every repository's push, below its own file: one of the same id in the repository replaces the default. | global rules, base rules |
| **Job's push requirements** | A direct job's own **push requirements** (issue #2555 part 3, level `job`, the highest): set by the person who submits it (`pushRequirements` on `POST /ui/api/direct-jobs`, `hopper job submit --push-requirements <file>`, the form's JSON field), kept on its record, so Run again keeps them. One of the same id replaces the repository's, a folder's and the default, a stricter or a looser threshold alike. A job of an issue has none. | job override, per-job rules |
| **Requirements file** | `.hopper/requirements.yaml` in a repository (issue #2555): its **push requirements**, as YAML (or JSON). The same file in a folder's `.hopper/` is that folder's: its requirements apply only to changes in the folder, and their checks run there. | config file, hopper.yaml |
| **Requirement condition** | What decides whether a **push requirement** applies to a push (issue #2555): `paths` (globs of the changed files), `fileTypes`, `repos`, `branches`, `bases`, `kinds` (kind of work), `labels`, `dependencies` (a dependency manifest changed), `workflows` (a GitHub workflow file changed), combined with `all`, `any` and `not`. It says why it holds or not, in words: the **skip reason** of a requirement that does not apply. | filter, matcher, trigger |
| **Evaluation** (push requirements) | One run of a commit's **push requirements** by its job (issue #2555, `sh "$HOPPER_REQUIREMENTS" check`): the requirements that apply run their checks on the job's machine, the hopper judges them, and records `push_requirements.evaluated` on the job's timeline — each requirement `passed`, `failed` or `skipped` with why, each check's command, thresholds and measured values, never its output. It passes when every requirement that applies passed. The git proxy takes a push of a commit that holds a requirements file, or that a **default push requirement** applies to, only after a passing evaluation of that commit. | pre-push run, verification, CI run |
| **Dry run** (sweep) | A machine whose sweep removes nothing (issue #2494, the `sweepDryRun` machine option or the **disk defaults**' dry run): each pass records what it would remove there as `machine.sweep_dry_run`, and again only once that changes. | preview, simulation |
| **Cleanup pass** | One real pass of the sweep on a machine (issue #2494): its reaps, the storage prune, the cache caps and the trim there. One with news (something removed, work newly kept, a dir it could not remove, a trim) is recorded as `machine.sweep_passed`, with what it removed by kind, the bytes freed and what it kept and why. | sweep run, cleanup run |
| **Dry run** (push requirements) | What an **evaluation** of a commit would run, answered without running it (issue #2555, `sh "$HOPPER_REQUIREMENTS" dry-run`): the push requirements that apply with their commands and thresholds, and the ones skipped with why. Nothing is recorded. | plan (an **infra plan** is a job's terraform plan), preview |
| **Push requirements summary** | The **evaluation** of a pull request's pushed commit, as a section of its body (issue #2555 part 4, `src/domain/push-requirements-summary.ts`): each push requirement's status (a skipped one's reason), each check's command, thresholds and measured values, between two hidden lines. The hopper adds it to a job's `pr create` and replaces it after each evaluated push of the branch (`push_requirements.summarized`), in a pull request the job may edit; the rest of the body stays the job's. | PR report, check summary, CI summary |
| **Merge gate** | The hopper's own merges (yolo mode) of one job repository in one sync pass, made one at a time (issue #939, `src/sources/github/merge-gate.ts`): a ready pull request that conflicts with one the hopper merged before it in the pass is not merged (`PullRequestSeen.mergeHeldBehind`, its card `merge held`); the next sync sees it conflict with the new base and tends it. Where the **hopper's mirror** cannot say, the merge is made. No batch of pull requests is checked together. | merge queue, merge train, batch |
| **Landing branch** | The branch where every job's pull request merges after the fast checks only (issue #1073, design.md "Landing batches"): a setting per repository (`landing`, `main` when left out), never read from GitHub's default branch (issue #1142); `main` for the hopper's own. Not the **tested branch**, which only a green **batch run** moves. | trunk, staging, integration |
| **Pull request housekeeping** | What the follow of a job's open pull request does by itself, with no job and no lane (issue #1143, design.md "Pull request housekeeping"): one based off the **landing branch** of a repository with landing batches — and not on another open pull request's head (a stack) — is **retargeted** to it (`job.pull_request_retargeted`); a **superseded** one is closed (`job.pull_request_closed` with `superseded`). Each act, and each **tend job** started, leaves one fixed comment on the pull request that says what the hopper does (`src/sources/github/pr-notes.ts`). A **stray pull request** gets the same, with no follow, at each round of every follow (issue #1143 part 4, `housekeepStrays`). | PR cleanup, PR janitor |
| **Stray pull request** | An open pull request a job named in its source state that no job follows, of an item no job works on: every job of the item ended (issue #1143 part 4, `strayPullRequests` in `src/domain/pull-requests.ts`). A tend whose tend job failed or was cancelled, a cancelled or rejected newer job, or a rework that ended with none leaves one. The newest job that names it holds it. It gets **pull request housekeeping** — retargeted, or closed as **superseded**, recorded with `stray` — but is never tended: what left it (a person, a cancel, a failed tend) stands. Its close ends no wait of its **work item**. Not one merged, closed or abandoned, nor one a proposal's follow-ons took. | orphan PR, abandoned PR, unfollowed PR |
| **Untracked pull request** | An open pull request of a job repository that no job names in its source state and the **work-item ledger** does not hold as an item's pull request (issue #1176, design.md "Untracked pull requests", `untrackedPullRequests` in `src/domain/untracked-pull-requests.ts`). Listed in the Pull requests view's Not tracked list and in its source's status (`untrackedPullRequests`), each with one reason: it links no issue (`no_issue`), its issue is no work item (`issue_not_in_intake`), its issue is one and its base is not the **landing branch** (`wrong_base`), its issue is one and a job of it follows another pull request (`second_pull_request`), or its issue is one and no job of it made it or follows it (`not_by_a_job`). Found by the **census**. One whose issue is not in Intake offers **Take into Intake**. | orphan PR, lost PR |
| **Take into Intake** | The person's act on an **untracked pull request** whose issue is no work item (issue #1176, the intake action `take_in`): the source gives the issue its label and assigns it to the connected account, `source.issue_taken_in`, and Intake lists it as a request card. Only on an issue the last **census** offers. | add to Intake, adopt |
| **Census** (of open pull requests) | The read, in a round of every follow after reconciling and the **stray pull requests**, of the open pull requests of every job repository of a source, in one batch, at most once an hour and at once on a sync a person asked for (issue #1176, `JobSource.censusPullRequests`): what lists the **untracked pull requests**. It also **retargets** one of them with the wrong base on a branch named for its issue to the landing branch, and closes a `second_pull_request` on a branch named for its issue that has merge conflicts, with a fixed note that names the followed one (issue #1208). | PR scan, PR sweep |
| **Superseded** | A job's open pull request with nothing left to land (issue #1143): its issue was closed as completed by another pull request's merge (the item is done), or its base branch already has every commit of it. Never a part ("Part of #N"): its issue's close is for the whole. The hopper closes it with why. | obsolete, stale |
| **Retarget** | Change an open pull request's base branch (issue #1143): the hopper moves a job's pull request based off the **landing branch** to it. GitHub's own word is "change the base". | rebase (that moves commits, not the base) |
| **Tested branch** | The branch a green **batch run** of the **landing branch** merges into (issue #1073): `dev` for the hopper's own. A setting per repository (`landingBatches`). No job's pull request merges into it. | target, release branch |
| **Batch run** | One run of the full test suite on the **landing branch**'s tip (issue #1073, `src/landing-batches/`): a branch `hopper-batch/<tip>` and a pull request of it into the **tested branch**, started after N merges or on a timer, and only when the landing branch has something new and its tip was not tested before. Green, the hopper merges it; red, it closes it, and failing checks start **bisect runs** for the **culprit**. Each step a `landing_batch.*` event, recorded before the GitHub call that acts on it: a batch run is recorded due (`landing_batch.due`) before its branch and pull request (issue #1589). No issue's work: never followed as a job's pull request, so a red one flags no member (issue #2132). Not the **merge gate**, which checks nothing together. | merge queue, merge train, trial branch |
| **Bisect run** | One run of the full test suite on one point of a red **batch run**'s batch (issue #1073 part 2, `src/landing-batches/red.ts`): a branch `hopper-batch/bisect-<sha>` at that point and a pull request of it into the **tested branch**, closed when its checks end, never merged. The bisect takes the point in the middle of the points known to pass and to fail, until the **culprit** is found. | trial run, probe |
| **Cut-off check** | A check of a **batch run** or a **bisect run** that GitHub cancelled or that passed its time limit (issue #2086, conclusion `cancelled` or `timed_out`): no test failed in it, so it is no failing check. The run is recorded with `cutOff` and runs again at the same tip or point, at most twice in a row; it never starts a bisect, never makes a **culprit** and never files a **fix issue**. | timeout failure, flaky shard, interrupted run |
| **Culprit** | The first change of a red batch whose full test suite fails, found by **bisect runs** (issue #1073 part 2): a pull request's merge or a direct commit on the **landing branch**. The hopper reverts it from the landing branch (GitHub's revert of its pull request) and files a **fix issue**. | breaker, bad commit |
| **Fix issue** | The issue the hopper files for a **culprit** (issue #1073 part 2): through the user's own connection, the hopper label, assigned to their connected account, so Intake takes it; its text names the culprit, its issue, the revert, the failing checks with their output and the bisect. Once per culprit (`landing_batch.fix_filed`). | bug ticket, follow-up |
| **Back-merge** | The **tested branch** merged into the **landing branch** by the hopper, after a **batch run** had merge conflicts with the tested branch (issue #1073 part 4, `src/landing-batches/back-merge.ts`): a branch `hopper-batch/back-merge-<tip>` at the landing tip, the tested branch merged into it by GitHub's own merge, and a pull request of it into the landing branch, which the hopper merges; then the next batch run starts at once. A conflict GitHub cannot merge files one issue, so Intake takes it. Not the **base merge**, which merges a pull request's base into the pull request. | sync, catch-up merge, reverse merge |
| **Landing stage** | Where a work item's merged pull request stands on its way from the **landing branch** to the **tested branch** (issue #1073 part 3, `src/domain/work-item-landing.ts`): `waiting`, `batch_run`, `bisecting`, `culprit`, `reverted` or `promoted`, folded from the `landing_batch.*` events into the item's ledger row; of one pull request, which it names (issue #1232), so the item's next merge has none until an event names it. Beside the item's state, never one of them; a `culprit` or `reverted` one makes the item `failed` (issue #1175). Pull requests → Landing shows it. | batch status, landing state |
| **Delivery step** | One step of a change on its way to production (issue #2554, `DELIVERY_STEPS`, `src/domain/delivery-steps.ts`), in order: `queued`, `coding`, `pr_checks`, `landing` (the merge onto the **landing branch**), `landing_checks` (the checks there: a **batch run** for the hopper's own), `promotion_set` (the wait for the set's cutoff), `test_gate` (the test environment and its sign-off), `production`. **Measured** steps take their times from history; `promotion_set`, `test_gate` and `production` are **scheduled** by the **promotion set schedule**. | stage (a question's), phase, landing stage |
| **Delivery track** | One job's way through the **delivery steps** so far, read from its events (issue #2554 part 2, `deliveryTracks`, `src/domain/delivery-history.ts`): its repository, issue and pull request, the actual start and end of each step it reached, and the step it is in. The ended steps of a repository's tracks are its measured step durations (`stepHistory`). | timeline (a job's events), journey |
| **Step estimate** | A delivery step's p50 and p90 duration from its measured durations, with the sample size (issue #2554, `stepEstimate`): `measured`; with fewer samples than the minimum (`MIN_SAMPLES`, 5), `low` and no number at all. | guess, average |
| **Delivery forecast** | Where a change is (its **delivery step** and since when) and when it is expected at the end of each step left, at p50 and at p90 (issue #2554, `deliveryForecast`, `src/domain/delivery-forecast.ts`): the **normal path**, and with **expedite** also the **expedited path**; **slipping** with its **blocker**; a person's wait at a **delivery gate**; the time left split into a person's wait, the wait for a cutoff and machine time. A step with a `low` estimate is `unknown`, and so is each step after it. Pure: the same history, schedule and clock give the same forecast. | ETA, prediction, estimate |
| **Promotion set** | The changes promoted together at one cutoff of a repository's **promotion set schedule** (issue #2554, `src/domain/promotion-sets.ts`): a change whose landing checks end before a cutoff is in that cutoff's set; at the cutoff or after it, in the next one. Numbered by the cutoffs after the schedule's epoch. Not a **promotion** (one branch moved up), which a set may go through. | release train, batch (a **batch run** is the landing branch's test) |
| **Promotion set schedule** | A repository's cutoffs (ISO weekday and local time, in the owner's time zone), the minutes from a cutoff to the end of the test gate and from there to production, its epoch, and what **expedite** may do (`out_of_cadence` or `none`) (issue #2554, `PromotionSetSchedule`). A cutoff keeps its local time across daylight saving time. | cadence config, release calendar |
| **Delivery gate** | A **delivery step** that ends only by a person's act, with its owner (issue #2554): the test gate's sign-off, a merge or a production approval a person makes. A change at one shows a `human_gate` wait with the owner and since when. | human gate (the blocker's name), approval step |
| **Gate clearance** | A person's act that ends a **delivery gate** for one **promotion set** of a repository (issue #2554 part 5, `POST /ui/api/delivery-gate`, `delivery_gate.cleared`, `src/domain/delivery-gates.ts`): the test gate's sign-off or a production approval. Each change of the set goes on past the gate from the clearance, not before the step starts; the first clearance of a gate counts. It records the act and promotes nothing. | gate pass (a job's at the blast-radius gate), sign-off (a review item's), release |
| **Delivery settings** | Per repository, its **promotion set schedule** and its **delivery gates**, each with its owner (issue #2554 part 3, `DeliverySettings`, `src/domain/delivery-settings.ts`): the user's, an admin's to change in Settings → Delivery. A **delivery forecast** reads them; a scheduled step with no gate ends at its scheduled end, a gated one holds the change until a person ends it. | release config, delivery schedule |
| **Expedite** | A change on the fastest path its **promotion set schedule** allows (issue #2554): with `out_of_cadence`, a set of its own at once, no wait for a cutoff. Its **expedited path** is shown beside the normal one, and is never later. A person sets it as the **expedite flag** on a job, an issue or a pull request. | urgent, hotfix, fast lane |
| **Expedite flag** | A person's mark that one change is to **expedite** (issue #2554 part 4, `POST /ui/api/delivery-expedite`, `delivery_expedite.changed`, `src/domain/delivery-expedite.ts`): on a job, an issue or a pull request, on or off; the newest flag that names a change's job, issue or pull request decides. It changes the **delivery forecast** only, not the job, the queue or a promotion. | urgent label, priority (a job's own) |
| **Slipping** | A change whose current **delivery step** ran past its p90 (a scheduled step: past its scheduled end), with its **blocker**, one of `human_gate`, `ci_red`, `conflict`, `queue`, `rollout_failed`; the steps after it are forecast again from now (issue #2554). | late, overdue, stuck (a **stuck cause** is a job's) |
| **Blocker** (of a slipping change) | What holds a **slipping** change, from a fixed set (issue #2554, `BLOCKERS`): `human_gate` (always, at a **delivery gate**), `ci_red`, `conflict`, `queue` or `rollout_failed`; the change's own when it names one, else its step's default (`DEFAULT_BLOCKER`). | reason, cause (a **stuck cause** is a job's) |
| **Delivery ledger** | For each delivery step of a change, its predicted start and end (from a **delivery forecast**) and its actual ones (issue #2554, `deliveryLedger`, `src/domain/forecast-accuracy.ts`); the **forecast accuracy** is the mean absolute error of the predicted ends, per step and per repository (`forecastAccuracy`). | forecast log, history |
| **Waits on its batch** | A done work item whose pull request merged into the **landing branch** and whose **batch run** has not yet reached the **tested branch** (issue #1175, `awaitsBatch`): its issue stays open, since GitHub closes an issue only on a merge into the default branch. Intake shows it as done and offers no **reopened card**; the batch run's pull request names its issue with a closing keyword, so the batch's merge closes it. Read from GitHub too (issue #1337, `waitsOnBatch`), for an issue the ledger missed: an open batch run closes it or lists its merged pull request, or that merge came after the newest open batch run and within three hours. The done-check counts it done, Intake neither offers it nor runs it again, and the **job graph** shows its issue `done` with `awaitsBatch` ("Merged: waits on its batch run"). | pending close, merged on main |
| **Landing batches** | A repository's batch runs and their settings (issue #1073): its **tested branch**, the merges (`everyMerges`) and the minutes (`everyMinutes`) after which a batch run starts, and the **canary window**. Settings → Landing batches. | batching, merge queue |
| **Canary** | The step from `beta` to `stable` (issue #1073 part 5, `src/landing-batches/canary.ts`): a **stable candidate** on the `canary` branch reaches the **canary subset** first, and goes to `stable` when its **canary window** passes with no bug report that names it; else it is **held** and one issue is filed for Intake. Each step a `landing_batch.canary_*` event. `canary` is no **update channel**. | rollout, staged release, pre-release |
| **Stable candidate** | The commit on the `canary` branch that is ahead of `stable` (issue #1073 part 5): a commit of `beta` a maintainer promoted (`scripts/promote.sh canary`). | release candidate, RC |
