| **Canary subset** | The stable installs that take stable releases early (issue #1073 part 5): each install's own choice, Settings → Version → Take stable releases early (`UpdateSettings.canary`). They follow `canary` while it is ahead of `stable`. | canary group, ring, early adopters |
| **Canary window** | How many hours a **stable candidate** runs on the **canary subset** before it goes to `stable` (issue #1073 part 5): `canaryHours` per repository in **landing batches**; absent, no canary step. | bake time, soak period |
| **Reconciling** | The sync loop's match, at each round of every followed pull request of the **in-motion cycle** (issue #985: one batched read of the open pull requests of every such repository), of each done or failed job (issue #786) that follows no pull request — the newest of its item, no failed tend job — to its item's open pull request on the source (issue #763): one that closes the open issue, else a part of it, else one on a branch named for it; for an issue closed as completed, one that closes it by keyword or a part of it (issue #786). A match is followed from then on (`follow: open`, `job.pull_request_followed`), so a job that ended before the hopper followed pull requests is **PR waiting** like any other. | adopt, backfill, sync PRs |
| **Pull requests list** | The Pull requests view and `GET /api/pull-requests` (issue #637, `src/domain/pull-request-list.ts`): each **PR waiting** job's pull request, one closed without a merge, and one its item's tend job works on (`tending`, `tend failed`; issue #763), per job repository with its **Yolo mode**; each card's state (`open`, `closed`), checks, merge conflicts, age and why its merge waits, in plain words (`waiting for a person to merge`, `waiting for checks`, `conflicts with dev`, `ready, merging`, …; issue #677); the header how many repositories have yolo mode on and where merging waits. `hopper prs`. | PR board, merge queue |
| **Pull request diff** | A pull request's changes as a person reads them on the **Pull requests list** (issue #1169): Show diff on a card or a Landing card reads them with the person's own GitHub connection (`POST /ui/api/pull-requests/diff`, operator, one of the **job repositories** only) and shows them in a code block in diff mode. Kept nowhere. `ui/src/components/pull-request-diff.tsx`. | patch view, changes tab |
| **Tend again** | A person's act on a **PR waiting** card the hopper gave up on — its tend job ended failed, cancelled or rejected (issue #1010): the same step is tended at once (`tended.again`), from the card or `hopper pr tend <n>`, recorded on `job.pull_request_tended` with who pressed it (`person`, `via`). Least role: operator. | retry tend, re-tend |
| **Merge now** | A person's merge of a **PR waiting** job's open pull request from its card or `hopper pr merge <n>` (issue #677): on the **Yolo mode** path (`JobSource.follow` with `merge`), whatever yolo mode and its checks say; GitHub still refuses a draft, merge conflicts or a required check. Recorded on `job.pull_request_merged` with who pressed it (`person`, `via`); answered before the merge runs, and one that fails is `job.pull_request_act_failed` (issue #1194). Least role: operator. A merge of a pull request with no closing keyword that is no part closes the issue too: the hopper closes it as completed, with one comment that links the pull request. | force merge, manual merge |
| **Merge marker** | What the hopper keeps in a job's source state just before its own merge call — yolo mode or **Merge now** (issue #1588, `merging`, `MergeMarker`): the pull request, the head commit it merges, and who merges it (`byHopper`, or the person and `via` of the act). A stop after the call and before the record leaves the pull request merged on GitHub: read merged with the marker on it, at that head, it is the hopper's merge, so its issue is closed as after any merge of the hopper's, and who merged it is recorded. Seen open again, or ended, it goes. | merge lock, merge flag, pending merge |
| **No checks** | A job repository with nothing that verifies a pull request (issue #677, `GET /api/repo-checks`): no required status check on its default branch (branch protection or a ruleset), no workflow file that runs on pull requests, and no checks on its five newest pull requests. There **Yolo mode** merges unverified code, so the Pull requests page warns of it and recommends the job "Add PR checks to <repo>": **Queue job** files it as the person's own issue with the hopper label, assigned to their connected account; **Dismiss** hides the recommendation for that repository, the warning stays. Unknown while GitHub cannot be read, never no checks. `hopper repo checks <repo>`. | unprotected repo, no CI |
| **Repository setup** | What the hopper checks of each job repository on GitHub (issue #652, `GET /api/repo-checks` `setup`, design.md "Repository setup"): each **guarded branch** needs a pull request and a status check and takes no force push, and secret scanning and push protection are on. `set up`, `not set up` with each problem, or `unknown` when no problem was found but GitHub did not show part of it (branch protection and the security settings are shown only to a repository admin) or was not read. Sources shows it. | repo hardening, branch rules |
| **Guarded branch** | A branch the **repository setup** checks: the repository's default branch and each **release branch** it has. | protected branch (that is GitHub's own setting) |
| **Close PR** | A person's close of a **PR waiting** job's open pull request without a merge, from its card or `hopper pr close <n>` (issue #677): on the same path; its item is flagged `hopper:pr-closed`, as for any close without a merge. Recorded on `job.pull_request_closed` with who pressed it; answered before the close runs, and one that fails is `job.pull_request_act_failed` (issue #1194). | reject PR, abandon |
| **PR check** | The check `pr / test` (issue #672, `.github/workflows/pr.yml`) GitHub runs on every pull request to `dev`, `beta` or `stable`: it passes only when typecheck, lint, the UI build (`pr / gates`) and the tests in ten shards (`pr / shard (n)`), less the **host-service tests**, passed. The check **Yolo mode**'s merge needs on this repository. Read-only, no secrets. | CI, build check |
| **Host-service tests** | The test files that need a host service beyond the suite's own Postgres — containers they start, a real sshd, LDAP, a vault (`test/support/host-services.ts`). `HOPPER_TEST_HOST_SERVICES=0` leaves them out, as the **PR check** does. | integration tests (most of those run in the check) |
| **Cloud acceptance tests** | The test files under `test/cloud-acceptance/` (issue #1956): a vault-minted session used by the real `aws` CLI, curl's SigV4 and the AWS SDK against a mock AWS (moto) behind the **policy-assert proxy**, through the real composition root. Not in `npm test`: `npm run test:cloud` (`vitest.cloud.config.ts`), and the `cloud-acceptance` workflow, nightly and on a pull request that changes the vault, the sandbox module or the infra plans. | e2e, integration tests |
| **Policy-assert proxy** | The test proxy in front of moto (`test/support/policy-assert-proxy.ts`, issue #1956): it learns each session from the AssumeRole that minted it and allows a SigV4 call only as AWS would under that session's policies — the inline deny policy first, then `ReadOnlyAccess` as IAM's Read and List actions —, fails closed on what it cannot name, and logs every allow and deny. moto itself enforces no policy. | policy simulator, IAM mock |
| **Done-check backfill** | The one-time pass (issue #637, `src/failures/backfill.ts`) that judges each `not complete:` failure waiting on a person by today's **Completion** rule: done, its job ends finished (or **PR waiting**), its hand-off closes and its record turns `resolved`, one `job.finished` each; else it stays in Failures with a plain sentence. Runs at the first start of a build that has it; `hopper backfill done-check` runs it again. Idempotent. | migration, re-check |
| **Shipped** | A job's change is in the product (issue #171): its pull request merged — by a person, or by the job with **Yolo mode** on. On GitHub, its issue is **closed as complete**. Not needed for done (issue #579). | landed, delivered, merged |
| **Closed as complete** | A job's issue closed as completed (issues #350, #637): GitHub's `state_reason` completed, or its last close event's closer a merged pull request — by any pull request, commit or person, at any time. A close as not planned is not. Its job is complete; a failed job whose issue is closed as complete is finished instead, before the failure is reported and before any run again of it: the **work-item ledger** decides (`issueClosedAsComplete`, issue #1067), and the source's read of the issue (`JobSource.issueState`) is asked only when the ledger does not hold it closed, recorded as ledger input first. Whether it cancels a started job is the job's own work only: a close by the merge of an older pull request cancels it. | closed by the job, resolved |
| **Report** | Telling the source what happened to its job: the claim and the end. On GitHub: labels, and a finished job closes its issue; the hopper posts no comment. | |
| **Signal** | What a source tells the hopper: cancel. Questions are answered in the UI, never through a source. | |
| **Closing pull request** | The merged pull request whose merge closed an issue (the closer of the issue's last close event, `closingPullRequest`). Opened at or after the job's creation, it is the job's own: its close is no cancel signal, and the job runs on to its own end. | closer PR, linked PR |
| **Hopper marker** | The hidden first line of a comment the hopper once posted (it posts none now); lets the context filter tell old ones from the owner's text. | |
| **Leftover variable** | A `HOPPER_*` variable that is set but read by nothing (a removed part-choosing one, or a typo). One loud warning at boot names them all. | |
| **UI session** | A browser session created by a sign-in; the only way to mutate. Carries a UI role, the identity it was made for, and the user it acts for: every read and mutation it makes is that user's. The hopper's own, never the identity provider's token: every request renews it, and it ends at its idle timeout or its session maximum (issue #439). | |
| **Session lengths** | The sign-in config's `sessions` (issue #439), one pair for the hopper, set in Settings → Sign-in: the **idle timeout** (`idleHours`, default 168) — a UI session with no request for that long ends — and the **session maximum** (`maxHours`, default 720) — it ends that long after it started, however much it is used. Read at each lookup: a change applies to every session there is. | session hours, TTL, lifetime |
| **Session renewal** | A request of a UI session's moving its idle timeout on (`last_seen_at`, written at most once a minute). A gateway realm's session renews only when the token the gateway forwards checks out again for the same person; its issuer out of reach, it waits unrenewed for the **grace period** (5 minutes after the last good check). | refresh, keep-alive |
| **Session end** | A UI session ending, with its **reason** (`ui_session.ended`): `expired-idle`, `expired-absolute`, `refresh-refused`, `provider-unreachable`, `realm-changed`, `connection-ended` (signed in with GitHub, and the GitHub connection ended; only in events recorded before issue #1380, since then a connection that ends marks only the source), `logout`. Logged and recorded for the session's user. | logout (that is one reason), expiry |
| **Sign-in** | Starting a UI session: with the one-time login code (**local sign-in**, always `admin`; the sign-in config's `local.enabled`), **no sign-in**, with a **local account**, or through a **realm**. The login code's own path keeps its words (login code, `POST /ui/login`, "Log in"). | authentication |
| **Landing page** | What the UI shows logged out, and all it shows (issue #213): the hopper's name and the ways to sign in, or that the hopper could not be reached. No navigation, no view, no read but the session's. Until the session is read the page is blank, so nothing of the app shows before it. | sign-in screen, login page, read-only page |
| **Realm** | One named way of checking who signs in: an entry of the sign-in config's `realms` with a **realm type**, a label, on or off (`enabled`), and role rules. The realms are an ordered list, managed in Settings → Sign-in; a change applies at once. The login code and no sign-in are not realms. Not a plugin. | identity provider (that is the outside service an oidc, github or saml realm uses), provider, auth backend, security realm |
| **Realm type** | What a realm checks against: `ldap` (a directory), `oidc`, `github` (a **device realm**, issue #214), `saml`, `gateway` (the token an **auth gateway** forwards). There is no `password` type: the hopper's own email and password sign-in is the **local accounts**, no realm (issues #237, #1380). | realm kind, auth method |
| **Local account** | The hopper's own sign-in (issue #1380): an email (kept lowercase, the account's key) and a password, kept as a salted scrypt hash only, with the role it signs in with — no role rule applies to it. Its identities carry the reserved realm name `local-accounts`, their subject the email. The operator CLI adds one (`hopper local-account`, the first admin of a hopper with no identity provider); the instance admin manages them in Settings → Sign-in → Local accounts; an account's own user changes its password. `LocalAccount`, `local_accounts`, `GET /api/local-accounts`, `POST /ui/api/local-accounts`. | password account, user account (a user is the work a sign-in reaches), login |
| **Passkey** | A local account's WebAuthn credential (issue #1380): the account adds it in Settings → Sign-in → Your passkeys, and it then signs the account in in place of its password, at the host name where it was added only. The hopper keeps its public key, never a secret. `LocalAccountPasskey`, `local_account_passkeys`, `POST /ui/auth/passkey`. | security key, WebAuthn key, credential (alone) |
| **Source email match** | An instance setting, on by default (issue #1380): a **connected account** a person connects from Sources must have, among its verified emails at GitHub, the email their sign-in gave. The account keeps that email (`matchEmail`), and each renewal of its token checks it again; one that lost it reads **expired**, and the person stays signed in. A GitHub sign-in, and a sign-in that gave no email, have nothing to match. The instance admin turns it off in Settings → Sign-in. `GET /api/source-email-match`, `POST /ui/api/source-email-match`, `source_email_match.changed`. | email check, email verification (GitHub verifies the email; the hopper only compares) |
| **Personal access token** | A token a person makes at GitHub for their own account, and gives the hopper in Sources to connect GitHub with no **hopper's app** (issue #1380, `POST /ui/api/connected-accounts` `connect-token`). It becomes the **connected account**'s token, `grantedBy: 'token'`, kept in the user's vault under the **master key**. GitHub must take it, and the **source email match** applies. Nothing renews it: GitHub refusing it makes the connected account **expired**. The hopper never deletes it at GitHub. Its connection lists the repositories the token reaches, not where the app is installed. Not an **API token** (the hopper's own, for its API). | PAT, GitHub token, API token |
| **Form realm** | A realm of type `ldap`: it checks a username and password from the sign-in form. The form tries the form realms that are on in order; the first that accepts the password decides. | |
| **Redirect realm** | A realm of type `oidc` or `saml`: it sends the browser to its identity provider and back. Each one that is on is a sign-in button, in order. | SSO realm |
| **Device realm** | A realm of type `github` (issue #214): the person signs in with a **device code** entered at GitHub, through the **hopper's app**; the browser that began it polls until it is approved — or, when the runtime gives the app's client secret (`HOPPER_GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET`, issue #258), on the **sign-in origin** the browser goes to GitHub and back as with a **redirect realm**, the device code then the fallback. Nothing to set but its label and role rules. Every hopper has one (migration 23), and the first person to sign in with it becomes admin (issue #239). The token that signs the person in becomes their **connected account**. | GitHub OAuth realm |
| **Gateway realm** | A realm of type `gateway` (issue #215): neither a form realm nor a redirect realm. An **auth gateway** in front of the hopper signed the person in; the realm checks the token the gateway forwards (`jwt`: its signature against the issuer's keys; `introspection`: the issuer's answer) and the UI takes the session by itself (`POST /ui/auth/gateway`). The hopper runs no sign-in of its own for it. | upstream auth, proxy auth, header auth, trusted header |
| **Auth gateway** | The outside proxy in front of the hopper that signs people in at an identity provider and forwards their token on every request (Envoy Gateway with OIDC, oauth2-proxy). Not configured in the hopper: the gateway realm is. | auth proxy, edge, ingress (in prose only) |
| **API door** | A read of `/api/` with a token in `Authorization: Bearer …` instead of a UI session (issue #255): the sign-in service checks the token as the UI's sign-in does — a JWT by the gateway realms, any other token by GitHub for the GitHub realm — and the request reads as the user that identity is already linked to (a GitHub token: only as the user whose **connected account** it is). Signs nobody in, never mutates; it may be exchanged for an **API token** (issue #718). The UI's sign-in is the UI door. | API key, bearer auth, personal access token, service token |
| **API token** | A short-lived token an agent acts through the API with, as a user (issue #718): `Authorization: Bearer hopper_at_…` on every `GET /api/*` and `POST /ui/api/*`, so every action of the UI is an action of the API. A UI session of kind `api`, kept hashed, living the admin's **API-token lifetime** at most, its role no higher than its minter's. An agent gets one by trading a **grant**'s **refresh token** (issue #881, `POST /ui/auth/token/refresh`), or by exchanging a sign-in the **API door** accepts (`POST /ui/auth/api-token`); an API token never mints another. A grant's token uses only the routes its **token scopes** cover. Access decides each use (`api_token#can_view`, `can_operate`, `can_administer`); a **box action** needs a **box token**. Not a job's proxy token: a job asks the hopper. | API key, access token, personal access token, service token, mutating token |
| **Grant** | What a person mints once for an agent (issue #881, Users & access → API tokens, `hopper token mint`), or approves on a **device grant request** (issue #892): a label, a holder, a role no higher than theirs, **token scopes**, a box flag, an end (90 days by default, or none: an admin's only), and the key it is bound to, when it is a **bound grant** (issue #892), and its limits, when it is a **limited grant** (issue #892). It lives while it is used: it dies 30 days after its last refresh, at its end, when revoked, or when its minter's sign-in grants no role. Only hashes of its refresh tokens are kept. Its agent trades its **refresh token** for **API tokens**. | OAuth client, app password, long-lived token, refresh grant |
| **Refresh token** | A grant's one-time token (issue #881, `hopper_rt_…`): traded at `POST /ui/auth/token/refresh` for a new **API token** and a new refresh token, which replaces it. A retry within 30 seconds gets the same pair; a spent one sent again after that ends its grant at once (**reuse detection**, `api_grant.reuse_detected`). | rotating key, renewal token |
| **Token scope** | What a **grant**'s **API tokens** may use (issue #881, `src/domain/token-scopes.ts`), named from the route: `GET /api/<area>/…` needs `<area>:read`, `POST /ui/api/<area>/…/<act>` needs `<area>:<act>`, `POST /ui/api/<area>` needs `<area>:edit`. A route whose body names one of several actions (`action`, or `kind`) has a scope for each too (issue #909): `<area>:<act>.<action>`, e.g. `vault:edit.approve-template`, which the route's scope covers. A grant holds `<area>:<act>`, `<area>:<act>.<action>`, `<area>:*`, `*:<act>` or `*`. A route no scope covers is refused: deny by default. | permission, OAuth scope, capability |
| **Scope registry** | Every **token scope** a **grant** may hold, as a person picks it (issue #909, `src/domain/scope-registry.ts`, `GET /api/scopes`): sections by area of work (Jobs, Intake, Infra changes, Vault and credentials, Machines, …), each scope with its plain description, the routes it covers, the least role they need, and whether one is a **box action**; and the **scope presets**. Built from the routes, so a new route's scope is listed with no edit; an area no section names is under Other. | permission list, scope catalog, capability map |
| **Scope preset** | A named set of **token scopes** in the **scope registry** (issue #909): `read-only`, `agent-intake`, `agent-approvals`, `full-admin`. One click ticks its scopes on the grant form, where a person can change them; `preset` at the mint, or `hopper token mint --preset`, gives its scopes, and its box flag unless `box` is named. A box grant when its scopes cover a box action. | role, template, profile |
| **API-token lifetime** | How long an **API token** lives (issue #881): the hopper's admin's setting, Users & access → API tokens, 5 to 15 minutes, 10 until set. A grant may ask for less. | token TTL, expiry |
| **Box token** | The **API token** of a **grant** a person minted with `box` from a UI session (issue #795, issue #881): the only kind that may do a **box action**, when Access allows `api_token#can_run_on_box` and its **token scopes** cover the route. Never minted by the exchange or by another API token. Off by default. | step-up token, elevated token, admin token |
| **Delegate approver** | A **grant** of the preset `delegate-approver` (issue #1138), which a person mints on Access requests (or `hopper token mint --preset delegate-approver`) for an agent that approves for them what waits for a person: a **grant ask**, a template, a sandbox request. It may also deny a grant ask and decline a sandbox request; it reads the vault's view and does nothing else (its **token scopes** are the preset's only). Its approvals record `by` as `<its label> (delegate of <the person>)`. Revoked on Access requests, it stops at once. | proxy approver, approval bot, approve-only token |
| **Approver policy** | What a **delegate approver** may never approve (issue #1138): `deny` entries, each matched in any case against every name the approval touches — the template, its secrets and assets, the machine or Windows host, what the job asked for. A match is refused (403); that approval stays the person's. | allow list, approval scope |
| **Device grant request** | An agent's ask for a **grant** with no token (issue #892, `POST /ui/auth/device-grant`, on the model of RFC 8628): the grant it wants, in a mint's fields. It gets a **device code** (`hopper_dc_…`, kept by the agent, only its hash by the hopper) and a **user code** (`BCDF-GHJK`), which it shows to a person. The person enters the user code in Users & access → API tokens (Approve a device) or `hopper token approve`, and approves or denies it; approved, the grant is that person's own mint, and the agent takes its first pair once at its poll (`POST /ui/auth/device-grant/poll`). In memory only, 10 minutes at most. Not a *login* (a device code a machine or job waits on at an outside service) and not a **device realm** (a person signing in to the hopper with GitHub). | device authorization, device flow, pairing code |
| **Bound grant** | A **grant** bound to a key its agent holds (issue #892, sender binding, on the model of DPoP, RFC 9449): the hopper keeps the key's thumbprint (`jkt`), and each refresh, each use of its **API tokens** (`Authorization: DPoP …`) and a **device grant request**'s poll needs a **DPoP proof** of that key. Bound at its birth (a device grant request with a proof, `jkt` at the mint) or at its first refresh with a proof; once bound, always. | DPoP token, key-bound token, pinned grant |
| **DPoP proof** | A short JWT an agent signs with its own key for one request (issue #892, RFC 9449), in the `DPoP` header: its public key, the method (`htm`), the URL (`htu`), when (`iat`, 60 seconds either way), a one-time id (`jti`), and for an API token its hash (`ath`). What a **bound grant** needs at each use. | DPoP header, signed request, proof of possession |
| **Limited grant** | A **grant** that names the repositories (`owner/name`) and the job sources its tokens act on, beside its **token scopes** (issue #892, `limits`): each mutation of its tokens must act only on the jobs, questions, review items, pull requests and items of these, or it is refused (403). A route that names no **limit target** is refused to it. Its reads are not limited. | scoped grant, repo-scoped token, restricted grant |
| **Limit target** | What one mutation acts on, as a **limited grant**'s limits are checked against it (issue #892): a repository and a job source, from the job, question, review item, pull request or item the route names (`src/http/ui/grant-limits.ts`). | resource, object, subject |
| **Box action** | A route that starts a job, runs a command, approves or merges work, updates the hopper or a client, or changes the work on a machine (issue #795; the list is `BOX_ACTIONS`, `src/http/ui/box-actions.ts`). With an API token it needs a **box token**. Reads, holds, cancels and settings are not box actions. | privileged action, dangerous action, execution |
| **Confirmation ask** | An API token's approval that a person must confirm (issue #909, design.md "A person confirms what an agent asks"): of an infra plan that is not low risk, or of a template's write, sync or apply profile. The hopper does not approve it: it answers 202 `{ waiting }`, records `approval.confirmation_asked` (at most once an hour for an item), which webhook subscriptions and notifiers that take it send on, and shows the ask on the item. A person approves the item in the UI: that is the confirmation. | per-item confirmation, step-up, second factor |
| **Identity provider** | The outside service a redirect realm sends the browser to (Google, Entra ID, Okta, a SAML IdP). Not configured as such: the realm is. | IdP (in prose only) |
| **Password sign-in** | Signing in with a username and password through the form realms (`POST /ui/auth/password`). | simple auth, basic auth |
| **First GitHub admin** | The first person to sign in through a GitHub realm (issue #239): admin from then on, whatever the realm's role rules say, while that realm is on, and the first **super admin**. Recorded once as `githubAdmin` (realm and subject) in the sign-in config, and only on a hopper nobody had signed in to with GitHub before. A role, not a user: they sign in as the user their identity is linked to. | first admin, owner, GitHub owner |
| **Instance admin** | An admin of the hopper (issue #240): a session with role `admin` that signed in through a realm or no sign-in — the first GitHub admin, a **super admin**, anyone a role rule makes admin — or whose user is a super admin's (their device link), or, while no super admin's realm is on, the oldest user's. Only they change sign-in realms, add users and hand out their login links, apply updates, use the plugin store, and read the users and the totals across them (issue #241); never another user's work. There is no special admin login: a new user's login link never makes one, and a device link gives no more than its maker's user had. "The hopper's admin" in the UI. | special admin login |
| **Sign-in config** | Everything about sign-in: the config record `sign-in` (the realms in order with their settings, the login code, no sign-in). Changed in Settings → Sign-in, every setting a form field, against its `version` (`POST /ui/api/realms`); the CLI reads and replaces it too (`hopper config … sign-in`). | auth config, auth.yaml, sign-in settings |
| **Realm secret** | A secret setting of a realm — `clientSecret` (oidc, gateway), `bindPassword` (ldap): set in Settings → Sign-in or from the sign-in environment and stored with the realm (issue #216), never answered back (`RealmView.secrets` names those set). A save that leaves it out keeps it; `null` removes it. The one kind of secret the hopper stores. | client secret variable, clientSecretEnv |
| **Sign-in environment** | The `HOPPER_SIGN_IN_*` variables (issue #216): `HOPPER_SIGN_IN_REALM_<NAME>_TYPE` and `_<SETTING>` (a field path in upper snake case) set up a realm, `HOPPER_SIGN_IN_LOCAL_ENABLED` the login code and `HOPPER_SIGN_IN_NONE_ROLE` no sign-in; each also `_FILE`. Applied to the stored sign-in config at every start: the environment wins. | auth env, env config |
| **No sign-in** | the sign-in config's `none: { role }`: anyone who reaches the UI gets a UI session with that role, without a credential (`POST /ui/auth/none`, taken by the UI by itself). Its identity's realm is `none`. | anonymous mode, no auth, guest |
| **Identity** | Who signed in, as every realm reports it: realm, subject, verified email, username, name, groups. | user, principal |
| **Super admin** | An admin who may also make others super admin or hand their own over, keeping admin (issue #242): one of the sign-in config's `superAdmins` (realm and subject each), admin whatever their realm's role rules while that realm is on. The first GitHub admin is the first; the login code and no sign-in never are. Not a UI role: every other admin is a **regular admin**, who may make others admin but not super admin. | owner, root, superuser, head admin |
| **UI role** | What a UI session may do: `viewer` (read), `operator` (+ jobs and questions), `admin` (+ configuration, device links, realms, making others admin; a **super admin** also makes others super admin). Granted by a realm's **role rules** (subjects, usernames, emails, email domains, groups; the highest match wins, else `defaultRole`, else no session); the login code grants `admin`. `admin` acts inside the session's own user; what is the instance's is the **instance admin**'s. Not a plugin role. | permission, role (alone: that is a plugin role) |
| **Sign-in origin** | Where a redirect realm's sign-in starts and ends: `HOPPER_PUBLIC_URL`, else `http://localhost:<port>`. Callback: `<origin>/ui/auth/<name>/callback`. | redirect host |
| **Binding** | A random value the browser keeps in `localStorage` when a redirect realm's sign-in begins and posts at the end with the ticket; a sign-in completes only in the browser that began it. | nonce (that is OIDC's) |
| **Public URL** | `HOPPER_PUBLIC_URL`: the origin people reach the UI at through a reverse proxy. Its host passes the Host guard (a **public request**: `/api/` only with a UI session), its origin may mutate, and it is the sign-in origin. | external URL |
| **Tunnel** | What forwards the **remote access URL** to the hopper's API (issues #795, #880, #968): run by the **tunnel service** of the **tunnel provider** picked — the compose service `tunnel` runs ngrok's agent, `cloudflared` Cloudflare's —, on a network only they and the hopper share, with only the API let through at its edge and in the hopper. The **tunnel watch** asks that agent's local API (`HOPPER_TUNNEL_API`, `HOPPER_CLOUDFLARED_API`) whether it is up, and looks up both agents' addresses. Each edge names the caller (`x-hopper-caller`, `cf-connecting-ip`), which the hopper reads from that agent alone, for its limits. | reverse tunnel, relay |
| **Remote access** | Whether the **tunnel** runs, set by the hopper's admin in Settings → Remote access (issues #880, #968): off by default; on or off, the **tunnel provider**, its one credential (a system secret of the instance's vault), and the host name. Turned on, the hopper mints the tunnel; turned off or switched, it releases it. Never in `.env`. | public access, exposure |
| **Remote access URL** | `https://<host name>`, the host name the hopper minted the **tunnel** at: where the tunnel reaches the hopper's API. Its host passes the Host guard as a **public request**'s while remote access is on. Not the **public URL**, which a reverse proxy serves. | tunnel URL, ngrok URL |
| **Tunnel service** | A compose service that runs a **tunnel provider**'s agent, as the hopper sees it: `tunnel` (ngrok) or `cloudflared`. It asks `GET /tunnel/agent` every 10 s with the key the stack makes in memory (`HOPPER_TUNNEL_KEY`), gets the **tunnel token** (and, for ngrok, the **remote access URL**) only while its provider is the one minted, starts its agent with it, and reports what the agent did. | sidecar, agent (alone: that is the provider's program) |
| **Tunnel provider** | Who carries the **tunnel** (issue #968): Cloudflare Tunnel or ngrok, equal alternatives the admin picks. The hopper asks its API with the admin's one credential — a Cloudflare API token, an ngrok API key — to **mint** the tunnel and to **release** it (`src/remote-access/`). | backend, vendor |
| **Mint** (tunnel) / **release** (tunnel) | What the hopper does at the **tunnel provider** when **remote access** is turned on / off, switched, or given another host name or credential (issue #968). Mint: the **tunnel token**, the host name and, at Cloudflare, the tunnel and its DNS record (`provisioned`). Release: revoke and remove what it minted; what it cannot remove is the **leftover**. Since issue #1609 the mint is a **saga** of its own kind per provider (`remote-access-mint-cloudflare`, `remote-access-mint-ngrok`): each provider call a **saga step** that keeps what it made before the next, all of it named for the instance (`hopper-<instance id>`) so a step tried again finds it, and the release as its **compensation**. | provision, deprovision, set up, tear down |
| **Tunnel token** | The secret a **tunnel service** runs its agent with: a Cloudflare tunnel's token, or an ngrok authtoken. Minted by the hopper (an ngrok authtoken given by hand before issue #968 is kept), a system secret of the instance's vault, answered to its tunnel service alone. | connector token |
| **Leftover** (remote access) | What a **release** could not remove at the **tunnel provider** (it did not answer, or the credential was revoked): listed in Settings → Remote access for a person to remove by hand. | orphan |
| **hopper.reachable** | What the Grok Bot routine is sent while the **tunnel** is up (issue #795): the **remote access URL** and where an agent exchanges its sign-in for an API token; at start, on a new URL, on recovery, and on a heartbeat. Not a domain event. | tunnel.up, announce |
| **LAN name** | A host name or address the UI answers to from other machines (`HOPPER_LAN_NAMES`), with the port. A request naming one is a **LAN request**: it reads `/api/` only with a UI session. | remote host |
| **Host link base** | The address people open the hopper at (issue #712): an origin the hopper answers to, an instance setting the admin sets in Settings → Host link (`POST /ui/api/host-link`); empty, its default `http://localhost:<port>`, on the computer that runs the hopper. A person's link — a notification's question link, a UI link, an artifact's link — is built on the user's **link base** (Settings → Artifacts), else the **public URL**, else the host link base when set, else a LAN name that is an IP or ends in `.local`, else the default (`personBase`, `src/artifacts/links.ts`): never a bare LAN name such as a container's. Containers and boxes keep the LAN names (the **hopper address**). | container link base, host URL, `HOPPER_HOST_URL` |
| **LAN peer** | A CIDR range a LAN request may come from (`HOPPER_LAN_PEERS`). Any other non-loopback peer is refused. | allowlist |
| **Allowed host** | A host name or address the hopper's admin adds in Settings → Allowed hosts (issue #1024), never in a file; or, for a hopper the Helm chart installs, one the chart's value `allowedHosts` names (issue #1421), given at launch in `HOPPER_LAN_NAMES`. It counts as a **LAN name**. | extra host, trusted host |
| **Cloud link** | This instance linked to a **hopper.io account** (issue #467): one record in the instance's settings (`cloudLink`), with the **instance id** as its id at hopper.io (`installId`, the same at every link, issue #465), the account and its state (`pending` until it registers with a **registration code**, then `registered`, and `pending` again when hopper.io's account owner unregisters it there; hopper.io says both in the cloud link's two closed messages, `register` and `unregister`, and the instance refuses any other). The hopper's admin links and unlinks it in Settings → hopper.io while the hopper runs. A link moves no data; an unlink removes the record and its open registration code, and nothing else. Its connection (issue #464; hopper.io's end built, issue #2039 part 1: one WebSocket that the instance opens, which starts with the **cloud link hello**; the instance's agent is part 2) is outbound from the instance, and only while the record exists: it carries allow-listed state out only, and the instance acts on nothing that comes in on it but a remote action its admin turned on (`docs/hopper-io-security.md`, issue #468). Not a machine's **link**. | cloud account, cloud sync, hopper.io link, hopper.io connection, link (alone), tunnel |
| **Registration code** | A one-time code that registers the **cloud link** at hopper.io (issue #465): minted by the instance admin in Settings → hopper.io (`POST /ui/api/cloud-link/registration-code`) while the instance is linked, for 10 minutes, kept only hashed, one at a time; the **hopper.io account** owner enters it at hopper.io, and the instance spends it when hopper.io hands it back for the link's own account. Not a **join code**, which adds a machine. | pairing code, claim code, link code |
| **Tailnet** | The Tailscale network this host is on (issue #1024). The hopper finds its MagicDNS name and addresses there at runtime, and each counts as a **LAN name**; while on one, a peer in Tailscale's ranges is a LAN peer. | VPN, mesh |
| **Device link** | `http://<LAN name>:<port>/#login=<code>`: a fresh login code as a link a logged-in browser hands another device, shown also as a QR code that changes when its code is used or expires. Works once, for 10 minutes. | pairing link, invite |
| **User menu** | The top bar's one button for the signed-in user, the user's initials, at every width (issue #666, `ui/src/app/user-menu.tsx`): it opens a menu with the user's name and role, the theme, the **device link** (an admin's, on a local sign-in) and, last and set apart, Sign out, which needs a second tap. | account menu, profile menu, avatar menu |
| **Payload version** | `schemaVersion` on every event: the version of that event type's payload schema. | |
| **Connected account** | The GitHub account a user's work comes through (issue #214): signing in with it (a **device realm**) connects it, and a user signed in at the edge (SSO, SAML, a gateway) connects it from Sources — both through the **hopper's app** by GitHub's device flow, or with a **personal access token** of their own, with no app (issue #1380). Kept in that user's store with the token GitHub granted and its refresh token, sealed under the **master key** (issue #441), which the **renewer** trades for a new pair before the token expires and when GitHub refuses it (issues #358, #441). **Expired** (`state: 'expired'`): its sign-in ended — GitHub refused the refresh token itself with no newer pair stored (revoked, or the app's authorization removed), or the token expired with nothing to renew it; a **renewal trouble** never ends it; it offers no login, its source pauses, the owner is told (`connected_account.expired`), until connected again — nothing falls back to another credential. **Unreadable** (`state: 'unreadable'`, issue #514): its tokens are sealed under a **master key** the runtime does not give; not expired — its source pauses saying to give the key back, nothing asks to connect again. One **grant** per connected account: the one a new grant replaces, or a disconnect drops, is revoked at GitHub first. Its job source (`github-account`) reads and labels that account's issues in its **job repositories** with it, and its jobs run with it (through their **credentials dir**, else `GH_TOKEN`), so what they do acts as the user with the app marked on it — the one way the hopper reads GitHub as the user (issue #359). A user may have more than one at a provider (issue #1448): the first, and each **further account**, each by its **connected account key**. `ConnectedAccountStatus`, `GET /api/connected-accounts`, `POST /ui/api/connected-accounts` (connect, connect-token, add-token, cancel, disconnect, choose). | connection (that is a machine's), linked account, integration |
| **Connected account key** | Which of a user's connected accounts (issue #1448): the first at a provider is keyed by its provider (`github`), so what was kept before stays as it was; each **further account** by its provider and a number from 2 (`github-2`, `github-3`, …). Its row, its tokens (`system/connected-account.<key>.…`), what it reaches and its **job repositories** are kept under it. `ConnectedAccountStatus.key`, the `account` option of a `github-account` job source. | account id, slot, index |
| **Further account** | A connected account beside the user's first at the same provider (issue #1448), for example a work account beside a personal one: added in Sources with a **personal access token** of the other account (`add-token`), never a device code or a sign-in; its login must not be connected already, and the **source email match** applies. It has its own **job repositories**, its own job source (`github-account-2`, …, added by the hopper, never in the plugins config) and its own end; removing it forgets it, its token and its job repositories. | second source, secondary account, linked account |
| **GitLab account** | The GitLab account a user attaches in Sources (issue #1448 part 2), at gitlab.com or at a GitLab of their own, with a **personal access token**: GitLab must take it and the **source email match** applies (a confirmed email). Its record (`gitlabAccount`, a user setting: who, where, the **picked projects**, the projects the token reaches) never holds the token; the token is the system secret `gitlab-account.access-token`. GitLab refusing the token ends this account only (`expired`). Not a **connected account**: those are GitHub's. | GitLab connection, GitLab source account |
| **Picked project** | A project of the **GitLab account** whose open issues labelled `hopper` Intake lists (issue #1448 part 2): `group/name`, any number of subgroups. GitLab's word, so not a **job repository**: a **GitLab job** works in its own picked project. | GitLab repository, job project |
| **GitLab source** | The `gitlab-account` job source (issue #1448 parts 2, 3): the hopper adds it while a **GitLab account** is attached, never the plugins config. It lists the **picked projects**' labelled open issues in Intake; one assigned to the account, of a trusted origin, with no end label, becomes a **GitLab job**; one of another origin is a **request card** a person approves (issue #1642); any other says why it is not taken. It writes only its labels to GitLab (`hopper:claimed`, then the end label). Paused with no account, no picked project, or an ended token. | GitLab intake |
| **GitLab job** | A job of a GitLab issue (issue #1448 part 3, its source `kind` `gitlab-account`): its `repo` is its **picked project**, never a GitHub repository. Its project is cloned from its GitLab; its git goes through the hopper (`/job/gitlab-git/`) with the **GitLab account**'s token, a push only to a branch of its own work; it opens its **merge request** with git's push options. The job page names the GitLab account that acts (**GitLab as**). | GitLab work, forge job |
| **Merge request** | GitLab's word for a pull request (issue #1448 part 3): what a **GitLab job** opens as it pushes its branch, with "Closes #N" in its description. A person reviews and merges it on GitLab, or the hopper does in yolo mode once its pipeline passed (issue #1642 part 2), at the head it read; the hopper follows it after the job ends, as a pull request. The job is done by it (issue #1642): one that closes the issue, open, not a draft, with no merge conflicts and no failed pipeline, or merged since the job began. | GitLab pull request, MR |
| **Shared rate limiter** | The one limiter every GitHub call and every **GitHub watch** of the hopper goes through (issue #436, `src/github-limiter/`): from GitHub's rate-limit headers, per credential and resource (a **rate-limit bucket**), it spreads the calls left evenly to the reset below a share of the limit, keeps a reserve, and holds calls after GitHub refused one for its rate. A `GET` asks conditionally (ETag): a 304 is a **cache hit** and costs no call. Its settings are the instance's, the hopper's admin's: Settings → GitHub rate limit. | rate limiter, throttle, GitHub throttle |
| **Pass reads** | Inside one **sync** of a GitHub source, each read of GitHub with the same arguments is asked once and its answer shared (issue #822, `src/sources/github/pass-reads.ts`). A write to a repository drops what was read of it; a failed read is not kept; nothing is kept past the sync. | sync cache, read cache |
| **Batched read** | The GraphQL reads of single issues and pull requests that a GitHub source asks with one credential at the same time, sent to GitHub as one request with each read an alias in it (issue #822, `src/sources/github/app/graphql-batch.ts`); at most 20 to a request. Each read gets its own answer or its own error. | combined query, bulk read |
| **Repository listing** | What one batched GraphQL sync of a GitHub source reads of a repository (issue #985, `src/sources/github/app/graphql-listing.ts`, `src/domain/github-listing.ts`): its open issues and pull requests that carry one of the source's labels, each with its labels, assignees and `updatedAt`, a pull request also with its head, base, mergeable and check rollup. Up to 25 repositories go in one request with one credential, each an alias; only a repository with more items than a page is asked again. A repository GitHub does not answer for fails only itself. | repo poll, repo scan |
| **Listing cache** | Each repository's latest **repository listing** and when it was read, in the user's store (issue #985, tenant migration 52, `src/store/github-listings.ts`). A new listing replaces the one before, so an item gone from it is gone from the cache. Derived data: lost, it is read again. Intake reads its issues from it (issue #985 part 2, `src/sources/github/listing.ts`), and lists a repository again once its listing is older than the **intake sync interval**. Not **pass reads**, which last one sync only. | issue cache, sync cache |
| **GitHub delivery** | One event GitHub sends the hopper over a webhook, direct or through a relay (issue #1079, `POST /github/webhook`, `src/http/github-deliveries.ts`): taken only when signed with the **GitHub webhook secret**, and only an `issues` event that can change intake (opened, edited, labeled, unlabeled, assigned, unassigned, closed, reopened). It makes the repository it names due at each job source whose job repository it is, which lists it again at a sync a few seconds later; `source.delivery_received`. It carries no job: the hopper still pulls. Not a **webhook subscription**'s delivery, which the hopper sends. | webhook event, push, hook call |
| **Change read** | A job source's conditional read of a job repository's issues changed since where its **listing cache** left off — the newest issue change its last listing or change read saw, kept apart from the rows (issue #1276) — (issue #1125, `issuesChangedSince`, `src/sources/github/listing.ts`): one REST call at each timed sync, a `304` (no call spent) while nothing changed. A new open issue with the label, or a listed one closed or unlabelled, makes the repository due, as a **GitHub delivery** does; a listed issue changed otherwise has its cache row read in place. What intake has with no webhook. | poll, probe |
| **GitHub webhook secret** | The key GitHub signs each **GitHub delivery** with (HMAC-SHA256, `X-Hub-Signature-256`; issue #1079): a **system secret** of the instance's vault (`github-webhook-secret`), made by the hopper on Settings → GitHub webhook and shown once, to paste into GitHub or the relay. A second make rotates it; removed, no delivery is taken. Not a **signing secret**, which signs what the hopper sends. | webhook secret (in prose only), hook secret |
| **Relay** (GitHub deliveries) | A program on a computer that reaches the hopper which takes GitHub's webhook deliveries and sends them on to `/github/webhook` (issue #1079), such as the GitHub CLI's `gh webhook forward`: GitHub needs no route to the hopper. | tunnel (that is remote access), proxy |
| **Intake sync interval** | How often intake lists the job repositories again, in minutes: `intakeSyncMinutes` of the shared rate limiter's settings (issue #985, default 15), the hopper's admin's on Settings → GitHub rate limit. Between listings, intake reads the **listing cache**; a sync a person asked for (Sync now) lists at once, and a **GitHub delivery** (issue #1079) or a **change read** (issue #1125) makes the repository due at the next sync. | poll interval (that is a source's `pollSeconds`, how often it syncs) |
| **Listed reads** | A job's reads of its issue — the issue and its comments — kept with the `updatedAt` its **listing cache** row showed, and answered from what was kept inside a discover or a check while that row shows no change (issue #985, `src/sources/github/listed-reads.ts`). A write to the repository drops them; a report, a follow or a rerun always reads GitHub (a follow reads the **in-motion cycle**'s batch first). Not **pass reads**, which last one sync only. | issue cache |
| **Call kind** | What a GitHub call was for, as Settings → GitHub rate limit counts the calls of the last hour (issue #985, `callKindOf`, `src/github-limiter/traffic.ts`): `intake`, `reconcile`, `pr_cycle` (the **in-motion cycle**), `per_job_read`, `write`, `watch` (a **GitHub watch**) or `job` (a running job's own request). Told from the port read the call was made under; the GitHub watches and the GitHub proxy name their own. A call is one GitHub charged: a 304 is a cache hit, not a call. A GraphQL call also counts its **points**, the drop in the graphql rate limit's calls left. | call type, category |
| **Deferred call** | A job source's GitHub call the **shared rate limiter** does not make, because it would wait more than a minute for the rate limit (issue #822). It says when it may go ("next retry at HH:MM UTC"); its work is kept and the next sync asks again. | throttled call, skipped call |
| **Intake reserve** | The calls of a **rate-limit bucket** the **shared rate limiter** keeps for job work (issue #983, `intakeReserve`, default 1000, Settings → GitHub rate limit as "Kept for job work"): at or below this many left, an **intake call** waits for the reset (a job source's is a **deferred call**), so a job can still report its end and a rerun can still read its issue. The reserve still holds for every call. | job reserve, lifecycle reserve |
| **Intake call** | A job source's GitHub read that looks for new jobs (issue #983): listing a repository's open labelled issues, the issues assigned to the account, a project's items, the installed repositories, a repository's event feed, and a repository's **change read** (issue #1125). Every other call of a job source is job work. | discovery call, poll call |
| **Rate-limit bucket** | What GitHub last told of one credential's rate limit for one resource (`core`, `graphql`, `search`): its limit, the calls left, when it resets, and a hold after a refusal (issue #436). No credential is ever shown. | quota, budget |
| **GitHub watch** | A poll of one GitHub item's state on an interval that fires on a change (the pointed-job architecture, issue #424; built in issue #427, `src/github-watch/`): one per **pointed job** that has not ended, on its issue or pull request, and one per job whose **thread ask** is posted and open (issue #435), with the user's own GitHub connection. Each poll reads a **watch snapshot** and compares it with the one stored before: a difference is one `github_watch.changed`, none is nothing. It polls through the **shared rate limiter** and the **poll schedule**, every **watch interval**. Not a **Watch** (a request a job waits on). | GitHub poller |
| **Pending approval** | A GitHub Actions run that waits for a deployment approval the user can give (issue #2088, design.md "Pending approvals"): `status=waiting`, with a pending deployment of an environment its connection can approve (`current_user_can_approve`), in a repository of an **intake org** that the app's installations reach and that is not archived (issue #2258; a personal access token's: one it reaches). One row on the Pending approvals page, with its repository, workflow, title, environments, waiting since and link. Read-only: the hopper never approves or rejects one. In code `PendingApproval`, `src/pending-approvals/`. | deployment request, deploy approval |
| **Intake org** | An owner (a GitHub organization or user) of a connected account's **job repositories** (issue #2088): the pending approvals read the repositories of each that the account reaches. `intakeOrgs`. | job org, source org |
| **Stale** (pending approval) | A **pending approval** when a newer waiting run of the same repository waits on each of its environments (issue #2088): an older promote candidate, for example. Shown last and marked "stale: a newer run waits"; still counted. | outdated, superseded |
| **Watch snapshot** | What one poll of a **GitHub watch** saw of its item (issue #427): its state (`open`, `closed` or `merged`, with GitHub's state reason), its labels, its comments and reviews (each by id, author and time, never their text) and the pull requests that reference it. The first poll stores one and records nothing. | state, cache |
| **Watch interval** | How often each **GitHub watch** polls (issue #427), in seconds, 10 to 86400, default 60: the user's setting, Settings → GitHub watches, an admin's. A change applies at once: the watches poll now, then on the new interval. | poll interval, period |
| **Poll schedule** | How **GitHub watches** poll (issue #436, `createPollSchedule`): each round of the poll interval in batches of the batch size, the batches spread evenly across the round, so many watches never ask GitHub at once. | poll queue, stagger |
| **Permission request** | An installation's of the hopper's app (issue #1378, `AppInstallation.permissionRequest`): the permissions the app asks for (`GET /apps/{slug}`) that the installation does not grant yet, which GitHub asked the installation's owner to accept when the app's owner added them. Empty: accepted. Sources shows it per owner, with the link where an owner reviews it. The hopper cannot start one: only the app's owner changes the app's permissions. | permission update, pending permissions, new permissions |
| **Checks read problem** | A repository whose pull request checks GitHub refuses to the source's connection (issue #1244, `ChecksProblem`, `checksProblems` on `GET /api/pull-requests`): one per repository, never per pull request, with why — the connection, the permissions it lacks, and the fix — and the open pull requests it holds. Made from the cards whose checks are `unknown` and marked `checksRefused`; a read that failed otherwise makes none. The Pull requests view says it once on the repository's group; Needs you counts it in its **Checks** row. Not a failure **problem**. | checks problem, permission alert |
| **Checks fallback** | The read of a pull request's checks over REST when GitHub refuses its GraphQL rollup and they are `unknown` (issue #1244, `checksFallback`, `src/sources/github/checks-fallback.ts`): its head commit's check runs and combined status, with the source's own connection, never another credential. Refused too, the checks stay `unknown` and say why (`checksWhy`): the connection, the permissions it lacks, and the fix; the card says `checks unknown: <why>`. A refused repository is not asked again for ten minutes. | auto-merge fallback, retry read |
| **Check log** | The log of a failed check that GitHub Actions ran (issue #993, `src/domain/check-logs.ts`): its job is the last number of the check's link. Before a `fix_checks` tend, the hopper reads it itself through the GitHub source (`JobSource.checkLogs`, the shared rate limiter, kept per run) and the **tend brief** quotes its **excerpt** — the lines that say something failed, each with the lines after it (its stack), and the test runner's summary, at most 40 lines — or why it could not be read. A job reads the whole log through the **GitHub proxy** (`hopper-gh check log <job> --repo <owner>/<name>`, op `check.log`). A job never asks a person for a log; it asks only when the hopper says why it cannot read it. Reading one needs the GitHub App's Actions read permission. | CI log, build log, job output |
| **GitHub proxy** | The hopper doing a GitHub operation a running job asks for (issue #563), with the job's own user's **connected account** (issue #1380; before it, the hopper's oldest user's) — so the job's machine needs no GitHub login and spends no device code. Operations: `issue.create`, `issue.comment`, `issue.view`, `pr.create` (from the job's pushed branch, on its own repository), `pr.view`, `check.log` (a failed check's full **check log**, issue #993), `repo.view`, and `issue.comments`, `pr.diff`, `pr.commits`, `pr.checks` (issue #1074), `pr.reviews` (issue #1072), `run.list`, `run.view` (issue #2318); writes only on that user's **job repositories**, reads — the **repo queries** — on any repository the connection sees (issue #1015). A job may ask all of them, and each names the **acting identity**. An issue filed through it carries no labels or assignees and ends with the hopper's note naming the job and request by id, so it never becomes a job without a person. Rate-limited per job and per machine; every request is `github_proxy.done`, `.refused` or `.failed` on the job's timeline. `POST /job/github`, `src/github-proxy/`. | GitHub bot, broker login, gh relay |
| **Repo query** | A running job's read of GitHub through the **GitHub proxy** (issue #1074, design.md "Repo queries"): `issue.view`, `issue.comments`, `pr.view`, `pr.diff`, `pr.commits`, `pr.checks`, `pr.reviews`, `repo.view`, `check.log`, `run.list`, `run.view` (issue #2318). Answered from the **repo-query cache** when it can be, else from GitHub; each answer says which (`source`), when GitHub was read (`readAt`), and, where the **work-item ledger** holds the item, its word on it (`ledger`). A job reads GitHub only so. | GitHub read, repo read, API read |
| **Repo-query cache** | The answers to **repo queries** the hopper keeps in memory, one cache for every job (issue #1074, `src/github-proxy/repo-query.ts`): an answer is given again for 30 seconds, and a merged pull request's for as long as it is kept; a job's own write drops what it changed. Not the shared rate limiter's ETag cache, which still makes the GitHub call (a 304); not the **listing cache**. | proxy cache, read cache |
| **Proxy token** | What a job shows the **GitHub proxy** (issue #563): an HMAC of its user and job under a key derived from that user's **link key**, so it is never stored and is the same at every start of the job until a **lost-machine move** takes it away from its machine, which gives it a new one (issue #1569: the agent left there holds the one before, refused); honoured only while the job is at work (running or waiting on an answer). Kept in the job's **credentials dir** (`hopper/token`, `HOPPER_TOKEN_FILE`), never in an environment variable. Not a GitHub token. | job token, API key |
| **hopper-gh** | The command a job asks the **GitHub proxy** with (issue #563): a POSIX sh and curl script kept in its **credentials dir** beside its **proxy token** (`hopper/gh`), run as `sh "$HOPPER_GH" …`; `HOPPER_URL` is the job's **hopper address**. Its `help` is the whole manual. A machine whose connection keeps no files, or that cannot reach the hopper, gives its jobs none. | gh wrapper |
| **pr status** | A job's read of where a pull request stands (issue #1072, `hopper-gh pr status N --repo OWNER/NAME`, op `pr.status`): its state, draft, mergeable, checks and failed checks, why the hopper has not merged it yet (`waits`), and its **work item**'s state. Answered from the hopper's records first — the Pull requests list and the **work-item ledger**, the same answer a person sees —, then from a GitHub read of the last minute; GitHub is read only for a pull request the hopper does not know. `from` says which: `hopper`, `cache` or `github`. | pr checks, PR poll |
| **pr wait** | A job's wait until a pull request's checks end (issue #1072 part 2, `hopper-gh pr wait N --repo OWNER/NAME [--timeout SECONDS]`, op `pr.wait`, asked as **pr status**). On a pull request the hopper follows whose checks run, the hopper opens a watch on the job's **job stream** and ends it, with the pull request's status, when its own records show the checks ended or the pull request merged or closed — told by its events, never by a timer or a GitHub read; a job on its own wait (HOPPER_WAITING) is woken with the answer. Otherwise it answers at once: `wait: settled`, or `wait: none` for one the hopper does not follow. Not polling: the job asks once. |
| **pr edit** | A job's change of a pull request's title or body (issue #1734, `hopper-gh pr edit N --repo OWNER/NAME [--title TITLE] [--body TEXT \| --body-file FILE]`, op `pr.edit`), or its close with no merge (`hopper-gh pr close N`, op `pr.close`). Only for a pull request the job opened through the **GitHub proxy**, its **work item**'s, or the one it is the **tend job** of (issue #2127); an edit also of one its issue's title or body names — `#N`, `PR #N`, `pull request #N`, `owner/name#N` or its URL, a bare `#N` in code not —, read from its **item snapshot** (issues #2151, #2227); any other is refused, with why. | pr update |
| **run list**, **run view** | A job's read of a repository's GitHub Actions workflow runs (issue #2318, `hopper-gh run list --repo OWNER/NAME [--branch B] [--event E]`, op `run.list`: the newest 50, each run's id, name, event, branch, status and conclusion) and of one run's jobs (`hopper-gh run view RUN --repo OWNER/NAME`, op `run.view`: each job's id, name, status and conclusion; the id is the job of a **check log**). **Repo queries**: a run that no pull request names, such as a branch's push runs. | gh run, actions read |
| **pr reviews** | A job's read of a pull request's reviews (issue #1072 part 3, `hopper-gh pr reviews N --repo OWNER/NAME`, op `pr.reviews`), a **repo query**: each review (author, state, body, when) and the `decision` — the latest review of each reviewer that approves, asks for changes or was dismissed decides: `changes_requested`, `approved` or `none`. | review status, approvals |
| **GitHub calls** (of a job) | The calls the hopper made on GitHub for one job (issue #1072 part 3): its reads that reached GitHub (`github_proxy.done` with `source: github`) and its writes and pushes. Its **parts list** shows them as the part `github`, beside the reads the hopper answered with no call (`source: hopper` or `cache`). | API calls, GitHub requests, rate-limit use |
| **Bug report** | A report of a hopper bug that a person sends with **Report a bug** (issue #694): a title, what happened, what they expected, and the **report context**, filed on the hopper's own repository (`henningfutrell/hopper`) through that person's own GitHub **connected account**, so it is filed as them. It gets the label `user-report` — no `hopper` label and no assignee — so it never starts a job; an open one with a similar title is offered first, to add a comment to. `POST /ui/api/bug-reports`, `src/bug-reports/`. A running job files one about itself with `hopper-gh report bug`, through its own user's connection, marked as a job's, with no job id. When GitHub cannot take it now it is a **kept report**. Each one the person or their jobs filed is listed in **Your reports**. | issue report, feedback, ticket |
| **Kept report** | A **bug report** the hopper could not file when it was sent — no GitHub connection, or GitHub out of reach, refusing access to its repository, rate-limited or failing — kept in the person's store and sent later, by the same connection, as it was (issue #694). State `waiting` in **Your reports** until it is sent. `bug_report.kept`, then `bug_report.filed` with `later`. | queued report, pending report, offline report |
| **Your reports** | Settings → Your reports (issue #694): each **bug report** the person or their jobs filed, newest first, with its state — `waiting` (a **kept report**, with why), `open` or `closed` on GitHub, as last read. The hopper sends the kept ones and reads the states every 10 minutes; Refresh does it now. `GET /api/bug-reports`, `POST /ui/api/bug-reports/refresh`, `hopper report list|refresh`. | my reports, report history |
| **Report context** | What a **bug report** carries about the hopper, filled in by the hopper and shown to the person to read and edit before sending (issue #694): the System line only (issue #2478) — the platform, its release, the architecture and the Node version. Never the hopper's version or revision, its install type, a job or its failure: none of them belongs in a public issue, and the person adds what they want to say. Masked first: the host name, IP addresses and private host names. | diagnostics, telemetry |
| **Skill** | One thing the hopper can set up for a running job, baked into the hopper (issue #582, `src/skills/`): a name, one **catalog** line, and the full text the job reads only when it loads it. A skill with a **link** sets up access to an **asset**: Access must allow the box's **template** that operation on that asset first. A skill may need a credential (issue #583): it names the kinds it takes and how to get one, and when the box's template gives none the vault opens a **credential request**. The broker answers a load with the text, that a person is asked, or a clear no with the reason; never silence. Not a Claude Code skill, and not a plugin. | capability, tool, MCP tool |
| **Credential request** | What the vault asks a person for when a job on a box needs a credential its **template** does not give (issue #583, design.md "The dynamic vault"): for a **skill** that needs one, or for any other service in the job's own words (`--credential`). One open per template and skill or service, the jobs that wait on it and why, the kinds it takes (the first suggested) and how to get one. A person gives one — any kind; it becomes a **vault secret** in the template's scope — or declines. Kept in memory only; a job's **watch** on it is kept in the database, so after a restart the job's next subscription opens it again (issue #613). Vault, **Asked for**. | credential prompt, secret request, ask (an *ask* is a box's request for one vault secret) |
| **Vault grant** | A person's grant of the vault to a job's item (issue #652, design.md "Vault grants"): until there is one, a job on a box gets no vault secret, shared secret or minted credential, whatever its **template** gives. Kept for the item's source key (a job of no source: its id), so Run again and Rework keep it. Given or revoked on Vault, panel **Vault grants**, through the UI session; Access decides (OpenFGA `vault_grant#can_grant`). It widens nothing: the template's approved scope still bounds what a granted job gets. Shown with what it covers (issue #1124): the item's title, repository and link, its newest job's status, the date, and how it was made (Grant, `hopper vault grant`, or a **direct job**'s submission). **Stale** when no job of its item waits or is at work: it gives nothing now, and a person may revoke it. | vault permission, job grant, vault access |
| **Standing read grant** | A user's setting, off by default (issue #1472, design.md "The standing read grant"): on, a job on a box of a **template** whose needs the **read policy** holds entirely as reads, with no vault secret, gets the **vault grant** with no person, recorded as `vault.granted` by `standing read grant`. Any other template still waits for a person's grant. Kept for the job while it is at work, never for its item; turned off, it grants no job that has not asked yet. An admin's toggle in Vault, panel Sandbox approvals. | default grant, auto grant, read grant |
| **Grant ask** | What the hopper shows when a job on a box asked the vault with no **vault grant** (issue #652): the job, its box and template, what it asked for, its item. Kept in memory only, while the job is at work. A person grants the item the vault, or not. Or a person, or a **delegate approver**, denies the ask (issue #1138): it goes, and that job asks no more. | grant request, vault request |
| **Skill catalog** | What a job is answered when it asks the hopper what it can set up (`sh "$HOPPER_SKILL"`): one short line per **skill**, and how to load one. Few tokens; no skill's full text. | menu, tool list |
| **Served library skill** | A built-in **library skill** a job may also load by name through **hopper-skill** (issue #1909, `src/skills/library.ts`): `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" work` answers its `SKILL.md`, headed by its **library skill version**, the same files the work skill's credentials carry. The one is `work` (issue #2145; before it, `orchestrate`, now a **skill alias** of it). Not a **run skill**. | bundled skill, built-in skill (all library skills are) |
| **Repository copy** | A repository's own copy of a **served library skill** (a workflow repository's `workflow-orchestrate`, issue #1909): the job follows it, as the repository's rules win, and says so with `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" work --repo-copy DIR`; `skill.loaded` keeps its folder, git tree, commit and whether it had changes, beside the hopper's version. | repo override, local skill |
| **Fan-out key** | The **resend key** a job chooses for a sub-task it files (`hopper-gh issue create --required --key fanout-<task>`, issue #1909): the same key from the same job, also after it resumed elsewhere, or from a later job of the same issue (a Run again), is answered the issue filed first, so nothing is filed twice; another issue's job files its own. | task key, dedupe key |
| **Run skill** | Text the hopper sends to the user's own AI CLI for the length of one run, built into the hopper (issue #472, `src/run-skills/`, design.md "Run skills"): the plain files of one directory, named by a content hash (its **run skill id**), checked whole before the run, sent on stdin and never written on the machine. The first is `file-issue`, which drafts an **issue draft**. Not a **skill** (what the hopper sets up for a box), and not a Claude Code skill. | prompt pack, bundle, built-in skill |
| **Skill library** | The skills the hopper keeps for jobs (issue #1911, `src/skill-library/`, design.md "The skill library"): the **built-in library skills** that ship with the hopper — `work`, `research`, `proposal`, `iac`, `gitops-deploy` — and the user's own, each kept as **library skill versions** in the user's database, never on a machine. Read at `GET /api/skill-library`; changed by an admin at `POST /ui/api/skill-library` (`create`, `edit`, `duplicate`, `remove`, each a permission of its own). | skill store, skill registry, prompt pack |
| **Library skill** | One skill of the **skill library**: a name (lowercase letters, digits and dashes), a `SKILL.md` that a job reads first, and the text files beside it (scripts, assets), at most 32 files and 256 KiB. A built-in one is never edited or removed: a person **duplicates** it to change it. Not a **skill** (what the hopper sets up for a box) and not a **run skill**. | skill (that is the broker's), workflow skill, bundle |
| **Library skill version** | One kept state of a **library skill**'s files (`LibrarySkillVersion`): its content id (`version`, 16 hex over its files, as a run skill's id), its place `seq` (1, 2, …; 0 for a built-in one), where it came from (`created`, `duplicated` from a skill's version, `imported` from a repository's folder, `built-in`), who kept it and when. Never changed once kept; an edit keeps a new one, and the same files keep none. A removed skill's versions stay for the jobs that got them. | revision, release |
| **Imported skill** | A **library skill** whose newest version the hopper read from a folder of a GitHub repository (issue #1911, design.md "Importing a skill"), with the user's own connection: its source names the repository, the folder, the ref it follows and the commit read. **Refresh** reads the folder again at that ref and keeps a new version only when its files changed; a skill a person edited since its import is not refreshed. | synced skill, linked skill, mirror |
| **Kind of work** | What a job is asked to do, for its **work skill** (`WorkKind`): `code` (the work itself), `research` or `proposal`, read from its spec as its **job cost kind** is. | job type, job kind, mode |
| **Work skill** | The **library skill** a job gets for its **kind of work** (issue #1911): chosen at its first start by the **work skill settings** and kept on its timeline (`job.work_skill_chosen`: kind, name, version, `seq`, and which setting chose it), so each later start, on any machine, gets the same **library skill version**; an edit or a new setting reaches the next job. Its files go into the job's **credentials dir** under `work-skill/<name>/`, `HOPPER_WORK_SKILL` names its `SKILL.md`, and the job rules end with its line (`[hopper work skill]`), for any agent CLI. | job skill, orchestration skill |
| **Required skill** | A **library skill** that another one names in its `SKILL.md` front matter's `requires:` (issue #1911, design.md "Skills that require others"): a job gets it, and the skills it requires in turn, beside its **work skill**, under `work-skill/<name>/`, at most 8, each version kept for the job with the work skill's (`job.work_skill_chosen` `requires`). One the library does not have is said in `missing` and in the job's line, and the job goes on without it. The built-in `work` requires none: since issue #2145 it holds what the built-in `orchestrate` held. | dependency, sub-skill, included skill |
| **Skill alias** | Another name of a built-in **library skill** (issue #2145, `SKILL_ALIASES` in `src/domain/skill-library.ts`): `orchestrate` names `work`. A `requires:`, a **work skill** setting, a **skill rule**, a read of the library or a load through **hopper-skill** that names the alias gets the skill it names, and records that skill's name; no skill takes the alias as its name. | skill synonym, redirect |
| **Work skill settings** | The user's choice of each **kind of work**'s **work skill** (`WorkSkillSettings`, the user's settings key `workSkills`): `kinds` for every repository, `repos` per repository (`owner/name` lowercased), which wins; a kind no setting names gets the built-in default (`code` → `work`, `research` → `research`, `proposal` → `proposal`). An admin's, at `POST /ui/api/work-skills` (`work_skills.changed`). A skill a setting names cannot be removed. | skill assignment, skill mapping |
| **Skill rule** | One rule of the **work skill settings**' `rules` (issue #1924, `SkillRule`): a **library skill** and the conditions a job must match to get it as a **conditional skill** — `repos` (`owner/name`), `labels` (of the job's item, any case; a label such as `iac` declares the job's area), `kinds` (its **kind of work**), `paths` (globs of the files the change touches, issue #1924 part 2: `*.tf` — no slash, a file name in any folder —, `src/**`; not known at the start, so the rule gives its skill then and the done check applies its **done criteria** only when the pull request changes a matching file) —, each a list of which one value must match, all conditions named together. At least one condition. An admin's, at `POST /ui/api/work-skills` (`rules`, all of them), or on the Skills page's Skill rules panel (part 5). A skill a rule names cannot be removed. | skill trigger, skill selector, job type |
| **Conditional skill** | A **library skill** a job gets beside its **work skill** because a **skill rule** matched what it works on (issue #1924): chosen at its first start with the work skill and kept on its timeline (`job.conditional_skills_chosen`: each skill's name, version, `seq`, `why` — the conditions that matched — and its **done criteria**), so each later start gets the same versions. Its files go into the job's **credentials dir** under `conditional-skill/<name>/`, `HOPPER_CONDITIONAL_SKILLS` names their index, and the job rules end with their line (`[hopper conditional skills]`). | extra skill, job-type skill, injected skill |
| **Allowed delete** | A resource address the job's issue lists after a line `Allowed deletes:` (issue #1924 part 2, `allowedDeletesIn`): a terraform plan that destroys or replaces it, by its resource line (`# <address> will be destroyed`, `must be replaced`), does not count against `plan-destroy`. Read from the text the job runs (its item's snapshot), never from the live issue or the job. A plan summary that names no address counts every delete. | approved delete, delete allowlist |
| **Done criteria** | What a **library skill**'s `done.json` asks of a job's own pull request — one that ships a part too (part 4), or a GitLab job's merge request, whose head pipeline's jobs and their logs are its checks and its notes its comments (part 4) — before the job is done (issue #1924, `DoneCriterion`), checked by the hopper itself, never taken from the job: `checks-passed` — every check whose name matches `checks` (`*` any text) ran and passed —, `plan-destroy` — no terraform plan in those checks' output or the pull request's comments since its head commit destroys or replaces more than `max` (0) resources, and one is there; a delete of an **allowed delete** does not count —, and `person-merges` (part 3) — a person merges a pull request that changes a file its `paths` match (all, absent; files not known count), never **Yolo mode**; no miss at the done check. A miss is the cause **Done criteria unmet**: the job is sent back to rework, and at the retry limit it is a person's, with the reason. Yolo mode reads them again just before its own merge, and one not met, or a `person-merges` that applies, holds the merge (`mergeError`) until the next sync; a person's Merge now still merges. | acceptance criteria, definition of done, gates |
| **Skills page** | The skill library's own top-level view (issue #1911, `#skills`, design.md "The Skills page"): the **work skill settings** per kind of work, for every repository and per repository, and the **library** — each **library skill** with its source and version — where an admin creates, edits, duplicates and removes skills. | skill manager, skills settings |
| **Issue draft** | What a `file-issue` **run skill** answers (issue #472): a title, a body, suggested labels and notes for the user, with the **draft constraints** it breaks and the run skill id that made it. The user reads and edits it; nothing goes to GitHub until they file it (issue #471). | draft issue |
