| **Draft constraint** | One rule of what an **issue draft** may hold (issues #472, #475, `src/run-skills/constraints.ts`): an id, the line the skill's prompt carries, and the check the hopper runs against the **draft scope**. One list for both, so they cannot drift. | rule, policy |
| **Draft probe** | Before an **issue draft**, the check of whether a **draft provider** can draft on the machine the user picked, and the models it lists there (issue #474, `src/drafting/probe.ts`, design.md "Drafting on the user's AI CLI"): the machine's refusals, then the CLI's own sign-in status and model list there, never a model call and never the account it names. A client target is usable for claude, unchecked. | provider check, preflight |
| **Draft provider** | The AI CLI an **issue draft** runs on (issue #474, `src/drafting/`, design.md "Drafting on the user's AI CLI"): `claude`, `codex`, `cursor-agent`, `opencode` or `omp`, signed in on the user's machine under that machine's account, the user's pick with the machine. One locked-down turn: no tools, no session kept, no user settings where the CLI can leave them out. The hopper holds no model key for it and never reads the CLI's login. | drafter, AI provider, model provider |
| **Draft scope** | What one user's **issue drafts** are checked against (issue #475, design.md "Draft constraints"): the repositories they may target, the user's own names (machines, host names, accounts, name, email) and the values of secrets the hopper holds. The caller gives it, never the model. | allow-list, context |
| **Draft mask** | One kind of change the hopper made to an **issue draft** or its context before the user saw it (issue #475): the **draft constraint** it serves and how many times, never the value. | redaction, scrub |
| **Windows host** | A Windows computer a person set up for **Windows sandbox steps** (issue #731): a name, an ssh destination, its pinned host key and a working directory, kept in the user's config record `windows-hosts` (Settings → Windows hosts), and the vault secrets its steps may be given (`secrets`). OpenSSH with key auth and PowerShell; no hopper client, lane or agent runs there. Not a machine: no job is routed to it. | Windows seat, Windows box (a **Windows machine** is a machine) |
| **Windows label** | `hopper:win` or `hopper:windows` on an item (any case, issues #918, #1350): the work needs Windows. Its job goes only to a **Windows machine**; while none can take it, it waits queued (`waiting for a Windows lane: …`), never on another machine. Its issue context tells it that it runs on a Windows machine. | win label, Windows routing |
| **Windows machine** | A machine whose option `windows` is true (issue #1350, `MachineSnapshot.windows`): the only kind that takes a job with a **Windows label**. It takes other jobs too. Set by a person; never guessed from a probe. Not a **Windows host**. | Windows seat, Windows lane (a lane of a Windows machine) |
| **Box step** | One step of a job's work run by the hopper in a sandbox of the template the job's own **sandbox request** was approved for, when the job asks (`sh "$HOPPER_STEP" box TEMPLATE`, issues #1004, #1349): a **need step** in a **need box** of that template, made for the step and burned after it, minted each of the template's AWS roles, clusters and namespaces it reads — and with an approved plan (`--plan-id`) its changes too. The template's vault secrets come too, after the job's **vault grant** (issue #1875): copied into the need box as files, read there with `"$HOPPER_SECRET" get NAME`, never in its environment, never to the job. Nothing is kept between steps. The job stays on its own machine. Events `step.started`, `step.finished`, `step.refused`, each with `box`. | box exec, remote command, sandbox command |
| **Windows sandbox step** | One step of a job's work that needs Windows, run by the hopper on a **Windows host** over ssh, in PowerShell, in its working directory, when the job asks (`sh "$HOPPER_STEP" run HOST`, issue #731): the inputs copied there first, the step's output streamed back, then its exit code and its outputs. Kept in the instance's `windows_steps` table from the ask to an hour after its result and run by its `windows-step` **saga** (issue #1611), so a stop of the hopper loses no step and no result, and never runs a step twice. One at a time per host, by its **host lease**; the host's scratch directory is swept after each step. A step may ask for vault secrets its host gives (`--secret NAME`): after the job's vault grant they go into the step's environment only. Events `step.started`, `step.finished`, `step.refused`, `vault.step_delivered`, `vault.step_refused`. | Windows job, remote step, Windows lane |
| **Host lease** (Windows step) | A **Windows sandbox step**'s lease on its **Windows host** (issue #1611, `leaseHost`, `src/domain/windows-steps.ts`): in the database, one step of a user at a time per host, across hoppers. The step takes it before it runs there and releases it once the host's scratch directory is swept; it lasts as long as the step's **saga**, so a hopper that stops leaves it to the next one. A step that took it once is never run again: a stop then ends the step with why, unless it was asked with **rerun**. Not a **hold** (a decider's) nor a saga's **lease**. | host hold, host lock, queue slot |
| **Rerun** (Windows step) | A **Windows sandbox step** the job asked with `--rerun` (`rerun=1`, issue #2117): it says the step is safe to run again (it only reads, or does the same each time). A stop of the hopper that cuts it short does not end it: the next try sweeps the host's scratch and runs it again from the start, its log says so, at most 3 runs in all (`STEP_RUNS_MAX`). Not a job's **Re-run**, and no box or need step takes it. | retry, resume, replay |
| **Need step** | One command of a running job's work that needs an account or a cluster (AWS, Kubernetes, helm, terraform), run by the hopper in a **need box** when the job names its **needs** (`sh "$HOPPER_STEP" need NEEDS --command …`, issue #1007): the job stays on its own machine, which never holds the credential. The step runs in a ready template that declares every need (more too); none, and it is asked for as a **template request** is, the step waiting until it is ready. The vault mints each need's **minted credential** for that step only, after Access and, for a change, the apply gate. Its output streams back; then its exit code and its outputs. Events `step.started` (the need box as `box`, its `template` and `needs`), `step.finished`, `step.refused`, `vault.minted`. Not a **Windows sandbox step**, which runs on a **Windows host**; a **box step** (issue #1349) is a need step of the template its sandbox request names. Since issue #1568 every need step, a **box step** too, is kept in the instance's `need_steps` table (`NeedStep`, `src/domain/need-steps.ts`) from the job's ask to an hour after its end — its **step ask key**, its **saga** (`need-step`), its inputs, log, result and outputs — so the job reads its log and result from any host, after a restart too; one whose **need box** started before a stop is never run again. | infra step, Linux step, remote command |
| **Need box** | The one-shot container a **need step** or **box step** runs in (issues #1007, #1349): its template's image with its tools, under the sandbox flags, the step's script in place of the entrypoint, labelled `io.hopper.need`, named `hopper-need-<template>-<id>`; its credentials and inputs in its environment, its outputs back in its output after a mark, with their **outputs sha256**; it starts only on a home volume it made (issue #1371), and is burned — removed with that volume — when the step ends, and kept in the **sandbox runs** ledger. Like a **check box**, it joins no hopper, is no machine, has no lane and takes no job. One that outlives its step, or a home it leaves, the need box sweep burns, beside the hopper and on each client target dialled in (issue #1349). | step container, job box |
| **Outputs sha256** | The sha256 of a **need step**'s outputs tar, made in its **need box** and printed after the outputs (issue #2288, `outputsSha256`): the hopper computes it again on arrival, reads the box's log once more on a difference, and ends the step with a problem and no outputs when they still differ or the box gave none. Kept with the step's result, in `step.finished`, and sent to the job in `x-hopper-step-outputs-sha256`, which `hopper-step` checks before it unpacks. | checksum, hash, digest of each file |
| **Mint binding** | The Secret a sandbox's Kubernetes token is bound to (issue #1371, `MintBinding`, `src/vault/bindings.ts`): the minter makes it, labelled `io.hopper.mint`, in the service account's namespace, and asks for the token bound to it; when the need box or check box ends, whatever its outcome, the hopper deletes it and the API server refuses the token within its token cache time, 10 s by default (`vault.unbound`). Recorded before the minter makes it (`vault.binding_named`, issue #1628) and as `bound` on the mint's event, so the reconcile pass unbinds one a hopper that stopped left. An AWS session or an EKS token has none: it lives to its floor. | token binding, bound object, revocation secret |
| **Saga** | Work of several steps that crosses a process, machine or system boundary, kept as one record in the instance's `sagas` table (issue #1524, `Saga`, `src/domain/sagas.ts`): its kind, its key (one saga per kind and key), its user, its state (`running`, `compensating`, `done`, `compensated`, `failed`), its **saga steps** in order, its data (what its steps need and made) and its next try. The **saga coordinator** drives it under a **lease**, so it goes on after a stop, on any host, from its last committed step. Not a **work item**, which is one item of a source. | work item, workflow, job, transaction |
| **Saga step** | One step of a **saga**: a do, idempotent by its **step key** (`<saga>/<step>`, the same at each try and on each host), and maybe a **compensation** that undoes it. `pending` (it may have started), `done` or `compensated`, with its tries and last error. | stage, task, activity |
| **Compensation** (saga) | The undo of a **saga step** (a sandbox burned, a credential revoked, a worktree removed), run newest first for each step done and the step whose do started, when the saga is aborted or a step fails past its tries. Idempotent, and a no-op when there was nothing to undo. | rollback, revert (a revert is GitHub's), cleanup |
| **Lease** (saga) | A host's hold on a **saga** (owner, until, on the database's clock, issue #1954): while it lasts no other host drives the saga; the holder renews it while a step runs, and a host that stops renews nothing, so the lease ends and any host's **saga coordinator** claims the saga. | lock, claim (a job is claimed by a lane) |
| **Saga coordinator** | The one part per process that drives **sagas** (`src/sagas/coordinator.ts`): a step at a time, each recorded before the next, under its **lease**, with tries and backoff from the record; it takes the sagas due at start and every 5 s. | saga runner, orchestrator, scheduler |
| **Kill point** | A named place in the hopper's code where a test of the **crash harness** ends the process with SIGKILL (issue #1954, `src/sagas/kill-points.ts`): before and after each **saga step**'s do and each **compensation**, `saga:<kind>:<step>:before-do|after-do|before-compensate|after-compensate`; and before and after each call of the **GitHub outbox** (issue #1959), `outbox:<op>:before-send|after-send`, the second once GitHub answered and before the rows are `sent`; and before and after each write a job asks the **GitHub proxy** for (issue #1959 part 2, `src/github-proxy/resend.ts`), `proxy:<op>:before-send|after-send`, also a job's push through the git broker (issue #1954 part 4, `proxy:git.push:before-send|after-send`), and `proxy:issue.create:before-link` between a required child and its sub-issue link; inside a person's act on a job (issue #1959 part 3), `follow:merge:after-call` once GitHub merged, `follow:merge:after-issue-close` between the issue's close and its comment, and `give-back:rerun:after-give` between Run again's give-back and its new job; and before and after the commit of an assessment (issue #1961), `failures:assess:before-commit|after-commit`; and after each GitHub write of the landing batches' runner, before its event (issue #1954 part 8, `src/landing-batches/once.ts`), `landing:<write>:after-call`; and in the item reconciler (issue #1954 part 9, `src/work-items/reconcile.ts`), `item:block:inside-tx` inside a block's transaction, `item:<kind>:after-record` once a correction's event is kept, `item:run_again:before-call|after-call` around a run again, and `give-back:rerun-by-<by>:after-give` between its give-back and its new job. Armed only in a test run that names it (`HOPPER_TEST_RUN` and `HOPPER_TEST_KILL_POINT`); a deploy passes each one. | crash point, failpoint, breakpoint |
| **Crash harness** | The tests that run hoppers as processes of their own over one test database and end one with SIGKILL at a **kill point**, or drop its database connection at a statement, then let another hopper go on (issue #1954, `test/crash/`). Each effect is counted at the outside service's fake; a **randomised run** picks the kill points by a seed it prints. | chaos test, fault injection |
| **Fake model server** | A loopback server that the real agent CLIs talk to in place of a model, with a dummy key (issue #1958, `test/support/fake-model-server.ts`): it speaks the Anthropic Messages API and the OpenAI Responses and Chat Completions APIs, streamed or not, and answers a script of **model steps**, one per turn of the conversation: text, a tool call, or a fault (429, overloaded, a server error, a stream cut mid-delta, a malformed event, slow tokens). It is also the run's proxy, so a CLI's other calls are refused there and recorded. The **acceptance tests** (`test/acceptance/`) run the real print-mode CLIs through the real executors against it. | mock LLM, stub model |
| **Model step** | One turn of a **fake model server**'s script (`ModelStep`): what the model answers at that turn. A side call of a CLI (one that offers no tools: a title, a quota check) takes no step. | model turn, fixture |
| **MCP server double** | A loopback MCP server for the **acceptance tests** (issue #1958, `test/support/fake-mcp-server.ts`): it speaks MCP's Streamable HTTP transport with JSON answers, offers one tool (`echo`), and keeps each tool call in memory for the test. | mock MCP, MCP stub |
| **Screen-drift check** | The nightly check that the screens the herdr fakes record (`src/executors/herdr/fake-screens.ts`) are still what the real Claude Code TUI renders in a real herdr pane (issue #1958, `test/acceptance/tui-screens.test.ts`, `test/support/screen-drift.ts`): every recorded line must be on the real screen, whitespace aside, and the check names the first one that is not. | screenshot test, snapshot test |
| **Job lease** | A hopper's hold on a claimed or running job (issue #1662, `JobLease`, `src/domain/job-leases.ts`; the user's `job_leases` table): its owner (the engine of one hopper) and until when (on the database's clock, issue #1954). The claim takes it, the runner renews it while it drives the job and releases it when the run ends or the hopper stops; a job with no live job lease is **taken over** by any hopper on the same database, as a restart recovers it. | lock, claim (a job is claimed by a lane), ownership |
| **Claim lock** | The lock under which one hopper at a time reads and applies a Decision for a user's jobs (issue #1662, `JobLeaseRepository.lockClaims`): a transaction-level advisory lock, so two hoppers on one database never claim one job twice. | decision lock, mutex |
| **Takeover** | What a hopper does with a claimed or running job whose **job lease** ended (issue #1662, `takeOver`, `src/engine/recovery.ts`): it takes the lease, then reattaches, moves, requeues or fails the job as a restart recovery does; `job.reattached` says `its hopper stopped`. A running job on an open **job-run saga** whose executor reattaches is the saga pass's to take up instead. | failover, adoption (a pane answer adopts a waiting job) |
| **Sweep lease** | A host's hold on one sweep, recovery or engine pass of one user (issues #1627, #1662, `SweepLease`, `src/domain/leases.ts`; design.md "Sweep leases: one host per sweep"): a row of the instance's `sweep_leases` table, its name (`<user id>/<sweep>`), owner and until (on the database's clock, issue #1954). Only its holder runs the sweep; the host renews every lease it holds every 10 s and releases them at its stop, so a host that stops leaves each to another host 30 s on. Modelled on the saga **lease**. | lock, mutex, leader election |
| **Host lease** | The **sweep lease** a host holds from its start to its stop (`host/<owner>`, issue #1627): while it is live, so is the host. Another host reads it before it fails a **tool sign-in** that host runs. | heartbeat, presence |
| **Daemon lock** | The lock each daemon takes on its database at its start (`holdDaemonLock`, issue #212): shared, so several hoppers run on one database (issue #1662). An operator command that needs the database alone takes it exclusive (`holdSoleLock`), and refuses while any daemon holds it. | single-writer lock, database lock |
| **Job-run saga** | A claim's run as the **saga** kind `job-run` (issue #1662, `src/engine/job-run.ts`), keyed by the job and its attempt: its steps claim, credentials, pane, run, checkpoint, end, done by the runner of whichever hopper holds the saga. Its data keeps the job's way to the hopper as placed on its machine and the executor's session state, never a secret. Its lease is the hopper's hold on the run: when it ends, another hopper takes the run up from its last committed step. | job saga, run workflow |
| **Relayed act** | A person's cancel or park, or a preemption, of a job that another hopper drives (issue #1662, `RelayedAct`, `src/domain/job-leases.ts`): written on the job's **job lease**, and done by the hopper that holds that lease at its next tick, once. | forwarded command, remote cancel, message |
| **Job-move saga** | The **saga** of kind `job-move` a **lost-machine move** of kind `away` begins (issue #1569, `src/engine/job-move-saga.ts`), one per job and attempt: its one step, `end-left`, ends the agent the job left on the lost machine once that machine answers (Claude exits, the job's scope stops, the pane closes; the work tree stays), tried again for about a week on any host. Until it is done the job is not placed back on that machine. | fence, cleanup saga |
| **Sandbox run** | One sandbox the hopper made for one step of a job (a **need step** or a **box step**), from its start to its burn (issue #1349, `SandboxRun`, `src/domain/sandbox-runs.ts`): a row of the instance's `sandbox_runs` table with its job, step id, template, need box, machine, start, end, outcome (`succeeded`, `failed`, `timed-out`: past its time limit, `cancelled`: the job stopped waiting for it, `interrupted`: a hopper that stopped mid-step left it, ended at the next start, issue #1371, `swept`: the sweep burned it, its step not ended), exit code, and when it was seen destroyed — or, `machineRemoved`, when the hopper closed it because its client target was removed. The ledger of sandboxes, since a sandbox is no machine; `GET /api/sandboxes` answers the user's latest (`runs`). Not a **sandbox record**, which holds a sandbox box that joined as a machine. Access requests' panel **Sandbox runs** lists **sandbox requests**. | sandbox job, box run, step box |
| **Pane key** | The key of a job's herdr tab (issue #1569, design.md "The pane key", `src/executors/herdr/pane-key.ts`): `<jobId first 8> · <attempts>.<n>` — the job, its attempt and the pane of the run —, in the tab's label after the lane. A create of the tab whose answer was lost is tried again, and the tab it made is found by this key (`findTab`), so no second tab is made. Not a **step ask key** or a **saga step**'s key. | tab id, pane id, idempotency token |
| **Keyed create** | A call through a **client target**'s link that creates something there — a box (`POST /runtime`, a container create) or a herdr tab (`POST /herdr`, `tab create`) — keyed by the name it makes and a hash of the whole call (issue #1628, `src/client/once.ts`). The client answers a create sent again with the first answer for its key, for 10 minutes, and creates nothing again; a create that created nothing is not kept. In the client's memory only. | idempotency key, dedupe cache |
| **Resend key** | The key `hopper-gh` sends with each issue create and comment (`key=`, issue #1607), and each pull request create (issue #1959 part 4): one per command, the same each time it sends the request again after no answer came back. The hopper answers a key of the job's that is already done with its first answer (`replayed`), so a lost answer files nothing twice. Kept on `github_proxy.done`, and on `github_proxy.sending` before the call (issue #1959 part 2): a key that was sending with no answer recorded is looked for on GitHub by its **post key** before the write is made again. A job may choose it with `--key` (issue #1909): a **fan-out key**. Not the **step ask key**. | idempotency key, request id, dedupe token |
| **Post key** | The key the hopper writes at the end of a new issue or comment it posts for a **promotion** or a **bug report** (issue #1591, design.md "Posted once", `src/domain/post-key.ts`): the id of the row it keeps before GitHub is asked, as a hidden line. For a job's issue, comment or pull request with a **resend key** (issue #1959 parts 2 and 4, design.md "The GitHub proxy under crashes"), `proxy-` and a hash of the resend key and the job's issue: a send again after a crash looks for it. Before a create, the hopper looks for it on GitHub, so a stop or a lost answer links what GitHub made, never posts a second. A promotion's row on its way is a **promotion send**; a bug report's is `sending`. Not a **pane key** or a **step ask key**. | idempotency token, marker, dedupe key |
| **Promotion send** | A **promotion**'s new issue or comment on its way (issue #1591): kept before GitHub is asked, its id the **post key**. GitHub's answer, or its key found on GitHub at the next promotion or start, makes it the promotion; found nowhere, it goes. | pending promotion, draft |
| **Step ask key** | The key a job sends with the ask of a **need step** or a **box step** (`key=`, issue #1568): one step per job and key, so the same ask sent again is the same step, never a second one. `hopper-step` makes one per ask. Not the **saga step**'s key (`<saga>/<step>`). | request id, idempotency token |
| **Link** (skill) | What a **skill** sets up for one box: the vault secrets of the box's **template** a tool may use, through **hopper-secret**, for one **asset**, after Access allowed it. Names, never a value; the value comes just in time from the vault. | connection, tunnel, channel |
| **hopper-skill** | The command a job asks for **skills** with (issue #582): a POSIX sh and curl script kept in its **credentials dir** beside its **proxy token** and **hopper-gh**, run as `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" [NAME [ASSET]]`. `POST /job/skill`. With `--wait` it opens a **watch** and reads the answer from the **job stream** (issue #613); sh, curl and awk. | skill CLI |
| **Job stream** | What a running job subscribes to at `GET /job/stream` (issue #613, design.md "The job stream"): its own **stream events** over SSE, with its **proxy token**, replayed after `Last-Event-ID`, kept in the user's database so a restart loses none. `?request=ID`: one request's events, the stream ending after its last. Not the UI's `/api/events/stream` of domain events. | job feed, agent stream, event bus |
| **Stream event** | One event on a **job stream**: `type` and `payload`, the `request` it is of, and its **phase** — `started`, `progress`, `waiting` (a person is asked), `done`, `failed`, `expired`. The part that emits a type registers it with its phase, under its own name (`skill.loaded`); the hopper reads the phase, never the payload. Sent whole up to 4 KB, else as a **result pointer**. Not a domain event: never webhooked. | job event, message |
| **Result pointer** | A **stream event** sent without its payload: `ref: { url, bytes }`, where `GET /job/stream/results/<seq>` answers the whole event. One builder decides whole or pointer, so the event and its result are one object. | link, reference |
| **Watch** | A request a job waits on (issue #613): opened when a job's skill request is answered 202: by `hopper-skill --wait`, a new one; with no `--wait`, one per job and request (issue #998), so the hopper can end the job's **own wait** with the answer; with a **deadline** (an hour unless `--timeout` says; at most a day). It ends with the request's `done` or `failed` stream event, as `expired` (`hopper.expired`) at its deadline, or `failed` (`hopper.ended`) when its job ends. Kept in the database. The hopper asks a watch's request again when the vault changes and when the job subscribes. | wait (a *wait* is a Decision's, for a lane), subscription, poll |
| **Artifact** | How a running job presents its work to people (issue #624, design.md "Artifacts"; issue #675): a shareable, meaningful, dynamic page — a flowchart, a diagram, a chart, an interactive view — that shows the result itself, in whatever form shows it best, not a page of prose (an HTML artifact that shows nothing is kept with a warning). It has a title and a one-line **summary**, and every change to it is a **revision**. Kept in its user's database with its job, issue, type, size, SHA-256 and when it was made; served at a **stable URL** (`<hopper>/#artifacts/<id>`, the UI's Artifacts view) and its content off `/api/`, HTML and SVG only in a sandbox, where they may load the **artifact libraries**. Its owner is the user whose job made it. | document, attachment, output, file (in code: the domain word is artifact), slideshow |
| **Summary** (artifact) | One line that says what an **artifact** shows (issue #675, `--summary`): in the Artifacts list, on the job card, in a review card's embed and on the **share page**, at most 300 characters. | description, subtitle, caption |
| **Revision** (artifact) | One state of an **artifact** (issue #675), numbered from 1: every change — a put with `--to`, or a restore — makes the next, and the id and its links show the latest. Older ones stay readable until the retention sweep, unless **pinned**; a restore copies an old one as the newest, never overwrites. `#artifacts/<id>/<n>` and a public link's `?revision=N` pin one. | version (a proposal's is a version), history entry |
| **Pinned** (revision) | A **revision** the retention sweep keeps, and with it its **artifact** (issue #675): a person pins or unpins it in the UI. | kept, starred |
| **Merge** (artifacts) | An owner's act on **artifacts** (issue #687): each source's **revisions** are added to the target, oldest first, each still by the job that made it; then the source is removed. The target keeps its id and links; the newest added revision is its latest. | fold, combine, join |
| **Artifact libraries** | The diagram and chart libraries the hopper bundles and serves at `/artifact-lib/` — Mermaid, Chart.js, D3 — so a sandboxed **artifact** stays dynamic and still loads nothing from outside (issue #675). | CDN, vendor scripts |
| **Share page** | What a **public link** opens (issue #675): the hopper's own page, for a phone, with the **artifact**'s title and **summary** (and its Open Graph tags, for share previews) above its content, framed. | landing page, preview page |
| **hopper-artifact** | The command a job puts, lists, gets, shares and removes **artifacts** with (issue #624): a POSIX sh, curl and od script kept in its **credentials dir** beside its **proxy token** (`hopper/artifact`), run as `sh "$HOPPER_ARTIFACT"`. `--json` answers JSON; exit 0 done, 1 a no, 2 a bad call, 3 the hopper not reached. Its full help is the `artifacts` **skill**'s text. | artifact CLI |
| **Share** (artifact) | What lets someone other than its owner see an **artifact** (issue #624): with another user of the hopper, or a **public link**. Live until it is revoked, or a link until it expires; written to OpenFGA as a viewer of the artifact while live, and Access decides each view. | grant (a connected account's), permission |
| **Artifact-only issue** | An issue whose deliverable is an **artifact**, not a code change (issue #673): a line of its body says `Deliverable: artifact`. Its job is done when it made an artifact linked to the issue and a comment on the issue names it — `share ID --owner` posts that comment —; no pull request is needed. | report issue, document issue |
| **Public link** | A **share** of an **artifact** with anybody who holds its URL (`/artifact-link/<token>`), no session needed, until it expires (Settings → Artifacts: default and most hours). Its token is said once and only its hash is kept; an admin turns all of a user's off at once in Settings → Artifacts. | share link, public URL (the hopper's own) |
| **Content URL** | Where an **artifact**'s content loads for one viewer (`/artifact-content/<name>?v=<signed>`): signed by the read that checked the viewer, for an hour, under its owner's key, a system secret in the vault (issue #673), so it outlives a restart. A browser's `<img>` or `<iframe>` carries no session header, so the URL is the credential; the content route checks it, and Access again for another user's, at each load. | download link, signed link |
| **Renewer** | What keeps a **connected account**'s token alive (issue #441, `renewer.ts`): it renews the token an hour before it expires whether or not anything asks, at start too, and after a 401 — under the account's advisory lock, the row read again, the new pair kept by compare-and-swap on the refresh token used. | refresher, token refresh job |
| **Grant** | What GitHub hands the **hopper's app** for one account at one connect — a device flow or web flow connect, or a sign-in with GitHub (`Grant`, `device-flow.ts`): an access token and its refresh token. A renewal rotates the pair inside the grant; only a new connect makes a new one. GitHub keeps ten per user and app, and an eleventh revokes an older one (issue #514), so the hopper revokes the grant it replaces or drops (`revocation.ts`). | token (for the pair), authorization, OAuth token |
| **Renewal trouble** | A renewal that failed for a reason that may pass (issue #441): GitHub not answering, a 5xx, a rate limit, the lock held too long, the app's own credentials refused, a browser grant with no client secret. Shown on the account (`renewal`), tried again with backoff (`RETRY_MS`); it never ends the sign-in. | renewal error, soft failure |
| **Master key** | `HOPPER_MASTER_KEY`: the 32-byte key every **sealed secret** is sealed under (the **sealer**, `src/secrets/sealer.ts`, issue #451), and a **connected account**'s tokens (the token box, issue #441). Given at launch, as the variable or the file `HOPPER_MASTER_KEY_FILE` names — a mounted Secret (issue #1288) —, never from a volume of the hopper's own (issue #659); never in the database, which keeps only its **key fingerprint**. A fresh hopper given none makes one and shows it once, in the UI only, never in a log (issue #685, the **log mask**). Older keys, as `HOPPER_MASTER_KEY_PREVIOUS`, only open. None while secrets are kept: the hopper does not start, and names the **key fingerprint** it needs (issue #1296). It may be **sealed to the host**. | secret key (that was `HOPPER_SECRET_KEY`, gone since #56), token key (since #659) |
| **Launch setting** | A setting a container hopper's compose stack is given at launch, in the environment the compose command starts with — never in a file (issue #894): the **master key** and every other secret, and what compose itself needs (`HOPPER_PORT`, `HOPPER_BIND`, `HOPPER_IMAGE`, `COMPOSE_PROFILES`, …). Given at each `up` that recreates the hopper; a default an earlier update wrote into `compose.yaml` stays. The update requires the master key from the launch environment and stops without it (issue #970); it reads no secret from a file or the running hopper. A container install has no `.env`. | env file, .env setting |
| **Key fingerprint** | HMAC-SHA256 under the **master key** of a fixed label (issue #659): kept in the instance settings, compared at each start. Another key's fingerprint stops the start ("the master key does not match this database"). Never the key. | key id (the sealer's 16-digit mark in a sealed value), key hash |
| **Sealed to the host** | The **master key** kept by the host, encrypted so only that host opens it (issue #1296, `scripts/master-key.sh`): with `systemd-creds` (the host's TPM2, else its credential key) in `hopper-master-key.cred`, else under a **key-encryption key** in `hopper-master-key.enc`, beside `compose.yaml`. `master-key.sh run` unseals it at each launch and gives it as `HOPPER_MASTER_KEY` to that command only (`HOPPER_MASTER_KEY_SEALED` says how); the update unseals it too. Its rotation is the host's (`master-key.sh rotate`), which seals the new key in the same step. Replaces **limited** (issue #659: a hopper that started with no key; it does not start now). | sealed credential, unsealed key, limited (gone since #1296) |
| **Stack service** | `hopper-compose.service`, the user service that owns a Podman compose install's stack (issue #1320, `scripts/hopper-service.sh`, docs/deploy.md "The user service"): it starts the stack with the key **sealed to the host**, at boot with linger, so the published port's `rootlessport` belongs to it and not to the terminal or agent that ran `compose up`. Its timer, `hopper-compose-check.timer`, checks the port from the host each minute and restarts it when the port does not answer while the hopper's container runs. Restarts and updates go through it. Not on Docker or Kubernetes. | compose unit, podman-restart (that kept the containers before) |
| **Key-encryption key** | 32 random bytes of one host that a **master key sealed to the host** is sealed under when `systemd-creds` cannot seal there (issue #1296): `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/hopper/master-key.kek`, mode 600, made once, never in the compose folder or a volume. | KEK, host key |
| **Old token key** | The master key as an install from before #659 kept it: `HOPPER_TOKEN_KEY`, or the compose `secrets` volume's `token_key` (`HOPPER_TOKEN_KEY_FILE`; the compose file no longer mounts that volume). Read only while `HOPPER_MASTER_KEY` is unset, to move the install to it. | legacy key |
| **Master key rotation** | One command (issue #659: Vault → Master key, `POST /ui/api/master-key` `rotate`, `hopper master-key rotate`), the hopper's admin's: a new **master key**, every value an old key sealed sealed again under it in one transaction, the running hopper on it with no restart, the new key shown once (`source: rotated`). A start with the replaced key is then refused. Not with the vault in a container of its own, nor, with the key **sealed to the host**, without the host sealing the new key (`hostSeals`). Rotating at launch (`HOPPER_MASTER_KEY_PREVIOUS`) is the other way. `vault.master_key_rotated`. | key change, re-key |
| **Runtime secrets** | The compose volume `runtime-secrets` (issue #659): the files `postgres` makes at each start — the database password and URL, OpenFGA's and the vault container's preshared keys —, in memory (tmpfs), gone after a full stop. No secret is in a persistent volume. | secrets volume (that was the persistent one before #659) |
| **Held grant** | A GitHub sign-in's grant made while the user's **connected account** is healthy (issue #647, `held.ts`): checked at GitHub, kept apart in memory, never replacing the connection quietly. The person takes it (Use this sign-in: it replaces the connection) or drops it (Keep this connection: its token is deleted at GitHub); dropped by itself after 15 minutes. `ConnectedAccountStatus.held`, `take-held` / `drop-held`. | pending sign-in, staged grant |
| **Reconnect needed** | What the UI calls a **connected account** that is **expired** (issue #647): GitHub refused it — its refresh token, a renewal sent with no client secret (`incorrect_client_credentials`), or a 401 on the token it just renewed. Only connecting again (or a new sign-in with GitHub) mends it. | sign-in expired |
| **Credentials dir** | `<scratch>/credentials` on a job's machine (issue #441): the job's way to the hopper — its **proxy token**, `hopper-gh`, `hopper-skill`, `hopper-artifact` (issues #563, #582, #624) — kept through the machine's own connection, mode 600, removed by the reap. Never a GitHub token (issue #652; before it, the connected account's `gh/hosts.yml`). | token file (that is the github-app source's per-job file) |
| **Git through the hopper** | A job's git fetch and push to GitHub, sent to the hopper (issue #652, `src/github-proxy/git.ts`, `git-broker.ts`): the job's variables rewrite every GitHub remote to `<HOPPER_URL>/job/git/` and answer git's credential ask from its proxy token file; the hopper does the request on GitHub with the job's own user's connection. A push changes only a branch of the job's own repository (in the user's **job repositories**), never its default or a **release branch**, a tag or a delete; a fetch of any repository goes with that connection (issue #1015): a job reads any repository its user sees. A push is `github_proxy.done` / `.refused` / `.failed` with op `git.push`. | git proxy, push op |
| **Release branch** | A branch no job pushes to, whatever the repository's default branch is (issue #652, `RELEASE_BRANCHES`, `src/domain/landing-batches.ts`): `main`, `master`, `dev`, `beta`, `canary`, `stable` — besides the default branch. A pull request from one (a promotion) is no issue's work (issue #876): it never makes an issue done, is never followed for it, and its merge never closes it. | protected branch (that is GitHub's own setting) |
| **Untrusted issue text** | The issue's title, body and recent comments in a job's prompt (issue #652), inside a block between two markers (`UNTRUSTED_START`, `UNTRUSTED_END`) and after a line that says it is data, not instructions (`UNTRUSTED_LINE`): the job learns the change from it and does not follow an instruction in it that changes credentials, CI, branches or other repositories. The markers are removed from the text. The hopper's own **issue context** follows the block. | user input, issue prompt |
| **Job repositories** | The repositories a **connected account**'s jobs may come from, chosen in Sources from those the **hopper's app** reaches (issue #321): the user's setting, kept across a disconnect. Each connected account has its own, and a repository is one account's only (issue #1448): the **acting identity** for it. None chosen: its source is paused and takes no job — never every repository by default. `jobRepositories`, `choose`. | allowlist, selected repos, scope |
| **Hopper's app** | The GitHub App the hopper ships, which people sign in and connect through (issue #214): only its public client id and its public slug (for its install link) — no private key, no client secret: the device flow needs none. GitHub marks the app on what a person does through it, and it reaches only the repositories it is installed on. An install may name another (`HOPPER_GITHUB_CLIENT_ID`, `HOPPER_GITHUB_APP_SLUG`). Every GitHub read and write of the hopper goes through it, with the signed-in user's token (issue #1377). | OAuth app, OAuth client, integration app |
| **Installation** | Where an account installed a GitHub App, and the repos it reaches there (all of the account's, or chosen ones). The **hopper's app**: Sources lists each one the connected account can see with its repos and what GitHub granted the app there (`AppInstallation`, issues #253, #352). | |
| **Bot login** | The app's author name on GitHub; how old hopper comments are identified for the context filter (the marker is secondary). | |
| **Paused** (source) | A job source that must not discover new items right now — a connected account's source while that account is not connected, its sign-in expired, or no job repository is chosen. It still checks and reports its own active jobs. Only for sources; a job waiting on a question is *waiting answer*, never "paused". A paused source with no active jobs reports state `disabled` and is still paused. | |
| **Event** | One recorded state change, `seq`-ordered, in the event log. Wire type dotted (`job.queued`). | message |
| **Webhook subscription** | A URL + event filter + its **signing secret** that receives events. One row of the store's `webhooks` table — the only place it is kept (issue #78) — keyed by its `name`; *active* or paused. Changed by the UI's webhooks edits (**add** — with a signing secret typed in, or one the hopper makes; **edit** — url, events, active; **replace** and **rotate** — its signing secret; **remove**), one subscription each. One from before issue #451 with no stored secret names the runtime variable it reads (`secretEnv`). | hook |
| **Signing secret** | A webhook subscription's HMAC-SHA256 key (issue #451): the hopper's own, typed in or made by it (32 random bytes), kept as a **sealed secret**, write-only. **Replace** stores one typed in; **rotate** has the hopper make one, shown once (`generatedSecret`). Used from the next delivery. | webhook secret (in prose only), secret variable (that was `secretEnv`) |
| **Sealed secret** | A secret the hopper owns, kept in the database sealed by the **sealer** under the **master key** (issue #451): `hs1.<key id>.<salt>.<nonce>.<ciphertext>`, a key of its own per value (HKDF of the master key, a salt and its **context**), AES-256-GCM, padded. Its **context** is where it is kept (`webhook:<id>/signing-secret`): moved anywhere else it does not open. One that does not open is **unreadable** (`SecretUnreadable`), never "no secret". | encrypted secret, ciphertext (in prose only) |
| **Notifier** | The role that tells something outside about events (`Notifier` port: `start(events)`, `stop()`, and optionally `test()` and `sendOpen()`: its **actions** in the UI, issue #378). 0..n instances (the plugins config's `notifiers`; absent → the built-in `grok-bot` instance). Built-in plugin `grokbot-routine`. One that cannot run is dropped, its reason in `/api/plugins`. Not a *Webhook subscription*: it keeps a **notifier delivery**, not a delivery. | connector (issue #6's word for a source or notifier instance) |
| **Grok Bot routine webhook** | The one POST (URL and bearer key from the runtime — the variable or the mounted `<name>_FILE`, read at each use — `GROKBOT_WEBHOOK_URL` and `GROKBOT_WEBHOOK_KEY`, with the user's **secret prefix**, unless the `grokbot-routine` notifier's `urlEnv`/`keyEnv` name others) to a Grok Bot routine when a question reaches the human (`question.escalated_to_human` only, issue #481), or intake stops. Not a *Webhook subscription*; nothing stored. | |
| **Test event** | A marked payload sent once, on the owner's **Send test event**, to check a notifier's or a webhook subscription's URL and secret without waiting for a real event (issue #378): the notifier's `kind: question.escalated_to_human` with `test: true`; the subscription's signed event of the first type it names, with `data.test: true` (`webhook.test` for `*` only; issue #481). Never in the event log; no delivery row. | ping, dry run |
| **Offered** (question) | A question the notifier sends from the questions open at the human — when its routine becomes configured, or on **Send open questions** — rather than as it escalates (issue #378): `offered: true` in the body. | resent, replayed |
| **Install** | The directory the daemon runs from (`~/.local/lib/hopper` from `scripts/install.sh`), with `install.json`: the repository, tracked branch and commit it was built from. An image carries one too (`kind: image`, issue #409), written at its build; self-update checks it and lists its history but never applies to it. Without install.json, or with a field missing, self-update is unavailable. | deployment, copy |
| **Curl install** | `scripts/get.sh` piped from the repository's raw URL into bash (issue #87): checks Node.js ≥ 24, git, npm and systemd, clones or updates the **source clone** (`~/.local/share/hopper/source`, `HOPPER_SRC`), gives it a database — daemon.env's, `HOPPER_DATABASE_URL`, else the bundled Postgres with a fresh password — and runs its `scripts/install.sh`. | one-liner, bootstrap, installer |
| **Windows CLI wrapper** | `scripts/hopper.cmd` or `scripts/hopper.ps1` on a folder of the Windows PATH (issue #712): runs the operator CLI, `hopper`, with the person's arguments in the hopper's container in WSL — found by its compose labels, in the distro `HOPPER_WSL_DISTRO` names (else WSL's default) and the project `HOPPER_COMPOSE_PROJECT` names (else `hopper`) — through `wsl.exe -e podman exec`. It keeps no credential: the CLI uses the container's. `hopper autostart on|off|status` (`hopper.ps1`, which `hopper.cmd` runs from its folder) keeps that distro running after a Windows sign-in and after `wsl --shutdown`: linger, `podman-restart.service`, and a keep-alive loop a shortcut in the user's Startup folder starts, with no admin rights (`docs/deploy.md` "Keep it running on Windows"). `hopper doctor` checks from Windows, reading only, what that hopper needs — the Windows link, the networking mode and `.wslconfig`, the keep-alive shortcut, and in the distro systemd, linger and `podman-restart.service` — and gives the fix for each check that fails (`docs/deploy.md` "Check it from Windows"). | Windows client (that is a machine's client), Windows port |
| **Update** | A newer commit than the installed one on the update channel (the **update target**: `ref` + commit), with what's new in it. *Available* while the installed commit does not contain the target. **Applied** in flight: built beside the install, swapped in, then a restart. | upgrade, release |
| **What's new** | The plain-language lines an update brings, for people who use the hopper: the bullets at the update target that the installed commit lacks, newest first (issue #104): the **fragments** in `whats-new/` (one file per change, issue #824), then `WHATS-NEW.md`, where the release assembly moves them. Never commit subjects, hashes or issue numbers. | release notes, changelog, what changed |
| **Version history** | The versions the installed commit is made of, newest first: each commit of the tracked branch (first parent: a merged pull request is one version) that added what's new lines, with the day it landed and those lines (issue #246). Settings → Version history. | changelog, release history, install log |
| **Update channel** | What counts as newer: `dev`, `beta` or `stable`, every commit on the branch of that name (issues #282, #423). A setting in the store; unset, the branch the install came from when it is a channel, else `stable`. UI: `dev` / `beta` / `stable`. | track, stream, main, release |
| **Held update** | A newer head of the update channel that is not the merge of a pull request whose checks passed, or that GitHub could not be asked about (issue #652, `src/update/release-check.ts`): `UpdateState` `held`, with the reason. It is not announced, offered or applied. UI: "Update held". | blocked update, pending update |
| **Promotion** | Moving a channel's branch up to the next steadier one: `dev` → `beta` → `stable`, a fast-forward of the steadier branch to a commit the other one already has and built, by a maintainer with `scripts/promote.sh` (issues #282, #423; `docs/deploy.md` "Update channels and promotion"); `beta` → `stable` goes through the **canary** (issue #1073 part 5). Below `dev`, a green **batch run** merges the **landing branch** into `dev` (issue #1073): the hopper's own act, `landing_batch.promoted`. | release (a tag), deploy |
| **Auto-update** | The setting that applies an available update as soon as a check finds it. | |
| **Next install** | `<install>.next`: the update target built by `scripts/install.sh` in **build-only mode** (`HOPPER_INSTALL_INTO`), beside the running install, then swapped in; the replaced install stays as `<install>.prev`. | staged install (*stage* is a question's) |
| **Apply record** | `<data dir>/update/pending.json` (issue #1626): the apply of an update in flight — from, to, ref and its phase (`building`, `waiting`, `swapping`, `restarting`), written as each phase starts. A start that finds one settles the apply: `update.applied` when the install is on `to`, else `update.failed`; the next install is removed. | pending file |
| **Restart blocker** | A running job a restart would lose — its executor is non-idempotent and cannot reattach. Applying an update waits until there is none. | |
| **Shipped compose file** | The `compose.yaml` an image carries for its own commit (issue #697, `src/update/shipped-compose.ts`), stamped on its first line with its **version stamp** `# hopper version: <branch> <commit>`. `GET /api/update/compose`. A container install's update always puts in the shipped compose file of the image it updates to. | release compose |
| **Update command** | The one update of a container install (issues #697, #970, #1104, `src/update/update-commands.ts`, `scripts/update.sh`): `<runtime> run --rm --pull=always <image> hopper update script \| bash -s -- <image>`, in the folder of `compose.yaml`. It takes the master key from the launch or asks for it, and refuses without it; uses the compose tool and project (`hopper`) that started the stack; backs up the database; checks the new file with `compose.override.yaml` for a **compose conflict** first; puts in the new file from `hopper update carry` (the shipped compose file, the install's own settings as its defaults); pulls, stops the whole stack and starts it on the image; and checks the image, the health, the master key and the stack drift. No `.env`. Never the hopper service alone. | upgrade script, update commands |
| **Compose conflict** | What `compose.yaml` and `compose.override.yaml` clash on once compose merges them (issue #1246, `src/update/compose-conflicts.ts`): a volume target mounted twice or a host port published twice into one service, which stops the update before it changes anything (`hopper update check`, exit 4); or an override setting with another value than one `compose.yaml` fixes, a warning. **Fixable** when the override only repeats what `compose.yaml` gives: the update drops that entry when the person accepts it (`HOPPER_UPDATE_FIX_OVERRIDE=1` with no person), keeping `compose.override.yaml.bak`. | merge error, override clash |
| **Stack drift** | How the stack a container hopper runs in differs from the shipped compose file of its own version (issue #697, `src/update/drift.ts`): a service that must run and does not, settings the hopper's container lacks, a `hopper-vault` on another version — each a **stack gap**. For a **cluster install** (issue #1285): the image it runs is another channel's than the update channel, a gap of kind `image`; its fix is `helm upgrade` of the release. `GET /api/update/drift`; a banner over every view; the fix is the **update command**. | compose drift, stack mismatch |
| **Agent update skill** | The steps an agent follows to update a container hopper (issue #697, `src/update/update-skill.ts`), as Markdown: `GET /api/update/skill`, `hopper update skill`. | |
| **Delivery** | One attempt series sending one event to one subscription. `pending`, `retrying`, `delivered`, `failed`. A delivery asked while its destination's **circuit** is open fails, saying why; one with no token waits, due, and spends no attempt (issue #1014). | |
| **Notifier delivery** | One notification a **notifier** sends about an event, kept in the user's store until it is delivered or fails (issue #1587, `notifier_deliveries`, `src/domain/notifier-deliveries.ts`): its key — what the receiver dedupes by, sent as `x-hopper-delivery` —, the event, `pending`, `retrying`, `delivered`, `failed`, the attempts, the last error and the next try. A stop loses none: the next start sends what is due. Not a **delivery**, which goes to a webhook subscription. | notification row, notifier outbox |
| **Outbound send** | One POST the hopper makes to a webhook: a **delivery**'s attempt, or a **notifier**'s post (issue #1014). Each passes the **outbound guard** of its destination and goes into the **send log**. `src/outbound/`, `src/domain/outbound.ts`. | outgoing request, ping |
| **Outbound guard** | What every outbound send passes, one per destination (its URL) and one per process, so every user's sends to one destination share it (issue #1014): a **rate limit** (a token bucket: 60 at once, 5 more each second), a **dedupe** (a send whose key — the event, for a delivery or a notifier's post — was asked within 10 minutes is a **duplicate**, never posted), and a **circuit**. A send it holds is never posted. | rate limiter (*the shared rate limiter* is GitHub's), throttle |
| **Circuit** (outbound) | A destination's switch in the outbound guard: it **opens** on a **runaway** — more than 600 new sends asked of it within a minute — and stays open 5 minutes, while nothing is sent to it; opening logs one line naming its host and the last sender. `circuits` in `GET /api/outbound`. | breaker, kill switch |
| **Send log** | The user's record of every outbound send (`outbound_sends`, `GET /api/outbound`, issue #1014): one row per POST — `sent` or `failed`, with its status —, one per held send — `rate-limited`, `duplicate`, `circuit-open` —, its repeats within a minute counted on one more row (`count`). It names the sender (`webhook`, `notifier`), its name, the destination's host (never its path or query), the kind and the job. Kept 14 days. | outbound log, audit log (*the vault's audit trail* is another) |
| **Help pages** | The hopper's user and operator help (issue #782): one Markdown file per feature under `help/`, the one source; the daemon serves them as HTML at `/help/` for a person and as Markdown at `/help/<page>.md` (listed at `/help/index.json`) for an agent, and a job reads them with `sh "$HOPPER_SKILL" help`. Their contents page is `help/index.md`. Not the internal docs under `docs/`. | help docs, manual, user docs |
| **API reference** | The daemon's description of its own HTTP API (issue #68): the OpenAPI document `src/http/openapi.ts` builds, at `/docs/openapi.json`, rendered by Scalar at `/docs/`. Parameters and bodies are the schemas the routes parse with; the daemon refuses to start when a route under `/api/` or `/ui/` and the reference disagree. | API docs, swagger, spec |
| **Dashboard panel** | One part of the Dashboard the dashboard layout places: the numbers (KPI cards), lane timeline, attention, lanes, waiting, ended, ended per hour, usage, usage over time (the usage graph), live activity. | widget, tile, card |
| **List box** | A panel that lists items beside others on one screen: on the Dashboard attention, lanes, waiting, ended, live activity; the Queue's lists. Its list scrolls inside one height bound, so a long list does not stretch its row (issue #84). A list that is the page is no list box: it scrolls with the page (issue #2354). `Panel` `list`. | list panel, scroll box |
| **Dashboard layout** | The viewer's arrangement of the Dashboard (issue #73): which dashboard panels show, in what order, how wide (a third, two thirds or the full row), and each panel's settings (lane timeline window, ended-per-hour hours, live activity events). Set from the Dashboard's Customize, and rearranged by dragging on the Dashboard (Arrange) or in Customize (issue #86); kept per browser (`localStorage` `jh_overview`), not by the hopper. `ui/src/model/dashboard-layout.ts`. | dashboard config, widgets |
| **Header badge** | A Dashboard KPI card's total shown in the header on every screen (issue #1181): the cards the user picks in Customize, kept in the user's settings on the hopper. Each badge reads its card's own total, so the two never disagree. Default: one Lanes badge, the lanes holding a job (busy and draining). `HeaderBadge`, `src/domain/header-badges.ts`. | header counter, chip |
| **Telemetry** | What the hopper exports through OpenTelemetry (issue #1486, design.md "Telemetry"): traces, metrics and log records, to the exporters the launch's `OTEL_*` variables name, else to the endpoint of the **telemetry setting**. Off unless one names an exporter. Never carries a secret, a prompt, an item's text or event data. | observability, monitoring, tracing (each a part of it) |
| **Telemetry setting** | The hopper's admin's setting of the **telemetry** export (issue #1486, design.md "Telemetry settings", Settings → Telemetry): on or off, the OTLP endpoint and the signals. It applies at once, and only while the launch's `OTEL_*` variables name no exporter: the launch wins. Holds no credential: a collector's header is given at launch. | telemetry config (the launch's variables are part of it, not the setting) |
| **Telemetry summary** | The most useful numbers of the **telemetry**, kept in the hopper's own process since it started, telemetry on or off (issue #1486, design.md "Telemetry summary", `GET /api/telemetry`): the slowest actions, each kind of **job span**'s duration, and the GitHub calls by route. Names only, never a URL, an id or a value. The Resources page shows it as the Telemetry panel. | dashboard metrics, stats |
| **Job trace** | A job's one trace (issue #1486): a root span `hopper.job` from the first event seen to the job's end, and its **job spans**. A running job's processes get its run span as `TRACEPARENT` (its **trace context**), so its work and its requests to the hopper join it. | job timeline (the UI's event list) |
| **Trace context** | The variables a job's processes run with to join its **job trace** (issue #1486, design.md "Telemetry: a job's trace on its machine"): `TRACEPARENT`, and `TRACESTATE` when there is one, naming its open run span, else its root. None while telemetry is off. | trace id, trace header (the header is `traceparent`, which the job's scripts send) |
| **Job span** | A child span of a **job trace** for one part of the job's life, by kind: `queue`, `run`, `question`, `wait`, `park`, `sandbox` (one per sandbox request), `pull_request` (issue #1486). Timed by the job's events. | stage (a *stage* is where a question is), phase (`job.phase_changed`) |
| **Job step** | Work the hopper does for one job outside its events — the job's worktree on its machine, a follow of its pull request (the hopper's merge, Merge now, Close PR), the base merge, the start of a tend, the run of a need box (part 9) — as a span of the **job trace**: a child of its open **job span**, else its root, linked to the span that ran it (issue #1486 part 7, design.md "Telemetry: job steps"). | stage, job span (a job span is timed by events) |
| **Deploy span** | A span `hopper.job.deploy` in a **job trace** for the landing batch run that holds the job's pull request (issue #1486 part 9, design.md "Telemetry: a box's own processes and deploys"): as long as the batch run, linked to the batch run's own span `hopper.landing_batch`, ended with its outcome (`promoted`, `failed`, `restarted`). | deployment, release (a release is a promotion to a channel) |
| **Cause link** | A link from an outbox flush's or a sync's span, the root of a trace of its own, to the span of the action that asked for it: a person's request (issue #1486, design.md "Telemetry: the outbox and the sync"). A flush or sync asked for by many actions has a link to each; a timer's sync, and one asked for by the sync before it, has none. | parent span, follows-from |

## Events

| domain name | wire type |
|-------------|-----------|
| JobQueued | `job.queued` |
| JobPrioritized (by the router) | `job.prioritized` |
| JobHeld | `job.held` |
| JobApproved | `job.approved` |
| JobClaimed | `job.claimed` |
| JobStarted | `job.started` |
| JobProgressed | `job.progressed` |
| JobFinished | `job.finished` |
| JobFailed | `job.failed` |
| JobCancelled | `job.cancelled` |
| JobRequeued (via restart) | `job.requeued` |
| JobReattached (via restart) | `job.reattached` |
| JobLinkLost (its machine's link dropped while it ran; held) | `job.link_lost` |
| LaneOpened | `lane.opened` |
| LaneClosed | `lane.closed` |
| DecisionMade | `decision.made` |
| RouterModeChanged (retired, issue #211) | `router.mode_changed` (was `jev.mode_changed`); nothing emits it, stored ones still read |
| QuestionAsked | `question.asked` |
| QuestionEscalated (to a stage) | `question.escalated` |
| QuestionEscalatedToHuman (reached the human stage, once per question) | `question.escalated_to_human` |
| QuestionAnswered (by an escalation level, or the human) | `question.answered` |
| QuestionClosed (by the human, without an answer) | `question.closed` |
| QuestionDismissed (by the human; needs no action) | `question.dismissed` |
| QuestionExpired | `question.expired` |
| QuestionLapsed | `question.lapsed` |
| QuestionCancelled (its job ended or is gone) | `question.cancelled` |
| UpdateAvailable | `update.available` |
| UpdateStarted | `update.started` |
| UpdateApplied | `update.applied` |
| UpdateFailed | `update.failed` |
| PluginInstalled (from the plugin store) | `plugin.installed` |
| PluginRemoved (a store install) | `plugin.removed` |
| JobAccepted (by the pre-sort, or the user) | `job.accepted` |
| JobRejected (by the user, or the pre-sort) | `job.rejected` |
| JobUnassigned (its issue no longer assigned to its assignee, while started) | `job.unassigned` |
| JobReassigned (a flagged job's issue assigned to its assignee again) | `job.reassigned` |
| JobRespecified (a waiting job that has not started took the config as it is now) | `job.respecified` |
| QueueOrdered (by the user) | `queue.ordered` |
| QueueGateChanged | `queue.gate_changed` |
| JobRerun (by the user: Run again on a failed or finished job) | `job.rerun` |
| JobReworkRequested (a person sent a done job's pull request back for rework; a new job reworks it) | `job.rework_requested` |
| JobDismissed (by the user: a locked entry dismissed) | `job.dismissed` |
| ItemSnapshotRecorded (an item's approved text recorded: at intake, by the backfill, or accepted) | `item.snapshot_recorded` |
| ItemChangedSinceSnapshot (a job held: its item's text changed since the snapshot) | `item.changed_since_snapshot` |
| ItemOriginalKept (a person chose Rerun the original) | `item.original_kept` |
| ItemNewTextAccepted (the owner accepted an item's new text) | `item.new_text_accepted` |
| JobWorkKept (the reap kept a job's scratch dir: uncommitted or unpushed work in it) | `job.work_kept` |
| JobWorkRemoved (the sweep or the job's next reap removed a scratch dir the reap had kept: its work now pushed; or a person discarded it) | `job.work_removed` |
| JobWorkDiscardFailed (a person's discard of kept work failed on the job's machine, after the answer; the work is still kept) | `job.work_discard_failed` |
| JobGiveBackFailed (a person's Run again or Rework was answered, and its source's give-back failed after; no job was made) | `job.give_back_failed` |
| JobCleanupDeferred (its cleanup could not reach its machine; tried again) | `job.cleanup_deferred` |
| JobCleanedUp (a deferred cleanup went through, or one a stop cut short went through after the start, or the user marked it closed) | `job.cleaned_up` |
| JobPodRemoved (the sweep deleted an ended job's pod that was still there) | `job.pod_removed` |
| SourceStalled (a job source in error past the stall threshold) | `source.stalled` |
| ConnectedAccountExpired (a connected account's sign-in ended: reconnect needed) | `connected_account.expired` |
| ConnectedAccountRenewed (the renewer kept a new token; its new expiry, never a token) | `connected_account.renewed` |
| ConnectedAccountRenewalFailed (a renewal failed; its error code) | `connected_account.renewal_failed` |
| AuthPending (a job or run waits on a login; again with `renewed` when the same tool shows a new code) | `auth.pending` |
| AuthCompleted (the machine went on after the login) | `auth.completed` |
| AuthExpired (the login's code ran out first) | `auth.expired` |
| AuthCancelled (by the user) | `auth.cancelled` |
| AuthFailed (what waited on the login ended first) | `auth.failed` |
| JobAssessed (the failure assessor judged a failed job) | `job.assessed` |
| FailureGrouped (a failed job grouped into a problem) | `failure.grouped` |
| FailureResolved (a problem resolved, its held jobs released) | `failure.resolved` |
| HandoffOpened (a failed job handed off to a person: Needs a person) | `handoff.opened` |
| HandoffClosed (a hand-off ended: run again, continued, done by hand, won't do, or its job finished; `resolution` when a person resolved it) | `handoff.closed` |
| JobContinued (a failed job continued in its own agent session by Continue on its hand-off, or an ended job by Push it on its work not pushed) | `job.continued` |
| JobFinishBriefed (a job that left its own pull request in conflict, with failing checks or as a draft goes on with the finish brief) | `job.finish_briefed` |
| JobChecksRerun (a job ended done with its own pull request's checks red: the hopper ran its failed checks again once, before it sends the job back to fix them) | `job.checks_rerun` |
| JobChecksRerunAsked (the hopper asks GitHub to run those failed checks again: recorded before it asks, so a stop makes no second run again) | `job.checks_rerun_asked` |
| JobPullRequestTended (a PR waiting job's pull request with merge conflicts or failing checks: its item runs again with the tend brief) | `job.pull_request_tended` |
| JobPullRequestBaseMerged (a PR waiting job's pull request with merge conflicts: the hopper merged its base branch into it in its mirror and pushed, no job) | `job.pull_request_base_merged` |
| JobPullRequestRetargeted (a followed job's open pull request based off the **landing branch**: the hopper changed its base to it, no job) | `job.pull_request_retargeted` |
| JobPullRequestFollowed (reconciling found a done job's item's open pull request: the job follows it from now on) | `job.pull_request_followed` |
| JobPullRequestSeen (the follow saw what a followed pull request waits on change: its **pull request wait**) | `job.pull_request_seen` |
| WorkItemIssueClosed (GitHub closed a work item's issue, as a GitHub delivery or the ledger's fallback read said) | `work_item.issue_closed` |
| WorkItemIssueReopened (GitHub reopened a work item's issue, as a GitHub delivery or the ledger's fallback read said) | `work_item.issue_reopened` |
| UsageLimitsChanged (the soft and hard usage limits set in the UI) | `usage.limits_changed` |
| LanesRecommended (a machine's lane recommendation changed, recorded) | `lanes.recommended` |
| LaneTuningChanged (an admin changed a machine's lane tuning settings) | `lanes.tuning_changed` |
| LanesApplied (an admin applied a machine's lane recommendation) | `lanes.applied` |
| LanesUndone (an admin undid the last lane recommendation applied) | `lanes.undone` |
| LanesDismissed (an admin dismissed a machine's lane recommendation until it moves) | `lanes.dismissed` |
| BugReportFiled (a person filed a bug report, or added it to a similar open one) | `bug_report.filed` |
| BugReportFailed (a bug report was not filed: GitHub refused it for good) | `bug_report.failed` |
| BugReportKept (a bug report kept to send later: no GitHub connection, or GitHub could not take it now) | `bug_report.kept` |
| DirectJobSubmitted (a person submitted a direct job) | `direct_job.submitted` |
| DirectJobPromoted (a person promoted a direct job to GitHub, or promoted it again) | `direct_job.promoted` |
| DirectJobPromotionRevoked (a person revoked a direct job's promotion: GitHub keeps what was posted) | `direct_job.promotion_revoked` |
| IssueDrafted (File an issue drafted an issue with the person's own signed-in AI connection: nothing filed) | `issue_draft.drafted` |
| IssueDraftFiled (a person confirmed and filed an issue File an issue drafted) | `issue_draft.filed` |
| PointedJobCreated (a person made a pointed job through the API) | `pointed_job.created` |
| PointedJobStatusChanged (a pointed job moved along an allowed transition) | `pointed_job.status_changed` |
| PushRequirementsEvaluated (a job evaluated the push requirements of a commit in its work tree) | `push_requirements.evaluated` |
| PushRequirementDefaultsChanged (an admin changed the default push requirements) | `push_requirements.defaults_changed` |
| PushRequirementsSummarized (the hopper replaced the push requirements summary in a job's pull request after its push) | `push_requirements.summarized` |
